

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Geography 
 Solar System And Its Planets 
 Q.1.  What  are  the  streams  of  charged 
 particles  ejected  by  the  Sun  into  space 
 called ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Sunspots  (b) Solar wind 
 (c) Solar flares  (d) Coronal loops 
 Sol.1.(b)  Solar  wind.  It  is  a  constant 
 stream  of  charged  particles  (mostly 
 protons  and  electrons)  that  are  released 
 from  the  Sun's  upper  atmosphere,  known 
 as  the  corona.  The  solar  wind  blows  in  all 
 directions.  It  continues  moving  at  that 
 speed  for  about  10  billion  kilometers  (six 
 billion  miles).  Sunspots  are  dark  surface 
 spots,  Solar  flar es  are  sudden  bursts  of 
 energy,  and  Coronal  loops  are  arcs  of  hot 
 plasma shaped by magnetic fields. 
 Q.2.  Which  of  the  following  systems  can 
 be affected by a solar storm? 
 1.  GPS signal accuracy 
 2.  Power transmission systems 
 3.  Submarine communication cables 
 4.  Pipeline infrastructure 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) 2 and 3 only 
 (b) 1 and 2 only 
 (c) 3 and 4 only 
 (d) 1, 2, 3, and 4 all of these 
 Sol.2.(d)  1,  2,  3,  and  4  all  of  these.  Solar 
 storms  release  high-energy  charged 
 particles  and  electromagnetic  radiation 
 that  disturb  Earth’s  magnetosphere.  This 
 can  disrupt  GPS  accuracy,  power  grids, 
 submarine  cables,  and  pipelines  by 
 inducing  currents  and  affecting  signal 
 transmission.  Hence,  all  four  listed 
 systems are vulnerable. 
 Q.3.  Consider the following statements: 
 1. 
 A  solar  eclipse-  The  Moon  casts  a 
 shadow  on  Earth  when  it  comes 
 between the Sun and Earth. 
 2. 
 A  lunar  eclipse-  During  the  new  moon 
 phase,  the  Moon  passes  through  the 
 Earth's shadow. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct ? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Both 1 and 2  (b) 1 only 
 (c) 2 only  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.3.(b)  1  only.  Statement  2  :  Incorrect. 
 A  lunar  eclipse  occurs  at  the  full  moon 
 phase,  not  new  moon.  It  happens  when 
 the  Earth  comes  between  the  Sun  and 
 Moon,  and  Earth’s  shadow  falls  on  the 
 Moon. 
 Q.4.  Which  of  the  following  is  the  correct 
 sequence  in  the  life  cycle  of  a  star  like 
 the Sun? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Protostar → White Dwarf → Red 
 Giant → Main Sequence 
 (b) Nebula → Red Giant → Main 
 Sequence → White Dwarf 
 (c) Main Sequence → Red Giant → White 
 Dwarf 
 (d) Red Giant → Protostar → White 
 Dwarf 
 Sol.4.(c)  Main  Sequence  →  Red  Giant  → 
 White Dwarf. 
 The  life  cycle  of  a  medium-sized  star 
 (like  our  Sun)  follows  a  specific 
 sequence: 
 1.  Nebula  /  Protostar  stage  –  Stars  are 
 born  in  large  clouds  of  gas  and  dust 
 (nebula).  The  contracting  gas  forms  a 
 protostar. 
 2.Main  Sequence  –  The  star  spends 
 most  of  its  life  in  this  stable  phase, 
 where  hydrogen  fusion  occurs  in  the  core 
 (the Sun is currently in this stage). 
 3.  Red  Giant  –  After  hydrogen  in  the  core 
 is  exhausted,  the  star  expands  and  cools 
 to become a Red Giant. 
 4.  White  Dwarf  –  Finally,  after  shedding 
 its  outer  layers  (planetary  nebula),  the 
 star’s  remaining  dense  core  becomes  a 
 White Dwarf. 
 Q.5.  How  does  the  Faint  Young  Sun 
 paradox  challenge  our  understanding  of 
 early Earth's climate? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) It implies there was no liquid water on 
 early Earth. 
 (b) It proves the Moon once had an 
 atmosphere. 
 (c) It suggests that early solar output 
 was too weak to prevent global 
 freezing. 
 (d) It shows Earth was too hot due to 
 excessive solar radiation. 
 Sol.5.(c)  The  Faint  Young  Sun  paradox 
 arises  because  about  4  billion  years  ago, 
 the  Sun’s  luminosity  was  only  around 
 70%  of  its  current  strength,  which  should 
 have  left  Earth  frozen.  However, 
 geological  evidence  shows  that  liquid 
 water  existed,  supporting  life’s  early 
 evolution.  This  paradox  challenges 
 climate  science  by  suggesting  that 
 greenhouse  gases  or  other  warming 
 mechanisms  must  have  compensated 
 for  the  weaker  Sun,  preventing  global 
 freezing  and  maintaining  habitable 
 conditions on early Earth. 
 Q.6.  Which  type  of  galaxy  has  no  regular 
 shape and is rich in gas and dust? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Elliptical Galaxy 
 (b) Lenticular Galaxies 
 (c) Irregular Galaxy 
 (d) Spiral Galaxy 
 Sol.6.(c)  Irregular  Galaxy.  Irregular 
 galaxies  are  those  that  lack  a  distinct 
 shape  (unlike  elliptical  or  spiral  galaxies). 
 They  are  often  rich  in  gas  and  dust, 
 making  them  sites  of  active  star 
 formation.  Examples  include  the  Large 
 Magellanic  Cloud  (LMC)  and  Small 
 Magellanic  Cloud  (SMC)  near  the  Milky 
 Way.  Elliptical  Galaxy  –  Oval-shaped, 
 contains  older  stars,  and  has  little  gas  or 
 dust.  Lenticular  Galaxies  –  A  hybrid  form 
 between  elliptical  and  spiral  galaxies; 
 disk-shaped  but  lacking  significant  spiral 
 arms.  Spiral  Galaxy  –  Flat,  rotating  disk 
 with  spiral  arms,  contains  gas,  dust,  and 
 young stars (e.g., Milky Way). 
 Q.7.  Which  planet  has  the  longest  day  in 
 the solar system? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Venus (b) Mars (c) Jupiter (d) Earth 
 Sol.7.(a)  Venus.  A  day  on  a  planet  is 
 measured  by  its  rotation  period  (time 
 taken  to  complete  one  rotation  on  its 
 axis).  Venus  rotates  very  slowly  and  in 
 the  opposite  direction  (retrograde 
 rotation).  One  day  on  Venus  (sidereal 
 day)  =  243  Earth  days  →  the  longest  day 
 in  the  solar  system.  Venus  -  Rotation 
 period  =  243  Earth  days.Mars  -  Similar  to 
 Earth;  24  hours  37  minutes.  Jupiter  - 
 Fastest  rotation;  ~9  hours  56  minutes 
 (shortest day).Earth - 24 hours. 
 Q.8.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 about the Geoid: 
 1. 
 The  term  Geoid  refers  to  the  axis  of 
 the Earth. 
 2. 
 The  Earth  is  slightly  flattened  at  the 
 poles  and  bulges  at  the  equator  due 
 to  the  outward  force  caused  by  its 
 rotation. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.8.(b)  2  only.  Geoid  refers  to  the 
 shape of the Earth, not its axis. 
 Q.9.  Who  gave  the  expanding  universe 
 hypothesis? 
 (a) Galileo Galilei 
 (b) Edwin Hubble 
 (c) James Clerk Maxwell 
 (d) CV Raman 
 Sol.9.(b)  Edwin  Hubble.  The  most 
 popular  argument  regarding  the  origin  of 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 the  universe  is  the  Big  Bang  Theory.  It  is 
 also  called  the  expanding  universe 
 hypothesis.  Edwin  Hubble,  in  1920, 
 provided  evidence  that  the  universe  is 
 expanding.  As  time  passes,  galaxies 
 move  further  and  further  apart.  Galileo 
 Galilei  is  known  for  his  contributions  to 
 astronomy,  particularly  with  the  use  of 
 the telescope. 
 Q.10.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are correct? 
 Statement  1:  Callisto,  Europa,  and 
 Ganymede  are  natural  satellites  of 
 Jupiter. 
 Statement  2:  Triton  is  the  largest  moon 
 of Neptune. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 
 (b) Only Statement 2 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 
 (d) None of the above 
 Sol.10.(c)  Both  Statement  1  and  2. 
 Jupiter,  the  most  massive  planet  of  the 
 solar  system  and  the  fifth  in  distance 
 from  the  Sun.  Revolution  -  11  years  and 
 11  months,  Rotation  -  9  hours  56 
 minutes. 
 Q.11.  Which  is,  by  far,  the  closest  dwarf 
 planet  orbiting  at  only  2.8  times  Earth’s 
 distance from the sun ? 
 (a) Ceres  (b) Makemake 
 (c) Eris  (d) Pluto 
 Sol.11.(a)  Ceres  is  the  largest  object  in 
 the  asteroid  belt  between  Mars  and 
 Jupiter,  and  it  is  the  only  dwarf  planet 
 located  in  the  inner  solar  system.  It  was 
 the  first  member  of  the  asteroid  belt  to 
 be  discovered  when  Giuseppe  Piazzi 
 spotted it in 1801. 
 Q.12.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 about a nebula: 
 1. 
 A  nebula  is  a  cloud  of  dust  and  gas 
 inside  a  galaxy,  consisting  mainly  of 
 hydrogen,  helium,  and  other  ionized 
 gases. 
 2. 
 Originally,  the  term  nebula  was  used 
 to  describe  only  galaxies  within  the 
 Milky Way. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.12.(a)  1  only.  The  term  nebula  was 
 originally  used  for  any  diffuse 
 astronomical  object,  including  galaxies 
 outside the Milky Way. 
 Q.13.  A  distinctive  cross  -  shaped 
 constellation  best  seen  in  the  northern 
 hemisphere  during  the  summer  and  fall 
 months around September is: 
 (a) Cygnus  (b) Ursa Major 
 (c) Pegasus  (d) Cassiopeia 
 Sol.13.(a)  Cygnus.  It  is  a  northern 
 constellation  on  the  plane  of  the  Milky 
 Way,  deriving  its  name  from  the  Latinized 
 Greek word for swan. 
 Q.14.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding planetary atmospheres: 
 1. 
 Venus  has  thick  white  and  yellowish 
 clouds  of  sulphuric  acid  in  its 
 atmosphere. 
 2. 
 Mars’  atmosphere  is  mainly 
 composed  of  methane,  with  traces  of 
 sulfur dioxide. 
 3. 
 Uranus  is  the  coldest  planet  in  the 
 Solar System. 
 4. 
 Venus  is  often  referred  to  as  the 
 “morning star.” 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 2  (b) Only 1 and 2 
 (c) Only 2 and 3  (d) Only 3 and 4 
 Sol.14.(a)  Only  2.  Mars,  fourth  planet  in 
 the  solar  system  in  order  of  distance 
 from  the  Sun  and  seventh  in  size  and 
 mass.  It  is  a  periodically  conspicuous 
 reddish  object  in  the  night  sky.  Mars' 
 atmosphere  is  95.32%  carbon  dioxide, 
 with methane and sulfur dioxide. 
 Q.15.  The  colour  of  the  surface  of  Mars 
 is  red  because  of  the  presence  of  large 
 amounts  of  chemical  compounds  in  it. 
 What is the name of that compound ? 
 (a) Magnesium oxide   (b) Tin oxide 
 (c) Iron oxide                 (d) Nitrogen oxide 
 Sol.15.(c)  Iron  oxide.  Mars  appears 
 reddish  due  to  the  presence  of  ferric  iron 
 in  the  form  of  red  hematite  (Fe 
 2 
 O 
 3 
 ).  The 
 surface  pressure  of  Mars  is  610  Pascals, 
 and  its  gravity  is  about  one  -  third  of 
 Earth's.  A  Martian  day  lasts  24  hours  and 
 37  minutes.  Mars  has  two  moons: 
 Phobos and Deimos. 
 Q.16.  Which  dwarf  planet  orbits  the  sun 
 in  310  years  at  a  distance  between  38.5 
 to 53 AU ? 
 (a) Eris (b) Ceres (c) Makemake (d) Pluto 
 Sol.16.(c)  Makemake.  It  is  the  third-largest 
 and  second-brightest  known  dwarf  planet. 
 It  was  discovered  in  2005.  Pluto  was 
 discovered  in  1930.  It  was  initially 
 classified  as  the  ninth  planet  of  the  Solar 
 System  but  was  reclassified  as  a  dwarf 
 planet  in  2006.  Pluto  has  a  natural  satellite 
 named 'Charon'. 
 Q.17.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 second nearest star to Earth? 
 (a) Proxima Centauri    (b) Polaris 
 (c) Sirius                         (d) Delta Velorum 
 Sol.17.(a)  Proxima  Centauri.  It  is  the 
 closest  star  to  the  sun,  at  a  distance  of 
 4.24  light  -  years.  A  light  -  year  is  the 
 distance  light  travels  in  one  year,  which  is 
 about  9.461  x  10 
 12 
 kilometers.  Polaris 
 (North  Star)  is  located  nearly  directly 
 above  Earth's  North  Pole  along  its 
 rotational  axis.  Sirius  (Dog  Star)  is  the 
 brightest  star  in  Earth's  night  sky.  Delta 
 Velorum  is  a  triple  star  system  in  the 
 southern constellation of Vela. 
 Q.18.  Which  of  the  following  statements 
 most  accurately  describes  the  planet 
 Saturn? 
 (a) It is a gas giant made up mostly of 
 hydrogen and helium and the density 
 is less than that of water 
 (b) Its surface is reddish in colour. 
 (c) It was the seventh planet discovered 
 in the Solar System. 
 (d) Its cloud-like outer regions consist of 
 methane in the gaseous form and 
 ammonia in crystalline form. 
 Sol.18.(a)  Saturn's  density  (0.70  gm/cm 
 3 
 ) 
 is  less  than  that  of  water,  making  it  the 
 least  dense  planet  in  the  solar  system.  If 
 there  were  a  body  of  water  large  enough, 
 Saturn  would  theoretically  float.  The 
 presence  of  methane  and  ammonia  is 
 more  characteristic  of  Uranus  and 
 Neptune. 
 Q.19.  Which  of  the  following  rocky 
 planets  is  the  fastest  planet  in  our  solar 
 system  –  traveling  through  space  at 
 about  29  miles  (47  kilometers)  per 
 second? 
 (a) Earth (b) Mercury (c) Venus (d) Mars 
 Sol.19.(b)  Mercury.  It  is  the  nearest  to 
 the  sun.  It  takes  only  about  88  days  to 
 complete  one  round  along  its  orbit. 
 Venus:  It  is  considered  as  ‘Earth’s  -  twin’ 
 because  its  size  and  shape  are  very 
 much  similar  to  that  of  the  earth.  Venus 
 is  also  known  as  Lucifer.  Earth  is  the  fifth 
 largest  planet  (in  size).  It  is  slightly 
 flattened  at  the  poles  and  its  shape  is 
 described  as  a  Geoid.  Mars  -  The  fourth 
 planet  from  the  Sun  and  it  is  a  dusty, 
 cold,  desert  world  with  a  very  thin 
 atmosphere. 
 Q.20.  Which  of  the  following  is  one  of  the 
 largest spiral galaxies? 
 (a) NGC 6872  (b) Milky Way 
 (c) Cygnus A  (d) Maffei 1 
 Sol.20.(a)  NGC  6872,  which  is  one  of  the 
 largest  spiral  galaxies,  spanning  522,000 
 light-years  across,  approximately  fiv e 
 times  the  size  of  the  Milky  Way.  It  is 
 located  212  million  light-years  from  Earth 
 in the southern constellation Pavo.

 their  approximate  day  length  (rotation 
 period): 
 List I 
 (Planet) 
 List II 
 (Day Length in Hours) 
 A. Mercury  i. 25 
 B. Venus 
 ii. 1,408 
 C. Mars  iii. 5,832 
 D. Neptune  iv. 16 
 (a) A–ii, B–iii, C–i, D–iv 
 (b) A–iii, B–ii, C–i, D–iv 
 (c) A–ii, B–iv, C–i, D–iii 
 (d) A–iv, B–iii, C–ii, D–i 
 Sol.21.(a)  A–ii,  B–iii,  C–i,  D–iv.  Planet 
 and  Day  Length  in  hours:  Mercury 
 (1,408),  Venus  (5,832),  Earth  (24),  Mars

 takes  only  about  88  days  to  complete

 Q.22.  Who  was  the  first  to  develop  a 
 mathematical  predictive  heliocentric 
 model of the solar system ? 
 (a) Nicolaus Copernicus 
 (b) Galileo Galilei 
 (c) Pierre-Simon Laplace 
 (d) Immanuel Kant 
 Sol.22.(a)  Nicolaus  Copernicus.  Although 
 Aryabhatta  (5th  century  A.D.)  hinted  at 
 astronomical  ideas,  Copernicus  (1473  - 
 1543)  developed  the  first  mathematical 
 heliocentric  model,  where  planets 
 revolved  around  a  fix ed  Sun.  Galileo 
 supported  this  model,  and  Johannes 
 Kepler  later  refined  it  by  introducing 
 elliptical  orbits,  now  known  as  Kepler's 
 laws. 
 Q.23.  Who  calculated  the  approximate 
 location  of  the  planet  Neptune  by 
 studying  gravity-  induced  disturbances  in 
 the motion of Uranus? 
 (a) John Couch Adams 
 (b) Percival Lowell 
 (c) Johann Gottfried Galle 
 (d) Urbain-Jean-Joseph Le Verrier 
 Sol.23.(d)  Urbain  -  Jean  -  Joseph  Le 
 Verrier.  He  was  a  French  astronomer  and 
 mathematician.  Neptune  is  the  eighth 
 and  most  distant  planet  in  our  solar 
 system,  and  it  is  the  second  coldest 
 planet.  It  was  discovered  in  1846.  It  is  the 
 windiest  planet  in  our  solar  system.  One 
 day on Neptune takes about 16 hours. 
 Q.24.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are correct? 
 Statement  1:  Apogee  is  the  point  in  the 
 orbit  of  an  object  around  the  Earth  where 
 it is farthest from the Earth’s center. 
 Statement  2:  Perigee  refers  to  the  point 
 where the Moon’s orbit is closest to the 
 Earth. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 
 (b) Only Statement 2 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 
 (d) None of the above 
 Sol.24.(c)  Both  Statement  1  and  2. 
 Perihelion:  The  Earth  closest  to  the  Sun 
 on  3rd  January.  Aphelion:  The  Earth 
 farthest from the Sun on 4th July. 
 Q.25.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are incorrect? 
 Statement  1:  The  Big  Dipper  is  an 
 asterism  formed  by  the  seven  brightest 
 stars in the constellation Orion. 
 Statement  2:  Ursa  Major  is  the  largest 
 constellation in the entire sky. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 
 (b) Only Statement 2 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 
 (d) None of the above 
 Sol.25.(c)  Both  Statement  1  and  2. 
 Statement  1  is  incorrect  because  the  Big 
 Dipper  belongs  to  the  constellation  Ursa 
 Major,  not  Orion.  Statement  2  is  also 
 incorrect  because  Ursa  Major  (  Great 
 Bear)  is  the  third  -  largest  constellation  in 
 the  sky  and  the  largest  constellation  in 
 the Northern Hemisphere. 
 Q.26.  A  constellation  of  the  northern  sky 
 easily  recognised  by  a  group  of  fiv e 
 bright  stars  forming  a  slightly  irregular 
 ‘W’ is called: 
 (a) Cassiopeia  (b) Orion 
 (c) Ursa Major  (d) Cygnus 
 Sol.26.(a)  Cassiopeia.  It  is  a  prominent 
 constellation  in  the  northern  sky.  It  is 
 visible  during  winter  in  the  early  part  of 
 the  night.  It  looks  like  a  distorted  letter  W 
 or M. 
 Q.27.  Assertion  (A):  Saturn  is  the  second 
 largest  planet  in  our  Solar  System  and 
 has the most prominent ring system. 
 Reason  (R):  Saturn  is  larger  than  Jupiter 
 and  has  Titan,  the  largest  moon  in  the 
 Solar System. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) A is true, but R is false. 
 (d) A is false, but R is true. 
 Sol.27.(c)  A  is  true,  but  R  is  false.  Our 
 Solar  System  has  eight  planets  which 
 orbit  the  sun.  In  order  of  distance  from 
 the  sun  they  are:  Mercury,  Venus,  Earth, 
 Mars,  Jupiter,  Saturn,  Uranus,  and 
 Neptune.  Largest  to  smallest:  Jupiter, 
 Saturn,  Uranus,  Neptune,  Earth,  Venus, 
 Mars,  Mercury.  Saturn  moon  Titan  is  the 
 second  largest  moon  after  Ganymede 
 (Jupiter’s moon). 
 Q.28.  _________  oblong  orbit  overlaps  the 
 orbit of Neptune. 
 (a) Jupiter’s (b) Pluto’s (c) Venus’ (d) Mars’ 
 Sol.28.(b)  Pluto’s.  Neptune  is  the 
 outermost  Jovian  planet  in  the  solar 
 system.  Until  2006  Pluto  was  the  farthest 
 planet  from  the  Sun.  In  2006,  The 
 International  Astronomical  Union  (IAU) 
 downgraded  the  status  of  Pluto  to  that  of 
 a  dwarf  planet.  The  Jovian  planets  are 
 Jupiter,  Saturn,  Uranus,  and  Neptune, 
 which are also known as the gas giants. 
 Q.29.  Which  of  the  following  statements 
 about planet Saturn is true? 
 (a) Saturn is the fourth planet from the 
 Sun and the largest planet in our solar 
 system. 
 (b) Satellites like Enceladus and Titan of 
 Saturn are home to internal oceans, 
 could possibly support life. 
 (c) Saturn's environment is conducive to 
 life. 
 (d) Saturn is a massive ball made up 
 mostly nitrogen. 
 Sol.29.(b)  Saturn  is  the  second  largest 
 planet,  composed  of  hydrogen  and 
 helium.  It  has  146  moons.  Titan  -  It  is  the 
 largest  moon  of  Saturn  and  the  second 
 largest  moon  in  our  solar  system. 
 Enceladus  -  It  is  the  second  nearest  of 
 the  major  regular  moons  of  Saturn  and 
 the  brightest  of  all  its  moons.  It  was 
 discovered  in  1789  by  the  English 
 astronomer  William  Herschel.  It  is  found 
 that  Titan  has  methane  in  its  atmosphere 
 and  Enceladus  has  a  liquid  ocean  with 
 erupting plumes of gas and water. 
 Q.30.  Which  star  is  also  known  as  the 
 ‘Pole Star’ ? 
 (a) The East star  (b) The South star 
 (c) The West star  (d) The North star 
 Sol.30.(d)  The  North  star.  Polaris,  also 
 known  as  the  North  Star,  is  located 
 almost  directly  above  Earth's  north  pole 
 along  our  planet's  rotational  axis.  The 
 imaginary  line  that  runs  through  the 
 Earth  from  the  North  Pole  to  the  South 
 Pole  is  called  the  Earth's  axis  of  rotation. 
 The  Earth  rotates  around  this  axis, 
 similar  to  how  a  top  spins  around  its 
 spindle. 
 Q.31.  In  1905,  who  concluded  with 
 Thomas  Chamberlain  that  the  planets  of 
 the  Solar  System  originated  from  an 
 encounter  between  the  Sun  and  another 
 star? 
 (a) James Hutton     (b) Inge Lehmann 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (c) Charles Lyell        (d) Forest Moulton 
 Sol.31.(d)  Forest  Moulton.  He  was  an 
 American  astronomer  and 
 mathematician  known  for  his 
 contributions  to  celestial  mechanics.  He 
 co-developed  the  Chamberlin-Moulton 
 planetesimal  hypothesis,  which  proposed 
 a  model  for  the  formation  of  the  solar 
 system.  According  to  this  hypothesis, 
 planets  formed  from  smaller  bodies 
 called  planetesimals  through  the  process 
 of accretion. 
 Q.32.  Perihelion is when Earth is ______. 
 (a) under the sun 
 (b) farthest from the sun 
 (c) nearest to the sun 
 (d) above the sun 
 Sol.32.(c)  nearest  to  the  sun.  On  3rd 
 January,  the  earth  is  the  nearest  to  the 
 sun  (147  million  km).  Aphelion  is  the 
 point  of  the  Earth’s  orbit  that  is  farthest 
 away from the Sun. 
 Q.33.  Which  is  the  most  widely  accepted 
 model  to  explain  the  formation  and 
 evaluation of the solar system? 
 (a) Cloud hypothesis 
 (b) Gas hypothesis 
 (c) Nebular hypothesis 
 (d) Solar hypothesis 
 Sol.33.(c)  Nebular  hypothesis  -  The 
 planets  were  formed  out  of  a  cloud  of 
 material  associated  with  a  youthful  sun, 
 which  was  slowly  rotating.  The  theory 
 was  developed  by  Immanuel  Kant.  But 
 Mathematician  Laplace  revised  it  in 
 1796.  Other  Hypothesis:  Big  Bang  Theory 
 -  Propounded  by  George  Lamaitre  related 
 to the origin of the universe. 
 Q.34.  Which  trend  is  found  about  the 
 distance between the galaxies? 
 (a) Unpredictable  (b) Decreasing 
 (c) Constant                (d) Increasing 
 Sol.34.(d)  Increasing.  In  1929,  Edwin 
 Hubble  announced  that  almost  all 
 galaxies  appeared  to  be  moving  away 
 from  us.  He  found  that  the  universe  was 
 expanding  -  with  all  of  the  galaxies 
 moving  away  from  each  other.  This 
 phenomenon  was  observed  as  a  redshift 
 of a galaxy's spectrum. 
 Q.35.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding terrestrial planets: 
 1. 
 Terrestrial  planets  are  primarily 
 composed of rocks and metals. 
 2.  Mercury, Venus, Earth, and Mars are 
 classified as terrestrial planets. 
 3. 
 Terrestrial  planets  can  be  further 
 categorised  into  silicate,  iron, 
 coreless, and carbon (diamond) 
 planets. 
 4. 
 The  atmosphere  of  all  terrestrial 
 planets  is  primarily  made  up  of 
 hydrogen and helium. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only 1, 2 and 3  (b) Only 1 and 2 
 (c) Only 2, 3 and 4  (d) All of the above 
 Sol.35.(a)  Only  1,  2  and  3.  In  our  solar 
 system,  planets  are  broadly  classified 
 into  two  categories:  terrestrial  and 
 Jovian.  Terrestrial  planets  (Mercury, 
 Venus,  Earth,  and  Mars)  are  small,  rocky, 
 and  relatively  close  to  the  Sun.  Jovian 
 planets  (Jupiter,  Saturn,  Uranus,  and 
 Neptune)  are  gas  giants,  much  larger  and 
 further from the Sun. 
 Q.36.  Which  scientist  thought  of  the 
 concept  of  the  steady  state  of  the 
 universe? 
 (a) Harold Jeffrey     (b) Edwin Hubble 
 (c) Fred Hoyle           (d) Pierre - Laplace 
 Sol.36.(c)  Fred  Hoyle.  Steady  -  State 
 Theory  :  It  is  a  theoretical  model  in  which 
 the  Universe  is  constantly  expanding  but 
 with  a  fix ed  average  density.  The  theory 
 was  put  forward  by  three  scientists  Sir 
 Hermann  Bondi,  Thomas  Gold,  and  Sir 
 Fred Hoyle in 1948. 
 Q.37.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are incorrect? 
 Statement  1:  Comets  are  cosmic 
 snowballs  of  frozen  gases,  rock,  and  dust 
 that orbit the Sun. 
 Statement  2:  Meteorites  are  stars  that 
 fall  from  space  and  burn  completely 
 before reaching the Earth’s surface. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 
 (b) Only Statement 2 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 
 (d) None of the above 
 Sol.37.(b)  Only  Statement  2.  Meteorites  - 
 When  a  meteoroid  survives  its  trip 
 through  the  atmosphere  and  hits  the 
 ground,  it’s  called  a  meteorite.  Meteorites 
 typically  range  between  the  size  of  a 
 pebble and a fist. 
 Q.38.  What is the radius of the earth? 
 (a) 7456 km  (b) 6371 km 
 (c) 5619 km  (d) 4728 km 
 Sol.38.(b)  6371  km.  Earth  Distance  from 
 the  Sun  (150  million  km),  Surface  area: 
 510.1  million  km²,  Land  area:  149  million 
 km², Gravity: 9.8 m/s². 
 Q.39.  Each orbit of the International 
 Space Station (ISS) takes _____minutes. 
 (a) 80 - 82 (b) 85 - 87 (c) 90 - 93 (d) 83 - 86 
 Sol.39.(c) 90-93.  In 24 hours, the space 
 station  makes  16  orbits  of  Earth, 
 traveling  through  16  sunrises  and 
 sunsets.

 C. Saturn  iii. 9 hours 56 min 
 D. Uranus  iv. 10 hours 40 min 
 (a) A–ii, B–iii, C–iv, D–i 
 (b) A–iv, B–ii, C–i, D–iii 
 (c) A–ii, B–iv, C–iii, D–i 
 (d) A–i, B–iii, C–iv, D–ii

 Q.41.  In  early  1610,  who  discovered  with 
 his  newly  invented  telescope  that  Jupiter 
 has four moons? 
 (a) Simon Marius      (b) Tycho Brahe 
 (c) Galileo Galilei       (d) Johannes Kepler 
 Sol.41.(c)  Galileo  Galilei.  He  was  an 
 Italian  astronomer  and  scientist.  He 
 described  the  rings  of  Saturn,  the  phases 
 of  Venus,  sunsets,  and  the  bumpy  lunar 
 surface.  The  four  moons  he  discovered 
 were:  Io, Europa, Ganymede, and Callisto. 
 Q.42.  Who  was  the  first  to  accurately 
 describe  the  rings  of  Saturn  as  a  disc 
 around the planet in 1655? 
 (a) Hideki Yukawa 
 (b) Galileo Galilei 
 (c) Christiaan Huygens 
 (d) Giovanni Cassini 
 Sol.42.(c)  Christiaan  Huygens.  His  other 
 inventions  include  the  pendulum  clock, 
 centrifugal governor, and magic lantern. 
 Q.43.  The  moon  completes  _________ 
 rotation  on  its  axis  as  it  completes  one 
 revolution around the Earth. 
 (a) 3        (b) 2        (c) 1        (d) 4 
 Sol.43.(c)  1.  The  Moon  is  Earth's  only 
 natural  satellite  circling  the  planet  at  an 
 average  distance  of  384,400  kilometers. 
 The  Moon's  presence  helps  stabilize  our 
 planet  and  moderate  our  climate.  The 
 radius  of  the  Moon  is  1.74  ×  10 
 6 
 m. 
 Huygens is the Moon's tallest mountain. 
 Q.44.  Which  of  the  following  statements 
 regarding the moon is correct? 
 I. 
 The  size  of  the  illuminated  part  of  the 
 moon  visible  from  the  Earth  increases 
 each day after the new moon day. 
 II.  After the full moon day, the sunlit part 
 of the moon visible from the Earth 
 decreases in size every day. 
 (a) Both I and II  (b) Only I 
 (c) Neither I nor II  (d) Only II 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Sol.44.(a)  Both  I  and  II.  When  there  are 
 two  full  moons  within  a  month,  the 
 second  full  moon  is  called  a  Blue  Moon, 
 When  a  full  moon  occurs  at  its  perigee 
 (the  moon  is  closest  to  the  earth)  it  is 
 called a Supermoon. 
 Q.45.  The  ______  radiation  belts  are  giant 
 swaths  of  magnetically  trapped  highly 
 energetic charged particles that surround 
 Earth. 
 (a) Van Allen  (b) Aurora 
 (c) Kuiper  (d) Chinook 
 Sol.45.(a)  Van  Allen.  An  aurora  is  a 
 natural  phenomenon  which  is 
 characterized  by  a  display  of  a 
 natural-coloured  (green,  red,  yellow  or 
 white) light in the sky. 
 Q.46.  In  which  of  the  following  periods  is 
 the  meteor  shower  named  Quadrantids 
 generally visible from Earth? 
 (a) August/September 
 (b) October/November 
 (c) May/June 
 (d) December/January 
 Sol.46.(d) December / January. 
 Quadrantids  are  best  viewed  in  the 
 Northern  Hemisphere  during  the  night 
 and  predawn  hours.  They  are  also  known 
 for  their  bright  fir eball  meteors.  The 
 Quadrantids  can  produce  over  100 
 meteors per hour in a moonless sky. 
 Q.47.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding solar eclipses: 
 1. 
 A  solar  eclipse  occurs  when  the  Moon 
 passes  between  the  Earth  and  the 
 Sun. 
 2. 
 Types  of  solar  eclipse  include  Total, 
 Hybrid, Annular, and Partial. 
 3. 
 In  an  annular  solar  eclipse,  the  Moon 
 appears  smaller  than  the  Sun  and 
 does not completely cover it. 
 4. 
 A  penumbral  eclipse  is  a  type  of  solar 
 eclipse. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only 1, 2 and 3  (b) Only 1 and 3 
 (c) Only 2 and 4   (d) All of the above 
 Sol.47.(a)  Only  1,  2  and  3.  Penumbral 
 (Lunar  eclipse).  A  solar  eclipse  occurs 
 when  the  Moon  passes  between  the 
 Earth  and  the  Sun.  Types  of  solar  eclipse: 
 Total  (Completely  blocking  the  face  of 
 the  Sun),  Hybrid  (the  Moon  is  farther 
 away  from  Earth,  it  appears  smaller  than 
 the  Sun  and  does  not  completely  cover 
 the  Sun)  and  Annular  (when  Sun  is  at  or 
 near its farthest point from Earth). 
 Q.48.  In  which  of  the  following  months 
 may the meteor shower named Lyrids be 
 seen from Earth? 
 (a) April  (b) February (c) June (d) August 
 Sol.48.(a)  April.  The  radiant  point  for  the 
 Lyrid  meteor  shower  is  near  the 
 constellation  Lyra,  which  has  the  bright 
 star Vega in the east. 
 Longitudes and Latitudes 
 Q.49.  At  what  latitude  does  the  easterly 
 jet  stream  blow  over  peninsular  India 
 during the summer months? 
 (a) 24°N    (b) 30°N    (c) 14°N    (d) 28°N 
 Sol.49.(c)  14°N.  The  Jet  Stream  is  a 
 geostrophic  wind  blowing  horizontally 
 through  the  upper  layers  of  the 
 troposphere,  generally  from  west  to  east. 
 The  tropical  easterly  jet  stream,  located 
 between  8  and  35  degrees  north  latitude, 
 is  connected  to  the  southwest  monsoon 
 in India. 
 Q.50  .  In  which  month  is  the  InterTropical 
 Convergence  Zone  (ITCZ)  located  around 
 20°N-25°N  latitude  (over  the  Gangetic 
 plain) ? 
 (a) May  (b) August  (c) June  (d) July 
 Sol.50.(d)  July.  The  Inter  Tropical 
 Convergence  Zone  (ITCZ)  is  a  low 
 pressure  zone  located  at  the  equator 
 where  trade  winds  converge,  and  so,  it  is 
 a  zone  where  air  tends  to  ascend.  The 
 Inter-Tropical  Convergence  Zone  located 
 around  20°N-25°N  latitudes  (over  the 
 Gangetic  plain)  in  July  month  is 
 sometimes called the monsoon trough. 
 Q.51.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are incorrect? 
 Statement  1:  Longitude  directly 
 determines  the  temperature  of  ocean 
 waters. 
 Statement  2:  Ocean  currents  influence 
 ocean  water  temperature  by  transporting 
 warm and cold water masses. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 
 (b) Only Statement 2 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 
 (d) None of the above 
 Sol.51.(a)  Only  Statement  1.  Statement 
 1  is  incorrect  because  longitude  does  not 
 directly  affect  ocean  water  temperature. 
 Factors  determining  ocean  water 
 temperature  :  Latitude  -  Affects  the 
 amount  of  solar  radiation  received. 
 Unequal  distribution  of  land  and  water  - 
 Affects  heat  absorption  and  distribution. 
 Ocean currents - Transport heat and cold 
 water masses globally. 
 Q.52.  Which  of  the  following  statements 
 is INCORRECT regarding the Coriolis 
 force? 
 (a) It deflects the wind to the right in the 
 north and left in the south. 
 (b) It is directly proportional to the angle 
 of altitude 
 (c) Deflection is less when the wind is 
 high 
 (d) It is absent at the equator. 
 Sol.52.(c)  Deflection  is  less  when  the 
 wind  is  high.  Coriolis  force  :  An  apparent 
 force  caused  by  the  earth’s  rotation.  The 
 Coriolis  force  acts  perpendicular  to  the 
 pressure  gradient  force  (pressure 
 gradient  force  is  perpendicular  to  an 
 isobar).  It  is  maximum  at  the  poles  and  is 
 absent  at  the  equator.  The  Coriolis  force 
 also affects ocean currents. 
 Q.53.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are correct? 
 Statement  1:  The  Torrid  Zone  lies 
 between  the  Tropic  of  Cancer  (23.5°  N) 
 and the Tropic of Capricorn (23.5° S). 
 Statement  2:  The  Frigid  Zones  are 
 located  between  the  Tropics  and  the 
 Arctic/Antarctic Circles. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 
 (b) Only Statement 2 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 
 (d) None of the above 
 Sol.53.(a)  Only  Statement  1.  The  zones 
 located  between  the  Tropics  and  the 
 Arctic/Antarctic  Circles  are  the 
 Temperate  Zones.  The  Frigid  Zones  are 
 the  coldest  regions,  located  between  the 
 Arctic  Circle  (66.5°  N)  and  the  North 
 Pole,  and  between  the  Antarctic  Circle 
 (66.5°  S)  and  the  South  Pole.  The  Torrid 
 Zone  experiences  high  temperatures 
 year-round due to direct sunlight. 
 Q.54.  Which  is  the  Standard  Meridian  of 
 India? 
 (a) 81°30ʹ E  (b) 82°30ʹ E 
 (c) 83°30ʹ E  (d) 84°40ʹ E 
 Sol.54.(b)  82°30ʹ  E.  The  Standard 
 Meridian  of  India,  which  passes  through 
 Mirzapur  in  Uttar  Pradesh,  also  crosses 
 the  following  states:  Madhya  Pradesh, 
 Chhattisgarh,  Odisha,  and  Andhra 
 Pradesh. 
 Q.55.  Which  of  the  following  sets  of 
 statements  are  correct  about  Inter 
 Tropical Convergence Zone (ITCZ) ? 
 A. 
 The  ITCZ  is  a  broad  trough  of  low 
 pressure in equatorial latitudes. 
 B. 
 This  is  where  the  northeast  and  the 
 southeast trade winds converge. 
 C. 
 The  ITCZ  is  the  equatorial  trough 
 normally  positioned  about  5°N  of  the 
 equator  and  remains  there  throughout 
 the year. 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 D. 
 The  ITCZ  moves  over  to  the  plane  of 
 Ganga in Summer. 
 (a) Only A and C  (b) A, B and C 
 (c) Only B and C  (d) A, B and D 
 Sol.55.(d)  A,  B  and  D.  Intertropical 
 Convergence  Zone  (ITCZ)  -  The  region 
 that  circles  the  Earth,  near  the  equator, 
 where  the  trade  winds  of  the  Northern 
 and  Southern  Hemispheres  come 
 together.  Seasonal  shifts  in  the  location 
 of  the  ITCZ  drastically  affects  rainfall  in 
 many  equatorial  nations,  resulting  in  the 
 wet  and  dry  seasons  of  the  tropics  rather 
 than  the  cold  and  warm  seasons  of 
 higher latitudes. 
 Q.56.  During  October-November,  the 
 apparent  movement  of  the  sun  is 
 towards the _____. 
 (a) North  (b) North-East 
 (c) North-West  (d) South 
 Sol.56.(d)  South.  The  position  of  the 
 rising  sun  keeps  on  moving  towards  the 
 south  in  the  period  from  21st  June  to 
 22nd  December.  This  period  is  called 
 Dakshinayan  .  From  22nd  December  to 
 21st  June,  the  sun  keeps  on  moving 
 towards  the  north.  This  period  is  called 
 Uttarayan.

 List I (Channel)  List II (Separates) 
 A. 8° Channel  i. Andaman Islands 
 and Nicobar Islands

 Lakshadweep main 
 group 
 D. 11° Channel  iv. Amindivi Islands & 
 Cannanore Islands 
 (a) A–ii, B–iii, C–i, D–iv 
 (b) A–iii, B–ii, C–iv, D–i 
 (c) A–iv, B–ii, C–i, D–iii 
 (d) A–ii, B–iv, C–iii, D–i 
 Sol.57.(a) A–ii, B–iii, C–i, D–iv. 
 Q.58.  The  southern  part  of  India  lies  in 
 the _________ belt. 
 (a) rain (b) temperate (c) polar (d) warm 
 Sol.58.(d)  Warm.  The  Tropic  of  Cancer 
 passes  through  the  middle  of  India.  The 
 southern  parts  being  closer  to  the 
 Equator,  experience  high  temperatures 
 throughout  the  year.  The  northern  parts 
 on  the  other  hand  lie  in  the  warm 
 temperate zone. 
 Q.59.  Which  of  the  following  states  of 
 India  is  NOT  crossed  by  the  Tropic  of 
 Cancer (23°30′ N)? 
 (a) Gujarat  (b) Rajasthan 
 (c) Chhattisgarh  (d) Kerala 
 Sol.59.(d)  Kerala.  The  Tropic  of  Cancer 
 (  23°30′  N  )  passes  through  eight  states  in 
 India  -  Gujarat  (Jasdan),  Rajasthan 
 (Kalinjarh),  Madhya  Pradesh  (Shajapur), 
 Chhattisgarh  (Sonhat),  Jharkhand 
 (Lohardaga),  West  Bengal  (Krishnanagar), 
 Tripura  (Udaipur)  and  Mizoram 
 (Champhai). 
 Q.60.  On  which  of  the  following  two 
 dates does the equinox fall ? 
 (a) 21 June and 23 September 
 (b) 21 March and 21 June 
 (c) 21 March and 23 September 
 (d) 23 September and 22 December 
 Sol.60.(c)  21  March  and  23  September  . 
 Equinox  is  the  situation  when  the  equator 
 receives  the  vertical  rays  of  the  Sun  and 
 day  and  night  are  equal.  Spring  equinox  - 
 21st  March,  Autumn  equinox  -  23 
 September.  21st  June  (winter  solstice  in 
 Southern  Hemisphere,  Summer  solstice 
 in  Northern  hemisphere)  -  The  rays  of  the 
 sun  fall  directly  on  the  Tropic  of  Cancer. 
 22  December  (Summer  solstice  in 
 Southern  hemisphere,  winter  solstice  in 
 Northern  Hemisphere)  -  The  rays  of  the 
 sun  fall  directly  on  the  Tropic  of 
 Capricorn. 
 Q.61.  From  the  north  most  to  south  most 
 point of India, what is the extent of India? 
 (a) 4109 km  (b) 2914 km 
 (c) 3214 km  (d) 3785 km 
 Sol.61.(c)  3214  km.  Extent  of  India:  East 
 to  west  (2,933  km),  land  frontier  (15,200 
 km),  coastline  (7,516.6  km).  The 
 westernmost  point  (Ghuar  Moti  in  Kutch). 
 The  easternmost  point  (Kibithu  in 
 Arunachal  Pradesh).  Indira  Col 
 (northernmost  point).  Indira  Point  (Great 
 Nicobar  Island,  Andaman  sea)  is  the 
 Southernmost  point  of  India  while 
 Kanyakumari  is  the  southernmost  point 
 of the Indian Mainland. 
 Q.62.  What  is  the  zone  between  the  arctic 
 circle and north pole called? 
 (a) Torrid zone 
 (b) Frigid zone 
 (c) North temperate zone 
 (d) South temperate zone 
 Sol.62.(b)  Frigid  zone.  The  North  Frigid 
 Zone,  between  the  North  Pole  at  90°  N 
 and  the  Arctic  Circle  at  66°33′48.7"  N, 
 covers 4.12% of Earth's surface. 
 Q.63.  Consider the following statements: 
 1. 
 Isochrones  are  lines  joining  points 
 located  at  equal  travel  time  from  a 
 common centre. 
 2. 
 Isohalines  are  lines  connecting  points 
 of equal temperature in the ocean. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.63.(a)  Only  1.  Isohaline  -  A  line 
 drawn  on  a  map  to  indicate  connecting 
 points  of  equal  salinity  in  the  ocean. 
 Isonomal  -  A  line  on  a  chart  connecting 
 points  of  equal  abundance  values  of  a 
 plant  species  sampled  in  different 
 sections  of  an  area.  Isobronts  -  A  line 
 drawn  through  geographical  points  at 
 which  a  given  phase  of  thunderstorm 
 activity occurred simultaneously. 
 Q.64.  1°  latitude  is  equal  to  approximately 
 ______ km. 
 (a) 111     (b) 145     (c) 133     (d) 122 
 Sol.64.(a)  111  km.  This  distance 
 decreases  marginally  as  one  travels  to 
 poles  and  increases  marginally  as  one 
 goes towards the equator. 
 Continents and Oceans 
 Q.65.  What  is  the  primary  reason  for  the 
 asymmetrical  distribution  of  continents 
 and oceans across the Earth's surface? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Seafloor spreading and plate 
 tectonics 
 (b) Earth's elliptical orbit 
 (c) Magnetic anomalies in the core 
 (d) Variations in solar radiation 
 Sol.65.(a)  The  Earth's  continents  are 
 fragments  of  a  once-unified 
 supercontinent  (Pangaea)  that  have  been 
 continuously  drifting,  colliding,  and 
 rearranging  due  to  the  movement  of 
 tectonic  plates.  This  dynamic  process, 
 driven  by  seafloor  spreading  at 
 mid-ocean  ridges  and  subduction  at 
 trenches,  has  resulted  in  the  current, 
 uneven  arrangement  of  landmasses  and 
 ocean  basins,  such  as  the  land-heavy 
 Northern  Hemisphere  and  the 
 ocean-dominated Southern Hemisphere. 
 Q.66.  Consider the following statements: 
 1. 
 The  Arabian  Sea,  located  in  the  North 
 Indian  Ocean,  covers  an  area  of  about 
 3.86 million square kilometres. 
 2. 
 The  Arabian  Sea  has  higher  salinity 
 due  to  high  evaporation  and  low  influx 
 of fresh water. 
 Which of the statements given above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.66.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  The  North  Indian 
 Ocean  is  a  region  of  the  Indian  Ocean 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 that  includes  the  Bay  of  Bengal  and  the 
 Arabian  Sea.  The  Red  Sea  is  located 
 between  Africa  and  Asia.  The  Java  Sea  is 
 located  in  Indonesia,  between  the  islands 
 of  Java  and  Borneo.  The  Andaman  Sea  is 
 located  in  the  northeastern  part  of  the 
 Indian Ocean. 
 Q.67.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 largest  crustal  plate  on  Earth  with  an 
 area of over 103,000,000 km 
 2 
 ? 
 (a) South American plate 
 (b) African plate 
 (c) Eurasian plate 
 (d) Pacific plate 
 Sol.67.(d)  Pacific  plate  :  It  is  the  largest 
 oceanic  plate.  Plate  Tectonics  theory 
 given  by  McKenzie,  Parker  and  Morgan 
 in  1967.  It  proposes  that  the  earth’s 
 lithosphere  is  divided  into  seven  major 
 and  some  minor  plates.  Major  plates  : 
 Antarctica  and  the  surrounding  oceanic 
 plate;  North  American  (with  western 
 Atlantic  floor  separated  from  the  South 
 American  plate  along  the  Caribbean 
 islands)  plate;  South  American  (with 
 western  Atlantic  floor  separated  from  the 
 North  American  plate  along  the 
 Caribbean  islands)  plate;  Pacific  plate; 
 India-Australia-New  Zealand  plate;  Africa 
 with  the  eastern  Atlantic  floor  plate; 
 Eurasia and the adjacent oceanic plate. 
 Q.68.  Consider the following statements: 
 1. 
 The  Peninsular  Plateau  of  India  was 
 earlier a part of the continent Africa. 
 2. 
 The  Peninsular  Plateau  of  India  was 
 earlier  a  part  of  the  continent  North 
 America. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.68.(b)  Only  2.  The  peninsular  plateau 
 of  India  was  a  part  of  the  supercontinent 
 Gondwana,  which  included  Africa,  South 
 America,  Australia,  and  Antarctica.  After 
 breaking  up  from  Gondwana,  the  Indian 
 subcontinent  drifted  northwards  and 
 collided  with  Eurasia  which  formed  the 
 Himalayas. 
 Q.69.  In  the  context  of  water  bodies, 
 Neap Tides occur : 
 (a) when the earth, moon and sun line up 
 in a straight line 
 (b) when the sun and moon are on the 
 opposite sides 
 (c) when there is so much interference by 
 continents 
 (d) when the sun and moon are at right 
 angles to each other 
 Sol.69.(d)  During neap tide, gravitational 
 and  centrifugal  forces  are  divided.  The 
 high  tides  of  a  neap  tide  are  lower  than 
 the  average  high  tide  and  the  low  tides  of 
 a  Neap  tide  are  higher  than  the  average 
 low  tide.  Spring  tides  -  When  the  sun,  the 
 moon  and  the  earth  are  in  a  straight  line, 
 the  height  of  the  tide  will  be  higher. 
 These  are  called  spring  tides,  and  they 
 occur twice a month. 
 Q.70.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 landlocked  Salt  Lake  in  South-West  Asia 
 between Israel and Jordan? 
 (a) Don Juan Pond  (b) Aral Sea 
 (c) Caspian Sea  (d) Dead Sea 
 Sol.70.(d)  Dead  Sea.  Its  salinity  of  340 
 grams  per  litre  of  water.  The  Dead  Sea 
 seawater  has  a  density  of  1.240  kg/L, 
 which  makes  swimming  in  its  waters 
 similar  to  floating.  The  Caspian  Sea  is 
 the  world's  largest  saltwater  lake  by 
 volume.  The  Aral  Sea  is  located  between 
 Kazakhstan  to  the  north  and  Uzbekistan 
 to the south. 
 Q.71.  Which  ocean  is  encircling  the 
 continent  of  Antarctica  and  extends 
 northward to 60 degrees south latitude? 
 (a) Atlantic Ocean      (b) Indian Ocean 
 (c) Pacific Ocean        (d) Southern Ocean 
 Sol.71.(d)  Southern  Ocean.  It  is  the 
 newest  of  the  world's  fiv e  oceans, 
 officially  designated  as  a  separate  ocean 
 by  the  International  Hydrographic 
 Organization  (IHO)  in  2000.  The  Atlantic 
 Ocean  is  between  the  Americas  and 
 Europe/Africa.  The  Indian  Ocean  is 
 between  Africa,  Asia,  and  Australia.  The 
 Pacific  Ocean  is  between  Asia  and  the 
 Americas. 
 Q.72.  The  name  of  the  strait  between  the 
 Pacific  and  Arctic  oceans,  separating  the 
 Chukchi  Peninsula  of  the  Russian  Far 
 East  from  the  Seward  Peninsula  of 
 Alaska, is the : 
 (a) Davis Strait  (b) Florida Strait 
 (c) Bering Strait  (d) Messina Strait 
 Sol.72.(c) Bering Strait.  Davis Strait : It is 
 located  between  Greenland  and  Baffin 
 Island  in  Canada.  It  connects  Baffin  Bay 
 with  the  Labrador  Sea  and  is  part  of  the 
 Northwest  Passage  in  the  Arctic.  Florida 
 Strait  :  It  is  a  channel  of  water  located 
 between  the  Gulf  of  Mexico  and  the 
 Atlantic  Ocean,  separating  the  Florida 
 Keys  and  Cuba.  Strait  of  Messina  :  It  is  a 
 narrow  passage  between  the  eastern 
 coast  of  Sicily  and  the  western  coast  of 
 Calabria  in  southern  Italy.  It  connects  the 
 Tyrrhenian Sea with the Ionian Sea. 
 Q.73.  In 1596, who suggested in his work 
 'Thesaurus  Geographicus'  that  the 
 continents  of  the  earth  were  not  always 
 located in their present positions ? 
 (a) Louis Agassiz 
 (b) Georges Cuvier 
 (c) Abraham Ortelius 
 (d) Dan Peter McKenzie 
 Sol.73.(c)  Abraham  Ortelius  was  a  Dutch 
 mapmaker  who  proposed  in  his  work 
 Thesaurus  Geographicus  that  the 
 continents  were  once  joined  together. 
 This  idea  laid  the  groundwork  for  the 
 continental  drift  theory,  later  fully 
 developed  by  Alfred  Wegener  in  1912. 
 Wegener  suggested  that  all  continents 
 formed  a  single  landmass  called 
 Pangaea  ("all  earth"),  surrounded  by  a 
 vast  ocean  known  as  Panthalassa  ("all 
 water"). 
 Q.74  .  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT  a 
 part of the ocean floor? 
 (a) The continental shelf 
 (b) The continental slope 
 (c) The deep sea shelf 
 (d) The deep sea plain 
 Sol.74.(c)  The  deep  sea  shelf.  A 
 continental  shelf  is  the  submerged  edge 
 of  a  continent  beneath  the  ocean.  The 
 continental  slope  extends  seaward  from 
 the  shelf  to  the  upper  edge  of  a 
 continental  rise  or  the  point  where  the 
 slope  begins  to  flatten.  An  abyssal  plain, 
 or  deep  sea  plain,  is  a  flat  region  on  the 
 ocean  floor ,  typically  found  at  depths 
 between  3,000  and  6,000  meters. 
 Oceanic  deeps  are  the  deepest  parts  of 
 the  ocean,  covered  with  fine-grained 
 sediments  such  as  clay  and  silt. 
 Examples  of  oceanic  landforms  :  Abyssal 
 plains,  Submarine  canyons,  Deep  ocean 
 trenches,  Guyots,  Seamounts,  Mid-ocean 
 ridges, Abyssal plains, etc. 
 Q.75.  The  thermocline  layer  is  the  ______ 
 layer of the ocean. 
 (a) second   (b) third   (c) fourth  (d) first 
 Sol.75.(a)  Second.  Scientists  have 
 divided  the  ocean  into  fiv e  main  layers. 
 Epipelagic  Zone  -  The  surface  layer  from 
 the  surface  to  200  meters.  Mesopelagic 
 Zone  -  The  twilight  zone  or  the  midwater 
 zone.  Bathypelagic  Zone  -  The  midnight 
 zone  or  the  dark  zone.  Abyssopelagic 
 Zone  -  The  water  temperature  is  near 
 freezing,  and  there  is  no  light  at  all. 
 Hadalpelagic  Zone  -  The  deepest  point  in 
 the  ocean  is  located  in  the  Mariana 
 Trench. 
 Q.76.  In  1962,  who  published  'The 
 History  of  Ocean  Basins',  which  outlined 
 the theory of how tectonic plates could 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 move, later called 'sea floor spreading' ? 
 (a) Alfred Wegener 
 (b) Louis Bauer 
 (c) Harry Hammond Hess 
 (d) George Edward Backus 
 Sol.76.(c)  Harry  Hammond  Hess,  an 
 American  geologist  and  naval  officer.  He 
 discovered  that  the  oceans  were 
 shallower  in  the  middle  and  identified  the 
 presence  of  Mid  Ocean  Ridges,  raised 
 above  the  surrounding  generally  flat  sea 
 floor  (abyssal  plain)  by  as  much  as  1.5 
 km.  Seafloor  spreading  is  a  geologic 
 process  in  which  tectonic  plates—large 
 slabs  of  Earth's  lithosphere—split  apart 
 from each other. 
 Q.77.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 correct  match  between  column-A  and 
 column-B ? 
 Column - A 
 (Elements in earth 
 crust) 
 Column - B 
 (Total % in earth 
 crust) 
 i. Oxygen  a. 8% 
 ii. Silicon  b. 28% 
 iii. Aluminium  c. 47% 
 iv. Iron  d. 5% 
 (a) i-a, ii-b, iii-c, iv-d     (b) i-c, ii-a, iii-b, iv-d 
 (c) i-c, ii-b, iii-a, iv-d     (d) i-b, ii-a, iii-c, iv-d 
 Sol.77.(c)  i-c,  ii-b,  iii-a,  iv-d.  Abundances 
 of  other  Element  in  the  Earth’s  Crust: 
 Calcium  (Ca)  -  3.63%,  Sodium  (Na)  - 
 2.83%,  Potassium  (K)  -  2.59%, 
 Magnesium  (Mg)  -  2.09%  and  others  - 
 1.41%.  Earth  has  three  layers:  crust 
 (upper  layer),  mantle  (middle  layer),  and 
 core (lower layer). 
 Q.78.  Continental  Shelf  which  is  the 
 shallowest part of the ocean has an 
 average gradient of: 
 (a) 1° or even less      (b) 2° or even less 
 (c) 8° or even less      (d) 5° or even less 
 Sol.78.(a)  1°  or  even  less.  The 
 Continental  Shelf  is  the  extended  margin 
 of  each  continent  occupied  by  relatively 
 shallow  seas  and  gulfs.  About 
 Continental  Shelf  :  The  shelf  typically 
 ends  at  a  very  steep  slope,  called  the 
 shelf  break.  Their  width  varies  globally, 
 with  an  average  of  about  80  km,  but  can 
 be  narrower  or  even  absent  in  some 
 regions,  such  as  the  coasts  of  Chile  and 
 the west coast of Sumatra. 
 Q.79.  Living  organisms  have  been  on 
 Earth  for  how  much  percent  of  Earth's 
 history? 
 (a) 60%     (b) 40%  (c) 80%     (d) 20% 
 Sol.79.(c)  80%.  Taking  Earth's  age  as 
 nearly  4.6  Billion  Years  (BY),  instead  of 
 13.7  BY  from  the  Big  Bang,  unicellular  life 
 appeared  nearly  3.8  BY  ago.  This 
 percentage  is  3.8/4.6  X  100  =  82.6%, 
 nearly. 
 Q.80.  Choose  the  correct  statement(s) 
 related to ‘continentality’. 
 A. 
 The  sea  exerts  a  moderating 
 influence on climate. 
 B. 
 As  the  distance  from  the  sea 
 increases,  the  people  experience 
 extreme weather conditions. 
 C. 
 As  one  goes  from  the  surface  of  the 
 earth  to  higher  altitudes,  the 
 atmosphere  becomes  less  dense  and 
 temperature decreases. 
 (a) B and C  (b) Only C 
 (c) A and C  (d) A and B 
 Sol.80.(d)  A  and  B.  Continentality  (i.e. 
 very  hot  during  summers  and  very  cold 
 during  winters)  found  in  inland  areas. 
 Continental  climates  are  common 
 between  40°  and  70°  north  latitude  and 
 experience  large  temperature  differences 
 between day and night. 
 Q.81.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 about oceanic trenches: 
 1. 
 The  Mariana  Trench  in  the  Pacific 
 Ocean  is  the  deepest  point  on  Earth, 
 with a depth of about 11,022 metres. 
 2. 
 The  Java  Trench,  located  in  the  Indian 
 Ocean,  is  deeper  than  the  Tonga 
 Trench  in  the  Southwestern  Pacific 
 Ocean. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.81.(a)  1  only.  Java  Trench  (also 
 known  as  the  Sunda  Trench):  Located  in 
 the  northeastern  Indian  Ocean,  its 
 maximum  depth  is  approximately  7,725 
 metres.  Tonga  Trench:  Located  in  the 
 Pacific  Ocean  near  the  Tonga  Islands,  its 
 deepest  point  (Horizon  Deep)  is 
 approximately  10,882  metres.  Other 
 Trenches:  Puerto  Rico  Trench  (8,400  m)  - 
 Atlantic  Ocean;  South  Sandwich  Trench 
 (7,432 m) - Southern Ocean. 
 Q.82.  What does El Niño refer to ? 
 (a) Hot water current 
 (b) Pressure water current 
 (c) High air pressure 
 (d) Low air pressur 
 Sol.82.(a) Hot water current.  The term El 
 Nino (Little Boy in Spanish) - Warming of 
 the  ocean  surface,  or  above-average  sea 
 surface  temperatures,  in  the  central  and 
 eastern  tropical  Pacific  Ocean  on  the 
 coast  of  Peru.  El  nino  leads  to  weakening 
 of  trade  winds  in  a  region.  La  Nina  (Little 
 Girl  in  Spanish)  -  The  periodic  cooling  of 
 ocean  surface  temperatures  in  the 
 central  and  east-central  equatorial 
 Pacific. 
 Q.83.  Which  of  the  following  oceans  is 
 surrounded  by  the  continent  of  North 
 America and South America to the east? 
 (a) Pacific Ocean       (b) Atlantic Ocean 
 (c) Indian Ocean        (d) Arctic Ocean 
 Sol.83.(a)  Pacific  Ocean  (world’s  largest 
 and  deepest  ocean):  It  represents  45 
 percent  of  the  global  ocean  surface.  The 
 International  Date  Line  (established  in 
 1884)  passes  through  the  mid-Pacific 
 Ocean.  The  Atlantic  Ocean  is  surrounded 
 by  the  continents  of  North  and  South 
 America  to  the  west,  and  Europe  and 
 Africa  to  the  east.  The  Indian  Ocean  is 
 bordered  by  Asia  to  the  north,  Africa  to 
 the west, and Australia to the east. 
 Q.84.  Consider the following statements: 
 1. 
 Due  to  the  attraction  of  the  Sun  and 
 the  Moon,  ocean  water  generally 
 experiences  two  high  tides  and  two 
 low tides in a day. 
 2. 
 Diurnal  tides  occur  when  there  are 
 two  high  tides  and  two  low  tides  each 
 day. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.84.(a)  Only  1.  When  the  Earth,  Sun, 
 and  Moon  line  up,  their  gravitational 
 power  combines  to  make  exceptionally 
 high  tides  where  the  bulges  occur,  called 
 spring  tides,  as  well  as  very  low  tides 
 where  the  water  has  been  displaced. 
 When  the  Sun  is  at  a  right  angle  to  the 
 Moon,  moderate  tides,  called  neap  tides. 
 There  are  generally  three  types  of  tides: 
 Diurnal  -  One  high  and  low  tide  each  day, 
 Semi-diurnal  -  Two  high  and  low  tides 
 each  day,  and  Mixed  -  Two  high  and  low 
 tides each day of different heights. 
 Q.85.  Which  of  the  following  oceans 
 extends into the Mediterranean Sea ? 
 (a) Indian Ocean  (b) Pacific Ocean 
 (c) Atlantic Ocean  (d) Arctic Ocean 
 Sol.85.(c)  Atlantic  Ocean  is  the  2nd 
 largest  ocean  in  the  world.  The 
 Mediterranean  is  an  intercontinental  sea 
 spread  between  Europe,  North  Africa, 
 and  Western  Asia  and  connected  to  the 
 Atlantic Ocean through the strait of 
 Gibraltar. 
 Q.86.  The  mohororvicic  (Moho) 
 discontinuity separates ? 
 (a) Earth's lithosphere and asthenosphere 
 (b) Earth's atmosphere and hydrosphere 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (c) Earth's crust and the inner core 
 (d) Earth's crust and the mantle 
 Sol.86.(d)  Earth's  crust  and  the  mantle. 
 Moho  discontinuity  -  It  occurs  at  an 
 average  depth  of  about  8  kilometers 
 beneath  the  ocean  basins  and  32 
 kilometers beneath continental surfaces. 
 Q.87.  Which  is  the  correct  sequence  of 
 oceans  according  to  size  (from  largest  to 
 smallest) ? 
 (a) Pacific Ocean > Antarctic Ocean > 
 Indian Ocean > Atlantic Ocean 
 (b) Pacific Ocean > Indian Ocean > 
 Antarctic Ocean > Atlantic Ocean 
 (c) Pacific Ocean > Atlantic Ocean > 
 Indian Ocean > Antarctic Ocean 
 (d) Pacific Ocean > Indian Ocean > 
 Atlantic Ocean > Antarctic Ocean 
 Sol.87.(c)  Pacific  Ocean,  the  world's 
 largest,  was  named  by  Ferdinand 
 Magellan.  The  International  Date  Line 
 passes through it. 
 Q.88.  The  percentage  of  earth's  water 
 found in the oceans is _________. 
 (a) 94 percent  (b) 97.2 percent 
 (c) 90.2 percent  (d) 92.2 percent 
 Sol.88.(b)  97.2  percent.  Water  covers  71 
 percent  of  Earth's  surface.  Distribution  of 
 Earth’s  water:  Oceans  (97.2%)  ,  Glaciers 
 (2.1%),  Groundwater  (0.61  percent); 
 Freshwater  lakes  (0.009  percent),  Inland 
 seas  (0.008  percent),  Soil  Moisture 
 (0.005  percent),  Atmosphere  (0.001 
 percent),  Rivers  (0.0001  percent).  2.7  % 
 of earth’s water is fresh. 
 Q.89.  How thick is the lithosphere? 
 (a) 25 to 45 km  (b) 10 to 25 km 
 (c) 10 to 200 km  (d) 5 to 10 km 
 Sol.89.(c)  10  to  200  km.  The  lithosphere 
 is  the  outermost  and  mechanical  layer  of 
 the  Earth  that  consists  of  the  entire  crust 
 and  the  top-most  portion  of  the  mantle. 
 Mechanically,  the  earth’s  layers  can  be 
 divided  into  lithosphere,  asthenosphere 
 (80-200 km), mesospheric mantle, outer 
 core, and inner core. 
 Q.90.  The  crust  is  the  Earth's  outermost 
 layer  that  is  less  than  ______  percent  of 
 Earth  by  mass,  with  oceanic  crust  and 
 continental crust often consisting of 
 more felsic rocks. 
 (a) 5        (b) 1        (c) 2        (d)10 
 Sol.90.(b)  1.  The  average  density  of 
 oceanic  crust  is  3.0  g/cm 
 3 
 ,  while 
 continental  crust  has  an  average  of  2.7 
 g/cm 
 3 
 .  The  mass  of  the  continental  crust 
 is  2.195.  The  continental  crust  (about  30 
 km)  is  composed  of  lighter  (felsic) 
 sodium  potassium  aluminum  silicate 
 rocks,  like  granite.  The  oceanic  crust 
 (about  5  km)  is  composed  of  dense 
 (mafic)  iron  magnesium  silicate  igneous 
 rocks, like basalt. 
 Q.91.  Consider the following statements: 
 1. 
 The  movement  of  Earth's  plates  is 
 classified  into  three  main  types  of 
 tectonic boundaries. 
 2. 
 Convergent,  divergent,  and  transform 
 are  the  three  primary  types  of  plate 
 movements. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) 1 only                       (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2           (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.91.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  The  movement 
 of  the  plates  creates  three  types  of 
 tectonic  boundaries:  convergent,  where 
 plates  move  into  one  another;  divergent, 
 where  plates  move  apart;  and  transform, 
 where  plates  move  sideways  in  relation 
 to each other. 
 Q.92.  Movement of fault lines can cause: 
 (a) a flood  (b) sudden rain 
 (c) an earthquake  (d) a storm 
 Sol.92.(c)  An  earthquake.  It  is  a  sudden 
 shaking  or  trembling  of  the  earth  which 
 lasts  for  a  very  short  time.  Earthquakes 
 tend  to  occur  at  the  boundaries  of  earth’s 
 plates.  These  boundaries  are  known  as 
 fault  zones.  Over  80  percent  of 
 earthquakes  occur  around  the  edges  of 
 the  Pacific  Ocean,  an  area  known  as  the 
 'Ring  of  Fire'  .  Instrument  used  for 
 measuring Earthquake: Seismograph. 
 Q.93.  What  is  the  average  salinity  of  the 
 Indian ocean? 
 (a) 350 o/oo  (b) 35 o/oo 
 (c) 3.5 o/oo  (d) 3 o/oo 
 Sol.93.(b)  35  o/oo  (35  parts  per 
 thousand)  .  Salinity  is  the  amount  of  salt 
 present  in  1000  grams  of  water.  A 
 salinity  of  24.7  %  has  been  regarded  as 
 the upper limit to fix ‘brackish water’. 
 Q.94.  Tsunami is NOT caused by: 
 (a) Hurricanes 
 (b) volcanic eruptions 
 (c) undersea landslides 
 (d) Earthquakes 
 Sol.94.(a) Hurricanes.  Tsunamis are 
 giant  waves  caused  by  earthquakes  or 
 volcanic eruptions under the sea. 
 Q.95.  Which  of  the  following  is  a  warm 
 current? 
 (a) Labrador current   (b) Falkland current 
 (c) Canary current       (d) Gulf stream 
 Sol.95.(d)  Gulf  stream  current  in  the 
 North  Atlantic  and  the  Kuroshio  Current 
 in  the  North  Pacific  are  warm  currents. 
 Warm  Currents  -  Alaskan  Current, 
 Tsushima  Current,  Brazilian  Current  etc. 
 Cold  Current  -  Labrador  Current,  Canary 
 Current,  Benguela  Current,  Falkland 
 Current,  South  Indian  Ocean  Current, 
 Okhotsk  Current,  Humboldt  or  Peruvian 
 Current etc. 
 Q.96.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding oceanic landforms: 
 1. 
 An  Atoll  is  a  group  of  islands  found  in 
 tropical  oceans,  consisting  of  coral 
 reefs and a central depression. 
 2. 
 A  guyot  is  a  shallow  body  of  water 
 separated  from  the  ocean  by  a  barrier 
 reef. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.96.(a)  1  only.  Guyot  is  an  undersea 
 mountain.  Lagoons  are  shallow  bodies  of 
 water  that  are  separated  from  the  ocean 
 by  a  barrier  reef.  Seamounts  are 
 underwater  mountains  that  rise  above 
 the  ocean  floor  but  do  not  reach  the 
 surface. 
 Q.97.  Ocean  acidification  is  the  ongoing 
 decrease  in  the  pH  of  the  Earth’s  oceans, 
 caused  by  the  uptake  of  ______  from  the 
 atmosphere. 
 (a) Methane  (b) carbon dioxide 
 (c) nitrogen  (d) argon 
 Sol.97.(b)  Carbon  dioxide.  Ocean 
 acidification  is  the  worldwide  reduction 
 in  the  pH  of  seawater  as  a  consequence 
 of  the  absorption  of  large  amounts  of 
 carbon  dioxide  by  the  oceans.  It  results 
 in  the  alteration  of  marine  food  chains 
 and food supply to humans. 
 Q.98.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding  the  lowest  known  point  on 
 Earth: 
 1. 
 Challenger  Deep  is  located  in  the 
 western Pacific Ocean. 
 2. 
 The  Great  Barrier  Reef  is  the  world’s 
 largest coral reef located in Australia. 
 3. 
 Factorian  Deep  is  the  deepest  point  in 
 the Pacific Ocean. 
 Which of the statements given above is 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only (b) 2 only (c) 3 only (d) 1 and 3 
 Sol.98.(c)  3  only.  The  lowest  known 
 point  on  earth  is  called  Challenger  Deep, 
 which  is  present  in  the  Pacific  Ocean. 
 Factorian  Deep  is  the  new  deepest  point 
 in  Antarctica's  Southern  Ocean.  The 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Great  Blue  Hole  is  a  giant  marine 
 sinkhole off the coast of Belize. 
 Q.99.  In  coastal  areas,  during  the  night, 
 wind blows from land to sea because: 
 (a) low pressure is created on land 
 (b) land cools slower than water 
 (c) water cools slower than land 
 (d) high pressure is created at sea 
 Sol.99.(c)  water  cools  slower  than  land. 
 The  wind  blowing  from  land  towards  the 
 sea  is  the  Land  breeze.  The  wind  blowing 
 from  the  sea  towards  the  land  is  a  sea 
 breeze.  Both  are  induced  by  differences 
 that  occur  between  the  heating  or 
 cooling  of  the  water  surface  and  the 
 adjacent land surface. 
 Q.100.  The  process  of  seepage  of  water 
 into the ground is called ____. 
 (a) Infiltration  (b) depletion 
 (c) Storage  (d) replenishment 
 Sol.100.(a)  Infiltr  ation  occurs  when 
 surface water enters the soil. 
 Q.101.  Which  water  body  covers  an  area 
 of  1,55,58,000  and  makes  up  only 
 푘푚 
 2 
 4.3% of the global ocean? 
 (a) Atlantic Ocean          (b) Arctic Ocean 
 (c) Southern Ocean        (d) Indian Ocean 
 Sol.101.(b)  Arctic  Ocean  is  the  smallest 
 and  shallowest  of  the  world's  fiv e  major 
 oceans. 
 Q.102.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about the Sargasso Sea: 
 1. 
 The  Sargasso  Sea  is  the  only  sea  in 
 the  world  that  does  not  have  a  land 
 boundary. 
 2. 
 It  is  located  in  the  Pacific  Ocean  and 
 is  named  after  the  presence  of  brown 
 Sargassum seaweed. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above  is 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1                      (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2          (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.102.(b)  Only  2.  The  Sargasso  Sea  is 
 the  only  sea  in  the  world  without  a  land 
 boundary.  It  is  bounded  entirely  by  ocean 
 currents  (North  Atlantic  Gyre:  Gulf 
 Stream,  North  Atlantic  Current,  Canary 
 Current,  North  Equatorial  Current). 
 Statement  2  is  incorrect  -  Sargasso  Sea 
 lies  in  the  Atlantic  Ocean.  It  is  named  so 
 because of the presence of brown 
 Sargassum seaweed. 
 Q.103.  Match  the  following  Seismic 
 Zones of India with their characteristics: 
 List I 
 (Seismic Zone) 
 List II 
 (Characteristic) 
 A. Zone II  1.Least seismically 
 active region 
 B. Zone III 
 2. Moderate seismic 
 activity 
 C. Zone IV 
 3. High seismic 
 activity 
 D. Zone V 
 4. Most seismically 
 active region 
 (a) A-1, B-2, C-3, D-4   (b) A-2, B-1, C-4, D-3 
 (c) A-4, B-3, C-1, D-2   (d) A-3, B-4, C-2, D-1 
 Sol.103.(a)  A-1,  B-2,  C-3,  D-4.  The 
 Bureau  of  Indian  Standards  has 
 classified  regions  in  India  into  4  seismic 
 zones  on  the  basis  of  historical  seismic 
 activity.  These  are  zones  II,  III,  IV  and  V. 
 The  magnitude  scale  used  to  measure 
 earthquakes ranges from  1 to 12. 
 Q.104.  The  _____  is  responsible  for 
 Earth's magnetic field. 
 (a) inner core  (b) mantle 
 (c) outer core  (d) crust 
 Sol.104.(c)  outer  core.  On  Earth,  flowing 
 liquid  metal  in  the  outer  core  of  the 
 planet  generates  electric  currents.  The 
 rotation  of  Earth  on  its  axis  causes  these 
 electric  currents  to  form  a  magnetic  field 
 which extends around the planet. 
 Q.105.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding Gondwanaland: 
 1. 
 Gondwanaland  included  present-day 
 South  America,  Africa,  Madagascar, 
 India, Australia, and Antarctica. 
 2. 
 North  America  was  also  a  part  of 
 Gondwanaland. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only                       (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2           (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.105.(b)  2  only.  Gondwana  was  a 
 large  landmass,  sometimes  referred  to 
 as  a  supercontinent.  The  remnants  of 
 Gondwana  make  up  around  two-thirds  of 
 today's  continental  area,  including  South 
 America,  Africa,  Antarctica,  Australia, 
 Zealandia,  Arabia,  and  the  Indian 
 subcontinent. 
 Neighbouring Countries of 
 India 
 Q.106.  Which  of  the  following  is  in 
 geographical proximity to Sri Lanka? 
 (a) Only Karaikal   (b) Karaikal and Yanam 
 (c) Only Mahe       (d) Only Yanam 
 Sol.106.(a)  Only  Karaikal.  Karaikal  is  a 
 town  in  the  Union  Territory  of 
 Puducherry.  Sri  Lanka,  formerly  known  as 
 Ceylon,  is  an  island  nation  in  South  Asia. 
 Located  in  the  Indian  Ocean,  southwest 
 of  the  Bay  of  Bengal,  it  is  separated  from 
 the  Indian  peninsula  by  the  Gulf  of 
 Mannar  and  the  Palk  Strait.  Sri  Lanka 
 shares  a  maritime  border  with  the 
 Maldives  to  the  southwest  and  India  to 
 the northwest. 
 Q.107.  Which  of  the  following  pairs  do 
 NOT share any borders? 
 (a) Bhutan and Arunachal Pradesh 
 (b) Bhutan and Meghalaya 
 (c) Bhutan and West Bengal 
 (d) Bhutan and Assam 
 Sol.107.(b)  Bhutan  and  Meghalaya.  The 
 Indian  states  of  West  Bengal  (183  km), 
 Arunachal  Pradesh  (217  km),  Sikkim  (32 
 km),  and  Assam  (267  km)  share  their 
 boundaries  with  Bhutan.  The  Kingdom  of 
 Bhutan  and  the  Republic  of  India  are 
 separated  by  a  699  km  long,  regional 
 border. 
 Q.108.  Which  of  the  following  countries 
 has  the  highest  Hindu  population  after 
 India? 
 (a) Bhutan  (b) Sri Lanka 
 (c) Myanmar  (d) Nepal 
 Sol.108.(d)  Nepal.  After  India,  the  next 
 nine  countries  with  the  largest  Hindu 
 populations  are,  in  decreasing  order  : 
 Nepal,  Bangladesh,  Indonesia,  Pakistan, 
 Sri  Lanka,  the  United  States,  Malaysia, 
 the  United  Arab  Emirates  and  the  United 
 Kingdom. 
 Q.109.  Assertion  (A):  New  Moore  Island,

 Reason  (R):  It  is  located  in  the  Bay  of

 between India and Bangladesh. 
 (a) Both A and R are correct, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A 
 (b) Both A and R are correct, but R is not 
 the correct explanation of A 
 (c) A is correct, but R is incorrect 
 (d) A is incorrect, but R is correct 
 Sol.109.(a)  Both  A  and  R  are  correct, 
 and R is the correct explanation of A. 
 Q.110.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 The  Maldives  are  located  to  the  south 
 of Lakshadweep Islands of India. 
 2.  The capital of Maldives is Male. 
 Which of the above statements is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                       (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2           (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.110.(c)  Both  1  and  2  .  The  Maldives 
 are  a  group  of  coral  atolls  that  were 
 formed  from  the  crowns  of  an  ancient 
 volcanic mountain range.

 Q.111.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  boundary  lines  of 
 India: 
 1. 
 The  McMahon  Line  is  the  boundary 
 between India and China. 
 2. 
 The  Radcliffe  Line  forms  the 
 boundary  between  India  and 
 Pakistan. 
 3. 
 The  Durand  Line  separates  Pakistan 
 and Afghanistan. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) 1 only                           (b) 1 and 2 only 
 (c) 1 and 3 only                (d) 1, 2 and 3 
 Sol.111.(d)  1,  2  and  3.  Pakistan  shares  a 
 border  with  India,  defined  by  the  Radcliffe 
 Line  (International  Border)  and  the  Line 
 of  Control.  India  has  15,106.7  Km  of  land 
 border  and  a  coastline  of  7,516.6  Km 
 including  island  territories.  India  shares 
 the  longest  border  with  Bangladesh 
 about  4,096.7  km  and  Shortest  with 
 Afghanistan  about  106  km.  The  Durand 
 line separates Pakistan and Afghanistan. 
 Q.112.  Which  trek  in  Bhutan  is 
 considered  to  be  the  crown  jewel  of 
 Bhutanese trekking? 
 (a) Jumolhari Trek 
 (b) Dagala Thousand Lakes Trek 
 (c) Snowman Trek 
 (d) Laya Gasa Trek 
 Sol.112.(c)  Snowman  Trek  .  It  is  a  27  day 
 track.  Other  Treks  in  Bhutan  -  Jumolhari 
 Trek,  Dagala  Thousand  Lakes  trek,  Laya 
 Gasa  Trek,  Druk  Path  Trek,  Tsaluna  Trek, 
 etc.  Treks  in  India  -  Kalindi  Khal  Trek, 
 Stok  Kangri  Trek,  Auden's  Col  Trek, 
 Chadar Trek, etc. 
 Q.113.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  Sino–Indian 
 border (LAC): 
 1. 
 The  Line  of  Actual  Control  (LAC)  is 
 divided  into  three  sectors  –  Western, 
 Central, and Eastern. 
 2. 
 The  Eastern  sector  of  the  LAC  lies 
 across  Himachal  Pradesh  and 
 Uttarakhand states. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only                      (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2          (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.113.(b)  2  only.  The  Indian  Military 
 has  divided  the  LAC  (Line  of  Actual 
 Control)  into  3  sectors  -  Western  sector 
 across  Ladakh  and  the  Chinese-held 
 Aksai  Chin,  The  central  sector  across 
 Himachal  Pradesh  and  Uttarakhand 
 states,  and  the  eastern  sector  across 
 Sikkim and Arunachal Pradesh states.

 Q.115.  Consider the following statements: 
 1.

 world's  largest  delta  and  contains  the 
 largest mangrove forests. 
 2.

 between India and Bangladesh.

 (c) Both 1 and 2              (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.115.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  Sunderban
