 Indian Drainage System 
 Q.116.  Which  of  the  following  rivers 
 forms  a  delta  along  the  Eastern  Coastal 
 Plains ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Narmada  (b) Tapi 
 (c) Mahi  (d) Godavari 
 Sol.116.(d)  Godavari.  The  Godavari  River, 
 also  called  the  Dakshina  Ganga  or  Ganga 
 of  the  South,  forms  a  large  delta  along 
 the  Eastern  Coastal  Plains  before 
 draining  into  the  Bay  of  Bengal.  It  is  the 
 second  longest  river  in  India  after  the 
 Ganga.  Major  delta-forming  rivers  on  the 
 east  coast:  Mahanadi,  Godavari,  Krishna, 
 and  Kaveri.  On  the  other  hand, 
 west-flowing  rivers  like  Narmada,  Tapi, 
 and  Mahi  do  not  form  deltas;  they  form 
 estuaries  because  of  their  short  course 
 and fast flow into the Arabian Sea. 
 Q.117.  Which  of  these  rivers  does  not 
 originate  in  the  Peninsular  Plateau  of 
 India ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Godavari  (b) Ganga 
 (c) Krishna  (d) Cauvery 
 Sol.117.(b)  Ganga.  The  Ganga  River 
 does  not  originate  in  the  Peninsular 
 Plateau.  Instead,  it  originates  from  the 
 Gangotri  Glacier  in  the  Himalayas  at 
 Gaumukh  in  Uttarakhand.  Origins  of  the 
 other  rivers  :  Godavari  —  Trimbakeshwar 
 near  Nasik,  Maharashtra  (Western 
 Ghats).  Krishna  —  Mahabaleshwar  near 
 Jor  village,  Maharashtra  (Western 
 Ghats).  Cauvery  (Kaveri)  —  Talakaveri  in 
 the  Brahmagiri  Hills,  Karnataka  (Western 
 Ghats). 
 Q.118.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  Ghaghara  River 
 system: 
 1. 
 The  Ghaghara  originates  from  the 
 Mapchachungo  glacier  in  the 
 Himalayas. 
 2. 
 It  forms  a  deep  gorge  at  Shishapani 
 before entering the plains. 
 3. 
 It  merges  with  the  Ganga  at 
 Allahabad  after  receiving  the  Sarda 
 River. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Only 1 and 3  (b) Only 1 and 2 
 (c) Only 2 and 3  (d) 1, 2 and 3 are correct 
 Sol.118.(b)  Only  1  and  2.  The  Ghaghara 
 River  originates  from  the  Mapchachungo 
 glacier  in  the  Tibetan  Himalayas.  It 
 carves  a  deep  gorge  at  Shishapani 
 before  entering  the  plains  of  Nepal. 
 Statement  3  is  incorrect  because  the 
 Ghaghara  joins  the  Ganga  river  near 
 Chhapra in Bihar, not at Allahabad. 
 Q.119.  The  Alaknanda  River  originates 
 from which of the following? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Satopanth Glacier (b) Zemu Glacier 
 (c) Milam Glacier        (d) Gangotri Glacier 
 Sol.119.(a)  Satopanth  Glacier.  The 
 Alaknanda  River,  a  major  tributary  of  the 
 Ganga,  originates  from  the  Satopanth 
 Glacier  near  Badrinath  in  Uttarakhand,  at 
 an  elevation  of  about  3,650  metres.  It 
 flows  through  important  confluences 
 (prayags)  such  as  Vishnuprayag, 
 Nandprayag,  Karnaprayag,  Rudraprayag, 
 and  Devprayag,  where  it  finally  meets  the 
 Bhagirathi  River  to  form  the  Ganga.  In 
 contrast,  the  Gangotri  Glacier  is  the 
 source  of  the  Bhagirathi  River,  the  Zemu 
 Glacier  feeds  the  Teesta  in  Sikkim,  and 
 the  Milam  Glacier  gives  rise  to  the 
 Goriganga in Uttarakhand. 
 Q.120.  Read  the  given  below  statements 
 carefully and choose the correct option: 
 Assertion  (A):  The  Chambal  River  is 
 known  for  its  badland  topography  called 
 the Chambal ravines. 
 Reason  (R):  The  Chambal  flows  through 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 a  deep  gorge  and  eroded  terrain  before 
 joining the Yamuna. 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) A is true, but R is false. 
 (d) A is false, but R is true. 
 Sol.120.(b)  The  Chambal  basin  is 
 famous  for  deep  ravines  formed  due  to 
 intensive  gully  erosion,  a  classic  example 
 of  badland  topography.  The  Chambal 
 indeed  flows  through  a  rugged,  eroded 
 landscape,  cutting  gorges  and  ravines 
 before  meeting  the  Yamuna  in  Etawah 
 district  (U.P.).The  badland  topography 
 (ravines)  is  formed  because  of  severe 
 erosion  by  the  river  and  its  tributaries  in 
 the  soft  alluvial  soils  of  the  Chambal 
 basin.  Hence,  the  reason  correctly 
 explains  the  assertion.  Chambal  and  its 
 tributaries  (Kalisindh,  Banas,  Sindh) 
 intensify  erosion.  These  ravines  stretch 
 across  Madhya  Pradesh,  Rajasthan,  and 
 Uttar Pradesh. 
 Q.121.  Which  of  the  following  is  an 
 erosional  landform  created  primarily  by 
 river action? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Moraine (b) Delta (c) Gorge (d) Levee 
 Sol.121.(c) Gorge. 
 A  gorge  is  a  deep,  narrow  valley  with  steep 
 sides,  formed  when  a  river  cuts  vertically 
 into  its  bed  through  the  process  of  erosion, 
 especially  in  the  upper  course  where  the 
 river  has  a  steep  gradient.  It  is  a  classic 
 erosional  landform  created  by  river  action. 
 Moraine  –  Depositional  landform  created 
 by  glaciers,  consisting  of  rock  debris.  Delta 
 –  Depositional  feature  formed  at  the  river’s 
 mouth  due  to  deposition  of  sediments. 
 Levee  –  A  natural  or  artificial  embankment 
 formed  alongside  a  river,  also  depositional 
 in nature. 
 Q.122.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  evolution  of 
 India's major drainage systems: 
 1. 
 The  ancient  Indo-Brahma  river  is 
 believed  to  have  split  into  three  major 
 drainage systems over time. 
 2.  The Ganga and its Himalayan 
 tributaries  represent  the  central 
 drainage system. 
 3. 
 The  western  drainage  system 
 consists  of  the  Brahmaputra  and  its 
 tributaries. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Only 2 and 3 
 (b) All 1, 2 and 3 are correct 
 (c) Only 1 and 3 
 (d) Only 1 and 2 
 Sol.122.(d)  Only  1  and  2.  The 
 Indo-Brahma  River  system  is  believed  to 
 be  an  ancient  unified  river  that  later 
 broke  into  three  drainage  systems:  the 
 Indus  (western),  the  Ganga  (central),  and 
 the  Brahmaputra  (eastern).  The  Ganga 
 and  its  Himalayan  tributaries  like 
 Yamuna,  Ghaghara,  Gandak,  and  Kosi 
 form  the  central  drainage  system. 
 However,  the  Brahmaputra  and  its 
 tributaries  constitute  the  eastern 
 drainage system, not the western one. 
 Q.123.  Which  river  is  the  main  source  of 
 water for the Hirakud Dam? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Godavari River  (b) Krishna River 
 (c) Mahanadi River       (d) Narmada River 
 Sol.123.(c)  Mahanadi  River.  The  Hirakud 
 Dam,  located  near  Sambalpur  in  Odisha, 
 is  built  across  the  Mahanadi  River.  It  is 
 one  of  the  world’s  longest  earthen  dams 
 (25.8  km)  and  serves  multiple 
 purposes—irrigation,  hydroelectric  power 
 generation,  and  flood  control  in  the 
 Mahanadi delta. 
 Q.124.  The  Jog  Falls  in  Karnataka  is 
 formed from which river? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Godavari  (b) Kaveri 
 (c) Sharavathi  (d) Krishna 
 Sol.124.(c) Sharavathi. 
 Jog  Falls,  also  called  Gerosoppa  Falls  or 
 Joga  Falls,  is  one  of  the  highest  plunge 
 waterfalls  in  India  (about  253  meters).  It 
 is  located  in  Shivamogga  district, 
 Karnataka.  It  is  a  major  hydroelectric 
 power  source  as  well,  with  the  Sharavathi 
 Hydroelectric Project built nearby. 
 Godavari  –  Flows  mainly  through 
 Maharashtra,  Telangana,  Andhra 
 Pradesh.  Kaveri  –  Famous  for 
 Shivanasamudra  and  Hogenakkal  Falls, 
 not  Jog  Falls.Krishna  –  Flows  through 
 Maharashtra,  Karnataka,  Telangana, 
 Andhra  Pradesh;  associated  with  Almatti 
 and Srisailam dams. 
 Q.125.  Trishulganga  is  a  stream  of  the 
 river: 
 (a) Ghaghara  (b) Beas 
 (c) Godavari  (d) Gandak 
 Sol.125.(d)  Gandak.  It  comprises  two 
 streams,  namely  Kaligandak  and 
 Trishulganga.  It  rises  in  the  Nepal 
 Himalayas  between  the  Dhaulagiri  and 
 Mount  Everest  and  drains  the  central  part 
 of  Nepal.  It  enters  the  Ganga  plain  in 
 Champaran  district  of  Bihar  and  joins  the 
 Ganga at Sonpur near Patna. 
 Q.126.  Select  the  odd  one  from  the 
 following  saltwater  lakes  on  the  basis  of 
 their formation? 
 (a) Pulicat lake  (b) Kolleru lake 
 (c) Chilika lake  (d) Sambhar lake 
 Sol.126.(d)  Sambhar  lake:  The  largest 
 inland  saltwater  lake  in  India  situated  in 
 Rajasthan.  Pulicat  lake  (Andhra  Pradesh 
 and  Tamil  Nadu),  Kolleru  lake  (Andhra 
 Pradesh),  and  Chilika  lake  (Odisha)  -  are 
 all  coastal  saltwater  lakes,  formed  by  the 
 seawater  entering  the  lake  through 
 creeks or rivers. 
 Q.127.  The  Zaskar  and  the  Nubra  are 
 tributaries of which river? 
 (a) Mahanadi  (b) Ganga 
 (c) Indus  (d) Kaveri 
 Sol.127.(c)  Indus  (Sindhu)  originates 
 from  a  glacier  near  Bokhar  Chu  in  the 
 Tibetan  region  at  an  altitude  of  4,164  m 
 in  the  Kailash  Mountain  range. 
 Tributaries  -  Chenab  River,  Jhelum  River, 
 Ravi  River,  Satluj  River,  Beas  River,  Shyok 
 River,  Gilgit  River,  Hunza  River,  Swat  River, 
 Kunnar River, Kurram River, Gomal River, 
 and Kabul River. 
 Q.128.  Which  of  the  following  rivers 
 flows  north  of  the  Tropic  of  Cancer  in 
 India? 
 (a) Mahanadi  (b) Tapi 
 (c) Penganga  (d) Yamuna 
 Sol.128.(d)  Yamuna.  Originates  - 
 Yamunotri  Glacier,  Flows  through  the 
 Himalayan  foothills  before  entering  the 
 Indo-Gangetic  Plain.  Tributaries  -  Tons, 
 Hindon,  Chambal,  Sind,  Betwa,  and  Ken. 
 The  Yamuna  River  joins  the  Ganges  River 
 near  Prayagraj  (Allahabad).  The  Mahi 
 River  is  the  only  river  in  India  that 
 crosses  the  Tropic  of  Cancer  twice. 
 Originates  in  the  Vindhya  Mountains  in 
 Madhya  Pradesh,  flows  through  Madhya 
 Pradesh,  Rajasthan,  and  Gujarat,  and 
 drains into the Arabian Sea. 
 Q.129.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  origin  of  major 
 Indian rivers: 
 1.  The spout emerging from the mouth 
 of  a  cow  statue  in  the  Mahadev 
 Temple  at  Mahabaleshwar  is 
 considered the source of the Krishna 
 River. 
 2. 
 The  Godavari  River  originates  from 
 Trimbakeshwar  in  the  Nashik  district 
 of Maharashtra. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (a) 1 only                    (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2        (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.129.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  Krishna  is  the 
 second  largest  east  flowing  Peninsular 
 river  which  rises  near  Mahabaleshwar  in 
 Sahyadri.  Origin  of  rivers  :  Kaveri  -

 Q.130.  What  is  the  salt  measurement  in 
 hypersaline lagoons? 
 (a) 30 to 35  (b) Less than 5 
 (c) Less than 100   (d) More than 100 
 Sol.130.(d)  More  than  100.  Hypersaline 
 lagoons  are  extreme  environments  with 
 salt  concentrations  significantly  higher 
 than  typical  seawater.  While  normal 
 ocean  salinity  is  around  35  parts  per 
 thousand  (3.5%),  hypersaline  lagoons 
 can  have  salt  concentrations  exceeding 
 100  parts  per  thousand  (10%).  These 
 environments  are  typically  found  in  arid 
 regions  with  high  evaporation  rates  and 
 limited  water  input,  such  as  the  Dead 
 Sea. 
 Q.131.  In  which  state  of  India  is  kund  or 
 Tanka used for water harvesting ? 
 (a) Rajasthan  (b) Andhra Pradesh 
 (c) Uttar Pradesh  (d) Punjab 
 Sol.131.(a)  Rajasthan.  This  region  faces 
 extreme  arid  conditions  and  low  rainfall, 
 so  local  communities  have  developed 
 innovative  systems  to  conserve  and 
 store  water.  Water  harvesting  structures 
 in  other  States:  Kere  (large  tanks)  in 
 Karnataka,  Cheruvu  in  Andhra  Pradesh, 
 Bandharas  and  Tals  in  Maharashtra, 
 Surangam  in  Kerala,  Eris  (tanks)  in  Tamil 
 Nadu,  Bhundis  in  Madhya  Pradesh  and 
 Uttar Pradesh. 
 Q.132.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  continental 
 shelves: 
 1. 
 The  Siberian  shelf  in  the  Arctic  Ocean 
 is  the  largest  continental  shelf  in  the 
 world. 
 2. 
 A  continental  shelf  is  the  edge  of  a 
 continent that lies under the ocean. 
 Which of the statements given above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) 1 only                        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.132.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  A  continental 
 shelf  is  the  edge  of  a  continent  that  lies 
 under  the  ocean.  Continents  are  the 
 seven  main  divisions  of  land  on  Earth.  A 
 continental  shelf  extends  from  the 
 coastline  of  a  continent  to  a  drop-off 
 point  called  the  shelf  break.  Example  of  a 
 Continental  Shelf  Landform:  Siberian 
 Shelf, Arctic Ocean. 
 Q.133.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 transboundary  river  between  India  and 
 Pakistan? 
 (a) Indus  (b) Ravi  (c) Beas  (d) Jhelum 
 Sol.133.(b)  Ravi.  This  river  flows  through 
 north-western  India  and  eastern 
 Pakistan.  It,  along  with  the  Beas  and 
 Sutlej  rivers,  was  allocated  to  India  under 
 the  Indus  Water  Treaty.  Indus  River  :  This 
 river  originates  in  western  Tibet,  passes 
 through  Kashmir,  and  flows  into  Pakistan 
 before  falling  into  the  Arabian  Sea.  It, 
 along  with  the  Chenab  and  Jhelum  rivers, 
 was  allocated  to  Pakistan  under  the 
 Indus Water Treaty of 1960. 
 Q.134.  In  which  state  of  India  was  the 
 Neeru-Meeru  programme  introduced  for 
 construction  of  water  harvesting 
 structures? 
 (a) Andhra Pradesh       (b) Rajasthan 
 (c) Uttar Pradesh  (d) Punjab 
 Sol.134.(a)  Andhra  Pradesh.  The 
 Neeru-Meeru  (Water  and  You)  program 
 was  launched  by  the  Water  Conservation 
 Mission  (WCM),  on  May  1,  2000.  It  was  a 
 Water  conservation  and  poverty 
 alleviation initiative. 
 Q.135.  Identify  a  water-harvesting 
 system found in Jaisalmer. 
 (a) Guls  (b) Johads  (c) Khadins  (d) Kuls 
 Sol.135.(c)  Khadins.  ‘Rooftop  rainwater 
 harvesting’  was  commonly  practised  to 
 store  drinking  water,  particularly  in 
 Rajasthan.  In  arid  and  semi-arid  regions, 
 agricultural  fields  were  converted  into 
 rain  fed  storage  structures  that  allowed 
 the  water  to  stand  and  moisten  the  soil 
 like  the  ‘khadins’  in  Jaisalmer  and 
 ‘Johads’  in  other  parts  of  Rajasthan.  In 
 hill  and  mountainous  regions,  people 
 built  diversion  channels  like  the  ‘guls’  or 
 ‘kuls’  of  the  Western  Himalayas  for 
 agriculture. 
 Q.136.  Which  of  the  following  rivers 
 marks  the  eastern-most  boundary  of  the 
 Himalayas ? 
 (a) Kali  (b) Teesta 
 (c) Satluj          (d) Brahmaputra 
 Sol.136.(d)  Brahmaputra  River  flows 
 through  the  northeastern  part  of  India, 
 originating  from  the  Angsi  Glacier  in 
 Tibet  and  passing  through  Arunachal 
 Pradesh  before  entering  Assam.  The 
 Teesta  River  rises  in  the  Pauhunri 
 Mountain  of  the  eastern  Himalayas  and 
 flows  through  the  Indian  states  of  Sikkim 
 and  West  Bengal.  The  Satluj  River 
 originates  in  Raksas  Tal  near  Mansarovar 
 in  Tibet.  The  Kali  River  flows  through  the 
 Uttara Kannada district of Karnataka. 
 Q.137.  Which  of  the  following  peninsular 
 rivers falls into the Arabian Sea? 
 (a) Mahanadi river         (b) Krishna river 
 (c) Sabarmati river        (d) Godavari river 
 Sol.137.(c)  Sabarmati  river.  It  is  one  of 
 the  major  west-flowing  rivers  in  India.  It 
 originates  from  Aravalli  hills.  Mahanadi 
 river:  Origin  -  Sihawa  (South  of 
 Amarkantak)  near  Raipur  (Chhattisgarh). 
 It  flows  through  Chhattisgarh  and  Odisha 
 and drains into the Bay of Bengal. 
 Q.138.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 The  Yamuna,  Gandak,  and  Kosi  are 
 important  tributaries  of  the  river 
 Ganga. 
 2. 
 The  Lohit  River  is  not  a  tributary  of 
 the  Ganga;  rather,  it  is  a  tributary  of 
 the Brahmaputra. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.138.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  The  river 
 Ganga  emerges  from  Gangotri  Glacier 
 and  ends  at  Bay  of  Bengal.  Ganges  is  the 
 longest  river  of  India.  It  is  2,525  km  long. 
 Its  tributaries  include  Yamuna,  Gandak, 
 Ramganga,  Son,  Ghaghra,  Gomti,  Kosi, 
 Tamsa,  Punpun,  and  Mahananda. 
 Brahmaputra  river  originates  under  the 
 name  of  Siang  or  Dihang,  from  the 
 Chemayungdung  glacier  of  the  Kailash 
 range  near  the  Mansarovar  lake.  It  enters 
 India  west  of  Sadiya  town  in  Arunachal 
 Pradesh.  Tributaries:  Dibang,  Lohit, 
 Siang, Burhi Dihing, Tista, and Dhansari. 
 Q.139.  Which  of  the  following  are  west 
 flowing rivers? 
 (a) Godavari and Mahanadi 
 (b) Krishna and Kaveri 
 (c) Ganga and Yamuna 
 (d) Mahi and Sabarmati 
 Sol.139.(d)  Mahi  and  Sabarmati.  Mahi 
 River  originates  from  Vindhyachal  Hills, 
 Madhya  Pradesh  and  meets  in  Bay  of 
 Khambhat.  Major  West-flowing  rivers  - 
 Indus,  Narmada,  and  Tapi.  Major 
 East-flowing  rivers:  Ganga, 
 Brahmaaputra,  Mahanadi,  Krishna,  and 
 Godavari.  Major  peninsular  rivers  - 
 Mahanadi,  Godavari,  Krishna,  Kaveri, 
 Narmada, and Tapti.

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Q.140.  Which  of  the  following  rivers  is  an 
 east flowing river? 
 (a) Mahi river  (b) Narmada river 
 (c) Godavari river  (d) Tapi river 
 Sol.140.(c)  Godavari  river.  It  rises  in  the 
 Nasik  district  of  Maharashtra  and 
 discharges  its  water  into  the  Bay  of 
 Bengal.  It  is  1,465  km  long  with  a 
 catchment  area  spreading  over  3.13  lakh 
 square  kilometers.  The  Penganga,  the 
 Indravati,  the  Pranhita,  and  the  Manjra 
 are  its  principal  tributaries.  The  Mahi 
 (Origin,  Northern  slope  of  Vindhyas), 
 Narmada  (Origin,  Amarkantak),  and  Tapi 
 (Origin,  Betul)  rivers  flow  westward, 
 draining into the Arabian Sea. 
 Q.141.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 The  confluence  of  the  Bhagirathi  and 
 Alaknanda rivers is at Devaprayag. 
 2. 
 The  Mandakini  river  meets  the 
 Alaknanda at Karnaprayag. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) 1 only                        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.141.(a)  1  only.  The  Five  Prayags  are 
 sacred  river  confluences  in  Uttarakhand. 
 At  Devaprayag,  the  Bhagirathi  and 
 Alaknanda  rivers  meet.  At  Rudraprayag, 
 the  Mandakini  and  Alaknanda  rivers 
 converge.  At  Nandaprayag,  the  Nandakini 
 joins  the  Alaknanda.  At  Karnaprayag,  the 
 Pindar  meets  the  Alaknanda,  and  at 
 Vishnuprayag,  the  Dhauliganga  merges 
 with the Alaknanda. 
 Q.142.  Which  state  of  India  has  the 
 maximum number of large dams? 
 (a) Maharashtra     (b) Madhya Pradesh 
 (c) Odisha               (d) Rajasthan 
 Sol.142.(a)  Maharashtra  leads  in  large 
 dams,  followed  by  Madhya  Pradesh  and 
 Gujarat.  Major  dams  in  Maharashtra 
 include  Jayakwadi,  Koyna,  and 
 Radhanagari.  India's  first  dam,  Kallanai, 
 was  built  over  2,000  years  ago.  Notable 
 post-independence  dams  include 
 Hirakud  (1957),  Gandhisagar  (1960), 
 Bhakra-Nangal  (1963),  and  Nagarjuna 
 Sagar (1967). 
 Q.143.  In  which  group  of  states  does  the 
 Luni River flow? 
 (a) Bihar and West Bengal 
 (b) Andhra Pradesh and Telangana 
 (c) Rajasthan and Gujarat 
 (d) Maharashtra and Karnataka 
 Sol.143.(c)  Rajasthan  and  Gujarat.  The 
 Luni  River  begins  near  Pushkar  valley  in 
 two  branches  called  the  Saraswati  and 
 the  Sabarmati.  These  two  branches 
 merge  at  Govindgarh,  and  from  there,  the 
 river  flows  out  of  the  Aravalli  as  the  Luni. 
 It  continues  westward  until  Telwara,  then 
 turns  southwest  to  join  the  Rann  of  Kutch 
 (Gujarat). 
 Q.144.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  land  of  Sapta 
 Sindhu mentioned in Vedic literature: 
 1. 
 The  Aryans  lived  in  the  land  known  as 
 Sapta  Sindhu,  meaning  the  land  of 
 seven rivers. 
 2. 
 The  seven  rivers  included  Indus, 
 Sutlej  (Sutudri),  Ravi  (Parusni), 
 Chenab  (Asikni),  Jhelum  (Vitasta), 
 Beas (Vipas), and Saraswati. 
 3. 
 The  Shipra  River,  flowing  through 
 Ujjain  in  Madhya  Pradesh,  was  also 
 one of the rivers of Sapta Sindhu. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 and 2 only                 (b) 2 and 3 only 
 (c) 3 only                            (d) 1, 2 and 3 
 Sol.144.(c)  3  only.  The  Shipra  (Kshipra), 
 a  195  km  tributary  of  the  Chambal  River 
 in  Madhya  Pradesh,  flows  across  the 
 Malwa  Plateau,  passing  through  Ujjain, 
 Ratlam,  and  Mandsaur  before  joining  the 
 Chambal.  The  Sapta  Sindhu,  mentioned 
 in  Vedic  literature,  refers  to  the  "Land  of 
 Seven  Rivers"  in  Punjab,  home  to  early 
 Aryan settlements. 
 Q.145.  Which  of  the  following  rivers 
 originates from the Himalayas? 
 (a) Godavari  (b) Tapi 
 (c) Narmada  (d) Ganga 
 Sol.145.(d)  Ganga.  Origin:  Gangotri 
 glacier  near  Gaumukh  (3,900  m)  in  the 
 Uttarkashi  district  of  Uttarakhand.  Here, 
 it  is  known  as  the  Bhagirathi.  It  cuts 
 through  the  Central  and  the  Lesser 
 Himalayas  in  narrow  gorges.  At 
 Devprayag,  the  Bhagirathi  meets  the 
 Alaknanda;  hereafter,  it  is  known  as  the 
 Ganga  (length  2,525  km).  Other  river 
 origin:  Godavari  -  Brahmagiri  hills;  Tapi  - 
 Satpura range; Narmada - Maikal ranges. 
 Q.146.  Subansiri,  Jia  Bharali,  Dhansiri 
 and  Puthimari  are  the  major  tributaries  of 
 which river? 
 (a) Godavari  (b) Mahanadi 
 (c) Brahmaputra  (d) Bhagirathi 
 Sol.146.(c)  Brahmaputra.  The  principal 
 tributaries  of  the  rivers  are  as  follows: 
 Godavari:  Pravara,  Purna,  Manjra, 
 Penganga,  Wardha,  Wainganga,  Pranhita 
 (the  combined  flow  of  Wainganga, 
 Penganga,  and  Wardha),  Indravati,  Maner, 
 and  Sabri.  Mahanadi:  Seonath,  Hasdeo, 
 Mand,  and  Ib  join  Mahanadi  from  the  left, 
 while  Ong,  Tel,  and  Jonk  join  from  the 
 right.  Bhagirathi:  Bhilangna  River,  Kedar 
 Ganga, Jadh Ganga, etc. 
 Q.147.  On  which  river  in  India  was  the 
 Somasila Dam built? 
 (a) Krishna river  (b) Tapti river 
 (c) Pennar river  (d) Sabarmati river 
 Sol.147.(c)  Pennar  river.  It  originates  in 
 the  Nandi  Hills,  Karnataka,  and  flows 
 through  Karnataka  and  Andhra  Pradesh 
 before  emptying  into  the  Bay  of  Bengal. 
 The  Somasila  dam  is  situated  in  the 
 Nellore  district  (Andhra  Pradesh). 
 Famous  dams  and  the  rivers  they  are 
 built  on:  Koyna  Dam  (Koyna  River),  Idukki 
 Dam (Periyar River). 
 Q.148.  The  largest  inland  salt  lake 
 ‘Sambhar’ is located in the ______of India. 
 (a) north - east  (b) north - west 
 (c) south - west  (d) south - east 
 Sol.148.(b)  north  -  west.  Sambhar  Salt 
 Lake  (located  in  Rajasthan)  spans  230 
 square  kilometers.  It  is  a  significant 
 depression  in  the  Aravalli  Range  and  is 
 commonly  referred  to  as  the  salt  lake  of 
 Rajasthan.  The  lake  receives  water  from 
 six  rivers:  Mantha,  Rupangarh,  Khari, 
 Khandela, Medtha and Samod. 
 Q.149.  Which  of  the  following  rivers  is 
 called ‘Singi Khamban’ in Tibet? 
 (a) Indus  (b) Brahmaputra 
 (c) Kosi  (d) Sutlej 
 Sol.149.(a)  Indus.  It  enters  Pakistan  near 
 Chilas  in  the  Dardistan  region. 
 Brahmaputra:  Originates  from  the 
 Chemayungdung  glacier  in  the  Kailash 
 range  and  is  known  as  Tsangpo  in  Tibet. 
 Sutlej:  Originates  near  Mansarovar  in 
 Tibet,  where  it  is  called  Langchen 
 Khambab.  River  Kosi:  Originates  near 
 Mount  Everest  and  is  known  as  the 
 sorrow of Bihar due to frequent flooding. 
 Q.150.  Match the pair correctly: 
 National 
 Waterways 
 Stretch 
 a) NW-1  1. Kakinada - Puducherry 
 b) NW-2  2. Kottapuram- Kallam 
 c) NW-3  3. Allahabad - Haldia 
 d) NW-5  4. Sadiya- Dhubri 
 5. Talcher - Dhamra 
 (a) a-1, b-2, c-4, d-5      (b) a-3, b-4, c-2, d-5 
 (c) a-4, b-3, c-5, d- 1     (d) a-2, b-5, c-3, d-1 
 Sol.150.(b)  a-3,  b-4,  c-2,  d-5.  NW-1  is 
 the  longest  waterway  in  India,  stretching 
 1,620  kilometers  from  Haldia  to 
 Allahabad.  NW-69  is  the  smallest  (5  km) 
 waterway  in  India.  It  is  part  of  the 
 Manimuthar River in Tamil Nadu.

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Q.151.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding Chilika Lake: 
 1. 
 Chilika  Lake  is  located  to  the  south  of 
 the Narmada delta. 
 2. 
 It  is  Asia’s  largest  brackish  water 
 lagoon  and  India’s  first  Ramsar 
 wetland site (1981). 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.151.(a)  1  only.  Chilika  Lake  is  located 
 to  the  south  of  the  Mahanadi  delta. 
 Chilika  Lake,  Asia’s  largest  and  the 
 world’s  second-largest  lagoon,  spans 
 Puri,  Khurda,  and  Ganjam  districts  in 
 Odisha.  Nalabana  Island,  a  bird 
 sanctuary,  lies  in  the  middle  of  Chilika 
 Lake,  while  Satapada,  where  the  lake 
 meets  the  Bay  of  Bengal,  is  home  to 
 Irrawaddy dolphins.  
 Q.152.  Delhi  ridge  is  the  water  divide 
 between which of the following rivers? 
 (a) Ganges and Sind 
 (b) Indus and Mahi 
 (c) Ganges and Indus 
 (d) Ganges and Yamuna 
 Sol.152.(c)  Ganges  and  Indus.  The  uplift 
 of  the  Potwar  Plateau  during  the 
 Pleistocene  upheaval  in  the  western 
 Himalayas  rises  the  Delhi  ridge.  The 
 major  water  dividers  of  India:  Vindya 
 range:  Divided  the  Ganga  drainage  basin 
 and  Narmada  river  valley.  Satpuda  range: 
 Divided  Narmada  river  and  tapi  river 
 valley. 
 Q.153.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  canal  systems 
 and Doabs of Punjab: 
 1. 
 The  Bist  Doab  Canal  System  lies 
 between the Sutlej and Beas rivers. 
 2. 
 The  Makhu  Canal  System,  built  in 
 1950,  draws  water  from  the  Sutlej 
 river. 
 3. 
 Doab  Bari  lies  between  the  Beas  and 
 Chenab rivers. 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above  is 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) 3 only  (d) 1 and 3 only 
 Sol.153.(c)  3  only.  Punjab's  Doab 
 systems  include:  Doab  Chaj  (Chenab  and 
 Jhelum),  Doab  Rachna  (Ravi  and 
 Chenab),  Doab  Bari  (Beas  and  Ravi,  also 
 called  Majha),  and  Suj  Doab  (Satluj  and 
 Yamuna, part of the Malwa region). 
 Q.154.  Which  of  the  following  pairs  of 
 Indian rivers is west flowing? 
 (a) Krishna and Kaveri 
 (b) Mahanadi and Krishna 
 (c) Narmada and Tapi 
 (d) Mahanadi and Godavar 
 Sol.154.(c)  Narmada  and  Tapi.  Narmada 
 River  (Rewa  River),  the  longest 
 west-flowing  river,  originates  from  the 
 Maikal  ranges  and  flows  through  Gujarat, 
 Madhya  Pradesh,  and  Maharashtra.  The 
 Tapi  River,  the  second-largest  west 
 flowing  river,  originates  from  Betul 
 district  in  Madhya  Pradesh  and  flows 
 parallel  to  the  Narmada.  Other  important 
 west-flowing  rivers  include  Periyar, 
 Bharathappuzha,  Netravati,  Sharavathi, 
 and Mandovi. 
 Q.155.  Match  the  following  dams  with 
 the rivers on which they are built. 
 a. Hirakud Dam  (i) Chambal river 
 b. Nagarjuna Sagar Dam  (ii) Narmada River 
 c. Sardar Sarovar Dam  (iii) Krishna River 
 d. Tehri Dam  (iv) Mahanadi 
 river 
 e. Rana Pratap Sagar 
 dam 
 (v) Bhagirathi river 
 (a) a-iv, b-iii, c-ii, d-v, e-i 
 (b) a-i, b-iii, c-ii, d-v, e-iv 
 (c) a-iv, b-iii, c-ii, d-i, e-v 
 (d) a-iii, b-iv, c-ii, d-v, e-i 
 Sol.155.(a)  a-iv,  b-iii,  c-ii,  d-v,  e-i.  The 
 Hirakud  Dam,  one  of  the  longest  in  the 
 world,  is  located  near  Sambalpur  District, 
 Odisha.  The  Nagarjuna  Sagar  Dam,  a 
 masonry  dam,  spans  the  border  between 
 Andhra  Pradesh  and  Telangana.  The 
 Sardar  Sarovar  Dam,  a  concrete  gravity 
 dam,  is  situated  near  Kevadiya  in 
 Gujarat's  Narmada  District.  The  Tehri 
 Dam,  a  multi-purpose  rock  and  earth-fill 
 embankment  dam,  is  in  New  Tehri, 
 Uttarakhand.  The  Rana  Pratap  Sagar 
 Dam,  a  gravity  masonry  dam,  stands  53.8 
 meters high in Rawatbhata, Rajasthan. 
 Q.156.  Which  of  the  following  rivers  is 
 joined by river Chambal from the south? 
 (a) Kosi (b) Yamuna (c) Teesta (d) Ganga 
 Sol.156.(b)  Yamuna.  It  rises  from  the 
 Yamunotri  Glacier  in  the  Himalayas.  It 
 flows  parallel  to  the  Ganga  and  as  a  right 
 the  bank  tributary  meets  the  Ganga  at 
 Allahabad.  Its  biggest  tributary  is 
 Chambal  River  on  the  right.  Other 
 important  tributaries  include  the  Hindon, 
 Sarda, and Giri rivers on the right and 
 Betwa and Sindh on the left. 
 Q.157.  Choose  the  coasts  that  are 
 running along the Arabian sea. 
 A. Northern Circars,       B Malabar, 
 C. Konkan,                       D. Coromandel 
 (a) Only A and C  (b) A, B and C 
 (c) B, C and D  (d) Only B and C 
 Sol.157.(d)  Only  B  and  C.  The  western 
 coast,  between  the  Western  Ghats  and 
 the  Arabian  Sea,  is  a  narrow  plain  with 
 three  sections:  Konkan  (Mumbai  to  Goa) 
 in  the  north,  Kannad  Plain  in  the  center, 
 and  Malabar  Coast  in  the  south.  On  the 
 Bay  of  Bengal  side,  the  plains  are  wide 
 and  level,  called  the  Northern  Circar  in 
 the north and the Coromandel Coast in 
 the south. 
 Q.158.  Which  river  flows  into  the  Wular 
 lake  in  the  north  and  then  into 
 Baramulla? 
 (a) Beas   (b) Sutlej  (c) Jhelum  (d) Ravi 
 Sol.158.(c)  Jhelum  River  originates  from 
 a  spring  at  Verinag  in  the  southeastern 
 part  of  the  Kashmir  Valley.  It  first  flows 
 northward  into  Wular  Lake,  located  in  the 
 northwestern  part  of  the  valley.  After 
 leaving  Wular  Lake,  it  changes  direction, 
 flowing  southwards.  At  Baramulla,  the 
 river  enters  a  gorge  in  the  hills,  carving  a 
 steep-sided  narrow  passage  through  the 
 Pir  Panjal  Range  below  Baramulla. 
 Eventually,  it  merges  with  the  Chenab 
 River at Trimmu. 
 Q.159.  Which  river  originates  from 
 ‘Rakshastal  north-western  tip’  near 
 Mansarovar ? 
 (a) Sutlej River  (b) Narmada River 
 (c) Ravi River  (d) Tapti River 
 Sol.159.(a)  Sutlej  River  enters  India 
 through  the  Shipki  La  Pass  in  the  Kinnaur 
 district  of  Himachal  Pradesh.  It  is  an  east 
 bank  tributary  of  the  Indus  River,  meeting 
 the  Indus  at  Mithankot  in  Pakistan.  Also 
 known  as  Shatadru  in  ancient  times,  the 
 Satluj  is  the  only  Indus  tributary 
 originating  from  Tibet.  The 
 Bhakra-Nangal  Dam  is  built  across  the 
 Satluj,  and  the  Indira  Gandhi  Canal  also 
 draws water from it. 
 Q.160.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  drainage  patterns 
 in India: 
 1. 
 Radial  drainage  pattern  is  formed 
 when  rivers  discharge  their  waters 
 into a central depression or lake, as in 
 Loktak Lake (Manipur). 
 2. 
 Centripetal  drainage  pattern  is 
 formed  when  rivers  originate  from  a 
 hill  and  flow  in  all  directions,  as  in  the 
 case of Narmada. 
 Which of the above statements are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.160.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  The  drainage 
 system  formed  when  rivers  discharge 
 their  waters  from  all  directions  in  a  lake 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 or  depression,  as  in  the  case  of  Loktak 
 Lake  in  Manipur,  is  called  Centripetal. 
 Radial  drainage  pattern:  When  the  rivers 
 originate  from  a  hill  and  flow  in  all 
 directions. Example - Narmada. 
 Q.161.  In  which  of  the  following  districts 
 is Baira Siul Dam located ? 
 (a) Bilaspur  (b) Chamba 
 (c) Kinnaur  (d) Mandi 
 Sol.161.(b)  Chamba.  The  Baira  Siul 
 hydroelectric  plant  is  an  operational 
 hydroelectric  power  facility  located  in 
 Chaurah,  Himachal  Pradesh.  It  is  a  type 
 of  Earthen  /  Gravity  &  Masonry  dam. 
 Length:  160m  and  Height:  53m.  River: 
 Baira  +  Bhaled  +  Siul  /  Ravi.  Other  dam  of 
 Himachal  Pradesh  and  district:  Pandoh 
 Dam  and  Bassi  Dam  (Mandi),  Kol  Dam 
 and  Bhakra  dam  (Bilaspur), 
 Karchham-Wangtoo  Dam  (Kinnaur), 
 Chamera  Dam  (Chamba),  Parbati  Dam 
 (mandi). 
 Q.162.  Choose  the  correct  statement 
 about the peninsular rivers. 
 A. 
 The  Narmada  rises  in  the  Malwa 
 plateau. 
 B. 
 The  Godavari  rises  from  the  slopes  of 
 the Western Ghats near Nashik. 
 C. 
 The  Mahanadi  rises  in  the  highlands 
 of Chhattisgarh. 
 D. 
 The  Krishna  rises  from  a  spring  near 
 Mahabaleshwar. 
 (a) Only A, B and C    (b) Only A, C and D 
 (c) A, B, C and D  (d) Only B, C and D 
 Sol.162.(d)  Only  B,  C  and  D.  The 
 Narmada  River  rises  from  the 
 Amarkantak  Plateau  in  Anuppur  district 
 in  Madhya  Pradesh.  The  Peninsular 
 drainage  system  :  It  is  older  than  the 
 Himalayan  river.  These  rivers  are 
 characterised  by  fix ed  course,  absence 
 of  meanders  and  non-perennial  flow  of 
 water.  Most  of  the  major  rivers  (except 
 Narmada  and  Tapi  which  flow  east  to 
 west)  flow  from  west  to  east.  The 
 Chambal,  the  Sind,  the  Betwa,  the  Ken, 
 the  Son,  originating  in  the  northern  part 
 of the Peninsula belong to the Ganga 
 river system. 
 Q.163.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  India’s  water 
 treaties with its neighbours: 
 1. 
 The  Farakka  Treaty  (1996),  signed 
 with  Bangladesh,  relates  to  the 
 sharing of Ganga waters. 
 2. 
 The  Mahakali  Treaty  (1996)  was 
 signed between India and Pakistan. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only  `        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.163.(b)  2  only.  Farakka  water 
 -sharing  treaty  -  It  was  signed  by  then  PM 
 H.  D.  Deve  Gowda  (India)  and  PM  Sheikh 
 Hasina  Wajed  (Bangladesh).  India’s 
 treaty  with  its  Neighbours:  Nepal  Treaty  - 
 Mahakali  treaty  (1996),  and  Pakistan  - 
 Indus Waters Treaty (1960). 
 Q.164.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is  NOT  correct  about  River 
 Ganga ? 
 (a) River Ganga flows from the 
 Himalayas all the way to the Bay of 
 Bengal. 
 (b) River Ganga in India is more than 
 2500 km long and is the most 
 populated river basin in the world. 
 (c) River Ganga and its river basin 
 support lots of wonderful wildlife, 
 particularly river dolphins, otters, 
 freshwater turtles, and gharials. 
 (d) The water level of River Ganga never 
 depends on monsoon and climatic 
 condition of the Himalaya. 
 Sol.164.(d)  The  Ganga  river  depends  on 
 the  rains  brought  by  the  southwesterly 
 monsoon  winds  from  July  to  October  as 
 well  as  on  the  flow  from  melting 
 Himalayan  snow.  The  Wildlife  Institute  of 
 India  conducted  a  survey  revealing  that 
 49%  of  the  Ganga  River  has  high 
 biodiversity. 
 Q.165.  In  which  district  of  Uttarakhand 
 does  the  Ramganga  river  originate  from 
 the Doodhatoli ranges? 
 (a) Almora  (b) Champawat 
 (c) Chamoli  (d) Pauri Garhwal 
 Sol.165.(d)  Pauri  Garhwal.  The 
 Ramganga  is  a  smaller  river  that  begins 
 near  Gairsain  in  the  Garhwal  mountains. 
 It  changes  direction  to  the  southwest 
 after  crossing  the  Shiwalik  and  enters 
 the  plains  of  Uttar  Pradesh  near 
 Najibabad.  It  eventually  joins  the  Ganga 
 near Kannauj. 
 Q.166.  Trishulganga  is  a  stream  of  the

 Himalayas  between  the  Dhaulagiri  and

 rises  in  the  Eastern  Ghats.  Sarada: 
 Originated  from  Kalapani  in  the 
 Himalayas  and  flows  along  Nepal's 
 western  border  with  India  and  joins 
 Ghaghra River.

 Q.172.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  major  ports  of 
 India: 
 1. 
 Kandla  Port  (Deendayal  Port),  located

 Karachi  Port,  which  went  to  Pakistan 
 during partition. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only  (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.172.(b)  2  only.  Visakhapatnam  Port, 
 also  known  as  Vizag  Port,  is  a  major 
 Indian  port  located  on  the  east  coast,  in 
 Andhra  Pradesh.  It's  a  natural  harbor  and 
 one  of  India's  12  major  ports,  serving  as 
 a  key  gateway  for  cargo  and  trade, 
 particularly for the eastern coast of India. 
 Q.173.  Which  river  basin  originates  from

 Shimsha, Hemavati, Bhavani, Lokapavani.

 Tungabhadra  River  is  formed  by  the

 Mudigere  Taluk  of  Chikmagalur  District

 Q.175.  With  a  stretch  of  about  724  km,

 Madhya  Pradesh  and  flows  westwards

 Devarayanadurga  is  a  small  hill  station 
 located  in  the  Indian  state  of  Karnataka. 
 Girna  River  -  It  originates  near  Kem  Peak 
 in  western  ghats  of  Maharashtra  and  is  a 
 tributary  of  Tapti  river.  Ghataprabha  River 
 - Right bank tributary of the Krishna River. 
 Waghur  River  -  It  originates  near  Ajanta  in 
 the Aurangabad district of Maharashtra.

 Q.178.  Which river from the following 
 flows through a rift valley? 
 (a) Tapi  (b) Brahmaputra 
 (c) Sabarmati  (d) Ganga 
 Sol.178.(a)  Tapi.  Rift  valley  :  It  is  a 
 lowland  region  that  forms  where  Earth’s 
 tectonic  plates  move  apart.  Famous  rift 
 valleys  :  Great  Rift  Valley  (East  Africa), 
 East  Rift  Valley  or  Hualien  -  Taitung 
 (Taiwan),  Baikal  Rift  Valley  (Southern 
 Siberia).

 Sea. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the

 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the

 (c) A is true, but R is false. 
 (d) A is false, but R is true. 
 Sol.182.(a)  Both  A  and  R  are  true,  and  R 
 is  the  correct  explanation  of  A.  Tapti 
 River  -  A  river  in  central  India  that  flows

 westwards  before  draining  into  the

 Q.187.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding types of waterfalls: 
 1. 
 A  cataract  is  a  waterfall  with  an 
 enormous volume of water. 
 2. 
 In  a  cascade  waterfall,  water 
 descends  vertically,  losing  all  contact 
 with the bedrock. 
 3. 
 In  a  horsetail  waterfall,  water 
 descends in distinctly separate flows. 
 4. 
 In  a  punchbowl  waterfall,  water 
 maintains  continuous  contact  with  the 
 bedrock. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                    (b) 3 and 4 only 
 (c) 1, 2 and 3 only     (d) 1, 2, 3 and 4 
 Sol.187.(a)  1  only.  Types  of  Waterfalls: 
 Horsetail  -  Descending  water  maintains 
 some  contact  with  bedrock.  Plunge  - 
 Water  descends  vertically,  losing  contact 
 with  the  bedrock  surface.  Block  -  Water 
 descends  from  a  relatively  wide  stream 
 or  river.  Multi-step  -  A  series  of  waterfalls 
 one  after  another  of  roughly  the  same 
 size  each  with  its  own  sunken  plunge 
 pool.  Segmented  -  Distinctly  separate 
 flows  of  water  form  as  it  descends. 
 Cascade  -  Water  descends  a  series  of 
 rock  steps.  Punchbowl  -  Water  descends 
 in  a  constricted  form  and  then  spreads 
 out in a wider pool. 
 Q.188  .  Which  of  the  following  rivers  does 
 NOT flow in Kerala State? 
 (a) Bharathapuzha       (b) Periyar 
 (c) Pamba  (d) Penner 
 Sol.188.(d)  Penner  -  It  rises  in  Nandi 
 Hills  in  Chikballapur  District  of  Karnataka 
 and  falls  into  the  Bay  of  Bengal. 
 Bharathapuzha  (Ponnani)  river  is  Kerala's 
 second  longest  West  Flowing  River, 
 draining into the Arabian Sea. 
 Q.189  . A long, winding ridge of stratified 
 sand and gravel is known as: 
 (a) whorl   (b) esker   (c) lop   (d) arch 
 Sol.189.(b)  Esker.  These  are  ridges 
 made  of  sands  and  gravels,  deposited  by 
 glacial  meltwater  flowing  through  tunnels 
 within  and  underneath  glaciers,  or 
 through  meltwater  channels  on  top  of 
 glaciers.  Other  depositional  landforms: 
 Drumlins  are  smooth  oval  shaped  ridge 
 -like  features  composed  mainly  of  glacial 
 till  with  some  masses  of  gravel  and 
 sand.

 Achler  (Maharashtra).  Mullaperiyar  is  on 
 the  Periyar  River.  River  (Dams)  :  Kaveri 
 {Alalur  (Karnataka),  Amaravathi,  (Tamil 
 Nadu)}. 
 Q.191.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is  correct  regarding  large 
 dams? 
 I. 
 They  have  social  problems  because 
 they  displace  a  large  number  of 
 peasants  and  tribals  without 
 adequate  compensation  or 
 rehabilitation. 
 II. 
 They  have  Environmental  problems 
 because  they  contribute  enormously 
 to  deforestation  and  the  loss  of 
 biological diversity. 
 (a) Only l         (b) Both l and Il 
 (c) Only ll        (d) Neither I nor Il 
 Sol.191.(b)  Both  I  and  II.  Advantages  of 
 Dams  -  Irrigation,  tourist  attraction,  flood 
 control,  hydro  power  generation,  etc. 
 Disadvantages  of  dams  -  High 
 construction  cost,  unbalanced 
 sedimentation, deforestation, ecological 
 imbalance, endangered aquatic life etc. 
 Q.192.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding Indian rivers: 
 1. 
 The  Banas  River  originates  from  the 
 Khamnor  Hills  of  the  Aravalli  Range  in 
 Rajasthan  and  is  a  tributary  of  the 
 Chambal River. 
 2. 
 The  Tapi  River  originates  from  the 
 Satpura Range in Rajasthan. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only 1                      (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2          (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.192.(a)  Only  1.  Tapi  originates  from 
 Satpura  ranges  in  Madhya  Pradesh.  The 
 Banas  river  originates  from  the  Khamnor 
 hills  of  the  Aravali  range.  It  is  a  tributary 
 of  the  Chambal  River.  It  is  also  known  as 
 “Van  ki  Asha”.  Length  of  Banas:  512  km. 
 Tributaries:  Kothari,  Dai,  Dheel,  Menali 
 etc. 
 Q.193.  Jabalpur  is  located  on  the  banks 
 of which of the following rivers? 
 (a) Ganga  (b) Godavari 
 (c) Chambal  (d) Narmada 
 Sol.193.(d)  Narmada  (Reva  River,  1312 
 km):  India’s  5th  longest  and  largest 
 west-flowing  river,  running  through  a  rift 
 valley  between  the  Vindhya  and  Satpura 
 ranges.

 Q.204.  What  is  the  meaning  of  the  term 
 Moraine? 
 (a) Melting of accumulated snow 
 (b) Material transported and deposited 
 by glacier 
 (c) Valley with vertical sides and wide 
 floor 
 (d) The higher level tributary 
 Sol.204.(b)  A  moraine  is  material  left 
 behind  by  a  moving  glacier.  This  material 
 is  usually  soil  and  rock.  Moraines  are 
 divided  into  four  main  categories:  lateral 
 moraines,  medial  moraines,  supraglacial 
 moraines, and terminal moraines. 
 Q.205.  Which  of  the  following  ports  is 
 known as ‘Diamond Harbour’? 
 (a) Port Blair  (b) Kochi 
 (c) Kolkata  (d) Mangalore 
 Sol.205.(c)  Kolkata  Port (Haldia seaport) 
 is  the  only  riverine  Major  Port  situated  on 
 the  Hugli  river  in  West  Bengal.  Cochin 
 Port  or  Kochi  port  lies  on  two  islands  in 
 the  Vembanadu  Lake;  Willingdon  Island 
 and  Vallarpadam  and  is  important  for  the 
 export of spices and salt. 
 Q.206.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about lakes of India:

 the Jhelum River. 
 Statement  II:  Dal  Lake  in  Jammu  & 
 Kashmir is also of tectonic origin. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only II  (b) Both I and II 
 (c) Only I  (d) Neither I nor II 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Sol.206.(c)  Only  I.  Wular  Lake,  located  in 
 Jammu  and  Kashmir,  is  South  Asia’s 
 largest  freshwater  lake.  Formed  by 
 tectonic  activity,  it  is  fed  by  the  Jhelum 
 River  and  Madhumati  stream.  In  1990,  it 
 was  designated  a  Ramsar  Wetland  of 
 International  Importance.  Dal  Lake, 
 located  in  Srinagar,  Jammu  and 
 Kashmir’s  summer  capital,  is  an  urban 
 lake  and  the  union  territory’s  second 
 largest.  It  is  popularly  known  as  the  "Lake 
 of  Flowers,"  "Jewel  in  the  Crown  of 
 Kashmir," or "Srinagar’s Jewel."

 Punjab  Plains.  Brahmaputra  plain  lies  in 
 the state of Assam.

 Q.217.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding the Indus River: 
 Statement  I:  The  Indus  was  called 
 Vitasta  by  the  Iranians  and  Greeks  about

 Sanskrit origin of the word "Hindos".

 is/are correct? 
 (a) Only I                          (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II               (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.217.(d)  Neither  I  nor  II.  The  river 
 Indus  was  called  Hindos  by  the  Iranians 
 and  the  Greeks  about  2500  years  ago. 
 The  word  is  derived  from  the  Sanskrit 
 word "Sindhu'', which means "river".

 Q.229.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding hydrological units: 
 Statement  I:  A  watershed  is  a  natural 
 geo-hydrological  unit  of  land  that  collects 
 water  and  drains  it  through  a  common 
 point by a system of streams. 
 Statement  II:  Watersheds  are  classified 
 based  on  their  area  —  Macro  (>50,000  ha), 
 Sub-watershed  (10,000–50,000  ha),  Milli 
 (1,000–10,000  ha),  Micro  (100–1,000  ha), 
 and Mini (1–100 ha). 
 Which  of  the  statements  given  above 
 is/are correct? 
 (a) Only I                    (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II        (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.229.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  A  watershed, 
 also  known  as  a  drainage  basin  or 
 catchment  area,  is  an  area  of  land  where 
 all  the  surface  water  from  rain,  snowmelt, 
 etc.,  converges  and  drains  into  a  single 
 point,  such  as  a  river,  lake,  or  other  body  of 
 water. 
 Q.230.  A  channel  of  a  canal  where  water 
 flows  under  the  influence  of  gravity  is 
 called ______. 
 (a) lift channel  (b) command area 
 (c) warabandi system  (d) flow channel 
 Sol.230.(d)  flow  channel.  It  is  the  flow  of 
 water  induced  by  the  effect  of  gravity. 
 The  surface  of  the  flowing  liquid  is  at 
 atmospheric  pressure  and  free  to  air.  The 
 example of open channel flow includes 
 flow in canal, river, etc. 
 Q.231.  On  which  river  is  Peacock  Island 
 located? 
 (a) Mahanadi  (b) Ganga 
 (c) Brahmaputra  (d) Yamuna 
 Sol.231.(c)  Brahmaputra.  Peacock 
 Island  or  Umananda  Island  is  the 
 smallest  inhabited  river  island  in  the 
 middle  of  river  Brahmaputra,  flowing 
 through  the  city  of  Guwahati.  Its  name 
 derives  from  Assamese  Uma.  It  is  also 
 known as Bhasmachal. 
 Q.232.  Which  of  the  following  is

 (c) Dhora dam  (d) Song dam

 Q.233.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about the rivers of Nepal: 
 Statement  I:  The  Karnali  River,  also 
 called  Ghaghara,  originates  on  the 
 Tibetan Plateau near Lake Manasarovar. 
 Statement  II:  The  Seti  River  is  the  longest 
 river of Nepal. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only I                          (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II               (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.233.(b)  Only  II.  The  Karnali  River  is 
 the  longest  river  of  Nepal.  The  river  cuts 
 through  the  Himalayas  in  Nepal  and 
 eventually  joins  the  Sharda  River  at 
 Brahmaghat  in  India,  becoming  the 
 Ghaghara River 
 Q.234.  Which  type  of  dam  is  a  temporary 
 dam  constructed  to  exclude  water  from 
 the specific area? 
 (a) Coffer  (b) Impounding 
 (c) Detention  (d) Debris 
 Sol.234.(a)  coffer.  It  is  built  to  permit 
 damming,  diversion,  or  dewatering  and 
 construction  of  a  permanent  structure  in 
 a  dry  area.  A  detention  dam  is  built  to 
 catch  surface  runoff  and  stream  water 
 flow  to  regulate  the  water  flow  in  areas 
 below  the  dam.  Detention  dams  are 
 commonly  used  to  reduce  the  damage 
 caused  by  flooding  or  to  manage  the  flow 
 rate through a channel. 
 Q.235.  Suryadhar  lake  is  located  in  which 
 of the following states? 
 (a) HimachalPradesh 
 (b) Arunachal Pradesh 
 (c) Uttarakhand 
 (d) Meghalaya 
 Sol.235.(c)  Uttarakhand.  Famous  Lakes 
 in  Uttarakhand  -  Auli  Artificial  Lake, 
 Baraadsar  Lake,  Bedni  Kund  ,  Bhalu  Dam, 
 Bhimtal  Lake,  Bhulla  Tal,  Bisurital, 
 Brahmatal Trek etc.

 Q.237.  Rain is liquid ______. 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (a) precipitation  (b) condensation 
 (c) decantation  (d) sedimentation 
 Sol.237.(a)  Precipitation  is  any  product 
 of  the  condensation  of  atmospheric 
 water  vapor  that  falls  under  gravitational 
 pull  from  clouds.  The  main  forms  of 
 precipitation  include  drizzling,  rain,  sleet, 
 snow, ice pellets, graupel and hail. 
 Q.238.  Which  of  the  following  dams  is 
 located in Jharkhand? 
 (a) Koyna  (b) Maithon 
 (c) Mettur  (d) Sardar Sarovar 
 Sol.238.(b)  Maithon  dam  is  on  the  river 
 Barakar  in  the  district  Dhanbad  of 
 Jharkhand.  It  has  an  underground  power 
 station,  the  first  of  its  own  kind  in  the 
 whole of SouthEast Asia. 
 Q.239.  Assertion  (A):  The  mouth  of  the 
 Indus  River  lies  to  the  north  of  the  Tropic 
 of Cancer. 
 Reason  (R):  The  rivers  Mahanadi, 
 Krishna,  and  Godavari  lie  to  the  south  of 
 the Tropic of Cancer. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) A is true, but R is false. 
 (d) A is false, but R is true. 
 Sol.239.(b)  Both  A  and  R  are  true,  but  R 
 is  not  the  correct  explanation  of  A.  The 
 Tropic  of  Cancer  is  an  imaginary  line  on 
 the  Earth's  surface,  located  at 
 approximately  23.5  degrees  North 
 latitude.  In  India,  the  Tropic  of  Cancer 
 passes  through  eight  states:  Gujarat, 
 Rajasthan,  Madhya  Pradesh, 
 Chhattisgarh,  Jharkhand,  West  Bengal, 
 Tripura, and Mizoram 
 Q.240.  What  kind  of  depositional 
 landforms  are  formed  when  streams 
 flowing from higher levels break into foot 
 slope plains of low gradient? 
 (a) Point bars  (b) Flood plains 
 (c) Alluvial fans           (d) Natural levees 
 Sol.240.(c)  Alluvial  fans.  A  point  bar  is  a 
 depositional  feature  made  of  alluvium 
 that  accumulates  on  the  inside  bend  of 
 streams  and  rivers  below  the  slip-off 
 slope.  An  alluvial  fan  is  a  triangle-shaped 
 deposit of gravel, sand, and even smaller 
 pieces of sediment, such as silt 
 Q.241.  Where  is  the  Baglihar  Dam 
 located? 
 (a) Gujarat 
 (b) Himachal Pradesh 
 (c) Jammu & Kashmir 
 (d) Telangana 
 Sol.241.(c)  Jammu  and  Kashmir. 
 Baglihar  dam  is  a  run-of-the-river  power 
 project  on  the  Chenab  river.  Important 
 Dams  -  Kadana  Dam  (Gujarat),  Chamera 
 Dam  (Himachal  Pradesh),  Singur  Dam 
 (Telangana). 
 World Drainage System 
 Q.242.  Which  of  the  following  has  the 
 highest salinity? 
 (a) Great salt lake  (b) Lake Van 
 (c) Red sea  (d) Dead sea 
 Sol.242.(b)  Lake  Van.  Being  the  largest 
 lake  in  Turkey,  it  has  the  highest  salinity 
 (330%).  As  a  saline  soda  lake  fed  by 
 small  streams,  it  is  one  of  the  few 
 endorheic  lakes  over  3,000  square 
 kilometers.  High  salinity  usually  prevents 
 it  from  freezing,  despite  high  altitude  and 
 winter temperatures below 0 °C (32 °F). 
 Q.243.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about river landforms: 
 1. 
 A  river  in  its  middle  course  is  capable 
 of  forming  meanders  due  to  both 
 erosion and deposition. 
 2. 
 Landforms  such  as  V-shaped  valleys 
 and  waterfalls  are  typically 
 associated  with  the  upper  course  of  a 
 river. 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.243.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  A  meander  is  a 
 U-shaped  bend  in  a  river  formed  by 
 erosion  and  deposition.  A  V-shaped 
 valley  is  created  by  river  erosion.  A 
 waterfall  is  where  water  drops  vertically 
 over  a  ledge.  A  delta  forms  at  a  river's 
 mouth where it meets the sea or ocean. 
 Q.244.  As  of  2021,  which  is  the  world's 
 largest  drainage  basin  with  an  area  of 
 about 70,00,000 km 
 2 
 ? 
 (a) Congo basin  (b) Amur basin 
 (c) Amazon basin  (d) Nile basin 
 Sol.244.(c)  Amazon  basin.  The  Amazon 
 Basin,  formed  by  numerous  tributaries, 
 drains  parts  of  Brazil,  Peru,  Bolivia, 
 Ecuador,  Colombia,  and  a  small  section 
 of  Venezuela.  It  lies  directly  on  the 
 equator  and  experiences  a  hot,  humid 
 climate  year-round,  with  both  day  and 
 night  feeling  equally  warm  and  sticky. 
 High  rainfall,  high  temperature,  and  high 
 humidity are the features of the basin. 
 Q.245.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 largest artificial lake of Asia? 
 (a) Naini Lake  (b) Bhopal Lake 
 (c) Dal Lake  (d) Chilika Lake 
 Sol.245.(b)  Bhopal  Lake,  also  known  as 
 Bhojtal,  was  created  in  the  11th  century 
 by  King  Bhoja  of  the  Paramara  dynasty.  It 
 is  situated  in  Bhopal,  Madhya  Pradesh, 
 and  is  constructed  on  Kolans  river.  Lakes 
 in  Madhya  Pradesh:  Sangram  Sagar, 
 Shahpura  lake.  Dal  Lake  -  It  is  often 
 referred  to  as  Srinagar's  Jewel,  is  a 
 freshwater  lake  located  in  Srinagar, 
 Jammu and Kashmir. 
 Q.246.  Which  river  originates  south  of 
 the  equator  and  flows  north  through 
 north-eastern  Africa  to  drain  into  the 
 Mediterranean Sea? 
 (a) Lena River  (b) Nile River 
 (c) Amazon River  (d) Congo River 
 Sol.246.(b)  Nile  River.  Its  length  is  about 
 6,695  kilometers  (4,160  miles).  The  Nile 
 basin  is  huge  and  includes  parts  of 
 Tanzania,  Burundi,  Rwanda,  Congo 
 (Kinshasa),  Kenya.  Other  important  rivers 
 of  the  world  :  Lena  River  :  Origin  -  Baikal 
 range,  Drains  -  Laptev  Sea.  Amazon 
 River  :  Origin  -  Andes  Mountains,  Drains  - 
 Atlantic  Ocean.  Congo  River  (cross  the 
 equator  twice)  :  Origin  -  Africa  Rift,  Drains 
 - Atlantic Ocean.

 highest waterfall in the world.

 Q.251.  Which  sea,  located  in  the  north

 about 3000 ft, Location -  Antarctica.

 Q.255.  Name  the  state  which  is  the 
 largest producer of lignite coal ? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Maharashtra             (b) Gujarat 
 (c) Rajasthan                  (d) Tamil Nadu 
 Sol.255.(d)  Tamil  Nadu.  Coal  is  a  vital 
 mineral  in  India,  mainly  used  for  thermal 
 power  generation  and  iron  smelting.  It  is 
 found  in  Gondwana  and  Tertiary 
 deposits,  with  about  80%  being 
 non-coking  bituminous  coal.  Major 
 Gondwana  coalfields  lie  in  the  Damodar 
 Valley,  with  Jharia  being  the  largest  and 
 Raniganj  the  second.  Important  coal 
 mining  centres  include  Singrauli,  Korba, 
 Talcher,  Chanda–Wardha,  and  Singareni, 
 while  Tertiary  coal  is  found  in  Assam, 
 Meghalaya,  Arunachal  Pradesh,  and 
 Nagaland.  Lignite  deposits  occur  in 
 Tamil  Nadu,  Puducherry,  Gujarat,  and 
 Jammu & Kashmir. 
 Q.256.  Name the major coal producing 
 region located in Telangana state? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Singareni Coalfield 
 (b) Raniganj Coalfield 
 (c) Talcher Coalfield 
 (d) Jharia Coalfield 
 Sol.256.(a)  Singareni  Coalfield.  These 
 coal reserves are located in the Pranhita 
 -Godavari  Valley  and  are  managed  by  The 
 Singareni  Collieries  Company  Limited 
 (SCCL),  a  government-owned  coal  mining 
 company.  This  coalfield  is  a  crucial 
 source  of  coal  for  the  power  plants  and 
 other  industries  in  Southern  India.  Major 
 coalfields  in  India:  Raniganj  Coalfield 
 (West  Bengal),  Talcher  Coalfield 
 (Odisha),  Jharia  (Jharkhand),  Korba 
 (Chhattisgarh). 
 Q.257.  Which  of  the  following  river 
 valleys  is  NOT  known  for  coal  deposits  in 
 India ? 
 (a) Mahanadi  (b) Satluj 
 (c) Godavari  (d) Son 
 Sol.257.(b)  Satluj.  The  Satluj  originates 
 in  the  'Raksas  tal'  near  Mansarovar  at  an 
 altitude  of  4,555  m  in  Tibet  where  it  is 
 known  as  Langchen  Khambab.  Satluj,  is 
 the  longest  of  the  fiv e  rivers  that  flow 
 through  the  historic  crossroads  region  of 
 Punjab  in  northern  India  and  Pakistan. 
 The  Damodar  Valley  is  particularly  rich  in 
 coal  and  is  known  as  the  "Ruhr  of  India" 
 because  of  its  similarities  to  the  coal-rich 
 Ruhr region of Germany.

 Q.260.  Which  type  of  coal  is  found  in 
 Damodar valley? 
 (a) Anthracite  (b) Lignite 
 (c) Bituminous  (d) Peat 
 Sol.260.(c)  Bituminous  is  a  type  of  coal 
 with  a  carbon  content  ranging  from  60% 
 to  80%  and  a  relatively  low  moisture 
 content,  making  it  highly  efficient  for 
 energy  production.  It  has  a  high  calorific 
 value.  The  Damodar  Valley,  located  in  the 
 states  of  Jharkhand  and  West  Bengal  in 
 eastern  India,  is  renowned  for  its 
 extensive  bituminous  coal  deposits.  This 
 region  is  part  of  the  Jharia  and  Raniganj 
 coalfields,  which  are  significant 
 contributors to India's coal production. 
 Q.261.  Which  of  the  following  is  an 
 inosilicate  amphibole  mineral  used  in 
 highway  construction  and  as  railroad 
 ballast? 
 (a) Shale  (b) Olivine 
 (c) Geyserite  (d) Hornblende 
 Sol.261.(d)  Hornblende:  It  is  an 
 inosilicate  amphibole  mineral.  Uses: 
 Highway  construction:  As  an  aggregate 
 for  road  bases  and  surfaces.  Railroad 
 ballast: To provide drainage and stability 
 under railroad tracks. 
 Q.262.  Which  coal  is  low-grade,  brown 
 and soft with high moisture content? 
 (a) Metallurgical  (b) Lignite 
 (c) Bituminous  (d) Anthracite 
 Sol.262.(b)  Lignite  .  Bituminous: 
 Medium-grade,  black,  and  less  moisture 
 content  than  lignite,  used  for  electricity, 
 industrial,  and  domestic  purposes. 
 Anthracite:  High-grade,  hard,  black,  and 
 very  low  moisture  content,  used  for 
 residential  heating  and  industrial 
 processes.  Tamil  Nadu  is  the  largest 
 producer of lignite in India. 
 Q.263.  Match the following renewable 
 sources  of  energy  with  their  producing 
 regions in India correctly. 
 Renewable source 
 of energy 
 Regions in India 
 1. Tidal energy  a. Gulf of Khambhat 
 2. Wind energy  b. Nagercoil 
 3. Geo Thermal 
 energy 
 c. Puga valley 
 (a) 1-c, 2-b, 3-a  (b) 1-a, 2-b, 3-c 
 (c) 1-b, 2-a, 3-c  (d) 1-b, 2-c, 3-a 
 Sol.263.(b)  1-a,  2-b,  3-c.  Renewable 
 energy  comes  from  natural  sources  that 
 are  regenerated  more  quickly  than  they 
 are  used  up.  They  are  natural  and 
 self-replenishing,  and  usually  have  a  low- 
 or  zero-carbon  footprint.  Examples  of 
 renewable  energy  sources  include  wind 
 power,  solar  power,  bioenergy  (organic 
 matter  burned  as  a  fuel)  and 
 hydroelectric.

 Q.266.  Jharkhand  and  Chhattisgarh 
 states  are  rich  with  ____________  material 
 deposits. 
 (a) gold  (b) petroleum 
 (c) silicon  (d) coal 
 Sol.266.(d)  Coal.  Jharkhand  -  40%  of  the

 uranium,  and  pyrite.  Jharkhand  ranks  1st

 include  Korea,  Korba,  Raigarh,  and 
 Sarguja districts. 
 Q.267.  The  Salal  Project,  also  called  the 
 Salal  Hydroelectric  Power  Station,  is  on 
 which river? 
 (a) Chenab  (b) Krishna 
 (c) Ganga  (d) Ravi 
 Sol.267.(a)  Chenab.  Salal  Hydroelectric 
 Power  Station  is  a  run-of-the-river  power 
 project  in  the  Reasi  district  of  Jammu 
 and  Kashmir.  The  project  has  a  capacity 
 of  690  MW.  The  beneficiary  states  are 
 Uttar  Pradesh,  Jammu  and  Kashmir, 
 Punjab,  Haryana,  Delhi,  Himachal 
 Pradesh,  Chandigarh  &  Rajasthan. 
 Important  hydel  power  stations  and  the 
 rivers  on  which  they  are  located:  Bhakra 
 Nangal  (Sutlej  River),  Gandhi  Sagar  Dam 
 (Chambal  River),  and  Nagarjuna  Sagar 
 Dam (Krishna River). 
 Q.268.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding sources of energy: 
 1. 
 Solar  power  and  geothermal  energy 
 are  examples  of  conventional 
 sources of energy. 
 2. 
 Coal,  petroleum,  natural  gas,  and 
 nuclear  energy  are  conventional 
 sources of energy. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1                    (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2         (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.268.(a)  Only  1.  Mineral  fuels  like 
 coal,  petroleum  and  natural  gas  (known 
 as  fossil  fuels),  nuclear  energy  minerals, 
 are  the  conventional  sources  of  energy. 
 These  conventional  sources  are 
 exhaustible resources. 
 Q.269.  The  Durg-Bastar-Chandrapur  iron 
 ore belt lies in _____ states. 
 (a) Maharashtra and Karnataka 
 (b) Chhattisgarh and Maharashtra 
 (c) Maharashtra and Telangana 
 (d) Chhattisgarh and Jharkhand 
 Sol.269.(b)Chhattisgarh  and  Maharashtra. 
 The  Durg-Bastar-Chandrapur  iron  ore  belt 
 stretches  across  the  Durg  and  Bastar 
 districts  in  Chhattisgarh  and  the 
 Chandrapur  district  in  Maharashtra.  Iron 
 ore  mines  in  India:  Bailadila  (Chhattisgarh), 
 Joda  (Odisha),  Noamundi  (Jharkhand), 
 and Kudremukh (Karnataka).

 Sol.270.(b)  Hazira-Vijaipur-Jagdishpur 
 (HVJ)  is  India's  first  cross-country  natural 
 gas  pipeline  commissioned  in  1987-88 
 with  compressor  stations  at  Hazira, 
 Jhabua  (Madhya  Pradesh),  Vijaipur 
 (Madhya  Pradesh)  and  Auraiya  (Uttar 
 Pradesh).  It  transports  natural  gas  from 
 Hazira  in  Gujarat  to  Jagdishpur  in  Uttar 
 Pradesh.

 Q.275.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  coal  deposits  in 
 India: 
 1. 
 Tertiary  coal  deposits  are  mainly 
 found in Bihar and Haryana. 
 2. 
 Lignite  occurs  in  the  Himalayan 
 states  of  Himachal  Pradesh  and 
 Uttarakhand. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1                     (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2          (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.275.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  Tertiary  coals, 
 which  include  lignite  and  sub-bituminous 
 coal,  occur  in  Assam,  Arunachal  Pradesh, 
 Meghalaya  and  Nagaland.  It  is  extracted 
 from  Darangiri,  Cherrapunji,  Mewlong 
 and  Langrin  (Meghalaya);  Makum,  Jaipur 
 and  Nazira  (Assam);  Namchik  -  Namphuk 
 (Arunachal  Pradesh)  and  Kalakot 
 (Jammu  and  Kashmir).  The  brown  coal 
 or  lignite  occurs  in  the  coastal  areas  of 
 Tamil  Nadu,  Pondicherry,  Gujarat  and 
 Jammu and Kashmir. 
 Q.276.  Koderma Gaya Hazaribagh belt is 
 known for the production of which of the 
 following minerals? 
 (a) Bauxite  (b) Mica  (c) Coal  (d) Copper 
 Sol.276.(b)  Mica  deposits  are  found  on 
 the  northern  edge  of  the  Chota  Nagpur 
 Plateau,  with  the  Koderma  -  Gaya  - 
 Hazaribagh  belt  in  Jharkhand  leading 
 production.  Major  mica-producing 
 regions  include  Gaya,  Hazaribagh,  Ajmer, 
 Beawar,  Bhilwara,  Udaipur,  Dungarpur, 
 Banswara,  and  Nellore.  The  main 
 mica-producing states are Jharkhand, 
 Rajasthan, and Andhra Pradesh. 
 Q.277.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT 
 among  the  commercial  sources  of 
 energy? 
 (a) Petroleum  (b) Electricity 
 (c) Fuel wood  (d) Hydropower 
 Sol.277.(c)  Fuel  wood.  It  is  a  non  - 
 commercial  or  traditional  source  of 
 energy,  primarily  used  for  domestic 
 purposes  such  as  cooking  and  heating. 
 Commercial  sources  of  energy  are 
 typically:  Fossil  fuels  (petroleum,  coal, 
 natural  gas),  Electricity  (produced  from  a 
 variety  of  energy  sources),  Hydropower 
 (electricity  generated  from  water 
 resources).

 (Assam),  Rajasthan  (Largest  producer  of 
 Petroleum in India), Gujarat.

 Q.284.  What  are  the  main  energy  sources 
 for earth's internal heat engine? 
 (a) Radiogenic heat and oceanic tide 
 heat 
 (b) Heat from volcanoes and solar heat 
 (c) Solar heat and oceanic tide heat 
 (d) Radiogenic heat and primordial heat 
 Sol.284.(d)  The  Earth's  heat  is  the 
 internal  heat  that  arises  from  two 
 sources  :  The  decay  of  radioactive 
 isotopes  in  crustal  rocks  and  the  mantle, 
 and  primordial  heat  left  over  from  the 
 planet's  fier y  formation.  Geothermal 
 energy  is  the  heat  produced  deep  in  the 
 Earth's core.

 Q.287.  The approximate amount of silica 
 present in cement is: 
 (a) between 27% and 35% 
 (b) between 37% and 45% 
 (c) between 17% and 25% 
 (d) between 47% and 55% 
 Sol.287.(c)  Between  17%  and  25%. 
 Cement  -  It  was  first  introduced  in 
 England  in  1824  by  Joseph  Aspdin. 
 Composition  of  Cement:  Lime  (60-65%), 
 Silica  (17-25%),  Alumina  (3-8%), 
 Magnesia  (1-3%),  Iron  oxide  (0.5-6%), 
 Calcium  Sulfate  (0.1-0.5%),  Sulfur 
 Trioxide (1-3%), Alkaline (0-1%).

 Q.290.  The  main  mineral  constituents  of 
 the continental mass are ____ and _____ . 
 (a) silica, magnesium 
 (b) silica, alumina 
 (c) oxygen, carbon 
 (d) alumina, magnesium 
 Sol.290.(b)  Silica,  alumina.  It  is  often 
 termed  Sial  (Si-silica  and  Al  -  aluminum). 
 Earth  (three  layers):  Crust,  Mantle,  and 
 Core.  Crust  (outermost)  -  It  is  about  5 
 km  thick  under  the  oceans  and  about  30 
 km  thick  under  the  continents.  Mantle  - 
 The  major  constituent  elements  are 
 silicon  and  Magnesium.  Core  (mostly 
 Iron  and  nickel)  -  This  is  the  innermost 
 layer of the earth. 
 Q.291.  Which  state  among  the  following 
 has  the  largest  reserves  of  gold  ore  in 
 India as of 2021? 
 (a) Jharkhand  (b) Rajasthan 
 (c) Bihar  (d) Karnataka 
 Sol.291.(c)  Bihar.  Karnataka  (Land  of 
 Gold)  produces  80%  of  the  gold  in  India. 
 Three  goldfields  in  India  -  Kolar  Gold 
 Field  (Karnataka),  Kolar  Hutti  Goldfield 
 (Karnataka)  and  Ramgiri  Gold  Fields 
 (Andhra  Pradesh).  Largest  producer  of 
 Gold  in  the  world  -  China  .  The  largest 
 producers  of  gold  ore  -  Bihar  (44%), 
 Rajasthan  (25%),  Karnataka  (21%),  West 
 Bengal (3%) and Jharkhand (2%).

 Q.296  .  Peat  coal  has  ____  carbon  and 
 ____ moisture content? 
 (a) High, Low  (b) Low, high 
 (c) High, High  (d) Low, Low 
 Sol.296.(b)  Low,  high.  Peat  is  a  soft,  dark 
 brown  coal  that  is  formed  from  dead  and 
 decaying  organic  matter.  It  has  an  energy 
 density  of  15  MJ/kg.  It  has  a  low  heating 
 capacity.  India  has  the  fifth-largest  coal 
 reserves  in  the  world.  Coal  India  Limited 
 (CIL)  (Public  Sector  Undertaking)  was 
 established  on  1  November  1975  under 
 the  ownership  of  the  Ministry  of  Coal. 
 Headquarters: Kolkata.

 Q.299.  Given  below  are  the  Assertion 
 and  Reason,  choose  the  correct  options 
 regarding both the statements. 
 Assertion  (A):  Seasonal  agricultural 
 practices,  such  as  stubble  burning,  have 
 no  significant  impact  on  AQI  levels  in 
 India. 
 Reason  (R):  Stubble  burning  is  a 
 controlled  practice  that  doesn't  release 
 substantial  pollutants  into  the 
 atmosphere. 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) A is true, but R is false. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) Both A and R are false. 
 (d) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 Sol.299.(c)  Both  A  and  R  are  false. 
 Seasonal  stubble  burning  in  North  India 
 is  a  major  cause  of  severe  air  pollution, 
 raising  AQI  to  hazardous  levels.  This 
 uncontrolled  practice  releases  large 
 amounts  of  PM2.5,  PM10,  carbon 
 monoxide,  and  toxic  gases  into  the 
 atmosphere. 
 Q.300.  High  honey  yield  and  gentle 
 behaviour  in  bees  for  commercial 
 beekeeping  can  be  achieved  through  one 
 of the following methods: 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Using wild bee species with 
 aggressive defence traits 
 (b) Frequently relocating hives to prevent 
 bees from settling 
 (c) Avoiding bee farms and relying solely 
 on natural honey collection 
 (d) Introducing Italian bees that collect 
 more honey and remain in hives 
 longer 
 Sol.300.(d)  Italian  bees  (Apis  mellifera 
 ligustica)  are  preferred  in  commercial 
 apiculture  because  they  are  gentle  in 
 nature,  highly  productive,  and  stay  longer 
 in  hives.  They  gather  more  nectar 
 compared  to  indigenous  species,  making 
 them  suitable  for  large-scale  honey 
 production.  Unlike  wild  or  aggressive 
 species,  they  are  easier  to  manage, 
 ensuring  higher  yield  and  safer 
 beekeeping practices. 
 Q.301.  Match the columns: 
 Column A 
 (Cropping Season) 
 Column B 
 (Major Crops - 
 Shuffled) 
 A. Kharif 
 (June-September) 
 1. Vegetables, Fruits 
 , Fodder 
 B. Rabi 
 (October-March) 
 2. Rice, Cotton, Bajra, 
 Maize, Jowar, Tur 
 C. Zaid 
 (April-June) 
 3. Wheat, Gram, 
 Rapeseeds and 
 Mustard, Barley 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) A-3, B-2, C-1               (b) A-2, B-1, C-3 
 (c) A-1, B-2, C-3               (d) A-2, B-3, C-1 
 Sol.301.(d)  A-2,  B-3,  C-1.  Kharif  Crops 
 (Monsoon  season:  June–Sept).  Sown 
 with  onset  of  monsoon,  harvested  in 
 autumn.  Examples:  Rice,  Cotton,  Bajra, 
 Maize,  Jowar,  Tur  (Pigeon  pea).  Rabi 
 Crops  (Winter  season:  Oct–March). 
 Sown  in  winter,  harvested  in  spring. 
 Examples:  Wheat,  Gram,  Rapeseed, 
 Mustard,  Barley.  Zaid  Crops  (Summer 
 season:  April–June).  Short-season  crops 
 grown  between  Rabi  and  Kharif. 
 Examples:  Vegetables,  Fruits  (melon, 
 cucumber), Fodder.
