 Q.303.  The  term  'Aus',  'Aman'  and  'Boro' 
 are associated with which crop in India? 
 (a) Maize  (b) Wheat  (c) Jute  (d) Paddy 
 Sol.303.(d)  Paddy  is  basically  a  tropical 
 crop.  'Aus',  'Aman'  and  'Boro'  are  grown  in 
 states  like  Assam,  West  Bengal,  and 
 Odisha  throughout  the  year.  Aus:  Sown  in 
 the  summer,  along  with  pre-monsoonal 
 rains,  and  harvested  in  the  autumn. 
 Aman:  Sown  in  the  monsoon  and 
 harvested  in  the  winter.  Boro:  Sown  in  the 
 winter and harvested in the summer. 
 Q.304.  Which type of tea in large amount 
 is  exported  from  India  to  importing 
 countries? 
 (a) Herbal tea  (b) Regular tea 
 (c) Green tea  (d) Black tea 
 Sol.304.(d)  Black  tea.  India  is  the  world's 
 second  largest  tea  producer  after  China. 
 The  types  of  tea  exported  through  India 
 are:  Black  tea,  Regular  tea,  Green  tea, 
 Herbal  tea,  Masala  tea,  and  Lemon  tea. 
 The  majority  of  the  tea  exported  out  of 
 India  is  black  tea  which  makes  up  about 
 96% of the total exports. 
 Q.305.  The  people  of  Himachal  Pradesh 
 make  channels  to  bring  water  from  a 
 glacier  or  rainwater  to  villages  for  the 
 function  of  mainly  irrigation.  These 
 channels are locally known as: 
 (a) Khadins  (b) Guls or Kuls 
 (c) Johads  (d) Baori 
 Sol.305.(b)  Guls  or  Kuls.  In  hill  and 
 mountainous  regions,  people  built 
 diversion  channels  like  the  ‘guls’  or  ‘kuls’ 
 of  the  Western  Himalayas  for  agriculture. 
 Water  harvesting  system:  ‘Rooftop  rain 
 water  harvesting’  was  commonly 
 practised  to  store  drinking  water, 
 particularly  in  Rajasthan.  In  arid  and 
 semi-arid  regions,  agricultural  fields  were 
 converted  into  rain  fed  storage  structures 
 that  allowed  the  water  to  stand  and 
 moisten  the  soil  like  the  ‘khadins’  in 
 Jaisalmer  and  ‘Johads’  in  other  parts  of 
 Rajasthan.  Roof  top  rain  water  harvesting 
 is  the  most  common  practice  in  Shillong, 
 Meghalaya. 
 Q.306.  Which  of  the  following  states  is 
 the biggest producer of Pulses? 
 (a) Madhya Pradesh  (b) Haryana 
 (c) Punjab  (d) Bihar 
 Sol.306.(a)  Madhya  Pradesh.  Based  on 
 the  production  estimates  for  the  year 
 2022-23,  Madhya  Pradesh,  Maharashtra 
 and  Rajasthan  are  the  top  three  pulses 
 producing  states  in  the  country.  India  is 
 the  largest  producer  of  pulses  in  the 
 world. 
 Q.307.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  correctly  defines  the  green 
 revolution? 
 (a) It is a new strategy in agriculture to 
 produce food grains, especially 
 wheat and rice. 
 (b) It is a new strategy to increase the 
 share of the forest. 
 (c) It is a new strategy to use green 
 colour for all purposes. 
 (d) It is a new strategy to use only herbal 
 products. 
 Sol.307.(a)  The  Green  Revolution, 
 starting  in  the  1960s,  aimed  to  achieve 
 food  security  and  reduce  food  imports, 
 particularly  in  India.  It  involved  the 
 introduction  of  high-yielding  variety 
 seeds,  mechanized  farming,  improved 
 irrigation,  and  the  use  of  pesticides  and 
 fertilizers. 
 Q.308.  According  to  the  Agricultural  and 
 Processed  food  products  Export 
 Development  Authority  (APEDA) 
 2020-2021,  which  state  of  India  has  the 
 first rank in grapes production? 
 (a) Maharashtra  (b) Karnataka 
 (c) Bihar  (d) Uttar Pradesh 
 Sol.308.(a)  Maharashtra.  APEDA  was 
 established  in  1986,  operates  under  the 
 Ministry  of  Commerce  and  Trade  to 
 promote  the  export  of  agricultural  and 
 processed food products. 
 Q.309.  The  green  revolution  technology 
 resulted  in  an  increase  in  production  of 
 cereal  production  from  72.4  million  tons 
 in  1965-66  to  ________  million  tons  in 
 1978-79. 
 (a) 150.8  (b) 165.9  (c) 131.9  (d) 141.2 
 Sol.309.(c)  131.9.  The  cereal  production 
 was  increased  due  to  three  factors:  (i) 
 Increase  in  net  area  under  cultivation;  (ii) 
 Growing  two  or  more  crops  in  a  year  on 
 the  same  piece  of  land;  and  (iii)  Use  of 
 HYV seeds. 
 Q.310.  Consider the following 
 statements  regarding  the  Second  Green 
 Revolution Cell: 
 1. 
 The  headquarters  of  the  Second 
 Green  Revolution  (SGR)  Cell  is 
 located  at  ICAR–Research  Complex 
 for Eastern Region (RCER), Patna. 
 2. 
 The  benefits  of  the  first  Green 
 Revolution  were  mainly  confined  to 
 the eastern region of India. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                     (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2          (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.310.(a)  1  only.  The  Second  Green 
 Revolution  (SGR)  Cell  was  created  by  the 
 Ministry  of  Agriculture  and  Farmers' 
 Welfare,  Government  of  India  to  improve 
 agricultural  productivity  and  livelihood  in 
 the  Eastern  region  of  India  because  the 
 benefits  of  first  Green  Revolution  were 
 mainly  confined  to  north-western  states 
 of  Haryana,  Punjab  and  western  Uttar 
 Pradesh. 
 Q.311.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  about  the  aims  of  National 
 Mission  for  Sustainable  Agriculture 
 (NMSA) of India is/are correct? 
 1.  To make agriculture more productive. 
 2. 
 To  make  agriculture  more 
 sustainable. 
 3.  To promote organic farming. 
 (a) Only 1 is correct. 
 (b) Only 2 and 3 are correct. 
 (c) 1, 2 and 3 are correct. 
 (d) Only 1 and 2 are correct. 
 Sol.311.(c)  1,  2  and  3  are  correct. 
 National  Mission  for  Sustainable 
 Agriculture  (NMSA)  has  been  made 
 operational  from  the  year  2014-15  which 
 aims  at  making  agriculture  more 
 productive,  sustainable,  remunerative 
 and  climate  resilient  by  promoting 
 location  specific  integrated  /composite 
 farming  systems;  soil  and  moisture 
 conservation  measures;  comprehensive 
 soil  health  management;  efficient  water 
 management  practices  and 
 mainstreaming rainfed technologies. 
 Q.312.  What  was  a  significant  negative 
 environmental  impact  of  the  Green 
 Revolution? 
 (a) Deforestation 
 (b) Soil degradation 
 (c) Increase in air pollution 
 (d) Ozone layer depletion 
 Sol.312.(b)  Soil  degradation.  The  Green 
 Revolution  caused  soil  fertility  loss 
 through  the  use  of  chemical  fertilizers. 
 Chemical  fertilizers  also  kill  bacteria  and 
 other  microorganisms  in  the  soil.  Green 
 Revolution  also  led  to  groundwater 
 depletion  due  to  extensive  tubewell 
 irrigation, impacting long-term 
 environmental resources. 
 Q.313.  How  was  the  growth  in  all  food 
 grains after the Green Revolution? 
 (a) Unitary  (b) Proportionate 
 (c) Unpredictable  (d) Disproportionate 
 Sol.313.(d)  Disproportionate.  Larger 
 farmers  with  more  resources  were  able  to 
 afford  the  necessary  inputs,  like 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 high-yield  variety  (HYV)  seeds,  fertilizers, 
 and  irrigation,  and  thus  reaped  greater 
 benefits.  Smaller  farmers  often  lacked 
 access  to  these  resources  and  faced 
 higher  risks,  as  the  HYV  crops  were  more 
 vulnerable  to  pests,  potentially  causing 
 severe  losses.  About  65  percent  of  the 
 country’s  population  continued  to  be 
 employed  in  agriculture  even  as  late  as 
 1990. 
 Q.314  .  What  was  the  main  objective  of 
 the Second Green Revolution in India? 
 (a) Ensuring food security in the western 
 states 
 (b) Promoting conventional agricultural 
 practices 
 (c) Focusing on holistic development in 
 agriculture 
 (d) Increasing the use of chemical 
 fertilizers and pesticides 
 Sol.314.(c)  In  the  second  phase  of  the 
 green  revolution  (mid-1970s  to 
 mid-1980s),  the  HYV  technology  spread 
 to  a  larger  number  of  states  and 
 benefited  more  variety  of  crops.  The 
 spread  of  green  revolution  technology 
 enabled  India  to  achieve  self-sufficiency 
 in food grains. 
 Q.315.  Which  of  the  following 
 movements  created  regional  disparities 
 between  large  and  small  farmers  in 
 India? 
 (a) Green revolution  (b) Yellow revolution 
 (c) Pink revolution     (d) Blue revolution 
 Sol.315.(a)  Green  revolution  in  India 
 started  with  the  aim  of  increasing  India's 
 agricultural  production  to  make  India 
 self-sufficient in food grains. 
 Q.316.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements is/are correct? 
 I. 
 The  first  green  revolution  led  to 
 over-exploitation of natural resources. 
 II. 
 The  second  Green  Revolution  was 
 more focused on Southern India. 
 III. 
 The  second  green  revolution  ensured 
 in  achieving  sustainable  livelihood 
 security. 
 (a) I and III  (b) Only II 
 (c) II and III  (d) I and II 
 Sol.316.(a)  I  and  III.  The  Second  Green 
 Revolution  focuses  on  sustainable 
 agriculture  in  eastern  India,  rainfed  areas, 
 and  small  farmers,  promoting  climate- 
 resilient practices and livelihood security. 
 Q.317.  What  action  was  taken  by  the 
 government  to  celebrate  the 
 achievement  of  the  Green  Revolution  in 
 agriculture? 
 (a) Released a regular salary for the 
 farmers 
 (b) Distributed free food to all the people 
 (c) Released a special stamp, ‘Wheat 
 Revolution’ 
 (d) Distributed green vegetables to the 
 people 
 Sol.317.(c)  Green  Revolution  :  The  term 
 was  first  used  by  William  Gaud,  with 
 Norman  Borlaug  recognized  as  its  global 
 father  and  M.S.  Swaminathan  was  the 
 father  of  the  Green  Revolution  in  India. 
 India  adopted  a  new  agricultural  strategy, 
 boosting  wheat  and  rice  production.  In 
 July  1968,  Prime  Minister  Indira  Gandhi 
 commemorated  its  success  by  releasing  a 
 special stamp titled "Wheat Revolution." 
 Q.318.  What  is  the  economic  justification 
 for subsidies in the agriculture sector? 
 (a) It is mostly used to provide benefits 
 for rich farmers. 
 (b) It is a part of government expenditure 
 in the service sector. 
 (c) It is an incentive to provide the 
 benefits of advanced technology and 
 decrease the cost of production. 
 (d) It is used to provide benefits for 
 capital goods producers in 
 agriculture. 
 Sol.318.(c)  Agricultural  subsidies 
 support  food  security  and  development 
 by  helping  farmers  boost  production, 
 improve  food  availability,  and  reduce 
 poverty.  Input  subsidies  lower  costs, 
 incentivize  adoption,  increase 
 productivity,  and  stimulate  economic 
 growth. 
 Q.319.  In  West  Bengal,  how  many  rice 
 crops are grown in a year? 
 (a) Three    (b) Two    (c) Four    (d) One 
 Sol.319.(a)  Three.  In  states  like  Assam, 
 West  Bengal,  and  Odisha,  three  paddy 
 crops  are  grown  annually:  Aus,  Aman, 
 and Boro. 
 Q.320.  In  the  given  main  characteristics 
 of  farming  which  is  NOT  a  characteristic 
 of the Indian commercial farming? 
 (a) Slash and burn 
 (b) Insecticides and pesticides 
 (c) Chemical fertilisers 
 (d) High yielding variety seeds 
 Sol.320.(a)  Slash  and  burn  farming  is  a 
 form  of  shifting  agriculture  where  natural 
 vegetation  is  cut  down  and  burned  to 
 clear  the  land  for  cultivation.  In 
 commercial  farming,  higher  doses  of 
 modern  inputs  are  used  to  obtain  higher 
 productivity.  The  degree  of 
 commercialization  in  agriculture  varies 
 from  one  region  to  another.  For  example, 
 rice  is  a  commercial  crop  in  Haryana  and 
 Punjab,  but  in  Odisha,  it  is  a  subsistence 
 crop. 
 Q.321.  Which  of  the  following  types  of 
 crops  are  sown  after  the  end  of  the 
 monsoon season? 
 (a) Kharif crops  (b) Zaid crops 
 (c) Rabi crops  (d) Vital crops 
 Sol.321.(c)  Rabi  crops.  It  is  an 
 agricultural  crop  that  is  sown  in  the 
 winter and harvested in the spring. 
 Q.322.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT  one 
 of  the  consequences  of  the  Green 
 Revolution? 
 (a) Employment of agricultural workers 
 increased 
 (b) Rising prices and a shift in the mode 
 of payment of agricultural workers 
 from payment in cash to payment in 
 kind (grain) 
 (c) Wages of agricultural workers 
 increased 
 (d) The rich grew richer and many of the 
 poor stagnated or grew poorer 
 Sol.322.(b)  Impact  of  Green  Revolution  : 
 Positive  -  Increase  in  production  and 
 productivity  of  food  grains,  reduction  in 
 hunger,  and  technological  advancements 
 in  agriculture.  Negative  -  Decline  in  Soil 
 fertility,  High  production  costs,  Regional 
 disparities.

 Q.325.  Which  of  the  following  types  of 
 crops  are  sown  at  the  beginning  of  the 
 monsoon season? 
 (a) Rabi crops  (b) Kharif crops 
 (c) Vital crops  (d) Zaid crops 
 Sol.325.(b)  Kharif  crops.  These  crops 
 are  typically  planted  during  June  to 
 September  and  harvested  in  the 
 post-monsoon  season  (October  to 
 November).  Examples  of  Kharif  crops 
 include  rice,  maize,  cotton,  and  sorghum. 
 Rabi  crops  are  sown  after  the  monsoon 
 season,  usually  in  the  winter  months 
 (October  to  December),  and  harvested  in 
 the  spring  (March  to  May).  Zaid  crops  are 
 grown  in  the  intervening  period  between 
 Kharif  and  Rabi  seasons,  typically  in  the 
 summer months (March to June). 
 Q.326.  Which  of  the  following 
 statement/s  about  the  Green  Revolution 
 is/are CORRECT? 
 I. 
 India  became  self-sufficient  with  the 
 introduction of the Green Revolution. 
 II. 
 The  Green  Revolution  led  to  loss  of 
 nutrients  in  the  soil  from  farming  the 
 same crop over and over again. 
 III.  The Green Revolution led to depletion 
 of fresh ground water. 
 (a) Only I and III  (b) Only I and II 
 (c) I, II and III  (d) Only II 
 Sol.326.(c)  I,  II  and  III.  The  Green 
 Revolution  resulted  in  a  great  increase  in 
 production  of  food  grains  (especially 
 wheat  and  rice)  due  to  the  introduction 
 into  developing  countries  of  new, 
 high-yielding  variety  (HYV)  seeds.  The 
 green  revolution  enabled  the  government 
 to  procure  sufficient  amounts  of  food 
 grains  to  build  a  stock  which  could  be 
 used in times of food shortage. 
 Q.327.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  are  true  about  the  positive 
 impact  of  the  Green  Revolution  (GR)  on 
 yield levels in India? 
 I. 
 During  the  post  GR  period,  the  yield  of 
 rice  grew  at  a  much  faster  rate  than 
 that of wheat. 
 II. 
 GR  technology  had  penetrated  more 
 in wheat crop than in the rice crop. 
 III. 
 During  post  GR  period,  the  area  under 
 rice  achieved  a  relatively  slow  growth 
 when  compared  to  the  area  under 
 wheat. 
 (a) Only II and III  (b) Only I and III 
 (c) Only I and II  (d) I, II and III 
 Sol.327.(a)  Only  ii  and  iii.  The  Green 
 Revolution  technology  had  a  greater 
 impact  on  wheat  crops  than  on  rice 
 crops.  Important  Crops  in  the  Revolution: 
 Main  crops  were  -  Wheat,  Rice,  Jowar, 
 Bajra  and  Maize.  Non-food  grains  were 
 excluded  from  the  ambit  of  the  new 
 strategy.  Wheat  remained  the  mainstay 
 of  the  Green  Revolution  for  years. 
 Mankombu  Sambasivan  Swaminathan  is 
 known  as  the  "Father  of  India's  Green 
 Revolution"  for  his  work  to  increase  food 
 production and alleviate world hunger. 
 Q.328.  Which  of  these  is  NOT  true  of  the 
 second phase of the green revolution? 
 (a) It was adopted by farmers living in dry 
 and semi-arid regions of India. 
 (b) It was launched in areas with a 
 significant shift from dry to wet 
 (irrigated) cultivation. 
 (c) It covered the period of the 1960s and 
 1970s. 
 (d) It covered the period of the 1980s. 
 Sol.328.(c)  The  use  of  HYV  seeds  was 
 restricted  to  the  more  affluent  states 
 such  as  Punjab,  Andhra  Pradesh  and 
 Tamil  Nadu  in  the  1st  Phase(Mid  1960s 
 to  mid-1970s).  In  the  second  phase  of 
 the  green  revolution  (mid-1970s  to 
 mid-1980s),  the  HYV  technology  spread 
 to  a  larger  number  of  states  and 
 benefited more variety of crops. 
 Q.329.  Who among the following is NOT 
 associated with the Green Revolution? 
 (a) Norman Borlaug 
 (b) P Chidambaram 
 (c) Chidambaram Subramaniam 
 (d) MS Swaminathan 
 Sol.329.(b)  P  Chidambaram.  Green 
 Revolution:  Initiated  by  -  Norman  Borlaug 
 (1960s).  He  is  known  as  the  ‘Father  of 
 Green  Revolution’  in  world.  He  received 
 the  Nobel  prize  (1970)  for  developing 
 High  yielding  varieties  (HYVs)  of  wheat. 
 M.S.  Swaminathan  (Father  of  Green 
 Revolution  in  India).  The  Green  revolution 
 came  under  the  third  fiv e  year  plan 
 (1961-66).  Chidambaram  Subramaniam 
 was  an  Indian  politician  and  agricultural 
 scientist  who  played  a  crucial  role  in 
 India's Green Revolution. Award - Bharat 
 Ratna (1998). 
 Q.330.  Identify  the  crop  which  is  grown 
 in  Kharif  cropping  season  in  Northern 
 States of India. 
 (a) Mustard (b) Rice (c) Gram (d) Barley 
 Sol.330.(b) Rice.  Kharif is sown during 
 the  rainy  season,  typically  from  June  to 
 September  in  India.  Examples  include 
 paddy,  maize,  soybean,  groundnut,  and 
 cotton.  Rabi  grows  in  the  winter  season, 
 from  October  to  March.  Examples 
 include  wheat,  gram,  peas,  mustard,  and 
 linseed.  In  between  the  rabi  and  the 
 kharif  seasons,  there  is  a  short  season 
 during  the  summer  months  known  as  the 
 Zaid  season.  Examples  are  watermelon, 
 muskmelon, cucumber. 
 Q.331.  In  which  of  the  following  ways 
 were  farmers  encouraged  to  begin  to  use 
 high yielding variety seeds ? 
 (a) Farmers’ children were given free 
 education in government colleges 
 and universities. 
 (b) Agricultural labourers were paid 
 wages directly by the government. 
 (c) Farmers were given monetary support 
 in cash to cultivate food grains. 
 (d) The government provided seeds and 
 other inputs at prices lower than 
 those prevailing in the market. 
 Sol.331.(d)  Farmers  were  encouraged  to 
 use  high-yielding  variety  (HYV)  seeds 
 during  the  Green  Revolution  through 
 various  forms  of  support  from  the 
 government.  The  government  made  HYV 
 seeds,  fertilizers,  pesticides,  and 
 irrigation  systems  available  at  subsidized 
 prices,  meaning  they  were  cheaper  than 
 market  rates.  This  helped  reduce  the 
 financial  burden  on  farmers  and 
 incentivized  them  to  adopt  new 
 agricultural  technologies.  HYV  seeds  are 
 resistant to insects and diseases and are 
 known for their high yields. 
 Q.332.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT  an 
 agro-based industry? 
 (a) Jute  (b) Fertiliser 
 (c) Sugar  (d) Edible oil 
 Sol.332.(b)  Fertiliser.  Agro-based 
 industries  depend  on  agricultural  raw 
 materials,  producing  mainly  consumer 
 goods.  Major  industries  include  cotton, 
 jute,  textiles,  and  edible  oil.  India's  first 
 sugar  mill  was  established  in  Pratappur, 
 Uttar  Pradesh,  in  1903.  The  Sugar  Cane 
 Act  of  1934  allowed  state  governments 
 to set the minimum price for sugarcane. 
 Q.333.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT 
 true with regard to the Green Revolution? 
 (a) Cultivation became more intensive in 
 nature. 
 (b) In its second phase, there was a shift 
 to from dry to wet (irrigated) 
 cultivation in dry and semi-arid 
 regions of India. 
 (c) Cultivation became more extensive in 
 nature. 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (d) There was change in the cropping 
 pattern and types of crops grown in 
 its second phase. 
 Sol.333.(c)  The  Green  Revolution  did  not 
 focus  on  expanding  the  area  under 
 cultivation  but  rather  on  making  the 
 existing  land  more  productive  (intensive 
 cultivation),  using  modern  inputs  and 
 technology. 
 Q.334.  Which  crops  are  benefitted  from 
 the  temperate  cyclones  arising  from  the 
 Mediterranean  Sea  that  cause  rainfall  in 
 Punjab? 
 (a) Cash crops  (b) Kharif crops 
 (c) Rabi crops  (d) Zaid crops 
 Sol.334.(c)  Rabi  crops.  Temperate 
 cyclones  cause  rainfall  in  Punjab, 
 Haryana,  Delhi  and  western  Uttar 
 Pradesh.  The  precipitation  is  in  the  form 
 of  snowfall  in  the  lower  Himalayas.  It  is 
 this  snow  that  sustains  the  flow  of  water 
 in  the  Himalayan  rivers  during  the 
 summer  months.  The  precipitation  goes 
 on  decreasing  from  west  to  east  in  the 
 plains  and  from  north  to  south  in  the 
 mountains. 
 Q.335.  In  July  1968,  Indira  Gandhi,  the 
 then  Prime  Minister  of  India,  officially 
 recorded  impressive  strides  of  the  Green 
 Revolution  in  agriculture  by  releasing  a 
 special stamp entitled _________ . 
 (a) Wheat Revolution  (b) Fasal Kranti 
 (c) Harit Kranti             (d) Green Revolution 
 Sol.335.(a)  Wheat  Revolution  (Part  of 
 Green  Revolution)  was  initiated  under  the 
 Third  Five  Year  Plan  (1961-66)  by 
 introducing  high-yielding  varieties  of 
 seeds of rice and wheat. 
 Q.336.  Which  of  the  following  reasons 
 can  be  attributed  to  the  excessive 
 pressure on agricultural land in India? 
 A.  Right of inheritance 
 B.  Size of unprofitable land holding 
 C.  Division of land 
 D.  Multiple cropping 
 (a) Only A and B  (b) B, C and D 
 (c) A, C and D  (d) A, B and C 
 Sol.336.(d)  A,  B  and  C.  Reasons  that 
 cause  pressure  on  Agriculture:  The  right 
 of  inheritance,  Division  of  land,  Rising 
 population,  Use  of  Excessive  Chemical 
 Fertilizer,  Salinization  of  soil, 
 Desertification,  Urbanisation, 
 Waterlogging,  Climate  Change,  Droughts, 
 Pollution etc. 
 Q.337.  Which  of  the  following  countries 
 is  related  with  high  yielding  variety  seeds 
 that  are  used  as  part  of  Green  Revolution 
 policies in India? 
 (a) Russia  (b) Mexico 
 (c) China  (d) The US 
 Sol.337.(b)  Mexico.  Green  Revolution  -  A 
 period  that  began  in  the  1960s  during 
 which  agriculture  in  India  was  converted 
 into  a  modern  industrial  system  by  the 
 adoption  of  technology  (The  use  of  high 
 yielding  variety  (HYV)  seeds,  mechanised 
 farm  tools,  irrigation  facilities,  pesticides, 
 and fertilizers). 
 Q.338.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  rice  varieties  in 
 India: 
 1. 
 IR8,  also  called  Miracle  Rice,  was  first 
 harvested  in  1967  and  helped  India 
 overcome a massive famine. 
 2. 
 Golden  Rice,  developed  in  1998,  is 
 genetically  modified  to  contain 
 pro-vitamin A. 
 3. 
 PETA  is  the  high-yielding  semi-dwarf 
 rice  variety  developed  by  the 
 International  Rice  Research  Institute 
 (IRRI) in 1967. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only                       (b) 2 only 
 (c) 3 only                       (d) 2 and 3 only 
 Sol.338.(d)  2  and  3  only.  IR8  is  a 
 high-yielding  semi-dwarf  rice  variety 
 developed  by  the  International  Rice 
 Research  Institute  (IRRI).  Golden  rice:  A 
 transgenic  rice  having  a  gene  for 
 carotene.  It  was  first  introduced  in  1999 
 by  Ingo  Potrykus  and  Peter  Beyer.  It 
 contains  pro-vitamin  A  which  upon 
 ingestion  is  converted  to  vitamin  A  in  the 
 human body. 
 Q.339.  For  Kharif  crops,  the  temperature 
 required  for  farming  is  above  25  degrees 
 Celsius  with  high  humidity  and  annual 
 rainfall above ________ cm. 
 (a) 16        (b) 50        (c) 100        (d) 35 
 Sol.339.(c)  100.  Kharif  crops  are  sown 
 during  the  monsoon  season,  which  lasts 
 from June to September in India. 
 Q.340.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding the Green 
 Revolution in India: 
 1. 
 Punjab  was  the  most  transformed 
 state due to the Green Revolution. 
 2. 
 The  Green  Revolution  had  maximum 
 impact  in  Punjab,  Haryana,  and 
 western Uttar Pradesh. 
 3. 
 The  eastern  region,  including  Assam, 
 Bihar,  West  Bengal,  and  Odisha,  was 
 the  most  benefitted  from  the  Green 
 Revolution. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 and 2 only               (b) 2 and 3 only 
 (c) 1 and 3 only               (d) 1, 2 and 3 
 Sol.340.(a)  1  and  2  only.  Statement  3  is 
 incorrect  because  the  eastern  region  had 
 minimal  impact,  not  maximum,  from  the 
 Green Revolution. 
 Q.341.  The  agricultural  land  below  the 
 main canal water level forms the ______. 
 (a) command area          (b) lift system 
 (c) flow system  (d) warebandi 
 Sol.341.(a)  command  area.  Lift  system  - 
 Water  is  lifted  from  lower  level  to  higher 
 level  with  the  help  of  pumps  and  other 
 equipment.  Flow  system  -  The  type  of 
 irrigation  in  which  water  is  available  at  a 
 higher  level  to  enable  supply  to  the  land 
 by  gravity  flow .  Warabandi  -  It  is  a  system 
 of  rotation  of  supply  of  water  according 
 to  a  predetermined  schedule  as  per  area 
 and  crop  needs  specifying  the  day,  time 
 and  duration  of  supply  to  each  holding  to 
 ensure  equitable  water  distribution 
 among farmers of an outlet command. 
 Q.342.  _______  land  is  suitable  for  regular 
 cropping. 
 (a) Arable  (b) Barren 
 (c) Marginal  (d) Urban 
 Sol.342.(a)  Arable  land  is  the  land  under 
 temporary  crops,  temporary  meadows 
 for  mowing  or  for  pasture,  land  under 
 market  or  kitchen  gardens,  and 
 temporary  fallow.  It  is  capable  of  being 
 ploughed  and  used  to  grow  crops.  Barren 
 land  -  Land  that  normally  cannot  be 
 brought  under  cultivation  with  the 
 available  technology.  Marginal  land  - 
 Land  that  has  little  or  no  agricultural 
 Value. 
 Q.343.  Which  group  of  crops  belong  to 
 fibr e  crops  and  are  produced  on  a  large 
 scale in India ? 
 (a) Gram and tur  (b) Jute and hemp 
 (c) Tea and coffee    (d) Wheat and rice 
 Sol.343.(b)  Jute  and  Hemp.  Jute  is 
 known  as  Golden  Fibre.  It  grows  well  on 
 well-drained  fertile  soils  in  the 
 floodplains.  MajorJute  producing  states 
 (West  Bengal,  Bihar,  Assam,  Odisha  and 
 Meghalaya). 
 Q.344.  Which  of  the  following  crops  is 
 the  only  genetically  modified  (GM)  crop 
 approved  for  commercial  cultivation  in 
 India? 
 (a) Bt Brinjal  (b) Mustard 
 (c) Bt Cotton  (d) Tomato 
 Sol.344.(c)  Bt  Cotton.  GM  crops  :  These 
 are  derived  from  plants  whose  genes  are 
 artificially  modified,  usually  by  inserting 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 genetic  material  from  another  organism. 
 India  approved  the  release  of  Bt  cotton 
 (Cry  1  AC)  for  commercial  cultivation  in 
 2002  -  Andhra  Pradesh,  Gujarat, 
 Maharashtra,  Madhya  Pradesh, 
 Karnataka,  and  Tamil  Nadu.  The  best 
 known variety of GM rice is golden rice. 
 Q.345.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 Extensive  cultivation  refers  to  farming 
 on  large  farms  with  moderately  low 
 inputs of labour and capital. 
 2. 
 Hybrid  cultivation  is  the  practice  of 
 cultivating  crops  only  on  small  plots 
 of land with no use of hybrid seeds. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only                    (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2        (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.345.(b)  2  only.  Hybrid  cultivation  -  It 
 involves  the  use  of  hybrid  seed  varieties 
 (created  by  crossbreeding  two  or  more 
 genetically  distinct  plants).  Example  - 
 Wheat  -  Sonalika  and  Kalyan  Sona,  Rice  - 
 Jaya and Ratna. 
 Q.346.  Which  of  the  following  crops  is 
 classified as fine grain? 
 (a) Bajra  (b) Ragi  (c) Maize  (d) Wheat 
 Sol.346.(d)  Wheat.  India  produces  a 
 variety  of  cereals,  which  are  classified 
 into  fine  grains  (rice,  wheat)  and  coarse 
 grains (jowar, bajra, maize, ragi, etc.). 
 Q.347.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 most  suitable  application  of  rainwater 
 harvesting? 
 (a) Used in cooking 
 (b) Irrigation 
 (c) Water for pets 
 (d) Use for industrial purposes 
 Sol.347.(b)  Irrigation.  Rainwater 
 harvesting  is  the  process  of  collecting, 
 storing,  and  purifying  rainwater  from 
 rooftops,  parks,  roads,  and  open 
 grounds.  It  is  used  for  drinking,  domestic 
 needs,  livestock,  small  irrigation,  and 
 replenishing groundwater levels. 
 Q.348.  What  is  meant  by  subsistence 
 agriculture ? 
 (a) Agricultural output is sufficient for 
 the country. 
 (b) Agriculturists produce primarily for 
 themselves. 
 (c) Agriculture generates sufficient 
 income. 
 (d) Agriculturists produce primarily for 
 the market. 
 Sol.348.(b)  Types  of  Subsistence 
 Farming  :  Primitive  subsistence  farming 
 or  shifting  cultivation  (oldest  form  of 
 agriculture)  -  It  happens  on  a 
 self-sufficient  basis,  and  farmers  raise 
 food  according  to  their  family’s  needs. 
 The  trees  are  cut  down  and  burnt  and  the 
 cleared  land  is  cultivated.  Other  names: 
 Shifting  cultivation  or  slash  and  burn 
 cultivation.  Intensive  subsistence  farming 
 -  The  farmer  cultivates  a  small  plot  of  land 
 using  simple  tools  and  more.  More  than 
 one crop is cultivated during a year. 
 Q.349.  Which  of  the  following  states  of 
 India  still  uses  the  ancient  bamboo  drip 
 irrigation method? 
 (a) Telangana  (b) Meghalaya 
 (c) Maharashtra  (d) Chhattisgarh 
 Sol.349.(b)  Meghalaya.  Bamboo  drip 
 irrigation  system  is  tapping  stream  and 
 spring  water  by  using  bamboo  pipes. 
 Types  of  Irrigation  systems:  Well  and 
 Tube  well,  Canal,  Tank,  Drip,  Sprinkler, 
 Furrow,  Surge,  Ditch.  The  word 
 Meghalaya  means  “abode  of  clouds”. 
 ‘Krem  Liat  Prah’  Longest  cave  of  India  is 
 in Meghalaya.  Hills  : Garo, Khasi, Jaintia. 
 Q.350.  Which  of  the  following 
 classification  bases  of  industry  applies 
 to  the  products  -  cotton,  jute,  wool,  silk 
 textile,  rubber,  tea,  sugar,  coffee,  edible 
 oil ? 
 (a) Key industries 
 (b) Consumer industries 
 (c) Agro based industries 
 (d) Mineral based industries 
 Sol.350.(c)  Agro-based  industries  use 
 plant  and  animal  products  as  raw 
 materials.  Mineral-based  industries,  like 
 iron  and  steel,  copper  smelting,  and 
 cement,  use  mineral  ores.  Consumer 
 industries  produce  goods  for  direct 
 consumer  use,  such  as  sugar, 
 toothpaste,  paper,  sewing  machines,  and 
 fans. 
 Q.351.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding Census of India 2011: 
 1.  4.6% of the total workforce was

 sector activities. 
 2. 
 The  total  number  of  agricultural 
 workers  was  263.1  million  as  per 
 Census 2011 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only                       (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2           (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.351.(a)  1  only.  54.6%  of  the  total 
 workforce  is  engaged  in  Agriculture  and 
 allied  sector  activities.  Census  2011  was 
 conducted  by  the  Registrar  General  of 
 India.  The  total  number  of  agricultural 
 workers  in  the  country  :  263.1  million 
 (118.8  million  cultivators  and  144.3 
 million  agricultural  labourers)  in  2011. 
 The  workforce  participation  rate  for 
 females  was  25.51%  against  53.26%  for 
 males. 
 Q.352.  Cultivation  of  rice  needs  high 
 rainfall ________. 
 (a) Low temperature and low humidity 
 (b) High temperature and low humidity 
 (c) High temperature and high humidity 
 (d) Low temperature and high humidity 
 Sol.352.(c)  High  temperature  and  high 
 humidity.  Fertile  riverine  alluvial  soil  is 
 best  for  rice  cultivation.  It  is  a  type  of 
 Kharif  crop.  West  Bengal  is  the  largest 
 rice-producing  state  in  India.  China  is  the 
 world's  largest  producer  of  rice  followed 
 by  India.  Methane  is  the  gas  produced  by 
 the rice field.

 Q.355.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  Agricultural 
 Census in India: 
 1. 
 The  Agricultural  Census  in  India  is 
 conducted every six years. 
 2. 
 The  Agriculture  Census  has  been 
 conducted  by  the  Ministry  of 
 Agriculture since 1980 -81 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) 1 only                       (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2           (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.355.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  Agricultural 
 Census  is  conducted  in  India  at  an 
 interval  of  5  years.  It  is  conducted 
 following  the  broad  guidelines  of  the 
 decennial  World  Census  of  Agriculture 
 (WCA)  developed  by  the  Food  and 
 Agriculture  Organization  (FAO)  of  the 
 United  Nations.  The  Agriculture  Census 
 has  been  conducted  by  the  Ministry  of 
 Agriculture  since  1970-71.  “The  Eleventh 
 Agricultural  Census”  (2021-22) 
 Launched  by  The  Ministry  of  Agriculture 
 & Farmers' Welfare. 
 Q.356.  Which  of  the  following  is  termed 
 as "sericulture" ? 
 (a) Cultivation of grapes 
 (b) Growing of fruits 
 (c) Breeding of fish 
 (d) Commercial rearing of silk worms 
 Sol.356.(d)  Commercial  rearing  of 
 silkworms.  ‘Bombyx  mori',  ‘Eri’,  ‘Muga’ 
 and  ‘Tasar’  are  silkworm  species  used  in 
 sericulture.  These  are  used  to  produce 
 silk  fiber  (protein)  from  their  silk  glands  . 
 Moriculture  refers  to  the  cultivation  of 
 mulberry  plants.  Other  types  of  farming  : 
 Aviculture  (birds),  Apiculture  (bees), 
 Pisciculture  (fish),  Viticulture  (grapes), 
 Fungiculture  (mushroom),  Arboriculture 
 (tree),  Silviculture  (fodder  crops), 
 Olericulture (vegetables). 
 Q.357.  There are regions where farmers 
 specialize  in  vegetables  only.  This  type  of 
 farming is known as ______. 
 (a) Mixed farming  (b) Pastoral farming 
 (c) Dairy farming    (d) Truck farming 
 Sol.357.(d)  Truck  farming.  Mixed 
 farming  includes  crop  cultivation  and 
 raising  animals.  Pastoral  farming 
 involves  livestock,  such  as  cattle,  sheep, 
 and  goats,  while  dairy  farming  focuses 
 on milk production from cows. 
 Q.358.  Which among the following terms 
 means growing of fruits? 
 (a) Apiculture  (b) Arboriculture 
 (c) Pomiculture  (d) Sericulture 
 Sol.358.(c)  Pomiculture.  Apiculture  -  The 
 maintenance  of  honeybees  and  hives. 
 Arboriculture  -  Cultivation  of  trees, 
 shrubs,  and  woody  plants  for  shading 
 and decorating. 
 Q.359.  It  is  a  tropical  as  well  as  a 
 subtropical  crop.  It  grows  well  in  hot  and 
 humid  climates  with  a  temperature  of 
 21°C  to  27°C  and  an  annual  rainfall 
 between  75cm  and  100cm.  Which  of  the

 information given above? 
 (a) Sugarcane  (b) Cotton 
 (c) Maize  (d) Rubber 
 Sol.359.(a)  Sugarcane.  India  is  the 
 second-largest  producer  of  sugarcane 
 after  Brazil.  Maize  -  Grown  in  tropics,  sub  -

 Q.360.  Cotton requires ____ temperature, 
 light rainfall or irrigation,_____ frost - free 
 days and bright sun-shine for its growth. 
 (a) high, 210  (b) low, 150 
 (c) low, 210  (d) high, 150 
 Sol.360.(a)  High,  210.  Cotton  crop

 under the Kharif Crop. 
 Q.361.  In  India  initially  HYVP  was 
 implemented in about how much area? 
 (a) 5 million hectares 
 (b) 3 million hectares 
 (c) 4 million hectares 
 (d) 2 million hectares 
 Sol.361.(d)  2  million  hectares.  HYVP 
 (High  Yielding  Variety  Programme)  was 
 restricted  to  only  fiv e  crops  –  Wheat,  rice, 
 jowar,  bajra  and  maize.  The  high  yielding 
 variety  of  seeds  (resistant  to  insects  and 
 diseases)  played  an  important  role  in  the 
 Green Revolution of India. 
 Q.362.  In  rainforests  which  kind  of 
 cultivation is practiced? 
 (a) Dry farming          (b) Vertical farming 
 (c) Terrace farming   (d) Slash and burn 
 Sol.362.(d)  Slash  and  burn  cultivation  or 
 swidden  cultivation,  is  commonly 
 practiced in rainforest areas. 
 Q.363.  The  Purple  revolution  was 
 launched  by  the  Union  Ministry  of 
 Science  &  Technology  in  Jammu  and 
 Kashmir.  This  revolution  is  related  to 
 cultivation of which crop? 
 (a) Marigold  (b) Kashmiri Rose 
 (c) Lavender  (d) Himalayan Indigo 
 Sol.363.(c) Lavender. The Purple 
 Revolution  was  initiated  in  2016  through 
 the  Aroma  Mission  of  the  Council  of 
 Scientific  and  Industrial  Research  (CSIR). 
 In  India,  the  lavender  crop  is  cultivated  in 
 low  rainfall  regions  and  on  the  slopes  of 
 hills  of  Himachal  Pradesh  (HP)  and  Uttar 
 Pradesh (UP). 
 Q.364.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  types  of 
 agriculture: 
 1. 
 Soilless  agriculture  is  called 
 Hydroponics. 
 2. 
 In  Dry  farming,  crops  are  cultivated 
 without  irrigation  in  regions  of  limited 
 rainfall. 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) 1 only                        (b) 2 only 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.364.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  In  shifting 
 agriculture,  a  clearing  is  made  in  the 
 forest,  usually  on  the  slopes  of  hills.  Dry 
 farming  is  the  cultivation  of  crops 
 without  irrigation  in  regions  of  limited 
 moisture,  typically  less  than  20  inches  of 
 precipitation annually. 
 Q.365.  The  word  agriculture  is  derived 
 from  the  Latin  words  ager  and  culture. 
 What does ‘ager’ mean? 
 (a) Rocks   (b) Soil   (c) Water   (d) Plants 
 Sol.365.(b)  Soil.  The  English  word 
 agriculture  derives  from  the  Latin  ager 
 (field)  and  colo  (cultivate)  signifying, 
 when  combined,  the  Latin  agricultura: 
 field  or  land  tillage.  Agriculture  is  the  art 
 and  science  of  cultivating  the  soil, 
 growing  crops  and  raising  livestock.  It  is 
 the backbone of the Indian economy. 
 Q.366.  India  lost  a  large  proportion  of  ___ 
 growing area to Pakistan during partition. 
 (a) sunflower  (b) jute 
 (c) soyabean  (d) cotton 
 Sol.366.(d)  Cotton  is  a  key  fiber  and  cash 
 crop  in  India,  with  Gujarat  and 
 Maharashtra  as  leading  producers.  The 
 first  cotton  mill,  Bombay  Spinning  and 
 Weaving  Company,  was  established  in 
 Bombay  on  7  July  1854.  Madhya 
 Pradesh  is  the  largest  producer  of 
 soybeans,  Karnataka  of  sunflower,  and 
 West Bengal of jute. 
 Q.367.  Which  of  the  following  is  the  main 
 food  crop  of  the  semi-arid  areas  of 
 central and southern India? 
 (a) Jowar  (b) Maize  (c) Bajra  (d) Ragi 
 Sol.367.(a)  Jowar  is  mainly  concentrated 
 in  the  peninsular  and  central  India. 
 Karnataka  is  the  highest  maize  producer. 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Rajasthan  is  the  state  with  the  highest 
 yield  of  bajra  in  India.  Karnataka  is  the 
 leading  producer  of  ragi  in  the  country 
 followed  by  Tamil  Nadu,  Uttarakhand, 
 Maharashtra and Andhra Pradesh. 
 Q.368.  Which  of  the  following  is  a  well  - 
 known  variety  of  coffee  initially  brought 
 to India from Yemen? 
 (a) Gesha  (b) Arabica 
 (c) Canephora            (d) Charrieriana

 Karnataka  (Kodagu,  Chikkamagaluru, 
 Hassan),  Kerala  (Malabar  region),  and

 Kodaikanal). 
 Q.369.  Assertion  (A):  Taramira  is  a 
 variety  of  oilseed  cultivated  mainly  in  the 
 drier regions of north-western India. 
 Reason  (R):  Arabica,  Robusta,  and 
 Liberica  are  major  varieties  of  oilseed 
 crops in India. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A 
 (c) A is true, but R is false 
 (d) A is false, but R is true 
 Sol.369.(c)  A  is  true,  but  R  is  false. 
 Taramira  is  an  important  oilseed  crop  of 
 drier  regions  of  north-western  India.  The 
 oil  is  not  directly  eaten,  although  it  is 
 mixed  with  mustard  oil  to  increase  the 
 pungency  of  the  latter.  Arabica,  Robusta, 
 and  the  less  common  Liberica  and  Excelsa 
 are the four main types of coffee beans. 
 Soil 
 Q.370.  Consider  the  following 
 characteristics: 
 1. 
 High  moisture-retaining  capacity, 
 often  developing  deep  cracks  during 
 dry hot weather. 
 2. 
 Ideal  for  tea  and  apple  cultivation  due 
 to  high  fertility  and  cold  climate 
 preference. 
 3. 
 Rich  in  lime,  iron,  magnesia,  and 
 alumina,  but  poor  in  phosphoric 
 contents. 
 Which  of  the  above  characteristics  are 
 primarily associated with Black Soils? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Only 1             (b) Only 1 and 3 
 (c) Only 2 and 3  (d) 1, 2 and 3 are correct 
 Sol.370.(b)  Only  1  and  3.  Black  soils, 
 also  called  Regur  soils,  have  high 
 moisture-retaining  capacity  due  to  their 
 clay  content,  causing  swelling  when  wet 
 and  deep  cracks  when  dry.  They  are  rich 
 in  lime,  iron,  magnesia,  and  alumina  but 
 lack  phosphoric  acid,  nitrogen,  and 
 organic  matter.  These  soils  are  ideal  for 
 cotton,  sugarcane,  jowar,  and  wheat 
 cultivation.  Tea  requires  well-drained, 
 acidic,  loamy  soils  (like  Laterite  soil)  in 
 humid  hilly  regions,  while  apples  grow 
 best in loamy soils of colder climates. 
 Q.371.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  correctly  differentiates 
 Alluvial soils from Red and Yellow soils? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Alluvial: from rocks; Red/Yellow: 
 river-transported 
 (b) Alluvial: older types most fertile; 
 Red/Yellow: colored by iron 
 (c) Alluvial: sandy loam to clay; 
 Red/Yellow: sandy loam, less fertile 
 uplands 
 (d) Alluvial: rich in phosphorus and 
 humus; Red/Yellow: rich in potash 
 Sol.371.(c)  Alluvial  soils  exhibit  a  wide 
 range  of  textures  from  sandy  loam  to 
 clay,  depending  on  the  river's 
 depositional  environment.  In  contrast, 
 Red  and  Yellow  soils,  especially  in  upland 
 regions,  are  often  coarser,  such  as  sandy 
 loam,  and  are  generally  less  fertile  than 
 the  nutrient-rich  alluvial  soils  found  in 
 river plains. 
 Q.372.  Which  of  the  following  agro  - 
 climatic  and  soil  conditions  is  most 
 suitable for the cultivation of wheat? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Arid crop; temperature above 40°C; 
 sandy loam soil 
 (b) Subtropical crop; high humidity; 
 laterite soil 
 (c) Temperate crop; winter temp 10-15°C, 
 summer 21-26°C; clay loam soil 
 (d) Tropical crop; 30-35°C temperature; 
 sandy soil 
 Sol.372.(c)  Wheat  is  a  rabi  crop  in  India 
 and  thrives  best  under  cool  winters  and 
 warm  summers.  The  ideal  climatic  and 
 soil  conditions  are:  Temperature:  Sowing: 
 around  10–15°C,  Ripening/Harvesting: 
 around  21–26°C,  Rainfall:  Moderate 
 rainfall  (75–100  cm),  with  irrigation 
 support  in  drier  regions.Soil:  Well-drained 
 clay  loam  or  alluvial  soil,  rich  in  humus,  is 
 best for wheat cultivation. 
 Q.373.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about plateaus: 
 1. 
 The  Deccan  Lava  Plateau  in  India  is 
 very  fertile  because  it  is  rich  in  black 
 soil,  which  is  highly  suitable  for 
 farming. 
 2. 
 The  African  Plateau  is  famous  for  its 
 fertile  black  soil,  whereas  the  Katanga 
 Plateau is primarily known for 
 agriculture. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1                    (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2         (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.373.(b)  Only  2.  Deccan  lava  plateau 
 is  a  volcanic  plateau  in  west-central  India 
 that  was  formed  by  the  solidification  of 
 lava  from  deep  within  the  Earth.  It  is  a 
 triangular  landmass  that  lies  to  the  south 
 of  the  river  Narmada.  The  African  plateau 
 is  famous  for  gold  and  diamond  mining. 
 The  Katanga  Plateau  in  the  Democratic 
 Republic  of  the  Congo  is  known  for  its 
 rich deposits of copper and uranium. 
 Q.374.  In  a  laterite  soil  profile,  which  of 
 the  following  elements  is  present  in  an 
 excessive amount? 
 (a) Iron (b) feldspar (c) Nitrogen  (d) Lime 
 Sol.374.(a)  Iron.  Laterite  soil  contains 
 excess  iron  due  to  intense  leaching  in 
 high  rainfall  and  temperature  areas.  The 
 leaching  process  removes  lime  and 
 silica,  leaving  iron  oxide  and  aluminum 
 compounds.  These  soils  are  poor  in 
 nitrogen,  phosphate,  and  calcium, 
 making  them  less  fertile  naturally. 
 However,  after  adding  manures  and 
 fertilizers,  they  are  suitable  for  tree  crops 
 like cashew nuts. 
 Q.375.  Which of the following factors is 
 primarily responsible for soil formation? 
 (a) Weathering  (b) Irrigation 
 (c) Degradation  (d) Mining 
 Sol.375.(a)  Weathering  :  It  involves  the 
 breakdown  of  rocks  and  minerals  into 
 smaller  particles  through  exposure  to 
 environmental  factors  like  wind,  water, 
 ice,  temperature  fluctuations  and  others. 
 Irrigation  :  It  supports  agriculture  and 
 affects  soil  moisture.  Degradation  :  This 
 refers  to  the  deterioration  of  soil  quality. 
 Mining : It can disturb existing soils. 
 Q.376.  Which  of  the  following  is  the  main 
 cause of land degradation in Jharkhand 
 and Chhattisgarh? 
 (a) Intensive use of manure 
 (b) Over-irrigation 
 (c) Deforestation due to mining 
 (d) Overgrazing 
 Sol.376.(c)  Deforestation  due  to  mining. 
 Human  activities  like  deforestation, 
 over-grazing,  mining,  and  quarrying 
 significantly  contribute  to  land 
 degradation.  Deforestation  due  to  mining 
 has  severely  impacted  states  like 
 Jharkhand  and  Odisha.  Over-grazing 
 affects  Gujarat,  Rajasthan,  and  Madhya 
 Pradesh,  while  excessive  irrigation 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 causes  waterlogging  and  soil 
 degradation  in  Punjab,  Haryana,  and 
 western Uttar Pradesh. 
 Q.377.  Which  is  the  largest  part  of  the 
 northern  plains  and  is  formed  of  older 
 alluvium? 
 (a) Terai  (b) Bhabar 
 (c) Bhangar  (d) Khadar 
 Sol.377.(c)  Bhangar.  It  is  characterized 
 by  slightly  elevated  terraces  and  is 
 composed  of  older,  more  compact 
 sediments  compared  to  the  newer  and 
 more  fertile  alluvial  deposits  found  in  the 
 Khadar  regions.  The  Bhangar  area  is  less 
 fertile  due  to  the  presence  of  calcareous 
 deposits known as "Kankar." 
 Q.378.  Which  unit  is  used  to  measure  the 
 concentration  of  contaminants  in  soil 
 and sediment? 
 (a) DPMO   (b) TDS   (c) PPBB   (d) PPM 
 Sol.378.(d)  PPM  (Parts  per  million)  is 
 commonly  used  to  express  very  dilute 
 concentrations  of  substances  in  various 
 environmental  contexts,  including  soil 
 and sediment analysis. 
 Q.379.  In  which  of  the  following 
 processes  nutrients  or  contaminants  are 
 carried  away  by  water  or  moved  into  the 
 lower layer of the soil ? 
 (a) Leaching  (b) Bleaching 
 (c) Sowing  (d) Ploughing 
 Sol.379.(a)  Leaching:  It  occurs  as 
 excess  water  removes  water-soluble 
 nutrients  out  of  the  soil,  by  runoff  or 
 drainage.  It  is  an  environmental  problem 
 for  agricultural  professionals  whether 
 chemical-heavy  fertilisers  or  chemicals 
 are swept out and find their way into 
 water bodies. 
 Q.380.  Assertion  (A):  The  black  soil  of 
 the  Deccan  Plateau  is  deficient  in 
 phosphorus. 
 Reason (R):  Black soil is rich in iron, lime, 
 calcium,  potassium,  aluminum,  and 
 magnesium  but  poor  in  nitrogen  and 
 organic matter. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) A is true, but R is false. 
 (d) A is false, but R is true. 
 Sol.380.(b)  Both  A  and  R  are  true,  but  R 
 is  not  the  correct  explanation  of  A.  Black 
 soil  or  cotton  soil  is  good  for  moisture 
 and  good  for  growing  cotton,  sugarcane, 
 wheat,  jawar,  rice,  citrus  fruits,  sunflower, 
 linseed,  etc.  Black  soil  requires  minimum 
 tillage  because  of  its  self-cultivating 
 feature. 
 Q.381.  Laterite  soil  develops  as  a  result 
 of which of the following reasons ? 
 (a) Compaction 
 (b) Leaching 
 (c) High temperature only 
 (d) Sedimentation 
 Sol.381.(b)  Leaching  -  It  is  used  to 
 extract  the  substances  from  the  solids. 
 Laterite  Soil:  It  develops  in  areas  with 
 high  temperature  and  high  rainfall.  These 
 are  the  result  of  intense  leaching  due  to 
 tropical  rains.  It  is  commonly  found  in 
 Karnataka,  Kerala,  Tamil  Nadu,  Madhya 
 Pradesh  and  the  hilly  areas  of  Odisha 
 and  Assam.  Laterite  Soil  is  most  suitable 
 soil for growing coffee in India 
 Q.382.  The  black  soil  area  of  the  _______ 
 is known as the Deccan Trap. 
 (a) Northern Plains 
 (b) Peninsular Plateau 
 (c) Coastal Plains 
 (d) Himalayan Mountains 
 Sol.382.(b)  Peninsular  Plateau.  It  is  an 
 ancient  landmass  composed  mainly  of 
 igneous  and  metamorphic  hard 
 crystalline  rocks.  It  was  formed  due  to 
 the  breaking  and  drifting  of 
 Gondwanaland.  Deccan  Plateau  -  A 
 triangular  landmass  that  lies  to  the  south 
 of  the  river  Narmada.  Black  Soil  Covers 
 most  of  the  deccan  plateau  which 
 includes  part  of  Maharashtra,  Madhya 
 Pradesh,  Gujarat,  Andhra  Pradesh  and 
 some part of Tamil Nadu. 
 Q.383.  In  which  of  the  following  states  of 
 India  is  the  maximum  extent  of  alluvial 
 soil found? 
 (a) Telangana  (b) Uttar Pradesh 
 (c) Karnataka  (d) Maharashtra 
 Sol.383.(b)  Uttar  Pradesh.  Alluvial  Soil 
 found  in  northern  plains  and  the  river 
 valleys.  It  is  rich  in  potash  but  poor  in 
 phosphorus.  Two  types  of  alluvial  soil  : 
 Khadar  -  New  alluvium,  deposited  by 
 floods  annually,  which  enriches  the  soil 
 by  depositing  fine  slits.  Bhangar  -  Old 
 alluvium,  deposited  away  from  the  flood 
 plains.  Alluvial  soils  are  intensively 
 cultivated. 
 Q.384.  What is the type of soil composed 
 of silt and dust ? 
 (a) Loam  (b) Marl  (c) Loess  (d) Podsol 
 Sol.384.(c)  Loess.  Properties  :  Colour  - 
 Yellowish  Brown,  Homogeneous  and 
 Highly  Porous.  Mostly  Found  (India)  - 
 Kashmir  Valley.  Loam  -  The  type  of  Soil 
 composed  of  sand,  silt,  and  clay  soil. 
 Marl  -  The  type  of  soil  composed  of 
 Clays  and  Slit.  Podsol  -  The  soil  derives 
 from  either  quartz-rich  sands  and 
 sandstone  or  sedimentary  debris  from 
 magmatic rocks. 
 Q.385.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about soils in India: 
 1. 
 Red  and  Yellow  soils  together  cover 
 about 40% of the total area of India. 
 2. 
 Black  soil,  also  called  Regur  soil,  is 
 suitable for cotton cultivation. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1                       (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2            (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.385.(a)  Only  1.  Alluvial  soil  is  found 
 on  about  40%  of  the  total  area  of  India.  It 
 is  deposited  by  surface  water,  commonly 
 found  in  river  valleys  and  northern  plains. 
 It  is  highly  fertile  and  rich  in  humus  and 
 lime.  Crops  cultivated  on  it  include 
 sugarcane,  rice,  maize,  wheat,  oilseeds, 
 and pulses. 
 Q.386.  The  colour  of  the  alluvial  soils 
 varies from ________. 
 (a) pale white to warm white 
 (b) light orange to dark orange 
 (c) orange to red 
 (d) light grey to ash grey 
 Sol.386.(d)  light  grey  to  ash  grey. 
 Alluvial  soils  are  formed  by  the 
 deposition  of  sediments  from  rivers  and 
 streams.  These  sediments  are  typically 
 composed  of  sand,  silt,  and  clay,  which 
 are  all  light-coloured  materials.  The 
 colour  of  the  alluvial  soils  can  also  be 
 affected  by  the  presence  of  organic 
 matter,  which  can  give  them  a  darker 
 colour.  Light  orange  to  dark  orange  is  the 
 colour  of  laterite  soils.  Orange  to  red  is 
 the colour of red soils. 
 Q.387.  Which  of  the  following  is  an 
 example of micronutrient supplied by soil ? 
 (a) Potassium  (b) Iron 
 (c) Calcium  (d) Sulphur 
 Sol.387.(b) Iron.  Soil is a major source of 
 nutrients  needed  by  plants  for  growth. 
 Macronutrients  in  soil  -  Nitrogen  (N), 
 Phosphorus  (P),  Potassium  (K),  Calcium 
 (Ca),  Magnesium  (Mg)  and  Sulphur  (S). 
 Micronutrients  in  soil  -  Chlorine  (Cl), 
 Boron  (B),  Iron  (Fe),  Manganese  (Mn), 
 Copper  (Cu),  Zinc  (Zn),  Nickel  (Ni)  and 
 Molybdenum (Mo). 
 Q.388.  Which  of  the  following  statements 
 is NOT correct regarding fertilizers ? 
 (a) They are prepared in factories 
 (b) They are very rich in plant nutrients 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (c) They provide humus to the soil 
 (d) They are man-made inorganic salt 
 Sol.388.(c)  Humus  is  the  organic 
 component  of  soil  that  is  formed  by  the 
 decomposition  of  plant  and  animal 
 matter.  It  can  be  produced  naturally  or 
 through  a  process  called  composting. 
 Manures are used to provide humus in 
 soil. 
 Q.389.  Arid  soils  are  characteristically 
 developed  in  ___________.  which  exhibit 
 characteristic arid topography. 
 (a) Southern Uttar Pradesh 
 (b) Western Uttar Pradesh 
 (c) Western Rajasthan 
 (d) Southern Rajasthan 
 Sol.389.(c)  Western  Rajasthan.  In  India, 
 the  Indian  Council  of  Agricultural 
 Research  (ICAR)  has  classified  soils  into 
 8  categories.  They  are:  Alluvial  Soil,  Black 
 Cotton  Soil,  Red  &  Yellow  Soil,  Laterite 
 Soil,  Mountainous  or  Forest  Soil,  Arid  or 
 Desert  Soil,  Saline  and  Alkaline  Soil, 
 Peaty and Marshy Soil. 
 Q.390.  Which  of  the  following  pairs  of 
 'soil - characteristics' is correct? 
 I. 
 Sandy  -  proportion  of  fine  particles  is 
 relatively higher 
 II. 
 Clayey  -  greater  proportion  of  big 
 particles 
 (a) Only I  (b) Neither I nor II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Only II 
 Sol.390.(b)  Neither  I  nor  II.  Sandy  Soil  : 
 Consists  of  small  particles  of  weathered 
 rock;  One  of  the  poorest  types  of  soil  for 
 growing  plants  (very  low  nutrients  and 
 poor  water  holding  capacity);  Good  for 
 the  drainage  system;  Usually  formed  by 
 the  breakdown  or  fragmentation  of  rocks 
 like  granite,  limestone  and  quartz.  Clay 
 Soil  :  Smallest  particles  (tightly  packed 
 together  with  each  other  with  very  little  or 
 no  airspace);  Good  water  storage 
 qualities;  Very  sticky  to  touch  when  wet 
 but  smooth  when  dried;  Densest  and 
 heaviest  type  of  soil  which  does  not  drain 
 well  or  provide  space  for  plant  roots  to 
 flourish. 
 Q.391.  Which  of  the  following  factors  of 
 soil  formation  determines  the 
 accumulation of the soil ? 
 (a) Fauna  (b) Flora 
 (c) Relief  (d) Parent rock 
 Sol.391.(c)  Relief:  Altitude  and  slope, 
 determine  accumulation  of  soil.  Other 
 Factors:  Fauna  is  all  of  the  animal  life 
 present  in  a  particular  region  or  time. 
 Flora  is  all  the  plant  life  present  in  a 
 particular  region  or  time,  generally  the 
 naturally occurring native plants. 
 Q.392.  Statement  I:  In  terrace  farming, 
 different  crops  are  grown  in  alternate 
 rows  and  are  sown  at  different  times  to 
 protect the soil from rain wash. 
 Statement  II:  In  contour  ploughing, 
 different  crops  are  grown  in  alternate 
 rows  and  are  sown  at  different  times  to 
 protect the soil from rain wash. 
 (a) Only Statement I is correct 
 (b) Only Statement II is correct 
 (c) Both Statements I and II are correct 
 (d) Neither Statement I nor Statement II 
 is correct 
 Sol.392.(d)  Neither  Statement  I  nor 
 Statement  II  is  correct.  Intercropping 
 crops  are  grown  in  alternate  rows  and 
 are  sown  at  different  times  to  protect  the 
 soil  from  rain  wash.  Contour  ploughing  - 
 Process  of  cutting  furrows  into  the  slope 
 of  the  land  in  order  to  control  the  flow  of 
 water. 
 Q.393.  In  which  of  the  soil  conservation 
 methods  in  the  coastal  and  dry  regions, 
 rows  of  trees  are  planted  to  check  the 
 wind movement to protect soil cover? 
 (a) Mulching  (b) Shelter belts 
 (c) Rock dam  (d) Contour barriers 
 Sol.393.(b)  Shelterbelts.  Mulching:  The 
 bare  ground  between  plants  is  covered 
 with  a  layer  of  organic  matter  like  straw. 
 It  helps  to  retain  soil  moisture.  Contour 
 barriers:  Stones,  grass,  soil  are  used  to 
 build  barriers  along  contours.  Trenches 
 are  made  in  front  of  the  barriers  to 
 collect water. 
 Q.394.  Identify  the  step  that  is  NOT  a 
 part of decomposition. 
 (a) Leaching  (b) Humification 
 (c) Catabolism  (d) Soil erosion 
 Sol.394.(d)  Soil  erosion  is  the 
 denudation  or  wearing  away  of  the  upper 
 layer  of  soil.  It  is  a  form  of  soil 
 degradation.  There  are  fiv e  steps  of 
 decomposition.  These  are  fragmentation, 
 leaching,  catabolism,  humification,  and 
 mineralization. 
 Q.395.  Statement  I:  Climate,  parent 
 material,  and  topography  are  important 
 factors that influence soil formation. 
 Statement  II:  Landslides  are  a  major 
 factor responsible for soil formation. 
 (a) Only Statement I is correct 
 (b) Only Statement II is correct 
 (c) Both Statements I and II are correct 
 (d) Neither Statement I nor Statement II 
 is correct 
 Sol.395.(a)  Only  Statement  I  is  correct. 
 Soil  formation  is  influenced  mainly  by 
 climate,  parent  material,  topography, 
 organisms,  and  time;  landslides  do  not 
 affect soil formation directly. 
 Q.396.  What  do  you  call  the  weight  a  soil 
 can  withstand  before  severe  damage 
 occurs to the structure of the soil? 
 (a) Field capacity    (b) Bearing capacity 
 (c) Bulk density       (d) Buffering capacity 
 Sol.396.(b)  Bearing  capacity  is  the 
 capacity of soil to support the loads that 
 are  applied  to  the  ground  above.  Field 
 capacity  is  the  amount  of  soil  moisture 
 or  water  content  held  in  the  soil  after 
 excess  water  has  drained  away  and  the 
 rate  of  downward  movement  has 
 decreased.  Bulk  density  is  defined  as  the 
 mass  of  the  many  particles  of  the 
 material  divided  by  the  total  volume  they 
 occupy.  Buffer  capacity  refers  to  the 
 amount  of  acid  or  base  a  buffer  can 
 neutralise  before  the  pH  changes  by  a 
 large amount. 
 Q.397.  __________  develops  on  crystalline 
 igneous  rocks  in  areas  of  low  rainfall  in 
 the  eastern  and  southern  part  of  the 
 Deccan Plateau. 
 (a) Red soil  (b) Black soil 
 (c) Alluvial soil  (d) Arid soil 
 Sol.397.(a)  Red  soil.  It  contains  a  high 
 percentage  of  iron  content,  which  is 
 responsible for its colour. 
 Q.398.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding soil horizons: 
 Statement  I:  The  A-horizon,  also  called 
 the  topsoil,  contains  humus  and  is  rich  in 
 organic materials. 
 Statement  II:  The  C-horizon  is  also  called 
 humus layer and is rich in organic matter. 
 Which of the above statements is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I                        (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II            (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.398.(a)  Only  I.  A-horizon  layer  is  the 
 top  layer  of  soil  which  is  also  known  as  a 
 humus  layer  and  is  mainly  rich  in  organic 
 materials.  B-horizon  is  typically  a  mineral 
 subsurface  horizon  and  is  a  zone  of 
 accumulation,  called  illuviation.  C- 
 horizon  :  This  soil  layer  lies  beneath  the  B 
 horizon  and  consists  essentially  of  the 
 more  or  less  weathered  parent  rock. 
 Bedrock:  Bedrock  in  geology  is  a  solid 
 rock  that  lies  under  loose  softer  material 
 within  the  crust  of  Earth  or  another 
 terrestrial planet. 
 Q.399.  _____ are thick deposits of glacial 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 clay  and  other  materials  embedded  with 
 moraines. 
 (a) Karewas  (b) Bhabars 
 (c) Duars  (d) Duns 
 Sol.399.(a)  Karewas  are  lacustrine 
 deposits  (deposits  in  a  lake)  in  the  valley 
 of  Kashmir  and  in  the  Bhaderwah  valley 
 of  the  Jammu  division  of  Jammu  and 
 Kashmir.  These  are  the  flat-t opped 
 mounds  that  border  the  Kashmir  valley 
 on all sides. 
 Q.400.  _______ is a deep red soil found in 
 tropical  regions  and  often  developed  on 
 mafic igneous bedrock. 
 (a) Arid  (b) Pedocal 
 (c) Laterite  (d) Saline 
 Sol.400.(c)  Laterite  .  Arid  soils  are 
 formed  from  the  fragmentation  of 
 adjacent  rocks  and  are  largely  blown 
 from  the  Indus  valley  area  and  coastal 
 regions.  Saline  soils  are  formed 
 whenever  climate,  soil,  and  hydrological 
 conditions  favor  the  accumulation  of 
 soluble salts in the root zone. 
 Q.401.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 Statement  I:  Diastrophism  is  the  process 
 that  moves,  elevates  or  builds  up 
 portions of the Earth’s crust. 
 Statement  II:  Weathering  is  the  process 
 of  molten  rock  (magma)  erupting  onto 
 the Earth’s surface. 
 Which of the following is correct? 
 (a) Both Statement I and II are correct 
 (b) Both Statement I and II are incorrect 
 (c) Statement I is correct but Statement II 
 is incorrect 
 (d) Statement I is incorrect but 
 Statement II is correct 
 Sol.401.(c)  Statement  I  is  correct  but 
 Statement  II  is  incorrect.  Denudation  is 
 the  process  of  wearing  away  the  Earth's 
 surface  by  weathering  and  erosion. 
 Volcanism  is  the  process  of  molten  rock 
 (magma)  erupting  onto  the  Earth's 
 surface. 
 Vegetation 
 Q.402.  Name  the  state  in  which  The 
 Bhitarkanika Mangroves are located? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Odisha                  (b) Andhra Pradesh 
 (c) Tamil Nadu          (d) West Bengal 
 Sol.402.(a)  Odisha.  The  Bhitarkanika 
 Mangroves  ecosystem  is  one  of  the 
 largest  in  India  and  is  situated  in  the 
 Kendrapara  district.  It  is  known  for  its 
 rich  biodiversity,  including  saltwater 
 crocodiles,  migratory  birds,  and 
 mangrove  forests.  The  area  is  protected 
 as  the  Bhitarkanika  National  Park  and  is 
 recognized for its ecological importance. 
 Q.403.  The  Ebony,  Mahogany,  Cinchona 
 etc.  are  trees  found  in  which  type  of 
 forests in India? 
 (a) Tropical Deciduous Forests 
 (b) Thorny Forests 
 (c) Mangrove Forests 
 (d) Tropical Evergreen Forests 
 Sol.403.(d)  Tropical  Evergreen  Forests 
 are  restricted  to  heavy  rainfall  areas  of 
 the  Western  Ghats  and  the  island  groups 
 of  Lakshadweep,  Andaman  and  Nicobar, 
 upper  parts  of  Assam  and  Tamil  Nadu 
 coast.  Other  trees  are  rosewood,  and 
 rubber.  Tropical  Deciduous  Forests  - 
 Teak,  Bamboos,  sal,  shisham.  Thorny 
 Forests  -  Acacias,  palms,  euphorbias  and 
 cacti.  Mangrove  Forests  -  Sundari  trees, 
 Palm, Coconut. 
 Q.404.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding forests in India: 
 Statement  I:  Magnolia  and  Laurel  trees 
 are  typically  found  in  Montane  forests  of 
 India. 
 Statement  II:  Montane  forests  show 
 variation  with  altitude,  including 
 evergreen  broad-leaf  trees  between 
 1000–2000  meters  and  coniferous  trees 
 between 1500–3000 meters. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I                         (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II              (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.404.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  In  mountainous 
 areas,  temperature  decreases  with 
 altitude,  leading  to  a  change  in 
 vegetation.  Between  1000–2000  meters, 
 wet  temperate  forests  with  evergreen 
 broad-leaf  trees  like  oaks  and  chestnuts 
 are  found.  Between  1500–3000  meters, 
 coniferous  trees  such  as  pine,  deodar, 
 and  cedar  dominate,  mainly  on  the 
 southern  slopes  of  the  Himalayas.  Higher 
 elevations  feature  temperate  grasslands, 
 and  above  3600  meters,  alpine 
 vegetation  takes  over  with  trees  like 
 silver fir   , junipers, and pines. 
 Q.405.  ________  leaves  are  used  to  make 
 bidis. 
 (a) Neem (b) Champa (c) Betel (d) Tendu 
 Sol.405.(d)  Tendu  tree  (Diospyros 
 melanoxylon  Roxb.)  belonging  to  Family 
 Ebenaceae. 
 Q.406  .  Sundari  trees  are  found  in  which 
 type of forests in India? 
 (a) Mangrove forests 
 (b) Tropical deciduous forests 
 (c) The thorn forests and scrubs 
 (d) Tropical evergreen forests 
 Sol.406.(a)  Mangrove  forests.  Sundari 
 tree  is  the  most  abundant  tree  in  the 
 mangrove  forests  of  the  Sunderban  delta 
 in  West  Bengal  and  the  Andaman  and 
 Nicobar  Islands.  The  Sunderbans  are 
 named  after  the  Sundari  tree.  Mangrove 
 forests  grow  only  at  tropical  and 
 subtropical  latitudes  near  the  equator 
 because  they  cannot  withstand  freezing 
 temperatures.  Uppu  ponna,  Boddu 
 ponna,  Urada,  Mada,  Telli  Mada,  Gundu 
 mada,  Kadili,  Palm,  coconut,  keora,  agar 
 and  Bella  are  some  of  the  typical 
 vegetation of the mangroves. 
 Q.407.  Which  of  the  following  states  has 
 mangrove forests? 
 (a) Telangana  (b) Andhra Pradesh 
 (c) Rajasthan  (d) Manipur 
 Sol.407.(b)  Andhra  Pradesh.  Mangroves 
 are  practically  evergreen  with  thick 
 leathery  leaves.  Such  types  of  forests  are 
 found  in  the  deltas  of  Sundarbans, 
 Mahanadi,  the  Godavari,  Krishna,  Kaveri 
 rivers  and  in  the  Andaman  and  Nicobar 
 Islands. 
 Q.408.  In  which  of  the  following  regions 
 is  the  tropical  evergreen  forests  found  in 
 India? 
 (a) Eastern Ghats  (b) Western Ghats 
 (c) Rann of Kutch  (d) Thar Desert 
 Sol.408.(b)  Western  Ghats.  Tropical 
 evergreen  forests  are  found  on  the 
 western  slopes  of  the  Western  Ghats, 
 northeastern  hills,  and  Andaman  and 
 Nicobar  Islands.  They  thrive  in  warm, 
 humid  areas  with  over  200  cm  of  annual 
 rainfall  and  temperatures  above  22°C. 
 These  forests  are  dense,  with  tall  trees 
 reaching  up  to  60  meters  and  remain 
 green  year-round.  Common  species 
 include rosewood, mahogany, and ebony. 
 Q.409.  In  which  of  the  following  parts  of 
 India  are  thorn  forests  and  scrub  forests 
 found? 
 (a) South-eastern India 
 (b) North-eastern India 
 (c) North-western India 
 (d) South-western India 
 Sol.409.(c)  North-western  India.  Thorn 
 and  scrub  forests  are  a  type  of  natural 
 vegetation  found  in  regions  that  receive 
 less  than  70  cm  of  annual  rainfall.  These 
 regions  typically  experience  dry  and  arid 
 climatic  conditions,  and  the  vegetation 
 here  has  adapted  to  conserve  water  and 
 withstand  harsh  environments.  It  is 
 found  in  parts  of  northwestern  India, 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 central  Deccan  Plateau,  and  parts  of  the 
 Thar  Desert.  The  main  plant  species 
 include  Acacia  (Babool),  Cactus, 
 Euphorbias, and Palms. 
 Q.410.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is  NOT  true  about  the  moist 
 deciduous forests of India? 
 (a) They grow in regions with rainfall of 
 100-200 cm. 
 (b) They are found in the foothills of 
 Himalayas. 
 (c) They are found in the plains of Bihar 
 and Uttar Pradesh. 
 (d) Sal, amla, shisham and mahua are 
 some of the main species of these 
 forests. 
 Sol.410.(c)  Moist  deciduous  forests 
 exist  mostly  in  the  eastern  part  of  the 
 country  –  northeastern  states,  along  the 
 foothills  of  the  Himalayas,  Jharkhand, 
 West  Odisha  and  Chhattisgarh,  and  on 
 the  eastern  slopes  of  the  Western  Ghats. 
 The  dry  deciduous  forests  are  found  in 
 areas  having  rainfall  between  100  cm 
 and  70  cm.  These  forests  are  found  in 
 the  rainier  parts  of  the  Peninsular  plateau 
 and  the  plains  of  Bihar  and  Uttar 
 Pradesh. 
 Q.411.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements is/are correct? 
 Statement  1:  Deciduous  forests  are 
 found on the foothills of the Himalayas. 
 Statement 2:  Alpine forests occur at 
 altitudes between 2,900 to 3,500 m in the 
 Himalayas. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 is correct 
 (b) Only Statement 2 is correct 
 (c) Both Statement 1 and 2 are correct 
 (d) Neither Statement 1 nor 2 is correct 
 Sol.411.(c)  Both  Statement  1  and  2  are 
 correct.  Deciduous  is  an  evergreen  forest 
 where  a  majority  of  the  trees  remain 
 “green”  throughout  the  year  because  they 
 shed  leaves  not  seasonally  but  at  various 
 periods  of  the  year.  Example  -  Maple, 
 oak,  and  elm.  Alpine  forests  :  Example  - 
 Spruce and birch. 
 Q.412.  In  the  Himalayas,  the  ________ 
 temperature  with  increasing  altitude 
 causes the change in vegetation. 
 (a) increasing  (b) decreasing 
 (c) fluctuating  (d) stable 
 Sol.412.(b)  decreasing.  The  Himalayan 
 ranges  show  change  in  vegetation  from 
 tropical  to  tundra.  Since  air  is  less  dense 
 at  higher  altitudes,  its  molecules  are 
 spread  farther  apart  than  they  are  at  sea 
 level.  These  molecules  have  fewer 
 collisions,  so  they  produce  less  heat.  At 
 the  bottom  of  the  mountain,  the 
 temperature  is  high  year-round  while  it  is 
 much  cooler  at  the  top  due  to  which  it  is 
 covered with snow. 
 Q.413.  Which  one  of  the  following 
 statements  regarding  vegetation  strata  is 
 NOT correct? 
 (a) Trees fall under the Top vertical 
 strata. 
 (b) The Second vertical contains shrubs 
 and small trees. 
 (c) The Bottom vertical contains 
 medium-sized shrubs and trees. 
 (d) The Middle vertical includes 
 medium-sized shrubs and trees. 
 Sol.413.(c)  Bottom  vertical  -  It  contains 
 the  shortest  plants  in  a  forest 
 ecosystem, such as mosses and ferns. 
 Q.414.  The  natural  vegetation  of  India  is 
 broadly divided into ______ groups. 
 (a) 7        (b) 9        (c) 6        (d) 5 
 Sol.414.(d)  5.  Vegetation  of  India  – 
 Tropical  evergreen  forest,  Tropical 
 deciduous  forest,  Thorny  bushes, 
 Mountain  vegetation  and  Mangrove 
 forests. 
 Q.415.  In  which  type  of  forests  are  wild 
 donkeys and camels found in India? 
 (a) Tropical evergreen forest 
 (b) Mountain forest 
 (c) Thorny forests and bushes 
 (d) Mangrove forest 
 Sol.415.(c) Thorny forests and bushes. 
 Animals  found  in  different  forests  in 
 India:  Tropical  Evergreen  Forests  - 
 Elephants,  Monkeys,  Lemurs  and  Deer. 
 Montane  Forests  -  Leopard,  Yak,  Sheep 
 and  Goat.  Mangrove  forest  -  Royal 
 Bengal  Tiger.  In  terms  of  area,  Madhya 
 Pradesh  has  the  largest  forest  area  in  the 
 country.  It  is  followed  by  Arunachal 
 Pradesh,  Chhattisgarh,  Odisha  and 
 Maharashtra. 
 Q.416.  Sal,  acacia  and  bamboo,  which 
 are  types  of  Dry  Tropical  Forest,  receive 
 what amount of annual rainfall? 
 (a) From 51 cm to 151 cm 
 (b) Between 200 and 250 cm 
 (c) 251 cm or more 
 (d) Below 25 cm 
 Sol.416.(a)  From  51  cm  to  151  cm. 
 Tropical  forests  (rainforests)  :  A 
 rainforest  is  an  area  of  tall,  mostly 
 evergreen  trees  and  a  high  amount  of 
 rainfall,  Earth’s  oldest  living  ecosystems, 
 with  some  surviving  in  their  present  form 
 for  at  least  70  million  years.  Types  of 
 Tropical  forests  -  Evergreen  Rain  (200 
 cm  or  more),  Monsoon  Deciduous  (100 
 to  200  cm),  Tropical  Savanna  (50  to  100 
 cm),  Dry  Thorny  Scrub  (Semi-arid)  (25  to 
 50 cm), Desert (Arid) (Below 25 cm). 
 Q.417.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 most widespread forest type in India? 
 (a) Littoral and Swamp Forest 
 (b) Tropical Thorn Forests 
 (c) Montane Forests 
 (d) Tropical Deciduous Forests 
 Sol.417.(d)  Tropical  Deciduous  Forests 
 (Monsoon  Forests):  It  Found  in  India 
 along  the  Equator  belt  between  the  tropic 
 of  Cancer  and  the  tropic  of  Capricorn, 
 (Major  states  -  Madhya  Pradesh,  Uttar 
 Pradesh,  Chattisgarh,  Odisha,  Some 
 Parts of Maharashtra). 
 Q.418.  What  type  of  vegetation  is  found 
 in  the  plateau  of  Meghalaya,  Sahyadri 
 and the central and southern islands of 
 the Nicobar group? 
 (a) Dry Tropical Deciduous Vegetation 
 (b) Dry Tropical Thorny Vegetation 
 (c) Alpine Vegetation 
 (d) Moist Tropical Semi - Evergreen 
 Vegetation 
 Sol.418.(d)  Moist  Tropical  Semi  - 
 Evergreen  Vegetation  are  transitional 
 forests  between  tropical  wet  evergreen 
 forests  and  tropical  deciduous  forests. 
 Examples  -  Laurel,  rosewood,  mesua, 
 white  cedar.  Tropical  Dry  Deciduous 
 Vegetation  -  Occurs  in  an  irregular  wide 
 strip  running  from  the  foot  of  the 
 Himalayas  to  Kanyakumari  except  in 
 Rajasthan,  Western  Ghats  and  West 
 Bengal.  Example  -  Teak,  bamboo. 
 Tropical  Thorn  Forests  -  Found  in 
 Rajasthan,  south-western  Punjab, 
 western  Haryana,  Kachchh  and 
 neighbouring  parts  of  Saurashtra. 
 Example - Babool, neem, cactii. 
 Q.419.  Plant  varieties  like  bamboo, 
 epiphytes,  aini,  semul,  gutel  and

 Himalaya.  Examples  -  Black  juniper  and

 Mainly  generated  in  Northern  middle 
 Himalayas  ranges  (1801  to  3001  m)  and 
 Southern  Niligiri  higher  Mountain  ranges. 
 Examples  -  Ferns,  oak  and  maple.  Dry 
 tropical  forest  -  Mainly  found  in  Indian

 Northern  Hilly  regions  and  some  states 
 of  Southern  India.  Examples  -  Sal,  acacia,

 Q.420.  Which  of  the  following  states  is 
 associated  with  the  tropical  thorn 
 forests? 
 (a) Andaman and Nicobar Islands 
 (b) West Bengal 
 (c) Madhya Pradesh 
 (d) Mizoram 
 Sol.420.(c)  Madhya  Pradesh.  Tropical 
 Thorn  Forests  and  Scrubs  (receive 
 rainfall  less  than  50  cm)  -  It  includes 
 semi  -  arid  areas  of  south  west  Punjab, 
 Haryana,  Rajasthan,  Gujarat,  Madhya
