 Babool, ber, wild date palm, Khair tree. 
 Q.421.  Mosses  and  lichens  are  found  in 
 which kind of forest? 
 (a) Tropical Rainforest 
 (b) Temperate Forest 
 (c) Coniferous Forest 
 (d) Tundra Forest 
 Sol.421.(d)  Tundra  Forest  -  It  has 
 relatively  little  rain  and  extremely  cold 
 temperatures.  It  is  composed  of  dwarf 
 shrubs, sedges, and grasses. 
 Q.422.  Major plantations are found in the 
 __________ region of the world. 
 (a) Tropical  (b) Grassland 
 (c) Temperate  (d) Desert 
 Sol.422.(a) Tropical. Plantation crops  - A 
 type  of  commercial  farming  where  a 
 single  crop  of  tea,  coffee,  sugarcane, 
 cashew,  rubber,  banana  or  cotton  are 
 grown.  Major  plantations  -  Rubber  in 
 Malaysia,  coffee  in  Brazil,  tea  in  India  and 
 Sri Lanka. Major food crops - Wheat, rice, 
 maize  and  millets.  Jute  and  cotton  are 
 fibr e crops. 
 Q.423.  Which  of  the  following  is  an 
 example of a terrestrial ecosystem? 
 (a) Ponds  (b) River 
 (c) Grasslands  (d) Oceans 
 Sol.423.(c)  Grasslands.  Terrestrial 
 ecosystem  -  It  is  a  land-based 
 community  of  species  that  includes 
 biotic  and  abiotic  interactions  in  a 
 specific  area.  Examples  -  Forest, 
 Grasslands,  Tundra,  Desert.  Aquatic 
 ecosystem  -  It  is  related  to  water.  It 
 includes  Freshwater  ecosystem  (Pond, 
 lake,  river)  and  Marine  ecosystem 
 (Ocean,  sea  or  estuary).  Kelp  is  a  type  of 
 large,  brown  seaweed  that  grows  in 
 shallow,  nutrient-rich  saltwater  near 
 coastal fronts around the world. 
 Q.424.  Which of the following 
 statements is incorrect? 
 Statement  1:  Taiga  forests  are  found  in 
 the  higher  latitudes  (50°–70°)  of  the 
 Northern  Hemisphere  and  are  mainly 
 coniferous. 
 Statement  2:  Tundra  forests  are 
 coniferous  and  found  in  the  higher 
 latitudes of the Northern Hemisphere. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 is incorrect 
 (b) Only Statement 2 is incorrect 
 (c) Both statements are incorrect 
 (d) Neither statement is incorrect 
 Sol.424.(b)  Only  Statement  2  is 
 incorrect.  Taiga  -  Trees  found  are  mainly 
 cone-bearing  trees  like  spruce,  pine,  fir , 
 and  hemlock.  Veld  -  Open,  uncultivated 
 country  or  grassland  in  southern  Africa. 
 Tundra  -  A  vast,  flat,  treeless  Arctic 
 region  of  Europe,  Asia,  and  North 
 America  in  which  the  subsoil  is 
 permanently  frozen.  Llanos  -  A  vast 
 tropical  grassland  plain  situated  to  the 
 east  of  the  Andes  in  Colombia  and 
 Venezuela,  in  northwestern  South 
 America. 
 Q.425.  In  India,  trees  of  which  forest  type 
 shed  their  leaves  for  about  six  to  eight 
 weeks in dry summer? 
 (a) Tropical Deciduous Forests 
 (b) Tropical Evergreen Forests 
 (c) Montane Forests 
 (d) Tropical Thorn Forests and Scrubs 
 Sol.425.(a) Tropical Deciduous Forests. 
 These forests spread over regions which 
 receive  rainfall  between  70-200  cm. 
 Tendu,  palas,  amaltas,  bel,  khair, 
 axlewood,  etc  are  the  common  trees  of 
 these forests. 
 Q.426.  In  India,  what  is  the  mean  annual 
 average  temperature  in  subtropical 
 vegetation zone ? 
 (a) Above 24° C  (b) 7°C to 17°C 
 (c) Below 7°C  (d) 17°C to 24°C 
 Sol.426.(d)  17°C  to  24°C.  Natural 
 vegetation  -  The  plant  life  that  grows  in 
 an  area  without  the  influence  of  human 
 activity.  Types  -  Forests,  Grasslands 
 (divided  into  savannas,  steppes  and 
 prairies,  depending  on  the  climate  and 
 other  environmental  factors),  Deserts 
 (These  are  arid  regions  with  very  little 
 rainfall  and  sparse  vegetation),  Tundra 
 (This  is  a  cold,  treeless  region  found  in 
 the  Arctic  and  on  high  mountain  slopes), 
 Wetland  (These  are  areas  with 
 water-logged soil, such as marshes). 
 Q.427.  Which  of  the  following  pairs  of 
 countries - name of grassland is correct? 
 I. Argentina - Pampas, II. Brazil - Steppe 
 (a) Neither I nor II  (b) Only II 
 (c) Only I  (d) Both I and II 
 Sol.427.(c)  Only  I.  Steppe  -  Europe  and 
 North  Asia,  Brazil  -  Campos,  Pustaz- 
 Hungary,  Prairies  -  USA,  Veld  -  South 
 Africa,  Downs  -  Australia,  Canterbury  - 
 New  Zealand,  Savannah  -  Africa,  Taiga  - 
 Europe and Asia. 
 Q.428.  The  wet  temperate  type  of  forests 
 are  found  between  a  height  of  _______ 
 metres. 
 (a) 2000 and 3000    (b) 1500 and 3000 
 (c) 1000 and 2000    (d) 500 to 1000 
 Sol.428.(c)  1000  and  2000.  Wet 
 temperate  forests:  Average  annual 
 rainfall  and  temperature  in  wet  temperate 
 forests  are  150-300  cm  and  11°C-14°C 
 respectively.  The  relative  humidity  is  over 
 80%  on  average.  Oak  trees  and  chestnut 
 trees  are  found  here.  Other  important 
 species  include  Chilauni,  Indian  chestnut, 
 Deodar,  Machilus,  Cinnamomum,  Litsea, 
 Plum, Blue pine, Birch, Oak, Hemlock, etc. 
 Q.429.  Thorny  bushes  are  found  in  _____ 
 region. 
 (a) mediterranean      (b) polar 
 (c) dry desert  (d) high rainfall 
 Sol.429.(c)  dry  desert  .  In  areas  where 
 there  is  less  than  70  cm  of  rainfall  ,  thorn 
 forests  and  shrubs  are  found  in  the 
 natural  vegetation.  This  type  of 
 vegetation  is  found  in  the  north-western 
 parts  of  the  country,  which  include  the 
 semi-arid  regions  of  Gujarat,  Rajasthan, 
 Chhattisgarh,  Madhya  Pradesh,  Uttar 
 Pradesh and Haryana. 
 Q.430.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about forests: 
 Statement  I:  Temperate  evergreen 
 forests  are  mainly  located  in  the 
 mid-latitudinal  coastal  regions  such  as 
 southeastern  USA,  southern  China,  and 
 southeastern Brazil. 
 Statement  II:  Tropical  rainforests  are 
 mainly  located  in  the  mid-latitudinal 
 coastal regions. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I                       (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II           (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.430.(a)  Only  I.  Temperate  evergreen 
 forests  are  mainly  located  in  the 
 mid-latitudinal  coastal  regions.  Tropical 
 rainforests  are  mainly  located  in  the 
 tropics,  between  the  latitudes  of  23.5°N 
 (the  Tropic  of  Cancer)  and  23.5°S  (the 
 Tropic of Capricorn)—the tropics. 
 Q.431.  The  birds  like  pheasant  and 
 monals  are  found  in  which  type  of 
 forest? 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (a) Temperate deciduous forest 
 (b) Tropical evergreen forest 
 (c) Temperate evergreen forest 
 (d) Mediterranean forests 
 Sol.431.(a) Temperate deciduous forest. 
 As  we  go  towards  higher  latitudes,  there 
 are  more  temperate  deciduous  forests. 
 These  are  found  in  the  north  eastern  part 
 of  the  USA,  China,  New  Zealand,  Chile 
 and  also  found  in  the  coastal  regions  of 
 Western  Europe.  They  shed  their  leaves 
 in  the  dry  season.  Common  trees  include 
 oak,  ash,  and  beech.  Animals  commonly 
 found  in  these  forests  are  deer,  foxes, 
 and wolves. 
 Q.432.  A  species  of  grass  named  ______

 Sunflower). 
 Q.433.  What  type  of  forests  are  found

 Q.434.  Which  type  of  biome  is  located  in 
 Eastern  North  America,  Western  Europe, 
 and Northeast Asia? 
 (a) Tropical Rainforest Biomes 
 (b) Coniferous Forest Biomes 
 (c) Aquatic Biomes 
 (d) Deciduous Forest Biomes 
 Sol.434.(d)  Deciduous  Forest  Biomes  - 
 This  biome  experiences  all  four  seasons, 
 these  forests  are  divided  into  fiv e  zones 
 based  on  the  heights  of  the  trees.  The 
 soil  is  particularly  fertile  and  nutrient-rich 
 and  is  located  in  North  America  (Canada, 
 the  United  States,  and  Mexico),  Europe, 
 and  western  regions  of  Asia  (Japan, 
 China,  North  Korea,  South  Korea,  and 
 parts of Russia). 
 Q.435.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  Social  Forestry  in 
 India: 
 Statement I:  The National Commission 
 on  Agriculture  (1976)  classified  Social 
 Forestry into 5 categories. 
 Statement  II:  The  motto  of  the  social 
 forestry  is  good  management  and 
 protection  of  the  forest  with  the 
 development of the environment. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only I                        (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II             (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.435.(a)  Only  I.  The  National 
 Commission  on  Agriculture  (1976) 
 classified  Social  Forestry  into  3 
 categories  -  Urban  forestry,  Rural  forestry 
 and  Farm  forestry.  Social  forestry  means 
 the  management  and  protection  of 
 forests  and  afforestation  on  barren  lands 
 with  the  purpose  of  helping  in  the 
 environmental,  social  and  rural 
 development. 
 Q.436.  Which  of  the  following  statements 
 about forests is INCORRECT? 
 (a) In a forest, trees form the uppermost 
 layer of vegetation. 
 (b) Forests protect the soil from erosion. 
 (c) In a forest, shrubs form the lowest 
 layer of vegetation. 
 (d) Forests influence climate, water cycle 
 and air quality. 
 Sol.436.(c)  The layer of vegetation in the 
 forest which is just below the shrub layer 
 is  called  the  herb  layer.  The  herb  layer 
 consists  mainly  of  herbs,  ferns  and 
 grasses.  This  layer  is  the  lowest  layer  of 
 vegetation  in  the  forest  (having  leafy 
 plants).  Very  little  sunlight  remains  for 
 the plants in the herb layer. 
 Q.437.  Coringa  is  a  beautiful  _____  forest 
 where  the  Godavari  joins  the  backwaters 
 of Bay of  Bengal. 
 (a) evergreen  (b) coniferous 
 (c) mangrove  (d) deciduous 
 Sol.437.(c)  Mangrove.  Coringa  is  the 
 second  largest  mangrove  formation  in 
 India,  next  to  Sundarbans.  The  Coringa 
 mangroves  are  bordered  on  the  northern 
 side by Kakinada Bay. 
 Q.438  .  Which  of  the  following  trees  will 
 you find in the Littoral forests? 
 (a) Chinar  (b) Ebony 
 (c) Sundari  (d) Cypress 
 Sol.438.(c)  Sundari  tree  is  found  in 
 Sundarbans  delta  in  West  Bengal  in 
 Mangrove forest (Littoral forests). 
 Climate 
 Q.439.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT  a 
 primary  factor  affecting  the  variation  of 
 insolation on Earth? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Presence of water bodies in an area 
 (b) Rotation of the Earth on its axis 
 (c) Transparency of the atmosphere 
 (d) Angle of inclination of the Sun's rays 
 Sol.439.(a)  Presence  of  water  bodies  in 
 an  area.  Insolation  refers  to  the  incoming 
 solar  radiation  received  on  Earth’s 
 surface.  Its  primary  variation  depends  on 
 factors  like  the  rotation  of  the  Earth  on 
 its  axis,  which  causes  day  and  night;  the 
 angle  of  inclination  of  the  Sun’s  rays, 
 which  changes  with  latitude  and 
 seasons;  and  the  transparency  of  the 
 atmosphere,  which  determines  how 
 much  radiation  penetrates  through 
 clouds,  dust,  and  gases.  The  presence  of 
 water  bodies,  however,  influences  the 
 distribution  and  moderation  of 
 temperature  rather  than  the  actual 
 amount of insolation received. 
 Q.440.  The  highest  rainfall  in  India  gets 
 recorded  in  the  Khasi  and  Jaintia  Hills.  In 
 which state will you find these hills? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Meghalaya                      (b) Nagaland 
 (c) Arunachal Pradesh        (d) Assam 
 Sol.440.(a)  Meghalaya.  The  Khasi  and 
 Jaintia  Hills  are  part  of  the 
 Garo-Khasi-Jaintia  range,  which  forms 
 the  central  and  eastern  parts  of  the  state 
 of  Meghalaya.  These  hills  are  renowned 
 for  receiving  some  of  the  highest  rainfall 
 in  the  world.  Their  unique  funnel-like 
 topography  traps  the  moisture-laden 
 winds  from  the  Bay  of  Bengal  branch  of 
 the  Southwest  Monsoon,  forcing  them  to 
 rise  and  shed  their  moisture  as  extremely 
 heavy  orographic  rainfall.  Mawsynram 
 and  Cherrapunji  (Sohra),  the  two  wettest 
 places  on  Earth,  are  located  in  the  Khasi 
 Hills. 
 Q.441.  Tropical  cyclones  originate  over 
 which of the following regions? 
 (a) Bay of Bengal, Arabian Sea and the 
 Indian Ocean 
 (b) Mediterranean Sea, Pacific Ocean and 
 the Indian Ocean 
 (c) Bay of Bengal, Arabian Sea and the 
 Atlantic Ocean 
 (d) Mediterranean Sea, Arabian Sea and 
 the Indian Ocean 
 Sol.441.(a)  A  tropical  cyclone  is  formed 
 over  warm  ocean  waters  near  the 
 equator.  The  centre  of  a  cyclone  is  very 
 calm  and  clear  with  very  low  air  pressure. 
 The  average  speed  is  120  kmph.  They 
 move  from  east  to  west  under  the 
 influence of trade winds. 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Q.442.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  regarding  orographic  rainfall 
 is correct? 
 (a) It occurs mainly during summer and 
 is common over equatorial doldrums 
 in theCongo basin. 
 (b) This type of precipitation occurs 
 when warm, humid air strikes on a 
 mountain range. 
 (c) This precipitation is heavy but of 
 short duration, highly localised and is 
 associated with minimum amount of 
 cloudiness. 
 (d) This type of precipitation is 
 characterised by seasonal reversal of 
 winds that carry oceanic moisture. 
 Sol.442.(b)  Orographic  rainfall  occurs 
 when  moist  air  hits  a  mountain  and  is 
 forced  to  rise.  As  the  air  ascends,  it 
 cools,  leading  to  condensation  and 
 saturation.  The  excess  moisture  then 
 falls  as  precipitation  on  the  windward 
 slopes. 
 Q.443.  The  climatic  diversity  of  India  is 
 characterised  by  its  distinctiveness,  and 
 one  of  the  following  factors  does  NOT 
 contribute to it is ___________. 
 (a) longitudinal extent 
 (b) latitudinal extent 
 (c) topography 
 (d) vegetation 
 Sol.443.(b)  The  latitudinal  extent 
 influences  temperature  and  rainfall 
 patterns  due  to  variations  in  solar  energy 
 and  atmospheric  density.  However,  it  is 
 not  a  major  factor  contributing  to  India's 
 climatic  diversity  compared  to 
 longitudinal  extent,  topography,  and 
 vegetation. 
 Q.444.  Which  state  lies  in  the  rain 
 shadow region of the Arabian Sea branch 
 of the southwest monsoon? 
 (a) Tamil Nadu  (b) Gujarat 
 (c) Goa  (d) Kerala 
 Sol.444.(a)  Tamil  Nadu.  The  Bay  of 
 Bengal  monsoon  branch  strikes 
 Myanmar  and  southeast  Bangladesh,  but 
 the  Arakan  Hills  deflect  much  of  it 
 towards  India.  It  enters  West  Bengal  and 
 Bangladesh  from  the  south  -  southeast, 
 splitting  into  two  branches.  One  moves 
 westward  along  the  Ganga  plains  to  the 
 Punjab  plains,  while  the  other  ascends 
 the  Brahmaputra  valley,  causing  rains  in 
 the  northeast.  A  sub-branch  hits 
 Meghalaya's  Khasi  hills.  The  Tamil  Nadu 
 coast  remains  dry  as  it  lies  parallel  to  the 
 Bay  of  Bengal  monsoon  branch  and  in 
 the  rain-shadow  region  of  the  Arabian 
 Sea branch. 
 Q.445.  Match  the  following  hot  winds 
 with their respective regions: 
 List I (Hot Winds)  List II (Regions) 
 a. Loo 
 i. North Africa & 
 South Europe 
 b. Chinook 
 ii.North and 
 Northwestern India 
 c. Sirocco  iii. Guinea Coast 
 d. Harmattan 
 iv. USA (Rocky 
 Mountain region) 
 (a) a – ii, b – iv, c – i, d – iii 
 (b) a – iii, b – ii, c – i, d – iv 
 (c) a – ii, b – i, c – iv, d – iii 
 (d) a – iv, b – iii, c – ii, d – i 
 Sol.445.(A)  a  –  ii,  b  –  iv,  c  –  i,  d  –  iii.  Loo 
 -  These  are  strong,  gusty,  hot,  dry  winds 
 blowing  during  the  day  over  the  north 
 and  northwestern  India.  In  West  Bengal, 
 similar  storms  are  known  as  ‘Kaal 
 Baisakhi’.  They  blow  in  the  summer 
 season.  Other  hot  winds  -  Foehn 
 (Europe),  ,  Santa  Ana  (USA),  Harmattan 
 (Guinea coast), Khamsin (Egypt). 
 Q.446.  The  climate  of  a  place  is  NOT 
 affected by which of the following? 
 (a) Relief           (b) Type of soil 
 (c) Location     (d) Distance from the sea 
 Sol.446.(b)  Type  of  soil.  Climate  refers 
 to  the  long-term  conditions  of  a  specific 
 region.  The  changes  in  climate  within  a 
 region  are  influenced  by  its  atmospheric 
 and  environmental  conditions.  The  six 
 major  factors  that  influence  the  climate 
 of  a  location  are  latitude,  altitude, 
 pressure,  and  wind  systems,  distance 
 from  the  sea  (continentality),  ocean 
 currents, and relief features. 
 Q.447.  Which  region  in  India  receives 
 less  rainfall  in  summer,  but  heavy  rainfall 
 in  winter  season  due  to  the  retreating 
 north easterly winds? 
 (a) Southern coastal region 
 (b) Western coastal region 
 (c) Northern coastal region 
 (d) Eastern coastal region 
 Sol.447.(d)  Eastern  coastal  region 
 stretches  from  West  Bengal  in  the  north 
 to  Tamil  Nadu  in  the  south  and  passes 
 through  Andhra  Pradesh  and  Odisha.  The 
 retreating  or  North-East  monsoon  brings 
 heavy  rainfall  to  the  Coromandel  coast 
 from  October  to  December.  This 
 monsoon,  blowing  from  land  to  sea, 
 gathers  moisture  and  causes  rain, 
 making  it  the  primary  rainy  season  for 
 southeastern  India,  including  Tamil 
 Nadu.  The  Coromandel  coast  lies 
 between Pulicat Lake and Kanyakumari. 
 Q.448.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding pre-monsoon rains 
 in India: 
 Statement  I:  Mango  showers  are 
 pre-monsoon  rains  that  occur  in  Kerala, 
 Karnataka, and Tamil Nadu. 
 Statement  II:  Kalbaisakhi,  also  known  as 
 Nor’westers,  are  violent  thunderstorms 
 occurring  during  summer  in  West  Bengal 
 and Assam. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I                       (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II            (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.448.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  Mango  showers 
 are  pre-monsoon  rains  that  occur  at  the 
 end  of  the  summer  season,  helping  the 
 mangoes  ripen;  These  are  also  called 
 April rains. 
 Q.449.  The tropical cyclones in Australia 
 are known as ________. 
 (a) hurricanes  (b) willy-willies 
 (c) cyclones  (d) typhoons 
 Sol.449.(b)  willy-willies.  Tropical 
 cyclones  are  intense  low-pressure  areas 
 that  form  in  the  atmosphere  between  30° 
 N  and  30°  S  latitudes.  They  can  extend 
 up  to  1,000  km  horizontally  and  12–14 
 km  vertically  from  the  surface.  Tropical 
 cyclones  rotate  counterclockwise  in  the 
 Northern  Hemisphere  and  clockwise  in 
 the  Southern  Hemisphere.  Their  names 
 in  different  countries  :  Hurricanes  - 
 United  States,  Typhoons  -  China  Sea  and 
 Pacific Ocean, Cyclones  - Indian Ocean. 
 Q.450.  What  is  the  duration  of  monsoon 
 in India? 
 (a) 100-120 days  (b) 40-60 days 
 (c) 150-200 days  (d) 50-80 days 
 Sol.450.(a)  100-120  days.  The  monsoon 
 season  in  India  lasts  from  June  to 
 September.  The  southwest  monsoon 
 (summer  monsoon)  brings  the  majority 
 of  annual  rainfall,  blowing  from  the  sea 
 to  land  across  the  Indian  Ocean,  Arabian 
 Sea,  and  Bay  of  Bengal.  In  contrast,  the 
 northeast  monsoon  (winter  monsoon) 
 blows from land to sea. 
 Q.451.  Which  of  the  following  group  of 
 months is the coldest in North India? 
 (a) February and March 
 (b) September and October 
 (c) December and January 
 (d) October and November 
 Sol.451.(c)  In  December  and  January, 
 temperatures  decrease  from  south  to 
 north,  with  the  Northern  Plains  averaging 
 10°C  to  15°C,  while  Chennai  on  the 
 eastern  coast  sees  averages  of  24°C  to 
 25°C.  Days  are  warm,  and  nights  are  cold 
 during this period.

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Q.452.  Which  of  the  following  parts  of 
 the  mountain  will  receive  the  most 
 rainfall from moisture-laden winds? 
 (a) Windward side       (b) Leeward side 
 (c) Upward side  (d) Relief side 
 Sol.452.(a)  Windward  side  -  The 
 on-shore  moisture  laden  winds  are 
 forced  to  rise  after  striking  against  the 
 mountains  and  give  heavy  rainfall  on  the 
 windward  side.  Leeward  side  -  It  receives 
 little  to  no  rainfall  and  is  known  as  the 
 rain  shadow  area.  Orographic  rainfall 
 occurs  when  saturated  air  mass  comes 
 across a mountain. 
 Q.453.  Which  theory  describes  the 
 collective  effects  of  changes  in  Earth's 
 movements  on  its  climate  over 
 thousands of years? 
 (a) Milankovitch theory 
 (b) Copernicus' heliocentric theory 
 (c) Plate tectonics theory 
 (d) Continental drift theory 
 Sol.453.(a) Milankovitch theory. 
 Copernican  heliocentrism,  proposed  by 
 Copernicus  in  1543,  positions  the  Sun  at 
 the  center,  with  Earth  and  other  planets 
 orbiting  it.  Plate  tectonics  is  a  scientific 
 theory  that  explains  how  major 
 landforms  are  created  as  a  result  of 
 Earth's subterranean movements. 
 Q.454.  Which  of  the  following  groups  of 
 places  in  India  has  little  differences  in 
 day and night temperatures? 
 (a) Chennai and Thiruvananthapuram 
 (b) Jaipur and Jaisalmer 
 (c) Bhopal and Indore 
 (d) Jammu and Kashmir 
 Sol.454.(a)  Chennai  and 
 Thiruvananthapuram.  Thar  desert 
 experiences  the  highest  diurnal  range  of 
 temperature.  This  is  because  of  the  fact 
 that  sand  (found  in  ample  quantity  in  this 
 region)  gains  and  loses  heat  very  quickly. 
 As  a  result  of  this  phenomenon,  there  is 
 a  wide  difference  between  day  and  night 
 temperatures  in  this  region.  The  day 
 temperature  may  rise  to  50°C  and  drop 
 down  to  near  15°C  the  same  night.  There 
 is  no  sea  closer  to  this  area  and  hence 
 there is no moderating effect. 
 Q.455.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  about  climate  is 
 INCORRECT? 
 (a) India’s climate has characteristics of 
 tropical as well as subtropical 
 climates. 
 (b) Coastal areas of India experience 
 less contrasts in temperature 
 conditions as compared to the 
 interior of the country. 
 (c) ‘Monsoon’ type of climate is found 
 mainly in south and southeast Asia. 
 (d) India experiences comparatively 
 stronger winters as compared to 
 central Asia. 
 Sol.455.(d)  Central  Asia  generally 
 experiences  stronger  winters  compared  to 
 India  due  to  its  continental  location  and 
 lack  of  moderating  influences  like  large 
 bodies  of  water.  The  Climate  of  India  is  the 
 tropical  monsoon  climate  type  with 
 regional  variations  in  temperature, 
 precipitation,  and  amount  of  rainfall 
 because of the large geographical area. 
 Q.456.  Western  disturbances  are 
 responsible for: 
 (a) dusty storms in eastern coastal area 
 of India 
 (b) hot winds during summers in 
 northern India 
 (c) extremely chilled weather of north - 
 east India 
 (d) rainfall during winters in north - 
 western part of India 
 Sol.456.(d)  The  western  cyclonic 
 disturbances  which  enter  the  Indian 
 subcontinent  from  the  west  and  the 
 northwest  during  the  winter  months, 
 originate  over  the  Mediterranean  Sea  and 
 are  brought  into  India  by  the  westerly  jet 
 stream. 
 Q.457.  According  to  Koeppen's  Scheme 
 of  classification  of  climate,  which  of  the 
 following  characteristics  is  associated 
 with 'Group E' ? 
 (a) The average temperature of the 
 coldest month is –3°C or below 
 (b) The average temperature for all 
 months is below 10°C 
 (c) The average temperature of the 
 coldest month is higher than –3°C 
 but below 18°C 
 (d) The average temperature of the 
 coldest month is 18°C or higher 
 Sol.457.(b)  Climatic  Groups  According  to 
 Koeppen:  A  -  Tropical:  Average 
 temperature  of  the  coldest  month  is  18° 
 C  or  higher.  B  -  Dry  Climates:  Potential 
 evaporation  exceeds  precipitation.  C  - 
 Warm  Temperate:  The  average 
 temperature  of  the  coldest  month  of  the 
 (Mid-latitude)  climates  is  higher  than 
 minus  3°C  but  below  18°C.  D  -  Cold  Snow 
 Forest  Climates:  The  average 
 temperature  of  the  coldest  month  is 
 minus  3°  C  or  below.  H  -  High  Land:  Cold 
 due to elevation. 
 Q.458.  El  Nino  is  a  climate  pattern  that 
 describes the unusual warming of 
 surface waters in which ocean? 
 (a) Indian Ocean  (b) Pacific Ocean 
 (c) Atlantic Ocean  (d) Arctic Ocean 
 Sol.458.(b)  Pacific  Ocean.  El  Nino  is  a 
 complex  weather  system  that  occurs 
 every  3-7  years,  causing  droughts,  floods, 
 and  extreme  weather  globally.  It  involves 
 warm  ocean  currents  off  Peru's  coast  in 
 the  Eastern  Pacific,  affecting  various 
 regions,  including  India.  El  Nino  is  an 
 extension  of  the  warm  equatorial  current 
 that  temporarily  replaces  the  cold 
 Humboldt  (Peruvian)  Current,  increasing 
 water temperatures by 10°C. 
 Q.459.  In  the  context  of  local  storms  of 
 hot weather season, Nor'Westers are: 
 (a) the pre-monsoon showers that are a 
 common phenomenon in Kerala and 
 coastal areas of Karnataka 
 (b) the dreaded evening thunderstorms in 
 Bengal and Assam 
 (c) the hot, dry and oppressing winds 
 blowing in the Northern plains from 
 Punjab to Bihar with higher intensity 
 between Delhi and Patna 
 (d) a nearby wind that blows coffee 
 flowers in Kerala 
 Sol.459.(b)  Nor Westers : Their notorious 
 nature can be understood from the local 
 nomenclature of ‘Kalbaisakhi’, a calamity 
 of  the  month  of  Baisakh.  These  showers 
 are  useful  for  tea,  jute  and  rice 
 cultivation.  In  Assam,  these  storms  are 
 known  as  “Bardoli  Chheerha”.  Some 
 Famous  Local  Storms:  Blossom  Shower: 
 Useful  for  coffee  flowers  blooming  in 
 Kerala.  Loo  :  Hot,  dry  and  oppressing 
 winds blowing in the Northern plains. 
 Q.460.  Which  of  the  following  States  / 
 Union  Territories  of  India  receives  the 
 highest average annual rainfall ? 
 (a) Rajasthan  (b) Ladakh 
 (c) Meghalaya  (d) Delhi 
 Sol.460.(c)  Meghalaya.  The  maximum 
 rainfall  of  this  season  is  received  in  the 
 north-eastern  part  of  the  country. 
 Mawsynram  in  the  southern  ranges  of 
 the  Khasi  Hills  receives  the  highest 
 average  rainfall  in  the  world. 
 Mawsynram,  the  wettest  place  on  the 
 earth  is  also  reputed  for  its  stalagmite 
 and  stalactite  caves.  Rainfall  in  the 
 Ganga  valley  decreases  from  the  east  to 
 the  west.  Rajasthan  and  parts  of  Gujarat 
 get scanty rainfall. 
 Q.461.  One  term  for  the  continuous 
 movement  of  water  through  the 
 lithosphere,  atmosphere  and 
 hydrosphere is: 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (a) hydro equilibrium cycle 
 (b) hydrological cycle 
 (c) hydrostatical equilibrium 
 (d) water circulation 
 Sol.461.(b) hydrological cycle. 
 Components  of  the  Hydrological  Cycle  : 
 Evaporation  -  Water  changes  from  liquid 
 to gas, primarily from bodies of water. 
 Q.462.  Identify  a  type  of  season  that  is 
 NOT a part of the four seasons of India. 
 (a) The southeast monsoon season 
 (b) The southwest monsoon season 
 (c) The hot weather season 
 (d) The cold weather season 
 Sol.462.(a)  The  southeast  monsoon 
 season.  Four  main  seasons  can  be 
 identified  in  India  :  The  hot  weather 
 season  (March  to  May),  The  southwest 
 monsoon  season  (June  to  September), 
 The  post  -  monsoon  or  retreating 
 monsoon  season  (October  to  December), 
 The  cold  weather  season  (January  to 
 February). 
 Q.463.  What  is  the  name  of  the  cold 
 phase  of  the  ENSO  climate  pattern  that 
 describes  the  unusual  cooling  of  the 
 surface waters of the region ? 
 (a) NINO-3 region  (b) ENSO-neutral 
 (c) Trans-Nino  (d) La Nina 
 Sol.463.(d)  La  Nina.  In  La  Nina,  the 
 tropical  Pacific  trade  winds  become  very 
 strong  and  an  abnormal  accumulation  of 
 cold  water  occurs  in  the  central  and 
 eastern  Pacific  Ocean.  El  Nino  is  a 
 climate  pattern  that  describes  the 
 unusual  warming  of  surface  waters  in 
 the  eastern  tropical  Pacific  Ocean.  El 
 Nino  is  the  “warm  phase”  of  a  larger 
 phenomenon called the El Nino-Southern 
 Oscillation (ENSO). 
 Q.464.  Sea  ice  helps  determine  Earth's 
 climate.  According  to  this,  what 
 percentage  of  the  sunlight  falling  on  sea 
 ice is reflected back into space ? 
 (a) 80%  (b) 70%    (c) 75%    (d) 85% 
 Sol.464.(a)  80%.  Sea  ice  reflects  a 
 significant  portion  of  sunlight,  which  is 
 essential  for  regulating  Earth’s 
 temperature.  As  sea  ice  diminishes,  more 
 solar  energy  is  absorbed  by  the  ocean, 
 resulting  in  higher  temperatures.  This 
 change  affects  global  climate  patterns 
 and  contributes  to  the  acceleration  of 
 climate  change.  Albedo  is  the 
 percentage  of  solar  radiation  reflected  by 
 a given surface. 
 Q.465.  Who  is  best  known  for  his 
 testimony  on  climate  change  to 
 congressional  committees  in  the  1980s, 
 which  helped  raise  wider  awareness  of 
 the global warming issue? 
 (a) James Hansen    (b) Shen Kuo 
 (c) Joseph Fourier    (d) Svante Arrhenius 
 Sol.465.(a)  James  Hansen.  He  is 
 referred  to  as  the  “father  of  global 
 warming”  because  of  his  early  warnings 
 about the phenomenon in the 1980s. 
 Q.466.  According  to  the  Koppen  climate 
 classification  system,  ‘Am’  is  the  code  for 
 which type of climate? 
 (a) Tropical wet and dry climate 
 (b) Tropical monsoon climate 
 (c) Humid subtropical climate 
 (d) Marine west coast climate 
 Sol.466.(b)  Tropical  monsoon  climate. 
 Koeppen’s  Classification  of  Climatic 
 Regions  of  India  is  based  on  mean 
 annual  and  monthly  temperature  and 
 precipitation  data.  He  identified  fiv e 
 major  climatic  groups:  four  based  on 
 temperature  (A,  C,  D,  E  for  humid 
 climates)  and  one  on  precipitation  (B  for 
 dry  climates).  These  groups  are  further 
 divided  into  types,  indicated  by  small 
 letters  that  describe  seasonality:  f  (no 
 dry  season),  m  (monsoon),  w  (winter 
 dry),  and  s  (summer  dry).  Climatic 
 Regions  of  India:  Amw,  As,  Aw,  BShw, 
 BWhw, Cwg, Dfc, Et, E. 
 Q.467.  Assertion  (A):  The  climate  of  a 
 particular  place  is  controlled  by  six  major 
 factors. 
 Reason  (R):  Latitude,  altitude,  pressure 
 and  wind  system,  distance  from  the  sea, 
 ocean  currents,  and  relief  features  are 
 the six main controls of climate. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A 
 (c) A is true, but R is false 
 (d) A is false, but R is true 
 Sol.467.(a)  Both  A  and  R  are  true,  and  R 
 is the correct explanation of A. 
 Q.468.  When  was  the  Indian 
 Meteorological  Department,  which 
 collects  data  on  temperature,  rainfall, 
 wind,  etc.,  and  makes  weather  forecasts, 
 established by the Government of India? 
 (a) 1873  (b) 1879  (c) 1876  (d) 1875 
 Sol.468.(d)  1875.  Indian  Meteorological 
 Department  -  An  agency  of  the  Ministry 
 of  Earth  Sciences  of  the  Government  of 
 India.  It  is  the  principal  agency 
 responsible  for  meteorological 
 observations,  weather  forecasting  and 
 seismology.  Formed  -  15  January  1875. 
 Headquarters  -  New  Delhi.  Regional 
 offices  -  Chennai,  Mumbai,  Kolkata, 
 Nagpur, Guwahati and New Delhi. 
 Q.469.  Choose  the  INCORRECT 
 statement related to monsoon. 
 (a) The intensity and position of the high 
 - pressure area, east of Madagascar, 
 affects the Indian  Monsoon 
 (b) The Southwest Monsoon winds blow 
 over the warm oceans, gather 
 moisture and bring widespread 
 rainfall over the  mainland of India. 
 (c) The difference in pressure over 
 Tahiti (Pacific Ocean), and Darwin in 
 northern Australia (Indian Ocean) is 
 computed to predict the intensity of 
 the monsoons. 
 (d) The western cyclonic disturbances 
 occur due to withdrawal or the retreat 
 of the monsoon 
 Sol.469.(d)  Western  Cyclonic 
 Disturbances  are weather phenomena of 
 the  winter  months  brought  in  by  the 
 westerly  flow  from  the  Mediterranean 
 region.  They  usually  influence  the 
 weather  of  the  north  and  north-western 
 regions  of  India.  It  originates  over  the 
 Mediterranean:  The  region  which 
 includes  the  Mediterranean  Sea  and 
 seven  Member  States,  either  partially 
 (France,  Portugal,  Italy,  Spain)  or 
 completely (Greece, Malta, Cyprus). 
 Q.470.  Which  winds  blow  across  the 
 Asian  continent  at  latitudes  north  of  the 
 Himalayas  roughly  parallel  to  the  Tibetan 
 highlands ? 
 (a) Trade winds          (b) Jet streams 
 (c) Southwest winds (d) Northwest winds 
 Sol.470.(b)  Jet  streams  -  A  narrow  belt 
 of  high  altitude  (above  12,000  m) 
 westerly  winds  in  the  Troposphere.  Their 
 speed  varies  from  about  110  km/h  in 
 summer  to  about  184  km/h  in  winter.  The 
 polar  jet  is  located  between  the  50°-60° 
 latitude  lines  in  both  the  northern  and 
 southern  hemispheres.  The  subtropical 
 jet is located around the 30° latitude line. 
 Q.471.  What  type  of  climate  is  found  in 
 North  Western  Europe,  West  Coast  of 
 North  America,  North  of  California, 
 Southern  Chile,  South-eastern  Australia 
 and New Zealand? 
 (a) Tropical wet and dry climate 
 (b) Cold snow forest climate 
 (c) Marine west coast climate 
 (d) Subtropical steppe climate 
 Sol.471.(c)  Marine  west  coast  climate  - 
 Under  the  Koppen  Climate  Classification, 
 it  comes  under  Warm  Temperate 
 (Mid-Latitude) Climates (Group C) and is 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 represented by ‘Cfb’. 
 Q.472.  Choose  the  INCORRECT 
 statement out of the following. 
 (a) There is an increase in rainfall 
 generally from east to west in the 
 Northern Plains. 
 (b) Most parts of the country receive 
 rainfall from June to September. 
 (c) The annual precipitation in Meghalaya 
 is over 400 cm. 
 (d) Some parts like the Tamil Nadu’s 
 coast gets a large portion of its rains 
 during October and November. 
 Sol.472.(a)  Rainfall  decreases  from  the 
 east  to  the  west  in  Northern  India 
 because  there  is  a  decrease  in  the 
 moisture  content  of  the  winds.  The 
 monsoon  approaches  the  Indian 
 landmass  in  two  branches:  The  Arabian 
 Sea  branch  (The  monsoon  winds 
 originating  over  the  Arabian  Sea)  and  the 
 Bay of Bengal branch. 
 Q.473.  In  which  type  of  rainfall,  winds 
 move  out  from  a  high-pressure  area  with 
 wind  direction  clockwise  in  the  northern 
 hemisphere,  and  anti-clockwise  in  the 
 southern hemisphere ? 
 (a) Cyclones  (b) Convection 
 (c) Anticyclone  (d) Orography 
 Sol.473.(c)  Anticyclone  -  It  is  the  center 
 of  high  pressure,  surrounded  by  closed 
 isobars.  Due  to  Coriolis  force,  blowing 
 winds  are  deflected  from  their  paths  to 
 the  right  in  the  Northern  hemisphere 
 (Clockwise)  and  left  in  the  Southern 
 hemisphere  (Anti  clockwise).  Cyclones 
 that  develop  in  the  mid  and  high 
 latitudes,  beyond  the  tropical  regions,  are 
 referred  to  as  middle  latitude  or 
 extratropical cyclones. 
 Q.474.  According  to  the  Koppen 
 classification,  Which  regions  experience 
 cold humid winters with short summers? 
 (a) South of Goa 
 (b) Western Rajasthan 
 (c) Arunachal Pradesh 
 (d) Northern Madhya Pradesh 
 Sol.474.(c)  Arunachal  Pradesh.  Koeppen 
 identified  a  close  relationship  between 
 the  distribution  of  vegetation  and 
 climate. 
 Q.475.  What  is  the  usual  term  used  to 
 describe the climate of India ? 
 (a) Polar climate  (b) Tropical monsoon 
 (c) Desert climate  (d) Arid climate 
 Sol.475.(b)  Tropical  monsoon  .  A 
 monsoon  is  a  seasonal  change  in  the 
 direction  of  the  prevailing,  or  strongest, 
 winds  of  a  region.  It  always  blows  from 
 cold  to  warm  regions.  The  Indian 
 Meteorological  Department  (IMD) 
 designates  four  official  seasons:  Winter 
 (December  to  early  April);  Summer  or 
 pre-monsoon  (April  to  June);  Monsoon  or 
 rainy  (June  to  September)  and  Post- 
 monsoon (October to December). 
 Q.476.  What is 'Mahawat' in India ? 
 (a) Cold waves during the winter season 
 (b) Cloud burst during the monsoon 
 season 
 (c) Cyclonic rainfall during the winter 
 season 
 (d) Hot and dry winds during the summer 
 season 
 Sol.476.(c)  Mahawat  is  the  total  amount 
 of  rainfall  in  the  cold  weather  season, 
 that  is  the  winter  season  which  is  caused 
 by  the  western  cyclonic  disturbances  in 
 the  north  and  north  -western  part  of 
 India. 
 Q.477.  The  target  for  the  first 
 commitment  period  of  the  Kyoto 
 Protocol  is  to  reduce  emissions  of 
 greenhouse  gasses  by  5.2%  from  1990 
 levels? 
 (a) 6  (b) 10  (c) 8  (d) 7 
 Sol.477.(a)  6.  Kyoto  Protocol:  It  was 
 adopted  on  11  December  1997  and 
 entered  into  force  on  16  February  2005.  It 
 was  adopted  in  Kyoto,  Japan.  It  was  the 
 result  of  the  Rio-1992  and  the  United 
 Nations  Framework  Convention  on 
 Climate  Change  (UNFCCC).  Kyoto 
 Protocol  cover  emissions  of  the  six  main 
 greenhouse  gases  Carbon  dioxide  (CO 
 2 
 ), 
 methane  (CH 
 4 
 ),  nitrous  oxide  (N 
 2 
 O), 
 hydrofluorocarbons  (HFCs), 
 perfluorocarbons  (PFCs),  and  sulfur 
 hexafluoride (SF 
 6 
 ). 
 Q.478.  In  the  context  of  mapping  climate 
 data,  what  term  refers  to  a  line  drawn 
 through  all  points  on  a  map  that  contain 
 the same amount of cloud? 
 (a) Isohels  (b) Isocheim 
 (c) Isonephs  (d) Isochalaz 
 Sol.478.(c)  Isonephs.  Isocheim  -  A  line 
 on  a  map  connecting  points  having  the 
 same  average  temperature  in  winter. 
 Isohel  -  A  line  on  a  map  connecting 
 points  having  the  same  duration  of 
 sunshine.  Isochalaz  -  A  line  of  constant 
 frequency of hail storms. 
 Q.479.  According  to  Koeppen's  Scheme, 
 which  type  of  climate  is  denoted  by  'Cwg' 
 in India? 
 (a) Monsoon with dry winter 
 (b) Semi-arid steppe climate 
 (c) Polar type 
 (d) Monsoon with dry summer 
 Sol.479.(a)  Monsoon  with  dry  winter  -  It 
 is  mostly  found  in  the  Ganga  Plain, 
 eastern  Rajasthan,  Assam,  and  the 
 Malwa  Plateau.  Amw  (Monsoon  type 
 with  a  short  dry  winter  season):  This 
 climate  is  found  in  the  western  coastal 
 region, south of Mumbai. 
 Q.480.  Which  of  the  following  is 
 incorrectly matched? 
 (a) Montane climate - Uttar Pradesh, 
 Bihar and Jharkhand 
 (b) Tropical monsoon climate - The 
 Western Ghats, the Malabar Coast 
 And Southern Assam 
 (c) Tropical semi-arid (steppe) climate - 
 Karnataka, central Maharashtra, some 
 parts of Tamil Nadu and Andhra 
 Pradesh 
 (d) Hot desserts, arid climate - rajasthan 
 and some parts of Gujarat 
 Sol.480.(a)  Montane  climate  -  Found  in 
 Assam,  Nagaland,  Mizoram,  Meghalaya, 
 Manipur,  Arunachal  Pradesh,  Higher  hills 
 of Tamil Nadu. 
 Q.481.  The  centre  of  a  cyclone  is  a  calm 
 area. It is called the_______of the storm. 
 (a) base  (b) focal point 
 (c) eye  (d) trigger point 
 Sol.481.(c)  Eye.  The  eye  of  a  storm  is 
 usually  circular  and  typically  25-40  miles 
 in  diameter.  It  is  surrounded  by  an 
 eyewall,  where  the  most  severe  weather 
 of a cyclone occurs. 
 Q.482.  What  would  happen  if  the 
 difference  in  pressure  in  Tahiti  was 
 negative? 
 (a) Above average late monsoon 
 (b) Drought 
 (c) Below average and late monsoon 
 (d) Above average and early monsoon 
 Sol.482.(c)  The  difference  in  pressure 
 over  Tahiti  (Pacific  Ocean)  and  Darwin  in 
 Northern  Australia  (Indian  Ocean)  is 
 computed  to  predict  the  intensity  of  the 
 monsoons.  Factors  Affecting  Monsoon  - 
 The  shift  of  the  position  of  Inter  Tropical 
 Convergence  Zone  (ITCZ);  The  presence 
 of  the  high-pressure  area,  east  of 
 Madagascar;  Heating  of  The  Tibetan 
 plateau  in  summers;  The  movement  of 
 the  jet  streams;  Southern  Oscillation  (SO) 
 -  Change  of  pressure  between  south 
 Pacific Ocean and eastern Indian Ocean. 
 Q.483.  What  type  of  climate  occurs 
 along  the  west  coast  of  continents  in 
 subtropical  latitudes  between  30°-40° 
 latitudes,  covering  Central  California, 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Central  Chile  along  the  coast  in  South 
 East and South Western Australia? 
 (a) Subtropical steppe climate 
 (b) Humid subtropical climate 
 (c) Mediterranean climate 
 (d) Marine west coast climate 
 Sol.483.(c)  Mediterranean  climate  :  Clear 
 skies  and  high  temperatures;  hot,  dry 
 summers  and  cool,  wet  winters.  The 
 climate  is  not  extreme  because  of 
 cooling  from  water  bodies.  This  unique 
 seasonal  pattern  makes  it  ideal  for 
 growing crops like olives, grapes, and 
 citrus fruits. 
 Q.484.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  the  climate  of  the 
 hills of the Western Ghats: 
 Statement  I:  The  average  temperature  in 
 the  hills  of  the  Western  Ghats  is  usually 
 below 25°C. 
 Statement  II:  Western  Ghats  are  intervein 
 by  major  rivers  like  the  Godavari,  Krishna 
 and Pennar. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I                        (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II            (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.484.(a)  Only  I.  Western  Ghats  are 
 known  as  the  Sahyadri  Hills.  It  covers  six 
 states  -  Gujarat,  Maharashtra,  Goa, 
 Karnataka,  Tamil  Nadu,  and  Kerala.  The 
 Eastern  Ghats  are  intervein  by  major 
 rivers  like  the  Godavari,  Krishna  and 
 Pennar. 
 Q.485.  Which  winds  are  also  known  by 
 the name of 'Roaring Forties' ? 
 (a) Polar easterlies    (b) westerly winds 
 (c) Polar winds           (d) Trade winds 
 Sol.485.(b)  Westerly  Wind  .  The  polar 
 easterlies  are  one  of  the  fiv e  primary 
 wind  zones,  known  as  wind  belts,  that 
 make  up  our  atmosphere's  circulatory 
 system.  Polar  winds  begin  near  the  North 
 and  South  Poles.  The  trade  winds  are  air 
 currents  closer  to  Earth's  surface  that 
 blow from east to west near the equator. 
 Q.486.  Which  among  the  following  wind 
 is  associated  with  extreme  cold  and 
 dryness? 
 (a) Monsoon Winds   (b) Westerlies 
 (c) Trade Winds  (d) Polar Easterlies 
 Sol.486.(d)  Polar  Easterlies  .  They 
 emanate  from  the  polar  highs,  areas  of 
 high  pressure  around  the  North  and 
 South  Poles.  They  flow  to  low-pressure 
 areas  in  sub-polar  regions.  Easterlies 
 (blow  from  the  east)  -  It  is  the  name  of 
 the  permanent  winds  that  come  from  the 
 direction  of  their  origin.  Monsoon  Winds  - 
 They  are  caused  when  the  air  over  land 
 gets  heated  and  rises,  causing  winds  to 
 blow  from  the  ocean  toward  land. 
 Westerlies  -  Prevailing  winds  from  the 
 west  toward  the  east  in  the  middle 
 latitudes. 
 Q.487.  The  summer  months  experience 
 __________ in the northern part of India. 
 (a) rising air pressure 
 (b) falling air pressure 
 (c) decreasing temperature 
 (d) heavy rain 
 Sol.487.(b)  Falling  air  pressure.  An 
 elongated  low-pressure  area  develops  in 
 the  region  extending  from  the  Thar 
 Desert  in  the  northwest  to  Patna  and 
 Chotanagpur  plateau  in  the  east  and 
 southeast  towards  the  end  of  May. 
 Circulation  of  air  begins  to  set  in  around 
 this  trough.  InterTropical  Convergence 
 Zone  (ITCZ)  -  Low  pressure  region 
 around which the southeast trade winds 
 and the northeast trade winds converge. 
 Q.488.  The  day  to  day  condition  of  the 
 atmosphere  at  a  place  with  respect  to 
 the  temperature,  humidity,  rainfall,  wind 
 speed etc. is called _______ at that place. 
 (a) climate  (b) weather 
 (c) environment  (d) ecology 
 Sol.488.(b)  Weather.  Climate  -  It  is  the 
 long-term  average  of  weather  conditions, 
 including  temperature,  humidity, 
 precipitation,  wind  patterns,  and  other 
 atmospheric  elements,  in  a  particular 
 region  over  an  extended  period,  typically 
 30 years or more. 
 Q.489  . Which of the following pressure 
 belts is known as the horse latitude? 
 (a) Equatorial Low Pressure 
 (b) Sub - Polar Low Pressure 
 (c) High Pressure 
 (d) Sub - Tropical High Pressure 
 Sol.489.(d)  Subtropical  high  pressure 
 belts  (Horse  latitude)  extend  30°  N  and 
 30°  S.  It  is  formed  because  the  warm  air 
 rising  from  the  equator  moves  towards 
 the  tropics  in  both  hemispheres. 
 Equatorial  Low  -  Pressure  belt  extends 
 from  0  to  5°  North  and  South.  The 
 subpolar  low  pressure  belt  extends 
 around  latitudes  60°  to  70°  North  and 
 South.  High  Pressure  belts  are  located  at 
 70°-  90°  North  and  South  latitudes.  There 
 are  majorly  seven  Pressure  Belts  on  the 
 surface  of  Earth  i.e.,  the  Equatorial  Low, 
 two  Subtropical  Highs,  two  Subpolar 
 Lows, and two Polar Highs. 
 Q.490.  When  the  grains  of  sand  are  very 
 fine  and  light  ,  the  wind  can  carry  it  over  a 
 very  long  distance  .  When  such  sand  is 
 deposited in large areas, it is called_____. 
 (a) loess  (b) moraines 
 (c) mushroom  (d) stacks 
 Sol.490.(a)  Loess.  It  is  a  clastic, 
 predominantly  silt-sized  sediment  that  is 
 formed  by  the  accumulation  of  wind  - 
 blown dust. 
 Q.491.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  about  the  main  reasons  for 
 the  excessive  cold  in  north  India  during 
 the  cold  weather  season  and  identify 
 which of the statements are correct. 
 1. 
 States  like  Punjab,  Haryana  and 
 Rajasthan,  being  far  away  from  the 
 moderating  influence  of  sea, 
 experience continental climate. 
 2. 
 The  snowfall  in  the  nearby  Himalayan 
 ranges creates a cold wave situation. 
 3. 
 Around  February,  the  cold  winds 
 coming  from  the  Caspian  Sea  and 
 Turkmenistan  bring  a  cold  wave  along 
 with  frost  and  fog  over  the 
 northwestern parts of India. 
 (a) 1 and 2 only  (b) 2 and 3 only 
 (c) 1, 2 and 3  (d) 1 and 3 only 
 Sol.491.(c)  1,  2  and  3.  Coastal  areas 
 have  moderate  temperatures  because 
 the  land  absorbs  and  radiates  heat  much 
 faster  than  sea.  So,  during  the  day  cool 
 air  from  the  sea  rushes  towards  the  land 
 and  during  the  night  cool  air  from  the 
 land rushes towards the sea. 
 Q.492.  Low  annual  range  of  temperature 
 and  high  annual  rainfall  are 
 characteristics of a _________. 
 (a) Mediterranean climate 
 (b) temperate climate 
 (c) tropical humid climate 
 (d) cold climate 
 Sol.492.(c)  Tropical  Humid  Climate:  Low 
 range  of  temperature,  Rainfall  exceeds 
 evapotranspiration  for  at  least  270  days 
 in  a  year,  between  Tropic  of  Cancer  and 
 Capricorn. 
 Q.493.  Which  of  the  following  codes 
 signify  Cold  Climate  with  Dry  Winters 
 according to Koeppen’s classification? 
 (a) EF       (b) Df       (c) ET       (d) Dw 
 Sol.493.(d)  Dw.  Koeppen  recognised  six 
 major  climatic  groups  based  on 
 seasonal  precipitation  and  temperature 
 patterns.  The  six  main  groups  are  A 
 (tropical),  B  (arid),  C  (temperate),  D 
 (continental), E (polar) and H (highland).

 and North America 
 (a) 40°- 70°  (b) 30°- 50°

 Q.495.  In  early  November,  the  low 
 Pressure  conditions  shift  over  the  Bay  of

 Q.496.  Name  the  port  that  is  privately 
 owned by the Adani Group? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Visakhapatnam Port, Andhra Pradesh 
 (b) Chennai Port, Tamil Nadu 
 (c) Kandla Port, Gujarat 
 (d) Mundra Port, Gujarat 
 Sol.496.(d)  Mundra  Port,  Gujarat. 
 Mundra  Port,  located  in  Gujarat,  is  India’s 
 largest  private  commercial  port,  owned 
 and  operated  by  the  Adani  Ports  and 
 Special  Economic  Zone  (APSEZ)  under 
 the  Adani  Group.  It  handles  bulk, 
 container,  and  liquid  cargo,  and  has 
 emerged  as  a  key  hub  for  India’s 
 international  trade,  unlike  other 
 government-owned major ports. 
 Q.497.  Select  the  INCORRECT  statement 
 from the following. 
 (a) The development of the cotton textile 
 industry in Britain led to the decline 
 of Indian textile producers. 
 (b) Cotton clothes made in England had 
 driven Indian clothes out of the 
 markets of Europe and America in 
 the beginning of the 20th century. 
 (c) The Sale and Devang communities of 
 South India were famous for weaving. 
 (d) The first cotton textile mill in India 
 was established in Bombay in 1854. 
 Sol.497.(b)  By  the  beginning  of  the  20th 
 century,  India  was  not  competing  in 
 European  and  American  markets 
 because  British  policies  had  already 
 eliminated  Indian  textile  exports  to  these 
 regions  by  the  19th  century.  The  decline 
 of  Indian  textiles  in  international  markets 
 occurred  much  earlier,  during  the  late 
 18th  and  19th  centuries,  following  the 
 Industrial Revolution in Britain. 
 Q.498.  Identify  the  oldest  iron  and  steel 
 company  of  India  from  the  following 
 options. 
 (a) Tata Iron & Steel Company (TISCO) 
 (b) Visvesvaraiya Iron & Steel Works 
 (c) Indian Iron & Steel Company (IISCO) 
 (d) Mysore Iron & Steel Works 
 Sol.498.(a)  Tata  Iron  and  Steel  Company 
 (TISCO)  :  Founded  by  Jamsetji  Tata  and 
 Dorabji  Tata  in  1907,  It  is  located  in 
 Jamshedpur  (Jharkhand).  Visvesvaraya 
 Iron  and  Steel  Plant:  Founded  in  1923, 
 this  plant  is  situated  in  Bhadravathi 
 (Karnataka),  and  specializes  in  the 
 production  of  pig  iron  and  steel.  Indian 
 Iron  and  Steel  Company  (IISCO): 
 Established  in  1918,  IISCO  is  an 
 integrated  steel  plant  located  in  Burnpur 
 (West  Bengal)  and  is  part  of  Steel 
 Authority of India Limited (SAIL). 
 Q.499.  Identify  the  group  of  districts  that 
 are  NOT  an  example  of  industrial 
 districts of India. 
 (a) Cuttack and Kota 
 (b) Kanpur and Agra 
 (c) Alwar and Bhiwani 
 (d) Jabalpur and Bhopal 
 Sol.499.(c)  Alwar  and  Bhiwani.  Cuttack 
 (Odisha)  is  known  for  textile  and 
 handicraft  industries,  especially  the  silver 
 filigr ee  (Tarakasi)  work.  Kota  (Rajasthan) 
 is  famous  for  its  engineering  and 
 chemical  industries.  Kanpur  is  a 
 significant  industrial  hub,  historically 
 known  for  its  leather  and  textile 
 industries.  It  is  sometimes  called  the 
 "Manchester  of  the  East."  Agra  is  widely 
 known  for  its  tourism,  but  it  also  has 
 industries  related  to  leather  goods, 
 footwear,  and  marble  stone  carving. 
 Jabalpur  (Madhya  Pradesh)  is  known  for 
 ordnance  factories,  defense-related 
 industries,  and  manufacturing  units. 
 Bhopal  (Madhya  Pradesh)  is  a  major 
 industrial  center  with  industries  like 
 electrical  goods,  chemicals,  and  the 
 famous Bharat Heavy Electricals Limited 
 (BHEL). 
 Q.500.  Consider  the  following 
 statements about industries in India: 
 Statement  I:  BHEL  (Bharat  Heavy 
 Electricals  Limited)  is  a  public  sector 
 industry  established  in  1964, 
 headquartered in New Delhi. 
 Statement  II:  Reliance,  Tata,  and  Adani 
 Groups are public sector industries. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I                        (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II             (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.500.(a)  Only  I.  Reliance  Industries 
 Limited  is  an  Indian  multinational 
 company  based  in  Mumbai, 
 Maharashtra.  It  was  founded  by 
 Dhirubhai  Ambani.  The  Tata  Group  is  one 
 of  India's  oldest  and  largest  business 
 conglomerates.  It  was  founded  in  1868 
 by  Jamsetji  Tata.  The  Adani  Group  is  an 
 Indian  multinational  conglomerate  based 
 in  Ahmedabad,  Gujarat.  It  was  founded  in 
 1988 by Gautam Adani. 
 Q.501.  Which  of  the  following  is  an

 (b) Bhojpur-Munger 
 (c) Jaipur- Ajmer 
 (d) Bilaspur-Korba

 region,  Hugli  region,  Ahmedabad  -Baroda 
 region,  Chottanagpur  industrial  belt, 
 Vishakhapatnam-Guntur  belt,  Gurgaon 
 -Delhi-Meerut  region  and  the  Kollam 
 -Thiruvanathapuram industrial cluster. 
 Q.502.  Where  was  the  first  successful 
 textile mill established in India? 
 (a) Kolkata  (b) Surat 
 (c) Mumbai  (d) Jaipur 
 Sol.502.(c)  Mumbai.  The  first  successful 
 textile  mill  in  India  was  the  Bombay 
 Spinning  and  Weaving  Company, 
 established  in  1854  in  Bombay  (now 
 Mumbai)  by  Cowasjee  Nanabhoy  Davar, 
 a  Parsi  businessman  and  banker. 
 Located  in  Tardeo,  Bombay,  it  began 
 production in 1856. 
 Q.503.  Where  is  the  oldest  centre  of  lace 
 work located in Gujarat? 
 (a) Kutch  (b) Rajkot 
 (c) Jamnagar  (d) Ahmedabad 
 Sol.503.(c)  Jamnagar.  Crafts  Centers  of

 Q.507.  Which  group  of  states  was 
 concentrated  in  the  cotton  production  in 
 the  early  years  to  develop  textile 
 industry? 
 (a) West Bengal and Odisha 
 (b) Punjab and Haryana 
 (c) Maharashtra and Gujarat 
 (d) Bihar and West Bengal 
 Sol.507.(c)  Maharashtra  and  Gujarat. 
 Cotton  is  a  tropical  crop  and  it  is  raised 
 in  India  as  a  Kharif  crop.  Climatic 
 condition  -  Temperatures  between  21 
 degrees  Celsius  and  30  degrees  Celsius. 
 It  grows  best  in  Black  soil.  India  grows 
 both  short  staple  (Indian)  cotton  as  well 
 as long staple (American) cotton called 
 ‘narma’ in north-western parts of the 
 country.

 Q.509.  Consider  the  following

 correct? 
 (a) Only I                          (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II               (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.509.(a)  Only  I.  Digboi  Oilfield  :  One  of 
 the  oldest  oil  fields  in  Asia,  discovered  in 
 1867,  and  home  to  Asia's  oldest  oil 
 refinery  (Established  in  1901).  It  is 
 located  in  Tinsukia  district,  Assam. 
 Naharkatiya  Oilfield  :  Discovered  in  1953, 
 with  oil  production  starting  in  1954.  It  is 
 located  in  Dibrugarh  district,  Assam. 
 Other  Oil  Refineries  in  Assam  -  Noonmati 
 Refinery,  Numaligarh  Refinery,  and 
 Bongaigaon  Refinery.  Kosamba,  Lunej, 
 Mehsana,  Nawagam,  Ankleshwar  and 
 Kalol  are  oil-producing  regions  located  in 
 Gujarat.

 Q.520.  With  respect  to  value  addition  in 
 the  textile  industry,  knitting  is  followed  by 
 which procedure? 
 (a) Garment manufacture 
 (b) Spinning 
 (c) Fiber production 
 (d) Dyeing and finishing 
 Sol.520.(d)  Dyeing  and  finishing.  In  the 
 textile  industry,  the  process  begins  with 
 fiber  production,  followed  by  spinning  to 
 create  yarn,  and  then  knitting  or  weaving 
 to  form  fabric.  Next  comes  dyeing, 
 printing,  and  finishing  to  enhance  the 
 fabric,  and  finally,  garment 
 manufacturing,  involving  cutting  and 
 sewing.  Thus,  the  step  following  knitting 
 is dyeing and finishing. 
 Q.521.  Which  of  the  following  is  dark 
 reddish coloured stone used as raw 
 material in aluminum smelting? 
 (a) Mica  (b) Gypsum 
 (c) Bauxite             (d) Conglomerate 
 Sol.521.(c) Bauxite.

 Q.523.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  best  links  a  current 
 conservation  initiative  for  the  Great 
 Indian  Bustard  with  its  geographical 
 location? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) National Avian Research Centre in 
 Maharashtra conducted habitat 
 simulation for GIB. 
 (b) Kalyan Sanctuary in Karnataka now 
 houses the largest wild GIB 
 population. 
 (c) India's first successful artificial 
 insemination of GIB took place in the 
 National Breeding Centre in 
 Jaisalmer, Rajasthan. 
 (d) A GIB conservation breeding centre 
 was set up in Kutch, Gujarat for 
 desert climate adaptation. 
 Sol.523.(c)  The  Great  Indian  Bustard 
 (GIB)  is  critically  endangered,  with  its  last 
 viable  wild  population  mainly  surviving  in 
 Rajasthan’s  Desert  National  Park.  To 
 save  the  species,  the  National  Bustard 
 Breeding  Centre  (NBBC)  was  established 
 in  Jaisalmer  and  Pokhran,  Rajasthan.  In 
 June  2021,  India  achieved  the  milestone 
 of  the  first  successful  artificial 
 insemination  and  captive  breeding  of  GIB 
 here. 
 Q.524.  What  is  the  role  of  National  Parks 
 under the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) To promote tourism 
 (b) To protect wildlife and their habitats 
 (c) To regulate trade 
 (d) To oversee elections 
 Sol.524.(b)  To  protect  wildlife  and  their 
 habitats.  Under  the  Wildlife  Protection 
 Act,  1972,  National  Parks  are  designated 
 areas  aimed  at  protecting  wildlife  and 
 preserving  natural  habitats.  Human 
 activities  like  hunting,  grazing,  forestry,  or 
 private  land  use  are  strictly  prohibited 
 inside  National  Parks.  Unlike  sanctuaries, 
 even  private  land  rights  cease  once  an 
 area  is  declared  a  National  Park.  While 
 tourism  may  occur  in  a  regulated 
 manner,  its  primary  objective  remains  the 
 conservation  of  ecosystems  and 
 protection of biodiversity. 
 Q.525.  Which  of  the  following  Biosphere 
 Reserves  is  correctly  matched  with  its 
 state? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Seshachalam Hills - Andhra Pradesh 
 (b) Dehang-Dibang - Meghalaya 
 (c) Pachmarhi - Gujarat 
 (d) Dibru-Saikhowa - Manipur 
 Sol.525.(a)  Seshachalam  Hills  -  Andhra 
 Pradesh.  The  Seshachalam  Hills  are 
 located  in  the  southern  part  of  Andhra 
 Pradesh  and  were  designated  as  a 
 Biosphere  Reserve  in  2010.  This  area  is 
 part  of  the  Eastern  Ghats  and 
 encompasses  the  famous  Tirumala 
 Venkateswara  Temple.  The 
 Dehang-Dibang  Biosphere  Reserve  is 
 located  in  the  state  of  Arunachal 
 Pradesh.  The  Pachmarhi  Biosphere 
 Reserve  is  situated  in  the  Satpura  Range 
 of  Madhya  Pradesh.  The  Dibru-Saikhowa 
 National  Park  and  Biosphere  Reserve  is 
 located in the state of Assam.

 Q.527.  Read  the  below  statements  and 
 choose the correct option: 
 Statement  1:  India's  first  initiative  to 
 track  Fishing  Cats  through  radio-collars 
 has  been  introduced  at  Coringa  Wildlife 
 Sanctuary. 
 Statement  2:  Fishing  Cats  are  primarily 
 found  in  coastal  wetlands  and  mangrove 
 ecosystems. 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Both Statements 1 and 2 are correct 
 (b) Neither Statement 1 nor 2 is correct 
 (c) Only Statement 1 is correct 
 (d) Only Statement 2 is correct 
 Sol.527.(a)  The  Wildlife  Institute  of  India 
 (WII),  Dehradun,  in  collaboration  with  the 
 Andhra  Pradesh  Forest  Department,  has 
 undertaken  India’s  first  Fishing  Cat 
 Collaring  Project  at  Coringa  Wildlife 
 Sanctuary  (Andhra  Pradesh)  to  track  the 
 movements  and  behaviors  of  these 
 endangered  felines.  Fishing  Cats 
 (Prionailurus  viverrinus)  are  semi-aquatic 
 wildcats  that  predominantly  inhabit 
 wetland  ecosystems,  including  coastal 
 wetlands,  swamps,  marshes,  tidal  creeks, 
 and  mangrove  forests.  These  habitats 
 are  critical  to  their  survival  and  hunting 
 behavior.

 Q.529.  Which  of  the  following  protected 
 areas are located in Telangana? 
 I.  Kawal Tiger Reserve 
 II.  Pranahita Wildlife Sanctuary 
 III. 
 Nagarjuna  Sagar–Srisailam  Tiger 
 Reserve 
 IV.  Kinnerasani Wildlife Sanctuary 
 (a) Only I and II               (b) Only II and III 
 (c) Only I, II, III and IV     (d) Only I and III 
 Sol.529.(c) Only I, II, III and IV.  Kawal 
 Tiger  Reserve  is  located  at  Adilabad 
 district  in  Telangana.  It  was  declared  a

 Q.530.  The  Betul,  Hoshangabad  and 
 Chhindwara  districts  of  Madhya  Pradesh 
 is  a  part  of  which  of  the  following 
 Biosphere Reserves? 
 (a) Manas  (b) Seshachalam 
 (c) Agasthyamalai       (d) Pachmarhi 
 Sol.530.(d)  Pachmarhi  Biosphere 
 Reserve  is  recognized  for  its  rich 
 biodiversity  and  has  been  designated  as 
 a  UNESCO  Biosphere  Reserve  in  2009. 
 The  Satpura  mountain  ranges  across 
 India  from  west  to  east  and  Pachmarhi 
 lies  directly  in  its  centre.  It  comprises 
 three  protection  sites:  the  Bori  Sanctuary, 
 Satpura  National  Park  and  Pachmarhi 
 Sanctuary.  Other  Biosphere  Reserve: 
 Manas  (Assam),  Seshachalam  (Andhra 
 Pradesh),  Agasthyamalai  (Kerala  and 
 Tamil Nadu). 
 Q.531.  As  of  the  June  2021,  how  many 
 biosphere  reserves  are  established  in 
 India? 
 (a) 18       (b) 20        (c) 26       (d) 15 
 Sol.531.(a)  18.  The  Government  of  India 
 started  the  Biosphere  Reserve  scheme  in 
 1986.  This  is  guided  by  the  UNESCO 
 MAB  programme,  as  India  is  a  signatory 
 to  the  landscape  approach  supported  by 
 MAB.  The  Nilgiri  Biosphere  Reserve  was 
 the  first  and  Oldest  biosphere  reserve  in 
 India established in 1986. 
 Q.532.  Which  national  park  in  the 
 Andaman  and  Nicobar  Islands  is  home  to 
 species  like  Megapode,  crab-eating 
 Macaque,  Nicobar  Pigeon,  and  Giant 
 Robber Crab? 
 (a) Raimona National Park 
 (b) Campbell Bay National Park 
 (c) Mouling National Park 
 (d) Papikonda National Park 
 Sol.532.(b)  Campbell  Bay  National  Park  , 
 established  in  1992,  spans  426.23 
 square  km  and  is  part  of  the  Great 
 Nicobar  Biosphere  Reserve.  Other 
 notable  parks  include  Raimona  National 
 Park  in  Assam  (famous  for  elephants 
 and  tigers),  Mouling  National  Park  in 
 Arunachal  Pradesh  (known  for  temperate 
 forests  and  Himalayan  wildlife),  and 
 Papikonda  National  Park  in  Andhra 
 Pradesh  (noted  for  Eastern  Ghats 
 biodiversity).

 Q.535.  Select  the  natural  habitat  of  the 
 Asiatic Lion from the following. 
 (a) Gir forest 
 (b) Sundarbans 
 (c) Kanha National Park 
 (d) Saranda forest 
 Sol.535.(a)  Gir  forest.  Asiatic  lions  are 
 an  endangered  species  and  their  534is 
 restricted  only  to  the  Gir  National  Park 
 situated  in  Gujarat.  India  is  the  only 
 country  in  the  world  to  have  both  tigers 
 and  lions.  Tigers  are  found  in  the  forests 
 of  Madhya  Pradesh,  the  Sundarbans  of 
 West  Bengal.  Number  of  Lions  in  India  - 
 674.  The  first  tiger  reserve  in  India  was 
 established in 1973 (Project Tiger).

 The  Salim  Ali  Centre  for  Ornithology 
 and  Natural  History  is  located  in 
 Tamil Nadu. 
 (a) Both I and II are correct 
 (b) Both I and III are correct 
 (c) Only III is correct 
 (d) Only I is correct.

 Q.537.  Which  biosphere  of  Southern 
 Andhra  Pradesh  is  home  to  many 
 endemic  species  including  the  famous 
 Red Sanders and Slender Loris? 
 (a) Cold Desert  (b) Pachmarhi 
 (c) Seshachalam  (d) Agasthyamalai 
 Sol.537.(c)  Seshachalam.  It  is  a 
 Biosphere  Reserve  situated  in  the 
 Seshachalam  Hill  ranges  of  the  Eastern 
 Ghats  in  southern  Andhra  Pradesh, 
 covering  a  total  area  of  4755.9  km². 
 Seshachalam  SLBR  was  designated  in 
 2011.  It  is  known  for  its  large  reserves  of 
 Red Sandalwood. 
 Q.538.  The  Keoladeo  National  Park  is 
 located in the state of _______. 
 (a) Gujarat         (b) Rajasthan 
 (c) Odisha         (d) Madhya Pradesh 
 Sol.538.(b)  Rajasthan.  Keoladeo 
 National  Park  in  Bharatpur  district  was 
 declared  a  UNESCO  World  Heritage  Site 
 in  1985.  States  and  National  Parks: 
 Rajasthan  -  Ranthambhore,  Sariska. 
 Gujarat  -  Gir,  Marine.  Odisha  -  Simlipal. 
 Madhya Pradesh - Bandhavgarh, Fossil, 
 Madhav, Sanjay-Dubri.

 Q.540.  Match the following.

 NationalPark

 National Park

 Corbett  National  Park  (Uttarakhand)  -

 Q.542.  Which  of  the  following  is  declared 
 as a Wild Ass Sanctuary? 
 (a) Ladakh  (b) Sunderbans 
 (c) Spiti Valley  (d) Rann of Kachchh 
 Sol.542.(d) Rann of Kachchh  (Gujarat). It 
 is the only home to wild ass in India. The 
 sanctuary was set up in 1972. 
 Q.543.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 closest  wild  relative  of  domestic  cattle 
 protected  in  some  of  the  famous 
 national  parks  of  India  like  Nagarhole 
 and Bandipur? 
 (a) Red angus  (b) Asiatic buffalo 
 (c) Nilgai  (d) Gaur 
 Sol.543.(d)  Gaur.  First  population 
 estimation  exercise  of  the  Indian  Gaur 
 (Bison)  was  carried  out  in  the  Nilgiris 
 Forest Division (Tamil Nadu). 
 Q.544.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  Biosphere 
 Reserves in India: 
 I. 
 Gulf  of  Mannar  Biosphere  Reserve  is

 II.

 marine  mammal,  is  found  in  the  Gulf 
 of Mannar.

 situated in Andhra Pradesh. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect?

 (c) Only III  (d) I and II 
 Sol.544.(a)  Only  I.  Gulf  of  Mannar 
 Biosphere  Reserve  (Tamil  Nadu,  India’s 
 first  marine  biosphere  reserve). 
 Biosphere  Reserves:  Khangchendzonga 
 (Sikkim),  and  Pachmarhi  (Madhya 
 Pradesh). 
 Q.545.  In  which  state  is  Gorumara 
 National Park located? 
 (a) Assam  (b) West Bengal 
 (c) Uttarakhand  (d) Bihar 
 Sol.545.(b)  West  Bengal.  Gorumara 
 National  Park  was  established  in  1994.  It 
 is  primarily  known  for  its  population  of 
 Indian  rhinoceros.  Other  National  Parks  : 
 West  Bengal  -  Jaldapara,  Sunderban, 
 Singalila,  Neora.  Assam  -  Kaziranga, 
 Nameri,  Manas.  Uttarakhand  -  Jim 
 Corbett,  Rajaji,  Gangotri.  Bihar  -  Valmiki. 
 Rajasthan  -  Ranthambore,  Keoladeo. 
 Madhya  Pradesh  -  Kanha,  Van  Vihar, 
 Satpura. 
 Q.546.  The  famous  Nelapattu  Bird 
 Sanctuary  is  located  in  which  of  the 
 following states? 
 (a) Tamil Nadu  (b) Andhra Pradesh 
 (c) Karnataka  (d) Madhya Pradesh 
 Sol.546.(b)  Andhra  Pradesh.  Nelapattu 
 Bird  Sanctuary  -  It  is  an  important 
 breeding  site  for  spot-billed  pelicans 
 (Pelecanus  philippensis).  Other  bird 
 sanctuaries  in  Andhra  Pradesh  -  Kolleru, 
 Uppalapadu,  Telineelapuram  and 
 Telukunchi.  Tamil  Nadu  -  Karikili  Birds 
 Sanctuary,  Chitrangudi.  Karnataka  - 
 Ranganathittu,  Attiveri.  Madhya  Pradesh  - 
 Ghatigaon, Karera Bustard Sanctuary. 
 Q.547.  Which  country  is  the  only  country 
 in  the  world  to  have  both  tigers  and 
 lions? 
 (a) Kenya           (b) South Africa 
 (c) India  (d) China 
 Sol.547.(c)  India.  The  natural  habitat  of 
 the  Indian  lion  is  the  Gir  forest  in  Gujarat. 
 Tigers  are  found  in  the  forests  of  Madhya 
 Pradesh,  the  Sundarbans  of  West  Bengal. 
 Number  of  Lions  in  India  -  674.  In  2023, 
 the  tiger  population  in  India  was 
 recorded at 3682. 
 Q.548.  The  Eld's  deer  subspecies,  which 
 is  classified  as  endangered  in  the  IUCN 
 Red List, is found only in the: 
 (a) Hemis National Park in Ladakh 
 (b) Ranthambore National Park in 
 Rajasthan 
 (c) Keibul Lamjao National Park in 
 Manipur 
 (d) Pench National Park in Madhya 
 Pradesh 
 Sol.548.(c)  Keibul  Lamjao  National  Park 
 in  Manipur.  IUCN  Headquarter  is  at  Gland, 
 Switzerland.  The  7  categories  of  the  IUCN 
 Red  List  are  Data  Deficient  (DD),  Least 
 Concern  (LC),  Near  Threatened  (NT), 
 Vulnerable  (VU),  Endangered  (EN), 
 Critically  Endangered  (CR),  Extinct  In  The 
 Wild (EW), Extinct (EX).

 Q.550.  Assertion  (A):  Laokhowa  Wildlife

 of the Subansiri River.

 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A.

 (d) A is false but R is true.

 70.13  km

 Q.551.  In  which  of  the  following  Indian 
 states  is  the  Barnawapara  Wildlife 
 Sanctuary located? 
 (a) Assam  (b) Karnataka 
 (c) Sikkim  (d) Chhattisgarh 
 Sol.551.(d)  Chhattisgarh  (Balodabazar 
 district).  It  was  established  in  1976  under 
 the  Wildlife  Protection  Act,  1972.  The 
 Jonk  River  flows  through  the  sanctuary, 
 which is a tributary of the Mahanadi river. 
 Q.552.  In which of the following states is 
 Kali Tiger Reserve located ? 
 (a) Arunachal Pradesh    (b) Karnataka 
 (c) Telangana                   (d) Assam 
 Sol.552.(b)  Karnataka.  Kali  Tiger 
 Reserve  is  a  protected  area  and  tiger 
 reserve  in  Uttara  Kannada  district,  in 
 Karnataka.  The  park  is  a  habitat  of 
 Bengal  tigers,  black  panthers,  and  Indian 
 elephants,  amongst  other  distinctive 
 fauna.  The  Kali  River  flows  through  the 
 whole tiger reserve. 
 Q.553.  Peppara  Wildlife  Sanctuary  is

 Sanctuaries  in  Kerala  :  Eravikulam,  Periyar, 
 Shendurney,  Chinnar,  Parambikulam, 
 Idukki, Wayanad, Thattekad. 
 Q.554.  Dandeli  Wildlife  Sanctuary  is 
 located in which of the following states? 
 (a) Karnataka  (b) Jharkhand 
 (c) Sikkim  (d) Kerala 
 Sol.554.(a)  Karnataka. 
 Q.555.  The  wetland  of  Ashtamudi  is 
 located in: 
 (a) Assam  (b) Tamil Nadu 
 (c) Kerala  (d) Karnataka 
 Sol.555.(c)  Kerala  (in  Kollam  District).  It 
 has  a  unique  wetland  ecosystem  and  a 
 large  palm  shaped  waterbody. 
 Ashtamudi  Wetland  is  included  in  the  list 
 of wetlands of international importance. 
 Q.556.  Which  sanctuary  is  famous  for 
 endangered  Estuarine  Crocodiles 
 conservation in India? 
 (a) Indira Gandhi Wildlife Sanctuary 
 (b) Bhitarkanika Wildlife Sanctuary, 
 Odisha 
 (c) Chinnar Wildlife Sanctuary, Kerala 
 (d) Dandeli Wildlife Sanctuary, Karnataka

 Sanctuary  (Odisha)  is  a  Ramsar  site  and 
 is  one  of  India’s  biggest  estuarine 
 crocodile  habitats  and  a  major  coastal 
 ecosystem.

 (a) National Chambal Sanctuary

 (d) Bhadra Wildlife Sanctuary

 the  Brahmagiri  Wildlife  Sanctuary 
 located?

 (c) Udupi  (d) Kodagu 
 Sol.558.(d)  Kodagu  (Coorg).  Brahmagiri 
 Wildlife  Sanctuary  is  situated  on  the
