 Physiographic Division of India 
 Q.559.  Which  of  the  following  plateaus  is 
 located in the northeastern part of India? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Malwa Plateau 
 (b) Deccan Plateau 
 (c) Chota Nagpur Plateau 
 (d) Shillong Plateau 
 Sol.559.(d)  Shillong  Plateau  .  This 
 plateau  is  part  of  the  peninsular  block 
 separated  from  the  Chota  Nagpur 
 Plateau  by  the  Garo–Rajmahal  Gap.  It  is 
 known  for  its  rich  mineral  resources  and 
 scenic  landscape.  Other  plateau  location: 
 Malwa  Plateau  -  Central  India.  Deccan 
 Plateau  -  Southern  &  Central  India.  Chota 
 Nagpur  Plateau  -  Eastern  India 
 (Jharkhand, Odisha, West Bengal). 
 Q.560.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 characteristic  feature  of  the  Western 
 Coastal Plains? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Mangrove forests 
 (b) High rainfall 
 (c) Large deltas 
 (d) Lagoons and backwaters 
 Sol.560.(d)  Lagoons  and  backwaters. 
 The  Western  Coastal  Plains  are  narrow 
 plains  lying  between  the  Western  Ghats 
 and  the  Arabian  Sea.  A  unique  feature  is 
 the  presence  of  lagoons  and  backwaters, 
 especially  in  Kerala  (e.g.,  Vembanad 
 Lake).  Unlike  the  Eastern  Coastal  Plains, 
 which  are  broader  and  have  large  deltas 
 (e.g.,  Ganga,  Mahanadi),  the  Western 
 Coastal  Plains  are  narrow  and  do  not 
 support major delta formation. 
 Q.561.  The  Western  Coastal  Plains  are 
 narrower  compared  to  the  Eastern 
 Coastal Plains because: 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) They receive less rainfall 
 (b) They are less populated 
 (c) They are older in formation 
 (d) They are bordered by the Western 
 Ghats 
 Sol.561.(d)  They  are  bordered  by  the 
 Western  Ghats.  The  Western  Coastal 
 Plains  are  much  narrower  than  the 
 Eastern  Coastal  Plains.  This  is  because 
 the  Western  Ghats  run  close  and  almost 
 parallel  to  the  Arabian  Sea  coast.  As  a 
 result,  there  is  very  little  flat  land  between 
 the  mountains  and  the  sea.  On  the  other 
 hand,  the  Eastern  Ghats  are  more  eroded 
 and  lie  farther  from  the  Bay  of  Bengal. 
 Hence,  the  Eastern  Coastal  Plains  are 
 wider,  while  the  Western  ones  remain 
 narrow. 
 Q.562.  Select  the  correct  order  of  the 
 following  plateaus  and  ranges  from 
 south to north in India. 
 (a) Malwa Plateau, Vindhya Range, 
 Satpura Range and Deccan Plateau 
 (b) Deccan Plateau, Vindhya Range, 
 Satpura Range and Malwa Plateau 
 (c) Deccan Plateau, Satpura Range, 
 Vindhya Range and Malwa Plateau 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (d) Malwa Plateau, Satpura Range, 
 Vindhya Range and Deccan Plateau 
 Sol.562.(c)  Deccan  Plateau:  The 
 southernmost  plateau,  covering  parts  of 
 Maharashtra,  Karnataka,  Andhra  Pradesh, 
 and  Telangana.  Satpura  Range:  Located 
 to  the  north  of  the  Deccan  Plateau  and 
 runs  through  Madhya  Pradesh  and 
 Maharashtra.  Vindhya  Range:  Lies  further 
 north  of  the  Satpura  Range,  stretching 
 across  Madhya  Pradesh  and  separating 
 the  northern  plains  from  the  southern 
 plateaus.  Malwa  Plateau:  Located  to  the 
 north  of  the  Vindhya  Range,  covering 
 parts of Madhya Pradesh and Rajasthan. 
 Q.563.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  regarding  temperature 
 variation in deserts: 
 I. 
 Deserts  experience  extreme 
 temperature  variation  between  day 
 and  night  due  to  low  humidity  and 
 sparse vegetation. 
 II. 
 In  the  Thar  Desert,  daytime 
 temperature  can  reach  around  50°C, 
 while nights may drop to nearly 15°C. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.563.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  Desert  like  the 
 ‘Thar  Desert’  experience  extreme 
 temperature  variations  due  to  low 
 moisture,  sparse  vegetation,  and  clear 
 skies.  Daytime  temperatures  can  reach 
 up  to  50°C,  while  nights  drop  to  around 
 15°C,  creating  a  difference  of  about  35°C 
 in 24 hours. 
 Q.564.  Which  physical  feature  of  India 
 prevents  the  cold  winds  from  central 
 Asia  from  entering  the  Indian 
 subcontinent? 
 (a) Indian plains  (b) Indian desert 
 (c) Coastal area  (d) Himalayas 
 Sol.564.(d)  Himalaya  :  It  acts  as  a 
 natural  barrier,  blocking  cold  winds  from 
 Central  Asia  and  protecting  the  climate 
 of  India.  This  mountain  range  effectively 
 protects  the  subcontinent,  and  maintains 
 its  relatively  warm  temperatures.  Coastal 
 area  :  Allows  winds  from  the  sea  to  enter 
 India. 
 Q.565.  In  which  of  the  following  districts 
 of Rajasthan are the ‘Barchans’ found? 
 (a) Udaipur  (b) Jaisalmer 
 (c) Bhilwara  (d) Alwar 
 Sol.565.(b) Jaisalmer.  Barchan, crescent 
 -shaped  sand  dune  produced  by  the 
 action  of  wind  predominately  from  one 
 direction.  The  Indian  desert  lies  west  of 
 the  Aravali  Hills,  with  sandy  plains  and 
 sand  dunes.  It  receives  very  low  rainfall 
 (below  150  mm  annually)  and  has  an  arid 
 climate with sparse vegetation. 
 Q.566.  Purvanchal  Himalayas  does  NOT 
 comprise of : 
 (a) Naga hills  (b) Pir Panjal range 
 (c) Manipur hills  (d) Mizo hills 
 Sol.566.(b)  Pir  Panjal  Range  in  the 
 Himalayas  is  a  part  of  the  Lesser 
 Himalayas.  The  Pir  Panjal  Range  starts 
 near  PatniTop  in  Jammu  and  Kashmir 
 and  runs  through  Himachal  Pradesh  to 
 Garhwal.  The  Purvanchal  Himalayas,  also 
 known  as  the  Eastern  Hills,  are  a 
 mountain  range  in  northeastern  India. 
 This  range  includes  the  Patkai  Hills,  the 
 Naga  Hills  in  Nagaland,  the  Manipur  Hills 
 in  Manipur,  and  the  Mizo  Hills  in 
 Mizoram. 
 Q.567.  Which  of  the  following  hills  is 
 located in the Eastern Ghats? 
 (a) Naga hills  (b) Khasi hills 
 (c) Shevaroy hills  (d) Anamudi hills 
 Sol.567.(c)  Shevaroy  hills.  The  Eastern 
 Ghats  are  a  series  of  discontinuous  low 
 hills  with  an  average  elevation  of  600 
 meters.  They  extend  parallel  to  the  east 
 coast,  from  the  southern  part  of  the 
 Mahanadi  valley  to  the  Nilgiri  hills.  The 
 highest  peak  in  this  region  is  Jindhagada 
 Peak  (1,690  meters).  Notable  hills  in  the 
 Eastern  Ghats  include  :  Mahendragiri  and 
 Niyamgiri  hills  in  Odisha,  Nallamala  hills 
 (Andhra  Pradesh),  and  Kollimalai  and 
 Pachaimalai hills in Tamil Nadu. 
 Q.568.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding Mawsynram: 
 I. 
 Mawsynram,  the  wettest  place  on 
 Earth,  is  located  in  the  Jaintia  Hills  of 
 Meghalaya. 
 II. 
 Cherrapunji  receives  more  rainfall 
 annually than Mawsynram. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.568.(d)  Neither  I  nor  II.  In  the 
 southern  ranges  of  Khasi  hills  is 
 Mawsynram,  which  receives  the  highest 
 rainfall  in  the  world.  Mawsynram,  located 
 about  16  kilometers  west  of  Cherrapunji, 
 is  recognized  as  the  wettest  place  on 
 Earth,  receiving  a  higher  annual  average 
 rainfall than Cherrapunji. 
 Q.569.  What  is  the  name  of  the  highest 
 peak of the Eastern Ghats? 
 (a) Kangchenjunga      (b) Mahendragiri 
 (c) Khasi  (d) Anamudi 
 Sol.569.(b)  Mahendragir.  This  hill  was 
 once  believed  to  be  the  highest  peak  in 
 the  Eastern  Ghats.  However,  in  April 
 2011,  a  team  led  by  Mr.  Venkat  Reddy  Y 
 found  that  the  elevation  of  Mahendra  Giri 
 Hill  is  actually  1,501  meters.  Jindhagada 
 Peak:  After  this  discovery,  it  was 
 determined  that  the  highest  peak  in  the 
 Eastern  Ghats  is  Jindhagada  Peak,  which 
 stands  at  1,690  meters.  It  is  located  in 
 the Araku Valley in Andhra Pradesh. 
 Q.570.  Which  sub-division  of  Himalayas 
 are famous for Karewa formations ? 
 (a) Sikkim Himalayas 
 (b) Arunachal Himalayas 
 (c) Uttarakhand Himalayas 
 (d) Kashmir Himalayas 
 Sol.570.(d)  Kashmir  Himalayas.  Karewa 
 -  thick  deposits  of  glacial  clay  and 
 moraines.  These  are  the  flat  topped 
 mounds  that  border  the  Kashmir  Valley 
 on  all  sides.  The  other  Himalayan  sub  - 
 divisions  are:  Uttarakhand  Himalayas: 
 Between  the  Ravi  and  Kali  rivers. 
 Arunachal  Himalayas:  Between  the  Tista 
 and  Brahmaputra  rivers.  Sikkim 
 Himalayas:  Between  the  Kali  and  Teesta 
 rivers. 
 Q.571.  The  Lushai  Hills  are  also  known 
 as _________. 
 (a) Khasi Hills  (b) Mizo Hills 
 (c) Naga Hills  (d) Patkai Hills 
 Sol.571.(b)  Mizo  Hills  (Mizoram  and 
 Manipur  ).  The  highest  point  of  Mizo  hills 
 is  the  Blue  Mountain.  Eastern  Hills  (The 
 Purvanchal)  are  the  southward  extension 
 of  Himalayas  running  along  the  north- 
 eastern edge of India. 
 Q.572.  The  central  stretch  of  the  Western 
 Coastal Plains of India is known as the: 
 (a) Coromandel coast   (b) Konkan coast 
 (c) Malabar coast          (d) Kannad plains 
 Sol.572.(d)  Kannad  plains  (Between  Goa 
 and  Mangalore)  .  Malabar  coast  -  Extends 
 from  the  southern  part  of  Goa  to 
 Kanyakumari. 
 Q.573.  The  Himalayas'  outermost  range 
 is called __________. 
 (a) Shiwaliks  (b) Kanchenjunga 
 (c) Dhaula Dhar        (d) Pir Panjal 
 Sol.573.(a)  Shiwaliks.  They  extend  over 
 a  width  of  10-50  Km  and  have  an  altitude 
 varying  between  900  and  1100  metres. 
 They  have  low  hills  like  Jammu  Hills,  etc. 
 Pir  Panjal  and  Dhauladhar  are  important 
 ranges of lesser Himalayas. 
 Q.574.  In  which  direction  is  the  general 
 elevation of the peninsular plateau of

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 India? 
 (a) From North to South 
 (b) From South to North 
 (c) From West to East 
 (d) From East to West 
 Sol.574.(c)  From  West  to  East.  The 
 Peninsular  Plateau  of  India  lies  south  of 
 the  Northern  Plains,  with  an  average 
 elevation  of  600-900  m.  It  consists  of 
 smaller  plateaus  and  hill  ranges  with 
 river  basins  and  valleys.  The  plateau  is 
 divided  into  three  groups:  Central 
 Highlands  (e.g.,  Malwa,  Bundelkhand), 
 Deccan  Plateau  (e.g.,  Maharashtra, 
 Karnataka),  and  Northwestern  Plateau 
 (e.g., Aravalli, Vindhya, Satpura ranges). 
 Q.575.  Which  of  the  following  hills  is 
 located in the Eastern most part of India? 
 (a) Jaintia Hills  (b) Khasi Hills 
 (c) Garo Hills  (d) Patkai Bum Hills 
 Sol.575.(d)  Patkai  Bum  Hills.  Patkai  Hills 
 (Arunachal  Pradesh,  Nagaland  and  Upper 
 Burma  region  of  Myanmar).  Western 
 Ghats  {Anamudi  (highest  peak), 
 Mannamala,  Meesapulimala,  Doddabetta 
 peak,  Kolaribetta  Peak,  etc).  The  Mizo 
 Hills,  located  in  the  southeastern 
 Mizoram  state,  were  formerly  called 
 Lushai Hills. 
 Q.576.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding the Eastern Ghats: 
 I. 
 The  average  elevation  of  the  Eastern 
 Ghats is about 600 metres. 
 II. 
 The  Eastern  Ghats  are  a  continuous 
 mountain  range  extending  from 
 Odisha to Tamil Nadu. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.576.(a)  Only  I.  Eastern  Ghats 
 comprises  the  discontinuous  and  low 
 hills  that  are  highly  eroded  by  the  rivers 
 such  as  the  Mahanadi,  the  Godavari,  the 
 Krishna,  the  Cauvery,  etc.  The  Eastern 
 Ghats  are  a  mesmerising  mountain  range 
 that  extends  across  the  states  of  Odisha, 
 Andhra  Pradesh,  Telangana,  Karnataka, 
 and Tamil Nadu. 
 Q.577.  Choose  the  correct  statements 
 about the islands of India. 
 a. 
 Lakshadweep  Islands  are  composed 
 of coral islands. 
 b. 
 Andaman  and  Nicobar  islands  are 
 composed of coral islands. 
 c. 
 It  is  believed  that  Lakshadweep 
 islands  are  an  elevated  portion  of 
 submarine mountains. 
 d. 
 It  is  believed  that  Andaman  and 
 Nicobar  islands  are  an  elevated 
 portion of submarine mountains. 
 (a) b and c            (b) a and b 
 (c) a and d            (d) c and d 
 Sol.577.(c) a and d. 
 Q.578.  What is the name of the physical 
 feature  that  is  the  longitudinal  valley  lying 
 between  the  Lesser  Himalayas  and  the 
 Siwalik in India? 
 (a) Duns  (b) Doab  (c) Bhabhar  (d) Terai 
 Sol.578.(a)  Duns  are  structurally  formed 
 valleys covered with boulders and gravel 
 from  the  erosion  of  the  Himalayas  and 
 Shivalik  uplands.  Major  duns  in  the 
 region  include  Dehra,  Kohtri,  Chaukham, 
 Patli,  and  Kota,  with  Dehradun  being  the 
 largest at 35 km long and 25 km wide. 
 Q.579.  The  Himalayas  have  uplifted  from 
 which of the following geosynclines? 
 (a) Tethys geosynclines 
 (b) Appalachians geosynclines 
 (c) Caledonian geosynclines 
 (d) Aravalli geosynclines 
 Sol.579.(a)  Tethys  geosynclines.  The 
 northward  drift  of  the  Indian  Plate 
 resulted  in  the  collision  of  the  plate  with 
 the  much  larger  Eurasian  Plate.  Due  to 
 this  collision,  the  sedimentary  rocks 
 which  were  accumulated  in  the 
 geosyncline  known  as  the  Tethys  were 
 folded  to  form  the  mountain  system  of 
 western Asia and Himalaya. 
 Q.580.  What  is  the  name  of  the  northern 
 part  of  the  western  coastal  plain 
 between Mumbai and Goa? 
 (a) Coromandel coast (b) Malabar coast 
 (c) Kannad plain  (d) Konkan coast 
 Sol.580.(d)  Konkan  coast.  The  central 
 stretch  of  Arabian  Sea  is  called  the 
 Kannad  Plain,  while  the  southern  stretch 
 is  known  as  the  Malabar  coast.  In  the 
 Bay  of  Bengal,  the  northern  part  is  called 
 the  Northern  Circar,  and  the  southern 
 part is the Coromandel Coast. 
 Q.581.  Which  of  the  following 
 physiographic  divisions  of  India  has 
 extensive alluvial soil ? 
 (a) The North Indian Plain 
 (b) The Island Group 
 (c) The Himalayan Range 
 (d) The Great Indian Desert 
 Sol.581.(a)  The  North  Indian  Plain  is 
 formed  by  alluvial  deposits  brought  by 
 rivers  such  as  the  Indus,  Ganga,  and 
 Brahmaputra.  These  plains  stretch  about 
 3,200  km  from  east  to  west  and  are 
 divided  into  three  zones:  Bhabar,  Tarai, 
 and  Alluvial  plains.  Bhabar  is  a  narrow 
 belt  near  the  Shiwalik  foothills,  while  lies 
 south  of  it.  The  alluvial  plains  include  the 
 Khadar  (new  alluvium)  and  Bhangar  (old 
 alluvium). 
 Q.582.  The  Great  Indian  desert/Thar 
 desert  is  located  on  the  western  margin 
 of  which  of  the  following  mountain 
 ranges? 
 (a) Vindhya range  (b) Aravalli range 
 (c) Eastern ghats  (d) Satpura range 
 Sol.582.(b)  Aravalli  range.  It  runs 
 approximately  670  km  (430  mi)  in  a 
 south-west  direction,  starting  near  Delhi, 
 passing  through  southern  Haryana  and 
 Rajasthan,  and  ending  in  Gujarat.  The 
 highest  peak  is  Guru  Shikhar  at  1,722 
 metres  (5,650  ft).  Vindhya  range  forms  a 
 dividing  line  between  the  Ganges  Plain 
 and  the  Deccan  Plateau.  Satpura  range  is 
 situated  between  two  west  flowing  rivers 
 - Narmada and Tapi. 
 Q.583.  Choose  the  correct  statements 
 about the Western Ghats. 
 a.   The height of the Western Ghats 
 progressively increases from north to 
 south. 
 b.   Mahendragiri is the highest peak in 
 the Western Ghats. 
 c.   The Western Ghats are higher than 
 the Eastern Ghats. 
 d.   The height of the Western Ghats 
 progressively decreases from north 
 to south. 
 (a) b, c and d  (b) a and b 
 (c) a and c  (d) Only c and d 
 Sol.583.(c)  a  and  c.  The  highest  peak  in 
 the  Western  Ghats  is  Anamudi  (2,695 
 metres). 
 Q.584.  Which  triangular  plateau  region  of 
 India lies south of the river Narmada? 
 (a) Deccan plateau 
 (b) Marwar plateau 
 (c) Baghelkhand plateau 
 (d) Malwa plateau 
 Sol.584.(a)  Deccan  plateau.  Its  broad 
 base  is  flanked  by  the  Satpura  Range  to 
 the  north,  while  the  Mahadev,  Kaimur 
 Hills,  and  Maikal  Range  extend  to  the 
 east.  The  Deccan  Plateau  is  higher  in  the 
 west and slopes gently eastwards. 
 Q.585.  Geologically,  which  of  the 
 following  physiographic  divisions  of  India 
 is  supposed  to  be  one  of  the  most  stable 
 land blocks? 
 (a) The Northern Plains 
 (b) The Himalayas 
 (c) The Peninsular Plateau 
 (d) The Indian Desert 
 Sol.585.(c)  The  Peninsular  plateau  is  a 
 tableland  composed  of  the  old 
 crystalline,  igneous  and  metamorphic

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 rocks.  This  plateau  consists  of  two  broad 
 divisions,  namely,  the  Central  Highlands 
 and  the  Deccan  Plateau.  The  part  of  the 
 Peninsular  plateau  lying  to  the  north  of 
 the  Narmada  river.  The  Indian  desert  lies 
 towards  the  western  margins  of  the 
 Aravali  Hills.  It  is  an  undulating  sandy 
 plain  covered  with  sand  dunes.  The 
 northern  plain  is  formed  of  alluvial  soil. 
 The  Himalayas,  geologically  young  and 
 structurally  fold  mountains  stretch  over 
 the northern borders of India. 
 Q.586.  With  reference  to  the  physical 
 features of India, the ‘Kayals’ are ______. 
 (a) Hillocks  (b) backwaters 
 (c) mountain passes    (d) valleys 
 Sol.586.(b)  Backwaters:  The  inland 
 water  bodies,  often  formed  by  the 
 merging  of  rivers  and  lakes  or  by  the 
 backflow  of  seawater,  creating  a  network 
 of  interconnected  waterways.  In  Kerala, 
 these  backwaters  are  locally  known  as 
 kayals. 
 Q.587.  Which  of  the  following  physical 
 divisions of India is densely populated? 
 (a) The North Indian Plains 
 (b) The islands 
 (c) The Himalayan Range 
 (d) The Thar Desert 
 Sol.587.(a)  The  North  Indian  Plains  has 
 been  formed  by  the  interplay  of  the  three 
 major  river  systems,  namely  the  Indus, 
 the  Ganga  and  the  Brahmaputra  along 
 with  their  tributaries,  which  makes  its  soil 
 highly  fertile.  Alluvial  soil  is  the  major  soil 
 found  in  the  plains.  The  swampy  belt  of 
 the plains is known as terai. 
 Q.588.  India  accounts  for  what  percent 
 of  the  total  geographical  area  of  the 
 world ? 
 (a) 3.2 %  (b) 5.2 %  (c) 4.8 %  (d) 2.4 % 
 Sol.588.(d)  2.4  %.  The  land  mass  of 
 India  has  an  area  of  3.28  million  square 
 km.  India  is  the  seventh  largest  country 
 in  the  world.  Largest  countries  in  the 
 world:  Russia,  Canada,  China,  United 
 States, Brazil, Australia, etc. 
 Q.589.  The  Standard  Meridian  of  India 
 (82°30’E)  passes  through  _______  state  of 
 India. 
 (a) Karnataka  (b) Madhya Pradesh 
 (c) West Bengal  (d) Punjab 
 Sol.589.(b)  Madhya  Pradesh.  The 
 Standard  Meridian  -  A  longitude  that 
 determines  a  standard  time  for  the  whole 
 country. 
 Q.590.  Assertion  (A):  Rajasthan  is  the 
 biggest state of India in terms of area. 
 Reason  (R):  Rajasthan  has  the  maximum 
 population among all Indian states. 
 (a) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) A is true but R is false. 
 (d) A is false but R is true. 
 Sol.590.(c) A is true but R is false.  Uttar 
 Pradesh  has  the  maximum  population, 
 not  Rajasthan.  Indian  states  biggest  in 
 terms  of  area:  Rajasthan,  Madhya 
 Pradesh,  Maharashtra,  Uttar  Pradesh, 
 Gujarat.  Smallest  state  (in  terms  of  area) 
 -  Goa.  Smallest  Union  Territories  (in 
 terms  of  area)  -  Lakshadweep,  Daman 
 and  Diu,  Chandigarh,  Puducherry,  Dadra 
 and Nagar Haveli, Delhi. 
 Q.591.  India's  total  length  of  coastline  of 
 the  mainland  including  Andaman  and 
 Nicobar and Lakshadweep is ________ 
 (a) 7416.6 km  (b) 7616 km 
 (c) 7516.6 km  (d) 7716 km 
 Sol.591.(c)  7516.6  km.  Length  of 
 Coastline  of  the  Indian  mainland  -  6100 
 km.  The  Coast  of  the  Indian  mainland  is 
 surrounded  by  Arabian  Sea  in  the  west, 
 Bay  of  Bengal  in  the  east  ,  and  Indian 
 Ocean  in  the  south.  India's  coastline 
 extends  to  Nine  states  (Gujarat, 
 Maharashtra,  Goa,  Karnataka,  Kerala, 
 Tamil  Nadu,  Andhra  Pradesh,  Odisha,  and 
 West  Bengal).  Largest  coastline  -  Gujarat 
 (1214 km). 
 Q.592.  The  plains  from  Ambala  to  the 
 Sunderban  stretch  over  nearly  1800  km, 
 but  the  fall  in  its  slope  is  hardly  ________ 
 metres. 
 (a) 550  (b) 300  (c) 600  (d) 450 
 Sol.592.(b)  300  .  In  other  words,  there  is 
 a  fall  of  just  one  meter  for  every  6  km. 
 Therefore,  the  river  develops  large 
 meanders.  Ambala  is  located  on  the 
 water  divide  between  the  Indus  and  the 
 Ganga river systems. 
 Q.593.  Which  of  the  following  factors  are 
 responsible  for  railways  having  not 
 developed  much  in  the  north-eastern 
 parts of India? 
 1. Highly dissected topography 
 2. Dense forests 
 3. Low population density 
 4. Heavy rainfall and frequent flooding 
 (a) 1, 2 and 3  (b) Only 3 and 4 
 (c) Only 2 and 3  (d) 1, 2 and 4 
 Sol.593.(d)  1,2  and  4.  The  northeastern 
 part  of  India  is  marked  by  the  presence 
 of  big  rivers,  dissected  relief,  dense 
 forests,  frequent  floods,  landslides, 
 international  frontiers,  etc.  The  first 
 railway  proposals  for  India  were  made  in 
 Madras  in  1832.  As  a  result,  the  country 
 got  its  first  train  in  the  form  of  the  Red 
 Hill  Railway  which  was  built  with  the  sole 
 purpose  of  transporting  granite  for 
 road-building in 1837. 
 Q.594.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 mountain  pass  in  the  Pir  Panjal  range  at 
 a  maximum  altitude  of  2832  meters  that 
 connects  the  Kashmir  Valley  in  the  Union 
 Territory  of  Jammu  and  Kashmir  with  the 
 Outer  Himalayas  and  the  plains  in  the 
 south? 
 (a) Mana Pass  (b) Banihal Pass 
 (c) Bara-Lacha Pass   (d) ZojiLa Pass 
 Sol.594.(b)  Banihal  pass  (Jammu  and 
 Kashmir)  is  situated  in  the  Pir  -  Panjal 
 Range.  It  connects  Banihal  with 
 Qazigund.  Mana  Pass  (Uttarakhand)  is 
 located  in  the  Greater  Himalayas  and 
 connects  Tibet  with  Uttarakhand.  Bara 
 lacha  pass  -  Connects  Himachal 
 Pradesh's  Lahaul  area  with  Ladakh.  Zojila 
 Pass connects Leh and Srinagar. 
 Q.595.  With  reference  to  the  major 
 regions of the northern plains, Bhabar is: 
 (a) the older alluvium along the river 
 beds forming terraces higher than the 
 floodplain 
 (b) a narrow belt of about 8 to 16 km that 
 runs in the west-east direction along 
 the foot of the Himalayas from the 
 river Indus to Teesta 
 (c) composed of newer alluvium and 
 forms flood plains along the river 
 banks 
 (d) a broad long zone of northern India 
 and southern Nepal running parallel 
 to the lower ranges of the Himalayas 
 Sol.595.(b)  Bhabar  is  a  narrow  belt  lying 
 parallel  to  the  slopes  of  the  Shiwaliks  in 
 Uttarakhand.  It  is  narrow  in  the  east  and 
 wide  in  the  western  and  northwestern 
 hilly  regions.  It  comprises  gravel  and 
 un-assorted  sediment  deposits.  Crop 
 cultivation  is  not  possible  in  this  tract, 
 only giant trees with deep roots grow. 
 Q.596.  In  which  of  the  following  regions 
 of  India  is  the  natural  habitat  of 
 White-bellied  Heron  largely  distributed? 
 (a) North-east  (b) Northern plains 
 (c) Deccan plateau   (d) Western coast 
 Sol.596.(a)  North-east  India,  the 
 easternmost  region  of  the  country, 
 comprises  eight  states:  Arunachal 
 Pradesh,  Assam,  Manipur,  Meghalaya, 
 Mizoram, Nagaland, Tripura, and Sikkim. 
 Q.597.  Deserts, rain,forests, coral reefs, 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 and  mangroves  are  features  of  ________ 
 diversity. 
 (a) genetic  (b) cultural 
 (c) species  (d) ecological 
 Sol.597.(d)  Ecological  diversity  includes 
 species  diversity,  genetic  diversity,  and 
 ecosystem  diversity.  They  are  collectively 
 considered  biodiversity.  It  is  the  sum  of 
 all  kinds  of  animals  and  plants  living  on 
 this earth along with their habitat. 
 Q.598.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 Tawang  is  a  tourism  hotspot  in

 2.  Kalimpong district belongs to Sikkim. 
 3. 
 Alipurduar  and  Jalpaiguri  are  districts 
 of West Bengal. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2

 Sol.598.(b)  Only  2.  Kalimpong  is  in  West

 Q.599.  The  archaeological  site  of 
 Atranjikhera is located in _______. 
 (a) Himachal Pradesh    (b) Uttarakhand 
 (c) Uttar Pradesh            (d) Maharashtra 
 Sol.599.(c)  Uttar  Pradesh.  This  site  was 
 first  identified  by  Sir  Alexander 
 Cunningham  in  1862  but  excavated  by  R 
 C Gaur in 1962. 
 Q.600.  In  the  state  of  ______,  the  general 
 direction  of  the  Himalayas  is  from 
 southwest to northeast. 
 (a) Manipur  (b) Nagaland 
 (c) Mizoram  (d) Arunachal Pradesh 
 Sol.600.(d)  Arunachal Pradesh. 
 Himalayas  in  the  Darjeeling  and  Sikkim 
 regions lie in an east-west direction.

 Q.602.  Which  among  the  following 
 states  does  NOT  share  its  border  with 
 Odisha? 
 (a) Chhattisgarh      (b) Jharkhand 
 (c) Bihar                    (d) West Bengal

 Q.603.  River  Sutlej  enters  India  through

 Q.605.  Name  the  Indian  port  that  is  the 
 largest exporter of iron ore ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Paradip Port 
 (b) Chennai Port 
 (c) Visakhapatnam Port 
 (d) Marmagao Port 
 Sol.605.(d)  Marmagao  Port  ,  Goa,  is  one 
 of  the  oldest  major  ports  on  the  west 
 coast  of  India.  It  is  the  largest  exporter  of 
 iron  ore  in  the  country,  handling  a 
 substantial  portion  of  India’s  iron  ore 
 exports  due  to  its  proximity  to  Goa’s  iron 
 ore  mining  belt.  Other  ports:  Paradip  Port 
 (Odisha)  :  Major  port  for  coal,  iron  ore, 
 and  crude  oil  but  not  the  largest  in  iron 
 ore  exports.  Chennai  Port  (Tamil  Nadu): 
 Handles  containers,  cars,  and  general 
 cargo,  not  primarily  iron  ore. 
 Visakhapatnam  Port  (Andhra  Pradesh): 
 Handles  iron  ore  and  coal,  but  Marmagao 
 surpasses it in ore exports. 
 Q.606.  Which  of  the  below  statements 
 is/are correct? 
 Statement  1:  India  supports  connectivity 
 through  the  North-South  Transport 
 Corridor. 
 Statement  2:  The  corridor  strengthens 
 India's trade with the Pacific Islands. 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Only Statement 2 is correct 
 (b) Only Statement 1 is correct 
 (c) Both 1 and 2 are correct 
 (d) Neither 1 nor 2 is correct 
 Sol.606.(b)  India  supports  connectivity 
 through  the  International  North-South 
 Transport  Corridor  (INSTC),  which  is  a 
 multi-modal  network  of  ship,  rail,  and 
 road  routes  aimed  at  connecting  India 
 with  Central  Asia,  Russia,  and  Europe  via 
 Iran.  This  strategic  corridor  is  designed 
 to  reduce  transit  time  and  cost  in  moving 
 goods  between  India  and  Eurasia. 
 However,  Statement  2  is  incorrect 
 because  the  corridor  has  no  direct 
 relevance  to  India’s  trade  with  the  Pacific 
 Islands, which are geographically distant 
 and lie in a completely different direction. 
 Q.607.  What  is  the  primary  mission 
 behind  India's  involvement  in  the 
 International  North-South  Transport 
 Corridor (INSTC)? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) To promote clean maritime security 
 (b) To bypass China's CPEC route via 
 Taiwan 
 (c) To improve connectivity with Eurasia 
 via multimodal routes 
 (d) To facilitate transit trade through the 
 Arctic 
 Sol.607.(c)  The  INSTC  (International 
 North-South  Transport  Corridor)  is  a 
 multi-modal  (ship–rail–road)  transport 
 network  covering  about  7,200  km.  It 
 connects  India  →  Iran  →  Russia  → 
 Central  Asia  →  Europe.  Main  goals: 
 Provide  India  with  a  shorter,  cheaper 
 trade  route  to  Eurasia  compared  to  the 
 Suez  Canal  route.  Strengthen  India’s 
 presence  in  Central  Asia  and  Russia 
 while  bypassing  Pakistan.  Enhance  trade 
 and  energy  connectivity  with  Eurasia 
 through integrated logistics. 
 Q.608.  What  is  the  length  of  the  Indian 
 Railways  network,  according  to  the 
 Railway Yearbook 2019-20 ? 
 (a) 67,956 km  (b) 1,604 km 
 (c) 63,950 km  (d) 2,402 km 
 Sol.608.(a)  67,956  km.  The  length  of  the 
 Indian  Railways  network,  according  to 
 the  Railway  Yearbook  2019-20  :  Broad 
 gauge  -  The  distance  between  the  rails  is 
 1.676  meters,  with  a  total  length  of 
 63,950  km.  Metre  gauge  -  The  distance 
 between  the  rails  is  1  meter,  with  a  total 
 length  of  2,402  km.  Narrow  gauge  -  The 
 distance  between  the  rails  is  either  0.762 
 meters  or  0.610  meters,  with  a  total 
 length of 1,604 km. 
 Q.609.  According  to  the  Indian  Railways 
 (2019-2020),  which  state  has  the  largest 
 railway track in India? 
 (a) Punjab  (b) Haryana 
 (c) Madhya Pradesh  (d) Uttar Pradesh 
 Sol.609.(d)  Uttar  Pradesh.  The  longest 
 railway  track  in  India  is  the 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Dibrugarh-Kanyakumari  Vivek  Express 
 (approx  4200  km),  running  from 
 Dibrugarh  in  Assam  to  Kanyakumari  in 
 Tamil  Nadu.  The  shortest  railway  line  is 
 the  3-kilometer  route  between  Nagpur 
 and  Ajni  in  Maharashtra.  The  fiv e  states 
 with  the  largest  route  kilometers  are 
 Uttar  Pradesh,  Rajasthan,  Maharashtra, 
 Gujarat, and Andhra Pradesh. 
 Q.610.  In  which  of  the  following  states 
 is the development of rail transport less, 
 due to its geographical conditions? 
 (a) Sikkim  (b) Haryana 
 (b) Bihar  (d) Uttar Pradesh 
 Sol.610.(a)  Sikkim  is  predominantly 
 mountainous,  characterized  by  steep 
 slopes  and  rugged  terrain,  which 
 complicates  the  construction  of  rail 
 infrastructure. 
 Q.611.  Through  which  routes  is  the 
 majority  of  India’s  international  trade 
 carried out? 
 (a) Railway and air    (b) Sea and air 
 (c) Only Railway        (d) Sea and railway 
 Sol.611.(b)  Sea  and  air.  India  has  a 
 coastline  of  approximately  7,516.6  km, 
 making  maritime  transport  essential  for 
 international  trade.  According  to  the 
 Ministry  of  Shipping,  about  95%  of  India's 
 trade  by  volume  and  70%  by  value  is 
 conducted  via  sea  routes.  A  smaller 
 portion  of  trade  occurs  through  land 
 routes  to  neighboring  countries, 
 including  Nepal,  Bhutan,  Bangladesh,  and 
 Pakistan. 
 Q.612.  According  to  the  Ministry  of  Road 
 Transport  and  Highways’  annual  report 
 (2020-2021),  which  position  does  India 
 have  in  terms  of  road  networks  in  the 
 world? 
 (a) First  (b) Second  (c) Fourth  (d) Third 
 Sol.612.(b)  Second.  India  has  the 
 second  largest  road  network  in  the  world 
 after  the  USA.  India’s  road  network 
 stands  at  over  63,71,847  km.  This 
 comprises  National  Highways, 
 Expressways,  State  Highways,  Major 
 District  Roads,  other  District  Roads  and 
 Village  Roads  (Ministry  of  Road 
 Transport  &  Highways  Annual  Report  FY 
 2021-22). 
 Q.613  .  As  of  Financial  year  2019,  which 
 of the following states has the highest 
 road density in India? 
 (a) Haryana  (b) Tamil Nadu 
 (c) Kerala  (d) Punjab 
 Sol.613.(c)  Kerala.  As  of  the  financial 
 year  2019,  Kerala  had  the  highest  road 
 density  among  states,  with  6.7  thousand 
 kilometers  of  roads  per  one  thousand 
 square  kilometers.  Among  union 
 territories,  Chandigarh  had  the  highest 
 road  density  in  India,  with  over  22.6 
 thousand  kilometers  per  one  thousand 
 square  kilometers.  Road  density  refers  to 
 the  total  length  of  all  roads  in  a  country, 
 including  highways,  national  roads,  and 
 city  or  rural  roads,  compared  to  the 
 country's land area. 
 Q.614.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 The  delta  channels  of  Mahanadi  and 
 Brahmani  rivers  along  with  the  East 
 Coast  Canal  are  connected  by 
 National Waterway-5. 
 II. 
 National  Waterway-3  connects 
 Prayagraj  in  Uttar  Pradesh  to  Haldia  in 
 West  Bengal  through  the 
 Ganga-Bhagirathi-Hooghly system. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.614.(a)  Only  I.  NW  5  connects  the 
 states  of  Odisha  and  West  Bengal, 
 stretching  623  km  in  total,  with  91  km  in 
 West  Bengal  and  532  km  in  Odisha. 
 National  Waterway-1  (NW-1)  spans  the 
 Ganga-Bhagirathi-Hooghly  river  system 
 from  Prayagraj,  Uttar  Pradesh,  to  Haldia, 
 West  Bengal.  National  Waterway-3,  also 
 known  as  the  West  Coast  Canal,  is  a  168 
 km  route  in  Kerala  from  Kollam  to 
 Kottapuram. 
 Q.615.  About  what  percentage  of 
 passenger  traffic  is  carried  by  roads  in 
 India? 
 (a) 77%    (b) 87%   (c) 57%   (d) 67% 
 Sol.615.(b)  87%.  The  Road  Transport 
 Sector  handles  about  60%  of  freight 
 traffic  in  India.  For  passenger  traffic, 
 roads  account  for  87%,  railways  for  12%, 
 and  both  air  and  waterways  contribute 
 approximately  0.5%  each,  as  per  the 
 Ministry  of  Road  Transport  and 
 Highways. 
 Q.616.  Which  of  the  following  is  an 
 example of an eastern coast port? 
 (a) Mumbai Port 
 (b) Chennai Port 
 (c) Mormugao Port 
 (d) New Mangalore Port 
 Sol.616.(b)  Chennai  Port  started  its 
 commercial  operations  in  1881.  The 
 major  ports  on  the  eastern  coast  : 
 Tuticorin  -  Tamil  Nadu,  Ennore  -  Tamil 
 Nadu,  Chennai  -  Tamil  Nadu,  Haldia  and 
 Kolkata  -  West  Bengal,  Paradip  -  Odisha, 
 Visakhapatnam  -  Andhra  Pradesh,  Port 
 Blair - Andaman and Nicobar Islands. 
 Q.617.  Which  of  the  following  ports  was 
 developed  as  a  Satellite  port  to  relieve 
 the pressure at the Mumbai port? 
 (a) New Mangalore Port 
 (b) Marmagao Port 
 (c) Deendayal Port Authority 
 (d) Jawaharlal Nehru Port 
 Sol.617.(d) Jawaharlal Nehru Port 
 (Nhava  Sheva),  established  on  26  May 
 1989,  is  India's  second-largest  container 
 port,  following  Mundra  Port.  New 
 Mangalore  Port,  in  Karnataka,  is  operated 
 by  the  New  Mangalore  Port  Trust. 
 Mormugao  Port,  commissioned  in  1885, 
 is located on Goa's western coast. 
 Q.618.  In  which  year  was  the  Konkan 
 Railway constructed? 
 (a) 1998    (b) 1978    (c) 1988    (d) 2008 
 Sol.618.(a)  1998.  Indian  Railways 
 founded  the  Konkan  Railway  on  19th  July 
 1990.  Konkan  Railways  crosses  146  river 
 streams,  nearly  2000  bridges,  and  91 
 tunnels.  The  states  of  Maharashtra,  Goa, 
 and  Karnataka  are  partners  in  this 
 undertaking. 
 Q.619.  The  Atal  Tunnel  has  been  built  by 
 Border  Road  Organisation  at  an  altitude 
 of  3000  metres.  Which  places  are 
 connected by this tunnel? 
 (a) Manali to Shimla 
 (b) Shimla to Srinagar 
 (c) Shimla to Ladakh 
 (d) Manali to Lahaul-Spiti 
 Sol.619.(d)  Manali  to  Lahaul-Spiti.  Atal 
 Tunnel  (also  known  as  Rohtang  Tunnel) 
 is  a  highway  tunnel  built  under  the 
 Rohtang  Pass  in  the  eastern  Pir  Panjal 
 range  of  the  Himalayas  on  the  Leh- 
 Manali  Highway  in  Himachal  Pradesh.  It 
 reduces  the  distance  between  Solang 
 Valley  and  Sissu  by  approximately  46  km. 
 Pir  Panjal  Railway  Tunnel  -  India's  longest 
 transportation railway tunnel.

 Q.621.  When  was  air  transport 
 nationalised in India? 
 (a) 1950   (b) 1952   (c) 1951  (d) 1953 
 Sol.621.(d)  1953.  The  Air  Corporations 
 Act  of  1953  led  to  the  nationalization  of 
 Indian  airlines  and  the  formation  of  two 
 corporations:  Indian  Airlines  for  domestic 
 routes  and  Air  India  International  for 
 international routes.

 Column A 
 (Railway zones)

 (Headquarters)

 Q.623.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 The  Howrah  Bridge  was  officially 
 opened in 1943. 
 II. 
 It  was  renamed  Rabindra  Setu  in  1965 
 in honour of Rabindranath Tagore. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.623.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  The  Howrah 
 Bridge  is  a  balanced  steel  bridge  over  the 
 Hooghly  River  in  West  Bengal,  India.  At 
 the  time  it  was  built  the  name  given  to  it 
 was  the  New  Howrah  Bridge.  It  was 
 renamed  Rabindra  Setu  in  1965  after  the 
 great  Bengali  poet  Rabindranath  Tagore. 
 But  it  is  still  popularly  known  by  the 
 name Howrah Bridge. 
 Q.624.  What  is  the  total  length  of  the 
 Golden  Quadrilateral,  built  by  the 
 National  Highways  Authority  of  India 
 (NHAI)? 
 (a) 5978 km  (b) 4658 km 
 (c) 5790 km  (d) 5846 km 
 Sol.624.(d)  5846  km.  The  Golden 
 Quadrilateral  is  a  major  highway  network 
 connecting  four  megacities  in  India: 
 Delhi,  Kolkata,  Chennai,  and  Mumbai.  The 
 routes  are  Delhi-Kolkata  via  NH-2, 
 Mumbai-Delhi  via  NH-8,  Mumbai-Chennai 
 via  NH-4,  and  Chennai-Kolkata  via  NH-5. 
 The  National  Highways  Authority  of  India, 
 established  in  1988,  operates  under  the 
 Ministry  of  Road  Transport  and 
 Highways. 
 Q.625.  Pipelines  as  a  means  of  transport 
 are  looked  after  by  the  _________  of  the 
 Government of India. 
 (a) Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas 
 (b) Ministry of Home Affairs 
 (c) Ministry of Railways 
 (d) Ministry of Road Transport and 
 Highways 
 Sol.625.(a)  Ministry  of  Petroleum  and 
 Natural  Gas.  Some  major  pipelines  in 
 India:  Hazira  -  Bijaypur  -  Jagdishpur 
 Pipeline  -  India's  longest  gas  pipeline, 
 which  runs  from  Gujarat  to  Uttar 
 Pradesh.  Jamnagar-Loni  LPG  Pipeline  -  It 
 transports  LPG  from  Jamnagar  to  Loni 
 near  Delhi.  Naharkatia-Nunmati-Barauni 
 Pipeline  -  India's  first  crude  oil  pipeline, 
 which  runs  from  Assam  to  the  Barauni 
 refinery. 
 Q.626.  As  of  2022,  which  of  the  following 
 is the longest national waterway in India? 
 (a) Allahabad-Haldia stretch 
 (b) Sadiya-Dhubri stretch 
 (c) Godavari-Krishna stretch 
 (d) Kottapuram-Kollam stretch 
 Sol.626.(a)  Allahabad-Haldia  stretch 
 (1,620  km).  It  is  divided  into  three  parts 
 for  developmental  purposes  -  (i) 
 Haldia-Farakka  (560  km),  (ii)  Farakka 
 -Patna  (460  km),  (iii)  Patna  Allahabad 
 (600  km).  National  Waterways  (NW)  of 
 India  :  NW  2  -  Sadiya-Dhubri  stretch  (891 
 km).  NW  3  -  Kottapuram-Kollam  stretch 
 (205  km).  NW  4  -  Specific  stretches  of 
 Godavari  and  Krishna  rivers  along  with 
 Kakinada-Puducherry  stretch  of  canals 
 (1078  km).  NW  5  -  Specified  stretches  of 
 river  Brahmani  along  with  Matai  river, 
 delta  channels  of  Mahanadi  and  Brahmani 
 rivers and East Coast canals (588 km). 
 Q.627.  How  many  major  ports  are  there 
 in India for maritime transportation? 
 (a) 21  (b) 15  (c) 18  (d) 13 
 Sol.627.(d)  13.  Ports  and  State  :  Chennai 
 Port  (Tamil  Nadu),  Ennore  Port  (Tamil 
 Nadu),  Shyama  Prasad  Mukherji  Port 
 (West  Bengal),  Deendayal  Port  (Gujarat), 
 Kochi  Port  (Kerala),  Port  Blair  (Andaman 
 and  Nicobar  Islands),  New  Mangalore 
 Port  (Karnataka),  Mormugao  (Goa), 
 Mumbai  Port  (Maharashtra),  Paradip 
 Port  (Odisha),  Tuticorin  Port  (Tamil 
 Nadu). 
 Q.628.  The  historical  Sher  Shah  Suri 
 Marg  is  called  the  National  Highway  no. 
 _________ between Delhi and Amritsar. 
 (a)  9  (b)  3  (c)  1  (d)  7 
 Sol.628.(c)  1.  Sher  Shah  Suri  built  the 
 Shahi  (Royal)  Road  to  strengthen  and 
 consolidate  his  empire  from  the  Indus 
 Valley  to  Bengal's  Sonar  Valley.  This  road 
 was  renamed  the  Grand  Trunk  (GT)  Road 
 during  British  rule,  connecting  Kolkata 
 and  Peshawar.  Currently,  it  extends  from 
 Amritsar  to  Kolkata  and  is  divided  into 
 two  segments:  National  Highway  (NH)-1 
 from  Delhi  to  Amritsar  and  NH-2  from 
 Delhi to Kolkata. 
 Q.629.  In  which  of  the  following  years 
 was the air transport in India launched? 
 (a) 1948  (b) 1932  (c) 1954  (d) 1911 
 Sol.629.(d)  1911.  Air  Transportation:  Air 
 transport  in  India  was  launched  between 
 Allahabad  and  Naini  (10  km).  George 
 Light  started  Air  Transport  in  India.  The 
 first  airmail  flight  was  piloted  by  French 
 pilot  Henri  Pequet.  Air  transport  was 
 nationalised  and  two  Corporations,  Air 
 India  International  and  Indian  Airlines 
 were formed in 1953. 
 Q.630.  In  India,  which  of  the  following 
 modes  of  transportation  reduces 
 trans-shipment  losses  and  delays  in 
 supply of petroleum and gas? 
 (a) Waterways               (b) Railways 
 (c) Pipeline  (d) Roadways 
 Sol.630.(c)  Pipelines  are  cost-effective 
 and  can  transport  large  volumes  of 
 liquids  or  gasses  over  long  distances 
 with  minimal  environmental  impact. 
 Advantages  -  Dedicated  Infrastructure, 
 Minimized  Handling,  Continuous  Flow, 
 Cost-Effective,  Environmental  Benefits, 
 Energy  Efficiency,  Reduces  Congestion. 
 Naharkatia-Noonmati  -Barauni  Pipeline  is 
 the  first  crude  oil  pipeline  constructed  in 
 India.  Jamnagar-Loni  LPG  Pipeline  is 
 India's longest pipeline. 
 Q.631.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT  a 
 part  of  the  six  communication  mail 
 channels in India? 
 (a) Metro channel      (b) Monthly channel 
 (c) Rajdhani channel (d) Business channel 
 Sol.631.(b)  Monthly  Channel.  The  six 
 communication  mail  channels  in  India: 
 Rajdhani  (Yellow  colored  mailboxes  are 
 installed  at  post  offices),  Metro  (Blue 
 colored  mailboxes  are  installed  at  post 
 offices),  Green  (Green  colored  mailboxes 
 are  installed  at  post  offices  in  large  and 
 metropolitan  cities),  Business  (Physical 
 mail  that  is  commercial  in  nature), 
 Periodical  (Intended  for  newspapers, 
 magazines,  and  other  periodical 
 publications)  and  Bulk  (Consists  of 
 letters,  newsletters,  leaflets,  and  other 
 similar products).

 Q.633.  Which  project  aims  to  develop 
 state  roads  along  coastal  border  areas, 
 including  connectivity  of  non-major 
 ports? 
 (a) Golden Quadrilateral 
 (b) Setubharatam Pariyojana 
 (c) District Headquarters Connectivity 
 Scheme 
 (d) Bharatmala 
 Sol.633.(d)  Bharatmala.  This  centrally 
 funded  project  is  an  ecosystem  of  road 
 development  which  includes 
 development  of  tunnels,  bridges, 
 elevated  corridors,  fly overs,  overpasses, 
 interchanges,  bypasses,  ring  roads  etc. 
 Golden  Quadrilateral  -  Highway  network 
 which  links  4  largest  metropolises:  Delhi 
 (north),  Kolkata  (east),  Mumbai  (west), 
 and  Chennai  (south).  Setu  Bharatam  -  To 
 make  all  National  Highways  free  of 
 railway level crossings. 
 Q.634.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 The  Grand  Trunk  Road  was  built  by 
 Sher Shah Suri. 
 II. 
 It  connects  Amritsar  and  Kolkata  in 
 India. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.634.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  Grand  Trunk 
 Road  (built  by  Sher  Shah  Suri)  -  One  of 
 the  oldest  road  routes  in  India.  Length  - 
 2400  km.  It  links  Central  Asia  to  the 
 Indian  Subcontinent.  The  route  was 
 called  Uttarapatha.  NH-44  -  India’s 
 longest  highway.  NH-548  -  India’s 
 shortest highways. 
 Q.635.  Which  waterway  provides 
 connectivity with mainland India through 
 the India - Bangladesh Protocol route? 
 (a) NW-10 (b) NW-15 (c) NW-2  (d) NW-83 
 Sol.635.(c)  NW  -  2  (891  Km)  Between 
 Sadiya  and  Dhubri  on  river  Brahmaputra.

 port  in  Assam.  NW  -  1  (1620  km):  Ganga 
 -  Bhagirathi  -  Hooghly  River  (Allahabad  - 
 Haldia).  It  is  the  longest  and  oldest 
 National  Waterways  in  India.  NW-10: 
 Amba River. NW-69: It is the smallest 
 waterway (5 km). 
 Q.636.  Asansol,  Howrah,  Malda  and 
 Sealdah  division  come  under  which 
 railway zone of India? 
 (a) West Central Railway 
 (b) Northern Railway 
 (c) Central Railway 
 (d) Eastern Railway 
 Sol.636.(d)  Eastern  Railway.  Formed  on  - 
 14  April  1952  by  amalgamating  three 
 lower  divisions  of  the  East  Indian 
 Railway:  Howrah,  Asansol  and  Danapur, 
 the  entire  Bengal  Nagpur  Railway  (BNR) 
 and  the  Sealdah  division  of  the  erstwhile 
 Bengal  Assam  Railway.  Headquarters  - 
 Kolkata.  Kalka  Mail  of  Eastern  Railway  is 
 the  oldest  running  train  in  the  history  of 
 Indian Railways. 
 Q.637.  Which  autonomous  body  was 
 operationalised  under  the  Ministry  of 
 Surface Transport in 1995 ? 
 (a) Border Roads Organization (BRO) 
 (b) Centre for Railway Information 
 System (CRIS) 
 (c) Inland Waterways Authority of India 
 (IWAI) 
 (d) National Highways Authority of India 
 (NHAI) 
 Sol.637.(d)  National  Highways  Authority 
 of  India  (NHAI)  -  Operationalised  in  1995 
 and  established  under  the  National 
 Highways  Authority  of  India  Act,  1988. 
 Headquarters  -  New  Delhi.  Other 
 organizations:-  Border  Roads 
 Organization  (BRO)  -  Formed,  7  May 
 1960.  Headquarters  -  New  Delhi.  Center 
 for  Railway  Information  System  (CRIS)  - 
 Founded,  July  1986.  Headquarters  -  New 
 Delhi.  Inland  Waterways  Authority  of 
 India  (IWAI)  -  Founded,  27  October  1986 
 and  headquartered  -  Noida  (Uttar 
 Pradesh). 
 Q.638.  Which  of  the  following  is  the  only 
 rack  railway  in  India  that  was  declared  a 
 UNESCO World Heritage Site in 2005? 
 (a) Nilgiri Mountain Railway 
 (b) Kangra Valley Railway 
 (c) Matheran Hill Railway 
 (d) Kalka–Shimla Railway 
 Sol.638.(a)  Nilgiri  Mountain  Railway. 
 Darjeeling  Himalayan  Railway  (In 
 UNESCO  list  in  1999),  Kalka  Shimla 
 Railway  (2008)  and  Chhatrapati  Shivaji 
 Terminus,  Mumbai  (2004).  There  are  43 
 World  Heritage  Sites  in  India.  Out  of 
 these,  35  are  cultural,  seven  are  natural, 
 and  one,  Khangchendzonga  National 
 Park,  is  of  mixed  type  (As  of  October 
 2024). 
 Q.639.  Select  the  natural  port  of  India 
 from the following. 
 (a) Mumbai Port  (b) Chennai Port 
 (c) Deendayal Port       (d) Kolkata Port 
 Sol.639.(a) Mumbai Port -  India’s largest 
 natural  port.  Chennai  Port  (Madras  Port) 
 -  It  is  a  man-made,  all-weather  harbor 
 with  wet  docks,  the  largest  port  in  the 
 Bay  of  Bengal.  Deendayal  Port  (Kandla 
 Port)  -  Largest  port  of  India  by  volume  of 
 cargo  handled.  Kolkata  Port  (Shyama 
 Prasad  Mookerjee  Port)  -  Oldest 
 operating  port  in  India,  built  by  East  India 
 Company. 
 Q.640.  Match  the  National  Waterways

 Goa.  NW  -  73  (Narmada  River)  -  Gujarat 
 and  Maharashtra.  NW  -  97  (Sundarbans

 Q.642.  _______  is  the  deepest  landlocked 
 and well - protected port. 
 (a) Kandla  (b) Mumbai 
 (c) Visakhapatnam     (d) Paradip 
 Sol.642.(c)  Visakhapatnam  -  It  is  a  major 
 port  (total  13  in  India)  located  in  Andhra 
 Pradesh  coast  of  Bay  of  Bengal.  It  is  a 
 natural  harbor.  The  major  commodities  in 
 this  port  include  Iron  ore,  manganese 
 ore,  steel  products,  general  cargo,  coal 
 and crude oil.

 Q.651.  Which  Indian  state  has  the 
 highest  proportion  of  its  workforce 
 population engaged in agriculture ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Rajasthan  (b) Uttar Pradesh 
 (c) Bihar  (d) Punjab 
 Sol.651.(b)  Uttar  Pradesh  has  the 
 highest  proportion  of  its  workforce 
 engaged  in  agriculture,  with  nearly  70% 
 of  its  population  depending  on  farming 
 and  allied  activities  for  livelihood. 
 Despite  this,  the  state  contributes 
 relatively  less  to  India’s  overall 
 agricultural  output  due  to  small 
 landholdings,  low  mechanization,  and 
 dependence  on  monsoon  rains.  Other 
 states  like  Bihar,  Rajasthan  and  Punjab 
 also  have  large  agricultural  bases,  but  in 
 terms  of  percentage  of  workforce 
 engaged,  Uttar  Pradesh  has  the  highest 
 proportion.

 (c) Lakshadweep  (d) Delhi 
 Sol.652.(d)  Delhi.  As  per  Census  2011, 
 Delhi  has  the  highest  population  density 
 among  Union  Territories  with  11,297 
 persons  per  sq  .km.  Other  UTs:

 Lakshadweep  -  2,149  persons/sq  .  km. 
 Highest  population  density  state  –  Bihar 
 (1,106  persons/sq  .  km).  Lowest 
 population  density  state  -  Arunachal

 Q.653.  What  is  the  term  for  the  shift  of 
 workers  from  agriculture  to  industry  and 
 services ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Industrialization 
 (b) Urbanization 
 (c) Occupational diversification 
 (d) Economic transition 
 Sol.653.(c)  Occupational  diversification. 
 The  shift  of  workers  from  the  agriculture 
 sector  to  industry  and  services  is  termed 
 occupational  diversification.  It  reflects 
 structural  changes  in  the  economy  as 
 development  takes  place.  In  a  developing 
 economy,  most  of  the  workforce  is 
 initially  engaged  in  agriculture.  With 
 industrialization  and  urbanization, 
 workers  gradually  move  to  secondary 
 (industry)  and  tertiary  (services)  sectors. 
 This  transition  is  also  linked  to  economic 
 development  and  is  a  sign  of 
 modernization.  In  India,  despite  progress, 
 a  significant  proportion  of  the  workforce 
 is  still  dependent  on  agriculture,  though 
 the  services  sector  contributes  the 
 highest share to GDP. 
 Q.654.  What  shape  does  a  population 
 pyramid  take  for  a  rapidly  growing 
 population? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Inverted triangle shape 
 (b) Circular shape 
 (c) Triangular shape 
 (d) Rectangular shape 
 Sol.654.(c)  Triangular  shape.  A 
 population  pyramid  for  a  rapidly  growing 
 population  has  a  broad  base  and  narrow 
 top,  forming  a  triangular  shape.  This 
 indicates  high  birth  rates,  a  large 
 proportion  of  young  people,  and  relatively 
 fewer  elderly  individuals.  Rectangular 
 shape  indicates  a  stable  or  slowly 
 growing  population  where  birth  and 
 death  rates  are  low  and  the  population  is 
 evenly  distributed  across  age  groups 
 (seen in developed countries). 
 Q.655.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements are correct? 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 1. 
 Birth  rate  measures  the  number  of 
 children  born  per  1000  people 
 annually. 
 2. 
 Pull  factors  include  opportunities  that 
 attract people to new regions. 
 3. 
 Death  rate  is  always  higher  than  birth 
 rate. 
 4. 
 Push  factors  can  include  civil  unrest 
 and lack of jobs. 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) All of the above         (b) 2 and 3 only 
 (c) 1, 2, and 4 only          (d) 1 and 3 only 
 Sol.655.(c)  1,  2,  and  4  only.  Birth  rate  is 
 correctly  defined  as  the  number  of  live 
 births  per  1000  population  in  a  year.  Pull 
 factors  are  positive  conditions  and 
 opportunities,  such  as  better  jobs, 
 education,  or  security,  that  attract  people 
 to  a  new  location.  Death  rate  is  not 
 always  higher  than  birth  rate;  in  fact, 
 most  countries  experience  higher  birth 
 rates,  except  in  advanced  economies 
 with  population  decline.  Push  factors  are 
 negative  conditions,  such  as  poverty, 
 unemployment,  political  instability,  or 
 civil  unrest,  that  compel  people  to  leave 
 their home region. 
 Q.656.  Which  Indian  Union  Territory  has 
 the most Scheduled Castes? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Delhi  (b) Chandigarh 
 (c) Ladakh  (d) Lakshadweep 
 Sol.656.(a)  Delhi.  As  per  the  2011 
 Census  of  India,  among  all  Union 
 Territories,  Delhi  has  the  highest 
 Scheduled  Caste  (SC)  population  in 
 absolute  numbers.  Scheduled  Castes 
 constitute  about  16.75%  of  Delhi’s  total 
 population,  which  is  the  largest  share 
 compared  to  other  Union  Territories. 
 Chandigarh  also  has  a  significant  SC 
 population  (around  18.9%),  but  in 
 absolute  numbers,  Delhi  far  exceeds  all 
 others  because  of  its  large  population 
 base.  Lakshadweep  and  Ladakh,  being 
 small  in  population,  have  negligible  SC 
 representation. 
 Q.657.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  best  explains  why  certain 
 regions exhibit high population density ? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Regions offering fertile land, 
 favorable climates, abundant natural 
 resources, and robust economic 
 opportunities are more likely to have 
 high population densities. 
 (b) High population density is 
 predominantly found in areas with 
 poor infrastructure and limited 
 access to essential services. 
 (c) Regions with limited natural 
 resources tend to have Areas with 
 scarce natural resources have higher 
 population concentrations because of 
 greater competition for few materials. 
 (d) Areas characterized by mountainous 
 terrain and arid climates often support 
 dense populations because of their 
 challenging living conditions. 
 Sol.657.(a)  Population  density  is  strongly 
 influenced  by  physical  and  economic 
 factors.  Fertile  soil  and  favorable  climate 
 support  agriculture,  ensuring  food 
 security.  Access  to  water  bodies  and 
 natural  resources  sustains  livelihoods. 
 Economic  opportunities  and 
 infrastructure  in  such  regions  attract 
 migration.  Hence,  such  areas  develop 
 into population-dense centers. 
 Q.658.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  best  captures  the  essence  of 
 Malthus'  theory  as  outlined  in  the 
 passage? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) The natural imbalance between food 
 supply and population growth leads 
 to unavoidable hardships unless 
 population is controlled. 
 (b) Voluntary checks on population are 
 more effective than natural calamities 
 in curbing overpopulation. 
 (c) Population grows in arithmetic 
 progression, while food production 
 grows geometrically. 
 (d) Agricultural production can grow 
 faster than population with 
 technological advancement. 
 Sol.658.(a)  The  statement  perfectly 
 encapsulates  the  core  argument  of 
 Thomas  Malthus's  theory  on  population. 
 He  posited  that  there  is  a  fundamental 
 tension  between  the  rates  of  population 
 growth  and  food  production.  Population, 
 when  unchecked,  increases  at  a 
 geometric  rate  (e.g.,  2,  4,  8,  16),  while  the 
 food  supply  increases  at  a  much  slower, 
 arithmetic  rate  (e.g.,  2,  3,  4,  5).  This 
 "natural  imbalance"  inevitably  leads  to  a 
 point  where  the  population  outstrips  the 
 available  resources,  resulting  in 
 "unavoidable  hardships"  like  famine, 
 disease,  and  war.  These  are  what 
 Malthus  called  "positive  checks."  The 
 only  way  to  avoid  this  fate,  he  argued,  is 
 through  "preventive  checks"—conscious 
 decisions  to  control  the  population,  such 
 as  delaying  marriage  and  practicing 
 moral restraint. 
 Q.659.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is/are  true  regarding  India's 
 rural  and  urban  population  trends  from 
 1901 to 2011? 
 1. 
 The  rural  population  has  consistently 
 increased in absolute numbers. 
 2. 
 The  percentage  share  of  rural 
 population  in  total  population  has 
 steadily declined. 
 3. 
 Urban  population  first  exceeded  100 
 million in 1981. 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Only 1 and 2  (b) All of the above 
 (c) Only 1 and 3  (d) Only 2 and 3 
 Sol.659.(a)  Only  1  and  2.  Despite  rapid 
 urbanization,  India’s  rural  population  kept 
 increasing  in  absolute  numbers  in  every 
 census  from  1901  to  2011.  It  rose  from 
 about  212  million  in  1901  to  over  833 
 million  in  2011.  However,  the  share  of  the 
 rural  population  declined  from  89% 
 (1901)  to  68.84%  (2011).  India’s  urban 
 population  crossed  100  million  in  the 
 1971  Census,  reaching  about  109  million 
 (10.78  crore),  and  further  increased  to 
 around 159 million in 1981. 
 Q.660.  Which  of  the  following  factors 
 contributes  to  the  low  child  sex  ratio  in 
 some  economically  developed  Northern 
 states of India? 
 1. 
 Increased  accessibility  to  prenatal 
 sex-determination methods 
 2. 
 High  levels  of  female  literacy  and 
 empowerment 
 3. 
 Societal  preference  for  male  children 
 in inheritance and family support 
 4. 
 Strengthened  enforcement  of  gender- 
 equal policies 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) 2 and 3  (b) 2 and 4 
 (c) 1 and 3  (d) 1 and 4 
 Sol.660.(c)  1  and  3.  The  low  child  sex 
 ratio  in  some  economically  developed 
 northern  states  of  India  can  be  attributed 
 primarily  to  two  key  factors.  Firstly, 
 increased  accessibility  to  prenatal 
 sex-determination  methods  has  led  to 
 the  misuse  of  medical  technology  for 
 sex-selective  abortions,  despite  being 
 legally  prohibited.  Secondly,  a 
 deep-rooted  societal  preference  for  male 
 children,  particularly  due  to  traditional 
 views  on  inheritance,  family  lineage,  and 
 support  for  aging  parents,  continues  to 
 influence family decisions.

 (b) Only Statement 2 is correct

 (d) Neither Statement 1 nor 2 is correct

 Q.662.  Which  of  the  following  states  has 
 the  lowest  population  in  India,  according 
 to the 2011 Census of India? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Maharashtra  (b) Odisha 
 (c) Punjab  (d) Madhya Pradesh 
 Sol.662.(c)  Punjab.  According  to  Census 
 2011,  Punjab’s  population  was  about 
 2.77  crore,  which  is  the  lowest  among 
 the  given  options  (Maharashtra  -  11.24 
 cr,  Madhya  Pradesh  -  7.26  cr,  Odisha  - 
 4.19  cr).  A  Census  is  the  process  of 
 collecting,  analyzing,  and  publishing 
 demographic,  social,  and  economic  data 
 of  a  country’s  population.  In  India,  it  is 
 conducted  every  10  years  by  the 
 Registrar  General  &  Census 
 Commissioner  (MHA).  The  first 
 synchronous  Census  was  held  in  1881, 
 and  since  then,  it  has  been  conducted 
 regularly  every  decade  till  2011.  It  is  the 
 largest  administrative  exercise  in  the 
 world. 
 Q.663.  Which  of  the  following  correctly 
 matches  the  population  growth  phase  in 
 India with its key characteristics? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Phase IV (Post-1981) - Sudden 
 increase in fertility rate due to 
 migration 
 (b) Phase III (1951-1981) - Population 
 explosion caused by falling death rate 
 and high fertility 
 (c) Phase I (1901-1921) - Rapid 
 population explosion due to low 
 mortality and high fertility 
 (d) Phase II (1921-1951) - High mortality 
 rate and declining birth rate 
 Sol.663.(b)  Phase  III  (1951-1981)  - 
 Population  explosion  caused  by  falling 
 death  rate  and  high  fertility.  India’s 
 population  growth  can  be  studied  in  four 
 phases:  Phase  I  (1901–1921)  –  Stagnant 
 Growth  /  High  Mortality.  Characterized  by 
 very  high  birth  and  death  rates,  frequent 
 famines  and  epidemics,  resulting  in 
 almost  stagnant  growth.  Phase  II 
 (1921–1951)  –  Steady  Growth.  After  the 
 "Year  of  Great  Divide"  (1921),  mortality 
 began  to  decline  due  to  improved  health 
 facilities,  but  birth  rates  remained  high, 
 leading  to  gradual  growth.  Phase  III 
 (1951–1981)  –  Population  Explosion. 
 This  period  saw  a  sharp  fall  in  death 
 rates  due  to  medical  and  technological 
 advancements,  while  fertility  remained 
 high,  leading  to  rapid  population  growth 
 (population  explosion).  Phase  IV 
 (Post-1981  –  Present)  –  Declining 
 Growth  Rate.  Birth  rates  started  declining 
 significantly  due  to  family  planning, 
 urbanization,  education,  and  awareness. 
 There  was  no  sudden  fertility  rise  due  to 
 migration. 
 Q.664.  In  each  of  the  following 
 questions,  a  statement  labeled  Assertion 
 (A)  is  given,  followed  by  a  statement 
 labeled Reason (R). 
 Assertion  (A):  Migration  can  be  driven  by 
 negative and positive circumstances. 
 Reason  (R):  Push  factors  make 
 individuals  migrate  away  from  a  region, 
 whereas  pull  factors  draw  individuals  to 
 a region. 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) A is false, but R is true. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (d) A is true, but R is false. 
 Sol.664.(c)  Migration  occurs  due  to  a 
 combination  of  negative  pressures  and 
 attractive  opportunities.  Push  factors  like 
 poverty,  unemployment,  natural 
 disasters,  and  political  instability  compel 
 people  to  leave  their  place  of  origin.  On 
 the  other  hand,  pull  factors  such  as 
 better  employment,  education, 
 healthcare,  and  safety  motivate  them  to 
 move  toward  a  new  region.  These 
 contrasting  influences  explain  why 
 migration  is  driven  by  both  negative  and 
 positive circumstances. 
 Q.665.  In  each  of  the  following 
 questions,  a  statement  labeled  Assertion 
 (A)  is  given,  followed  by  a  statement 
 labeled Reason (R). 
 Assertion  (A):  Migrants  who  enter  a  new 
 country or region are called immigrants. 
 Reason  (R):  Emigrants  are  people  who 
 leave their country to settle elsewhere. 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Both A and R are true, but R is not the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (b) Both A and R are true, and R is the 
 correct explanation of A. 
 (c) A is true, but R is false. 
 (d) A is false, but R is true. 
 Sol.665.(a)  The  terms  immigrant  and 
 emigrant  describe  the  same  act  of 
 migration  but  from  different 
 perspectives.  A  person  leaving  one 
 country  becomes  an  emigrant  there  but 
 simultaneously  an  immigrant  in  the 
 destination  country.  This  dual  usage 
 explains  why  both  statements  are  true 
 and interconnected. 
 Q.666.  Which  state  has  the  lowest

 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Manipur  (b) Arunachal Pradesh 
 (c) Sikkim  (d) Mizoram 
 Sol.666.(c)  Sikkim.  Based  on  the  2011

 also  indicate  that  Sikkim  will  remain  the

 (c) Puducherry  (d) Chhattisgarh

 was  the  15th  National  Census,

 Q.668.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Three  phases  are  involved  in  the 
 census operations in India 
 II. 
 The  first  phase  of  census  operations 
 is Houselisting/Housing Census. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Sol.668.(b)  Only  II.  The  Two  phases  are 
 involved  in  the  census  operations  in 
 India.  First  -  Houselisting/Housing 
 Census  under  which  details  of  all 
 buildings,  permanent  or  temporary,  are 
 noted  with  their  type,  amenities,  and 
 assets.  Second  -  Population  Enumeration 
 where  more  detailed  information  on  each 
 individual  residing  in  the  country,  Indian 
 national  or  otherwise  is  noted.  The  first 
 non-synchronous  census  was  conducted 
 in  1872  under  Lord  Mayo's 
 administration,  while  the  first 
 synchronous  census  took  place  in  1881 
 under  W.C.  Plowden.  The  16th  Census 
 (2021)  was  postponed  due  to  the 
 COVID-19 pandemic. 
 Q.669.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is/are  correct  according  to 
 the Census of India-2011? 
 a. 
 A  person  aged  seven  years  or  above, 
 who  can  both  read  and  write  with 
 understanding  in  any  language,  is 
 treated as literate. 
 b. 
 A  person  who  can  only  read  but 
 cannot write is not literate. 
 (a) Both (a) and (b) are correct 
 (b) Only (b) is correct 
 (c) Only (a) is correct 
 (d) Neither (a) nor (b) is correct 
 Sol.669.(a)  According  to  the  2011

 being  about  9%  higher  than  the  adult 
 rate. The female literacy rate is 65.46%. 
 Q.670.  As  per  Census  2011,  which  of  the 
 following  states  recorded  negative 
 growth in child sex ratio ? 
 (a) Maharashtra    (b) Himachal Pradesh 
 (c) Punjab  (d) Haryana 
 Sol.670.(a)  Maharashtra  recorded  a 
 significant  decline  in  child  sex  ratio,  from 
 913  girls  per  1,000  boys  in  2001  to  894 
 girls  per  1,000  boys  in  2011.  According 
 to  the  2011  census,  India's  sex  ratio  was 
 943  females  per  1,000  males,  and  the 
 child  sex  ratio  was  919  females  per 
 1,000 males.

 .  Agricultural  density

 is  the  number  of  farmers  per  unit  of 
 arable  land.  The  agricultural  population 
 includes  cultivators  and  agricultural 
 labourers and their family members. 
 Q.672.  According  to  Census  of  India

 Q.673.  As  per  the  definition  of  Census  of 
 India,  a  ‘marginal  worker’  is  a  person  who 
 works for: 
 (a) less than 183 days (or six months) in 
 a year 
 (b) more than 183 days (or six months) 
 in a year 
 (c) eight months in a year 
 (d) 83 days in a year 
 Sol.673.(a)  The  population  of  India 
 according  to  their  economic  status  is 
 divided  into  three  groups,  namely;  main 
 workers,  marginal  workers  and 
 non-workers.  A  Main  Worker  is  a  person 
 who  works  for  at  least  183  days  (or  six 
 months)  in  a  year.  Marginal  Worker  is  a 
 person  who  works  for  less  than  183  days 
 (or  six  months)  in  a  year.  The  proportion 
 of  workers  (both  main  and  marginal)  is 
 only  39.8  per  cent  (2011)  leaving  a  vast 
 majority  of  about  60  percent  as 
 non-workers. 
 Q.674.  The  Accelerated  Female  Literacy 
 Programme  was  initiated  in  districts 
 where  the  female  literacy  rate,  based  on 
 the 2001 census, was _______. 
 (a) below 35%  (b) below 45% 
 (c) below 30%  (d) below 50% 
 Sol.674.(c)  below  30%.  According  to  the 
 2001  Census,  47  districts  in  India  had  a 
 female  literacy  rate  below  30%.  Most  of 
 these  districts  were  located  in  Bihar, 
 Jharkhand,  Uttar  Pradesh,  and  Odisha. 
 The  Accelerated  Female  Literacy 
 Programme  aims  to  empower  women  by 
 helping  them  develop  literacy  skills  and 
 gain access to information and services. 
 Q.675.  According  to  Census  of  India 
 2011,  what  is  the  gap  between  male  and 
 female literacy rate in India? 
 (a) 21.59 %  (b) 18.30 % 
 (c) 24.84 %  (d) 16.68 % 
 Sol.675.(d)  16.68%.  According  to  the 
 2011  Census  of  India,  the  overall  literacy 
 rate  was  74.04%.  The  literacy  rate  for 
 males  was  82.14%,  while  for  females,  it 
 was  65.46%.  Literacy  rates  for  States  and 
 Union  Territories  (UTs)  :  Kerala  -  94% 
 (highest  among  states),  Bihar  -  61.80% 
 (lowest  among  states),  Lakshadweep  - 
 91.85%  (highest  among  UTs),  Dadra  and 
 Nagar  Haveli  -  76.24%  (lowest  among 
 UTs),  Mizoram  -  91.33%,  Goa  -  88.70% 
 and Delhi - 86.21%.

 population  in  the  0-6  age  group 
 registered  minus  3.08  percent  growth 
 with  minus  2.42  for  males  and  minus 
 3.80 for females.

 Q.680.  According  to  Census  2011,  which 
 district displays the highest population? 
 (a) Kollam (b) Ajmer (c) Thane (d) Pune 
 Sol.680.(c)  Thane  is  a  district  located  in 
 the  state  of  Maharashtra.  It  is  often

 its large number of water bodies. 
 Q.681.  Who  published  the  logistic 
 equation  model  of  population  growth  in 
 1838 ? 
 (a) Howard Thomas Odum 
 (b) Alfred Russel Wallace 
 (c) Pierre François Verhulst 
 (d) George Evelyn Hutchinson 
 Sol.681.(c)  Pierre  François  Verhulst.  He 
 was  a  Belgian  mathematician  who 
 earned  a  doctorate  in  number  theory 
 from  the  University  of  Ghent  in  1825.  He 
 is  renowned  for  the  logistic  growth 
 model,  which  is  widely  applied  in 
 population  growth  modeling.  This  model, 
 first  proposed  by  Pierre-François  Verhulst 
 in  1838,  states  that  the  reproduction  rate 
 is  proportional  to  both  the  existing 
 population  and  the  available  resources, 
 assuming all else is equal. 
 Q.682.  According to the Census of India 
 2011,  which  group  of  Union  Territories 
 has the highest density of population? 
 (a) Lakshadweep, Daman and Diu and 
 Puducherry 
 (b) Delhi, Chandigarh and Daman and Diu 
 (c) Delhi, Chandigarh and Puducherry 
 (d) Lakshadweep, Daman and Diu and 
 Chandigarh 
 Sol.682.(c)  Delhi,  Chandigarh  and 
 Puducherry.  In  2011,  the  population 
 density  of  India  was  382  persons  per 
 square  kilometer,  with  Arunachal 
 Pradesh  having  the  lowest  density  at  17 
 persons  per  square  kilometer;  Bihar  had 
 the  highest  density  at  1,106  persons  per 
 square  kilometer,  followed  by  West 
 Bengal  (1028),  Kerala  (860),  Uttar 
 Pradesh (829), and Haryana (573).

 Q.685.  According  to  the  Census  of  India 
 2011,  most  people  had  out-migrated 
 from ________ to other states in India. 
 (a) Rajasthan and Gujarat 
 (b) Uttar Pradesh and Madhya Pradesh 
 (c) Maharashtra and Gujarat 
 (d) Uttar Pradesh and Bihar 
 Sol.685.(d)  Uttar  Pradesh  and  Bihar. 
 According  to  the  2011  Census,  Uttar 
 Pradesh  and  Bihar  are  responsible  for  the 
 most  number  of  migrants  as  20.9  million 
 people  migrated  outside  the  state  from 
 the  two  states.  Reason  for  Migration  : 
 Lack  of  Employment  opportunities,  Lack 
 of  proper  Educational  and  Healthcare 
 Facilities,  Marriage.  Uttar  Pradesh  has 
 the  highest  share  of  out-migrants  while 
 Maharashtra  has  the  highest  share  of 
 in-migrants. 
 Q.686.  The  growth  rate  of  India's 
 population has been declining since ____. 
 (a) 1991   (b) 1951    (c) 1981    (d) 1971 
 Sol.686.(c) 1981.  The pace of population 
 growth  is  slowing  down  in  the  country. 
 Two  major  reasons  behind  this  decline:  a 
 decrease  in  the  fertility  rate  and 
 improvements  in  family  planning 
 practices.  The  population  growth  rate 
 was  23.6%  in  the  decade  1981-1991, 
 which  declined  to  21.5%  in  1991-2001. 
 This  rate  declined  further  to  17.6%  in  the 
 decade 2001-2011. 
 Q.687.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding Census in India: 
 I. 
 The  Census  Act  was  enacted  in  India 
 in 1947. 
 II. 
 The  first  census  of  Independent  India 
 was conducted in 1950. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.687.(d)  Neither  I  nor  II.  The  Census 
 Act  was  enacted  in  India  in  1948.  The 
 first  census  of  Independent  India  was 
 conducted  in  1951,  which  was  the 
 seventh  census  in  its  continuous  series. 
 The  enumeration  period  of  this  Census 
 was  from  9th  to  28th,  February  1951.  The 
 Census  Act,  1948  -  An  Act  to  provide  for 
 certain  matters  in  connection  with  the 
 taking of Census.

 Q.703.  Consider the following: 
 Which  of  the  following  reasons  may  be 
 considered  as  India’s  pull  factors  for 
 migration? 
 1.  Labour demand 
 2.  Natural disaster 
 3.  Protection of human and civil rights 
 4. 
 High  consumption  and  living 
 standards 
 (a) Both 1 and 4  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Only 1, 3 and 4     (d) Only 2, 3 and 4 
 Sol.703.(c)  Only  1,  3  and  4.  Pull  factors 
 attract  migrants  to  an  area,  like  better  job 
 opportunities  or  quality  of  life.  Push 
 factors  drive  people  to  leave,  such  as 
 poor living conditions or lack of jobs. 
 Q.704.  What  is  the  literacy  rate  of  males 
 in  India  as  per  the  report  published  in  the 
 National Survey of India-2022 ? 
 (a) 81.2%  (b) 83.4%  (c) 86.3% (d) 84.7% 
 Sol.704.(d)  84.7%.  According  to  the 
 National  Survey  of  India  2022,  the  overall 
 literacy  rate  in  India  is  77.7%,  with  female 
 literacy at 70.3%. 
 Q.705.  The  __________  results  from  an 
 increase  in  the  proportion  of  workers 
 relative to nonworkers in the population. 
 (a) age structure of population 
 (b) population momentum 
 (c) old-age dependency ratio 
 (d) demographic dividend 
 Sol.705.(d)  Demographic  dividend  refers 
 to  the  economic  growth  potential  from 
 changes  in  a  population's  age  structure, 
 typically  when  the  working-age 
 population  grows  larger  relative  to 
 dependents.  Population  momentum 
 describes  the  potential  for  growth  or 
 decline  based  on  age  structure,  even  with 
 constant  fertility,  mortality,  and  migration 
 rates.  The  old-age  dependency  ratio 
 (OADR)  is  the  number  of  people  aged 
 65+  compared  to  those  aged  20–64,  the 
 working - age group. 
 Q.706.  According  to  the  Census  of  India 
 2011,  which  of  the  following  states  has 
 the largest Buddhist population? 
 (a) Madhya Pradesh     (b) Uttar Pradesh 
 (c) Maharashtra            (d) West Bengal 
 Sol.706.(c)  Maharashtra.  As  per  Census 
 2011:  Among  121.09  crores  population 
 (religions)  are:  Hindu  96.63  crores 
 (79.8%),  Muslim  (14.2%),  Christian 
 (2.3%),  Sikh  (1.7%),  Buddhist  (0.7%),  Jain 
 (0.4%),  others  (0.9%).  Muslims  (14.2%) 
 form  the  largest  religious  minority  in 
 India.

 Q.708.  What  is  the  national  female

 Census of India 2011? 
 (a) 53.26%  (b) 30.02% 
 (c) 25.51%  (d) 53.03% 
 Sol.708.(c)  25.51%.  As  per  the  2011 
 Census,  female  workforce  participation 
 is  25.51%  compared  to  53.26%  for 
 males.  Rural  areas  have  higher  female 
 participation  (30.02%)  than  urban  areas. 
 In  Rural,  Himachal  Pradesh  has  the 
 maximum  female  workforce 
 participation  rate  (47.4)  whereas  Tamil 
 Nadu  has  maximum  (21.8)  among  major 
 states.  There  is  a  significant  gap 
 between  rural  and  urban  female 
 workforce  participation,  though  male 
 participation is similar in both sectors.

 6.7%, and Tamil at 5.7%.

 Q.712.  The  density  of  population  is  the 
 crude  relationship  between  humans  and 
 _________ . 
 (a) land  (b) family  (c) growth  (d) capital 
 Sol.712.(a)  land.  Population  density  is 
 calculated  by  dividing  the  total 
 population  of  a  region  by  the  total  land 
 area.  This  gives  an  idea  of  how  crowded 
 or  sparsely  populated  a  region  is. 
 According  to  Census  2011,  the 
 population  density  of  India  is  382  (per 
 square  km).  State  with  highest 
 population  density  -  Bihar  (1106  person 
 per  square  km).  State  with  lowest 
 population  density  -  Arunachal  Pradesh 
 (17 person per square km).

 Q.715.  What  is  the  name  of  the 
 large-scale  demographic  survey 
 conducted  to  provide  reliable  estimates 
 of  birth  and  death  rates  and  other  fertility 
 and  mortality  indicators  at  the  national 
 and sub-national levels? 
 (a) Sample Registration System (SRS) 
 (b) Enumeration of Human Resource 
 system (EHRS) 
 (c) Direct Recruitment System (DRS) 
 (d) Creation and bifurcation System 
 (CBS) 
 Sol.715.(a)  The  SRS  employs  a  dual 
 record  system  with  a  part-time 
 enumerator  and  a  full-time  supervisor. 
 The  enumerator  continuously  tracks 
 births  and  deaths  in  sample  villages  and 
 urban  blocks,  while  the  supervisor 
 conducts  an  independent  survey  every 
 six  months.  Data  from  both  sources  is 
 matched,  and  unmatched  events  are 
 re-verified.  It  was  launched  by  the  Office 
 of  the  Registrar  General,  India  as  a  pilot 
 in  1964-65,  the  SRS  became  fully 
 operational in 1969-70. 
 Q.716.  Which  of  the  following  is 
 expressed  as  the  total  number  of  deaths 
 per  thousand  population  per  year  in 
 India? 
 (a) Death Rate 
 (b) Age Specific Death Rate 
 (c) Incidence Rate 
 (d) Infant Mortality rate 
 Sol.716.(a)  Death  Rate.  Age-specific 
 mortality  rate  is  defined  as  the  number  of 
 deaths  in  a  specific  age  group  per 
 thousand  populations  in  the  same 
 age-group  in  a  given  year.  The  Crude 
 Death  Rate  (CDR)  is  defined  as  Number 
 of  deaths  per  thousand  population. 
 Infant  mortality  rate  refers  to  the  number 
 of  deaths  under  one  year  of  age  divided 
 by  1000  live  births  in  a  given  year. 
 Maternal  Mortality  Ratio  refers  to  the 
 number  of  women  who  die  as  a  result  of 
 complications  of  pregnancy  or 
 childbearing  in  a  given  year  per  100,000 
 live births in that year. 
 Q.717.  According  to  Census  2011,  what 
 is  the  estimated  percentage  of  people 
 below the poverty line in rural areas? 
 (a) 27.09%  (b) 13.04% 
 (c) 18.90%  (d) 25.70% 
 Sol.717.(d)  25.70%.  In  2011-12,  the 
 poverty  rate  was  estimated  at  13.7%  in 
 urban  areas,  and  21.9%  nationwide.  The 
 Below  Poverty  Line  (BPL)  is  an  economic 
 benchmark  that  identifies  individuals  and 
 households  that  are  economically 
 disadvantaged  and  may  need 
 government  assistance.  It  is  a  minimum 
 level  of  income  required  to  maintain  a 
 basic standard of living. 
 Q.718.  Consider  the  following 
 statements regarding population change: 
 1. 
 The  three  basic  components  of 
 population  change  are  Birth,  Death, 
 and Migration. 
 2. 
 Demography  is  derived  from  the 
 Greek  words  Demos  (Population)  and 
 Graphy (Science). 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.718.(c)  Both  1  and  2.  The  three 
 components  of  population  change  (i) 
 Birth  (ii)  Death  and  (iii)  Migration  are  core 
 to  the  domain  of  population  studies.  The 
 word  Demography  has  been  derived  from 
 the  Greek  words  ‘Demos’  and  ‘Graphy’ 
 which  mean  'Population'  and  ‘Science’. 
 Thus,  demography  is  the  scientific  study 
 of the human population. 
 Q.719.  What  is  the  percentage  share  of 
 the  population  working  in  the  agricultural 
 sector as per Census of India 2011? 
 (a) 0.567  (b) 0.58  (c) 0.546  (d) 0.503 
 Sol.719.(c)  0.546.  As  per  Census  2011, 
 the  total  number  of  agricultural  workers 
 in  the  country  has  increased  from  234.1 
 million  (127.3  million  cultivators  and 
 106.8  million  agricultural  labourers)  in 
 2001  to  263.1  million  (118.8  million 
 cultivators  and  144.3  million  agricultural 
 labourers)  in  2011.  However,  the  share  of 
 the  workforce  engaged  in  the  agriculture 
 sector  (comprising  cultivators  and 
 agricultural  labourers)  has  come  down 
 from  58.2  per  cent  in  2001  to  54.6  per 
 cent in 2011.
