 Q.723.  The  _________  birth  rate  indicates 
 the  number  of  live  births  occurring  during 
 the  year  per  1000  population  estimated 
 at midyear. 
 (a) neutral  (b) positive 
 (c) negative  (d) crude 
 Sol.723.(d)  crude.  Birth  rate  is  the  total 
 number  of  live  births  in  a  particular  area 
 (an  entire  country,  a  state,  a  district  or 
 other  territorial  unit)  during  a  specified 
 period  (usually  a  year)  divided  by  the 
 total  population  of  that  area  in 
 thousands.  Death  rate  is  expressed  as 
 the  number  of  deaths  in  a  given  area 
 during  a  given  time  per  1000  population. 
 Rate  of  natural  increase  refers  to  the 
 difference between the birth rate and the

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 death rate.

 Q.734.  Which  of  the  following  periods  is 
 referred  to  as  a  period  of  stagnant  or 
 stationary  phase  of  growth  of  India's 
 population? 
 (a) 1921-1941  (b) 1901-1921 
 (c) 1961-1981  (d) 1941-1961 
 Sol.734.(b)  1901-1921.  The  year  1921  is 
 known  as  the  year  demographic  divide 
 because  it  is  the  only  census  year  when 
 there  was  a  decrease  in  the  growth  of 
 population. 
 Q.735.  India  can  be  considered  to  be  a 
 country  with  Demographic  Dividend  due 
 to _______. 
 (a) high population above 70 years 
 (b) high population between 15 - 64 years 
 (c) high population between 10 - 20 years 
 (d) high total population 
 Sol.735.(b)  This  working  age  group  must 
 support  itself  as  well  as  those  outside 
 this  age  group  (i.e.,  children  and  elderly 
 people)  who  are  unable  to  work  and  are 
 therefore dependents. 
 Q.736.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 The  period  1951–1981  is  called  the 
 period  of  population  explosion  in 
 India. 
 2. 
 During  this  period,  India’s  population 
 grew from 261 million to 683 million. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.736.(a)  Only  1.  During  this  period  the 
 population  of  India  grew  rapidly  from  361 
 million  (1951)  to  683  million  (1981).  The 
 average  annual  growth  rate  of  this  period 
 was 2.14 percent. 
 Q.737.  What  does  the  Lincoln  index 
 measure? 
 (a) Population Size 
 (b) Population Natality Rate 
 (c) Population Mortality Rate 
 (d) Population Density 
 Sol.737.(a)  Population  Size.  Lincoln 
 index  described  by  Frederick  Charles 
 Lincoln  (in  1930).  Population  Natality 
 Rate  -  The  number  of  births  per  unit  of 
 time  in  a  population,  often  expressed  as 
 births  per  1,000  individuals  per  year. 
 Population  Mortality  Rate  -  The  number 
 of  deaths  per  unit  of  time  in  a  population, 
 often  expressed  as  deaths  per  1,000 
 individuals per year.

 statements:

 1. 
 As  per  Census  classification,  the

 defines  youth  as  persons  in  the  age

 Q.741.  According  to  the  Union  Home 
 Ministry,  the  census  2021  (which  got 
 postponed  due  to  COVID  19)  was 
 supposed  to  collect  data  for  _________  for 
 the first time. 
 (a) Schedule Tribes 
 (b) Schedule Castes 
 (c) Economically weaker sections 
 (d) Other Backward Castes 
 Sol.741.(d)  Other  Backward  Castes. 
 Fifteenth  Census  (2011).  The  Socio  - 
 Economic  and  Caste  Census  (SECC)  was 
 conducted  in  2011  for  the  first  time  since 
 1931.  29  questions  canvassed  during 
 Population Enumeration in census 2011.

 Q.744.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 1. 
 If  the  birth  rate  equals  the  death  rate, 
 the population remains constant. 
 2. 
 If  the  birth  rate  is  higher  than  the 
 death rate, the population decreases. 
 Which of the above is/are incorrect? 
 (a) Only 1  (b) Only 2 
 (c) Both 1 and 2  (d) Neither 1 nor 2 
 Sol.744.(b)  Only  2.  If  the  birth  rate  of  a 
 country  is  more  than  the  death  rate  then 
 the  population  will  increase.  Birth  Rate  - 
 The  number  of  babies  born  every  year 
 per  1000  people  in  a  population.  Death 
 Rate  -  The  number  of  deaths  every  year 
 per  1000  people  in  a  population.  If  Birth 
 Rate  >  Death  Rate:  Population  will 
 increase,  Birth  rate  =  Death  rate: 
 Population  will  remain  constant,  Birth 
 rate  <  Death  rate:  Population  will 
 decrease. 
 Q.745.  Which  of  the  following  factors 
 can affect distribution of population? 
 I.  Geographical factors 
 II.  Social factors 
 III.  Cultural factors 
 IV.  Economic factors 
 (a) I, II, III only  (b) I and II only 
 (c) I, II, III and IV  (d) II and III only 
 Sol.745.(c)  Geographical  Factors  - 
 Availability  of  water,  Landforms,  Climate, 
 Soils.  Economic  Factors  -  Minerals, 
 Urbanisation,  Industrialisation.  Social 
 and  Cultural  Factors  -  Religious  beliefs 
 attract  people,  better  housing,  health 
 facilities,  educational  institutions  and 
 transport systems etc. 
 Q.746.  Settlements  in  which  houses  are 
 built close to each other are called _____. 
 (a) Scattered settlement 
 (b) Compact settlement 
 (c) Line settlement 
 (d) Grid settlement 
 Sol.746.(b)  Compact  settlement.  Grid 
 settlements  are  settlements  in  which 
 houses  are  built  in  a  grid  pattern,  such  as 
 cities.  Scattered  settlements  are 
 settlements  in  which  houses  are  spaced 
 apart,  such  as  forests  or  deserts,  rural 
 areas.  Line  settlements  are  settlements 
 in  which  houses  are  built  along  a  road  or 
 river and canals.

 time  provided  three  codes;  these  are 
 Male-1,  Female-2  and  other-3  for 
 enumeration.

 Q.750.  Which  of  the  following  states  has 
 the  highest  fertility  rate  in  India 
 according to the 2011 census? 
 (a) Kerala  (b) Punjab 
 (c) Maharashtra  (d) Bihar 
 Sol.750.(d)  Bihar.  Total  fertility  rate 
 (TFR):  Total  number  of  children  born  or 
 likely  to  be  born  to  a  woman  in  her 
 lifetime  if  she  were  subject  to  the 
 prevailing  rate  of  age-specific  fertility  in 
 the population. 
 Q.751.  Why  is  there  low  population 
 growth  in  a  society  that  is 
 underdeveloped  and  technologically 
 backward? 
 (a) Because both death rate and birth 
 rate are high 
 (b) Because both death rate and birth 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 rate are low 
 (c) Because such countries usually have 
 strict government policies related to 
 population growth 
 (d) Because people migrate from 
 underdeveloped countries 
 Sol.751.(a)  The  first  stage  is  that  of  low 
 population  growth  in  society  because 
 both  the  death  rate  and  the  birth  rate  are 
 very  high  so  the  difference  between  the 
 two  (or  the  net  growth  rate)  is  low.  Stage 
 II  (Higher  than  death  rate),  Stage  III  (Low 
 birth  rate;  Curves  of  both  death  and  birth 
 rate  get  closer;  Population  growth  rate- 
 Very  low),  and  Stage  IV  (Birth  rate  curve 
 further  dips,  more  than  the  death  rate 
 curve; Net population goes down).

 / living on alms’?

 Census  2015  is  a  study  of  the

 parameters. 
 Q.753.  Growth  rate  of  population  refers 
 to the: 
 (a) difference between birth rate and 
 GDP 
 (b) addition of birth rate and death rate 
 (c) difference between birth rate and 
 death rate 
 (d) difference between death rate and 
 GDP 
 Sol.753.(c)  Population  growth  rate  is  the 
 change  in  the  number  of  individuals  over 
 a  specific  period  of  time.  The  rate  of 
 population  growth  is  the  rate  of  natural 
 increase  combined  with  the  effects  of 
 migration. 
 Q.754.  The distribution of the population 
 according  to  different  types  of 
 occupation is known as __________. 
 (a) occupational structure 
 (b) age composition 
 (c) adolescent population 
 (d) Migration 
 Sol.754.(a)  Occupational  structure  refers 
 to  the  aggregate  distribution  of  the 
 population  according  to  different 
 occupations  in  society,  defined  by  skill 
 level,  and  economic  status.  The  age 
 composition  refers  to  the  number  of 
 people  in  different  age  groups  in  a 
 country. 
 Q.755.  Which  period  in  India  is  referred 
 to as a steady population growth period? 
 (a) 1921-1951  (b) 1921-1941 
 (c) 1921-1961  (d) 1921-1931 
 Sol.755.(a)  1921-1951.  Four  phases  of 
 population  growth  in  India:  Stagnant 
 population  (1901-1921),  Steady  growth 
 stage  (1921-1951),  Rapid  growth  stage 
 (1951-  1981)  and  high  growth  with 
 definite  signs  of  slowing  down 
 (1981-2011).

 Q.757.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Neonatal  mortality  rate  refers  to  the 
 number  of  deaths  during  the  first  30 
 completed  days  of  life  per  1000  live 
 births. 
 II. 
 Infant  mortality  rate  refers  to  the 
 number  of  deaths  of  children  below  1 
 month of age per 1000 live births. 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.757.(d)  Neither  I  nor  II.  The  number 
 of  deaths  during  the  first  28  completed 
 days  of  life  per  1000  live  births  in  a  given 
 year  or  period  is  defined  as  neonatal 
 mortality  rate.  An  age-specific  mortality 
 rate  is  a  mortality  rate  limited  to  a 
 particular  age  group.  The  crude  death 
 rate  is  calculated  as  the  number  of 
 deaths  in  a  given  period  divided  by  the 
 population  exposed  to  risk  of  death  in 
 that  period.  The  Infant  mortality  rate 
 (IMR)  is  the  number  of  deaths  of  infants 
 under  one  year  of  age  per  1,000  live 
 births

 of  fiv e  in  the  country  as  per  Census  of 
 India 2011?

 Sol.758.(b)  55  .  As  per  Census  2011,  the 
 share  of  children  (0-6  years)  accounts  for 
 13.1  %  of  the  total  population  in  the 
 Country. 
 Q.759.  As  per  the  theory  of  demographic 
 transition,  the  post-transitional  stage  of 
 demographic  transition  is  characterised 
 by ______. 
 (a) low and nearly equal birth and death 
 rates 
 (b) falling birth rates and high death 
 rates 
 (c) high and nearly equal birth and death 
 rates 
 (d) falling death rates and high birth 
 Rates 
 Sol.759.(a)  The  demographic  transition 
 model  has  four  stages:  pre-industrial, 
 urbanizing/industrializing,  mature 
 industrial, and post-industrial. 
 Atmosphere 
 Q.760.  Read the following statements: 
 Statement  1:  The  process  of  vertical 
 heating  of  the  atmosphere  is  known  as 
 advection. 
 Statement  2:  The  transfer  of  heat 
 through  horizontal  movement  of  air  is 
 called convection. 
 Statement  3:  'Loo'  winds  in  northern 
 India  during  summer  are  caused  by 
 advection. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct ? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) All three statements 1, 2 and 3 are 
 correct 
 (b) Statements 2 and 3 are correct 
 (c) Only Statements 1 and 2 are correct 
 (d) Only Statement 3 is correct 
 Sol.760.(d)  Only  Statement  3  is  correct. 
 The  vertical  heating  of  the  atmosphere 
 through  the  upward  movement  of  heated 
 air  is  known  as  convection.  The  transfer 
 of  heat  through  the  horizontal  movement 
 of  air  is  called  advection.  The  'Loo'  is  a 
 hot,  dry  wind  that  blows  horizontally 
 across  the  plains  of  northern  India  during 
 summer,  which  is  a  classic  example  of 
 heat transfer by advection. 
 Q.761.  Which  layer  of  the  Earth's 
 atmosphere  is  primarily  responsible  for 
 absorbing  ultraviolet  radiation  from  the 
 Sun? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Thermosphere  (b) Stratosphere 
 (c) Mesosphere  (d) Troposphere 
 Sol.761.(b)  Stratosphere.  The 
 stratosphere,  lying  above  the 
 troposphere  (about  10–50  km  altitude), 
 contains  the  ozone  layer,  which  absorbs 
 most  of  the  Sun’s  harmful  ultraviolet  (UV) 
 radiation.  This  protective  function  shields 
 living  organisms  from  DNA  damage,  skin 
 cancer,  and  other  harmful  effects  of 
 excessive  UV  exposure.  The 
 thermosphere  absorbs  X-rays  and 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 gamma  rays,  while  the  mesosphere 
 mainly  helps  burn  up  meteors.  The 
 troposphere  is  the  lowest  layer  where 
 weather  occurs  but  does  not  absorb 
 significant  UV  radiation.  Thus,  the 
 stratosphere  is  primarily  responsible  for 
 UV absorption. 
 Q.762.  Read the following statements: 
 Statement  1:  The  temperature  in 
 Troposphere  decreases  at  the  rate  of  1°C 
 for every 165 meters of height. 
 Statement  2:  The  temperature  in 
 mesosphere  starts  to  decrease  with  the 
 increase  in  altitude  and  reaches  up  to 
 -100°C at around 80 km. 
 Which  of  the  following  statements  is/are 
 correct  ? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Only Statement 1 is correct 
 (b) Only Statement 2 is correct 
 (c) Both statements 1 and 2 are correct 
 (d) Both statements 1 and 2 are incorrect 
 Sol.762.(c)  Both  statements  1  and  2  are 
 correct.  Statement  1  describes  the 
 Normal  Lapse  Rate  in  the  troposphere, 
 where  temperature  decreases  by 
 approximately  1°C  for  every  165  meters 
 (or  6.5°C  per  kilometer)  of  ascent. 
 Statement  2  is  also  correct;  in  the 
 mesosphere,  temperature  again 
 decreases  with  increasing  altitude, 
 reaching  the  coldest  temperatures  in  the 
 Earth's  atmosphere,  around  -90°C  to 
 -100°C,  at  the  mesopause  (approx.  80-85 
 km). 
 Q.763.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Smog  is  a  dense  mass  of  water  drops 
 mixed  with  smoke  and  dust  particles 
 near the ground. 
 II. 
 Mist  and  fog  differ  mainly  on  the 
 basis of visibility reduction. 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.763.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  Fog  forms 
 when  an  air  mass  with  high  water  vapor 
 suddenly  cools,  causing  condensation  on 
 fine  dust  particles.  It  is  essentially  a 
 cloud  at  or  near  the  ground,  leading  to 
 poor  visibility,  often  reduced  to  zero.  Fog 
 is  denser  and  thicker  than  mist.  Fog 
 reduces  visibility  to  less  than  one 
 kilometer,  while  mist  reduces  visibility  to 
 one  to  two  kilometers.  Frost  forms  on 
 cold  surfaces  when  condensation  takes 
 place  below  freezing  point  (00C),  i.e.  the 
 dew  point  is  at  or  below  the  freezing 
 point. 
 Q.764.  Atmospheric temperature usually 
 decreases  with  altitude,  but  sometimes 
 this  phenomenon  gets  reversed  in  the 
 troposphere.  This  phenomenon  is  known 
 as _________. 
 (a) Temperature Inversion 
 (b) Adiabatic Lapse Rate 
 (c) Global Warming 
 (d) Temperature Convection 
 Sol.764.(a)  Temperature  Inversion 
 occurs  when  the  normal  pattern  of 
 temperature  decreasing  with  altitude  is 
 reversed,  and  the  air  becomes  warmer  as 
 you  go  higher.  This  phenomenon  can 
 lead  to  air  pollution  problems  and  is 
 often  seen  in  valleys  or  areas  with 
 stagnant  air.  Adiabatic  lapse  rate: 
 Change  of  temperature  with  a  change  in 
 altitude  of  an  air  parcel  without  gaining 
 or  losing  any  heat  to  the  environment 
 surrounding  the  parcel.  Temperature 
 Convection  -  The  process  by  which  heat 
 is  transferred  through  the  movement  of 
 fluid  (like  air  or  water)  due  to 
 temperature differences. 
 Q.765.  What  kind  of  radiation  is 
 absorbed by the ozone layer? 
 (a) Green radiation 
 (b) Infrared radiation 
 (c) Visible radiation 
 (d) Ultraviolet radiation 
 Sol.765.(d)  Ultraviolet  radiation.  The  UV 
 radiation  spectrum  is  divided  into  three 
 types  :  UVA,  UVB  and  UVC.  These  rays 
 can  affect  our  skin  in  different  ways. 
 Ozone  layer  :  It  is  a  region  in  the  Earth's 
 stratosphere  that  contains  a  high 
 concentration  of  ozone  (O 
 3 
 )  gas.  This 
 layer  acts  as  a  shield,  protecting  the 
 Earth  from  the  Sun's  harmful  ultraviolet 
 (UV)  radiation.  Infrared  radiation  (IR), 
 also  known  as  thermal  radiation,  is  that 
 band  in  the  electromagnetic  radiation 
 spectrum  with  wavelengths  above  red 
 visible  light  between  780  nm  to  1mm 
 (0.78 μm to 1000 μm). 
 Q.766  .  The  upper  portion  of  the  earth  is 
 known as: 
 (a) Stratosphere       (b) Troposphere 
 (c) Lithosphere  (d) Mesosphere 
 Sol.766.(c)  Lithosphere.  It  is  bounded  by 
 the  atmosphere  above  and  the 
 asthenosphere  (another  part  of  the  upper 
 mantle)  below.  The  stratosphere  is  the 
 second-lowest  layer  of  Earth's 
 atmosphere.  The  troposphere,  the  lowest 
 layer  of  the  atmosphere,  contains  about 
 75-80%  of  the  atmospheric  mass.  Most 
 clouds  form  in  the  troposphere,  where 
 nearly  all  weather  occurs.  The 
 mesosphere  is  the  third  highest  layer, 
 situated  between  the  stratosphere  and 
 the thermosphere. 
 Q.767.  The  zone  separating  the 
 troposphere  from  the  stratosphere  is 
 called the __________. 
 (a) Tropopause  (b) stratospause 
 (c) Stratomerge  (d) Tropomerge 
 Sol.767.(a)  Tropopause.  The  air 
 temperature  at  the  tropopause  is  about  - 
 80 
 o 
 C  over  the  equator  and  about  -  45 
 o 
 C 
 over  the  poles.  The  temperature  in  this 
 region  is  nearly  constant,  and  hence,  it  is 
 called  the  tropopause.  The  stratosphere 
 is  found  above  the  tropopause  and 
 extends  up  to  a  height  of  50  km.  One 
 important  feature  of  the  stratosphere  is 
 that it contains the ozone layer. 
 Q.768.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 All  weather  phenomena  like  rainfall, 
 storms,  and  winds  occur  in  the 
 Thermosphere. 
 II. 
 The  ozone  layer  is  present  in  the 
 Troposphere. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.768.(d)  Neither  I  nor  II.  All  the 
 changes  in  climate  and  weather  takes 
 place  in  the  Troposphere.  It  is  the 
 lowermost  layer  of  the  atmosphere  and 
 is  often  referred  to  as  the  "region  of 
 mixing”.  Its  average  height  is  13  km  and 
 extends  roughly  to  a  height  of  8  km  near 
 the  poles  and  about  18  km  at  the 
 equator.  Stratosphere  (contains  the 
 ozone  layer)  -  2nd  layer  of  the  Earth's 
 atmosphere,  extending  from  about  10  to 
 50  km  in  altitude.  Mesosphere  -  3rd  layer 
 of  the  Earth's  atmosphere,  extending 
 from  about  50  to  85  km  in  altitude.  Most 
 meteors  burn  up  in  this  atmospheric 
 layer.  Thermosphere  -  4th  layer  of  the 
 Earth's  atmosphere,  extending  from 
 about 85 to 500 km in altitude. 
 Q.769.  Which  of  the  following  claims 
 about  chlorofluorocarbons  (CFCs)  is 
 accurate? 
 (a) CFCs are used as repairers of the 
 troposphere 
 (b) CFCs are used as fuel for air crafts 
 (c) CFCs are used as repairers of the 
 ozone layer 
 (d) CFCs are used as refrigerants 
 Sol.769.(d)  CFCs,  banned  in  India  for 
 depleting  the  ozone  layer,  were  used  in 
 plastic  foams,  cleaning  electronics,  and 
 aerosol  cans.  They  are  now  replaced  by 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 HFCs,  which  have  an  ozone  depletion 
 potential of 0. 
 Q.770.  What  is  the  purpose  of  the  Earth's 
 magnetic field? 
 (a) To provide an energy source for life 
 on Earth 
 (b) To control the Earth's climate 
 (c) To shield the Earth from solar wind 
 and cosmic radiation 
 (d) To cause the tides in the ocean 
 Sol.770.(c)  Earth's  magnetic  field 
 (Geomagnetic  field)  -  The  magnetic  field 
 that  extends  from  Earth's  interior  out  into 
 space,  where  it  interacts  with  the  solar 
 wind,  a  stream  of  charged  particles 
 emerging  from  the  Sun.  Earth’s 
 Magnetism  -  Generated  by  convection 
 currents  of  molten  iron  and  nickel  in  the 
 earth’s  core.  These  convection  currents 
 are  caused  by  heat  escaping  from  the 
 core,  a  natural  process  called  a 
 geodynamo. 
 Q.771.  What  is  the  main  objective  of  the 
 'Graded Response Action Plan'? 
 (a) legalizing illegal colonies 
 (b) To control the cases of malaria 
 (c) introduction of electric vehicles 
 (d) controlling air pollution 
 Sol.771.(d)  Controlling  air  pollution. 
 Graded  Response  Action  Plan  (GRAP)  :  It 
 was  formulated  in  2016  and  notified  in 
 2017  for  Delhi  and  the  National  Capital 
 Region  (NCR)  by  the  Ministry  of 
 Environment, Forest and Climate Change. 
 Q.772.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is  NOT  correct  regarding  the 
 stratosphere ? 
 (a) It lies above the mesosphere. 
 (b) It extends up to a height of 50 km. 
 (c) It contains a layer of ozone gas. 
 (d) This layer is almost free from clouds. 
 Sol.772.(a)  Stratosphere  -  Its  height  of 
 the  bottom  varies  with  latitude  and  with 
 the  seasons.  Aviation  &  Jet  Streams  fly  in 
 the Stratosphere. 
 Q.773.  In  which  layer  of  atmosphere 
 does the meteorites burn up on entering 
 from  space? 
 (a) Stratosphere  (b) Thermosphere 
 (c) Mesosphere  (d) Exosphere 
 Sol.773.(c)  Mesosphere.  Meteorites  are 
 space  rocks  that  fall  to  Earth's  surface. 
 Meteorites  are  the  last  stage  in  the 
 existence of these types of space rocks. 
 Q.774.  Which layer of atmosphere helps 
 in radio transmission? 
 (a) Exosphere  (b) Thermosphere 
 (c) Mesosphere  (d) Stratosphere 
 Sol.774.(b)  Thermosphere.  It  is  a  layer  of 
 Earth's  atmosphere  that  is  directly  above 
 the  Mesosphere  and  below  the 
 Exosphere.  The  temperature  starts 
 increasing  again  with  increasing  height  in 
 this  layer.  Aurora  and  satellites  occur  in 
 this layer. 
 Q.775.  At  what  height  do  the  Jet  Streams 
 blow in India during winter months? 
 (a) 9-15 km  (b) 9-17 km 
 (c) 9-16 km  (d) 9-13 km 
 Sol.775.(d)  9-13  km.  Jet  Streams 
 (geostrophic  wind)  are  relatively  narrow 
 bands  of  strong  wind.  Types:  Polar-Night 
 Jet  Stream,  SubTropical  Jet  Streams 
 (westerlies),  Tropical  Easterly  Jet 
 Stream. 
 Q.776.  The  climate  of  Assam  can  be 
 described as: 
 (a) subtropical humid climate 
 (b) mountain climate 
 (c) tropical dry climate 
 (d) tropical monsoon rainforest climate 
 Sol.776.(d)  Tropical Monsoon Rainforest 
 Climate  .  Tropical  monsoon  climates 
 have  monthly  mean  temperatures  above 
 18  °C  (64  °F)  in  every  month  of  the  year 
 and  a  dry  season.  Tropical  monsoon 
 climate  is  the  intermediate  climate 
 between  the  wet  Af  (or  tropical  wet 
 climate)  and  the  drier  Aw  (or  tropical 
 savanna climate). 
 Q.777.  __________  is  a  naturally  occurring 
 phenomenon  that  is  responsible  for  the 
 heating  of  the  Earth’s  surface  and 
 atmosphere. 
 (a) Radiation  (b) Global warming 
 (c) Greenhouse effect (d) Global heating 
 Sol.777.(c)  Greenhouse  Effect  .  When  the 
 Sun's  energy  reaches  the  Earth's 
 atmosphere,  some  of  it  is  reflected  back 
 to  space  and  the  rest  is  absorbed  and 
 re-radiated  by  greenhouse  gases.  The 
 absorbed  energy  warms  the  atmosphere 
 and the surface of the Earth. 
 Q.778.  The  term  ‘monsoon’  is  derived 
 from a/an: 
 (a) Italian word  (b) Latin word 
 (c) Arabic word  (d) Greek word 
 Sol.778.(c)  Arabic  word  'mausim', 
 meaning  weather,  was  first  used  in 
 English  in  British  India  to  refer  to 
 seasonal changes in wind direction. 
 Rocks 
 Q.779.  What  is  Karst  topography 
 primarily associated with? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) Groundwater action in limestone or 
 dolomite regions 
 (b) River meandering in alluvial plains 
 (c) Glacial deposition in polar regions 
 (d) Wind erosion in arid deserts 
 Sol.779.(a)  Groundwater  action  in 
 limestone  or  dolomite  regions.  Karst 
 topography  develops  in  regions  where 
 limestone,  dolomite,  or  gypsum  rocks  are 
 present.  It  is  formed  due  to  the  chemical 
 action  of  groundwater  (carbonation  and 
 solution  processes).  Key  features  of 
 karst  landscapes:  Sinkholes  (dolines), 
 Caves  and  caverns,  Stalactites  and 
 stalagmites,  Underground  streams  and 
 disappearing  rivers.  Famous  example  in 
 India:  Karst  landforms  of  Meghalaya 
 (Krem caves). 
 Q.780.  The  igneous,  sedimentary  and 
 metamorphic  rocks  are  interconvertible 
 through a continuous process called: 
 (a) lithification (b) metamorphism 
 (c) rock cycle   (d) weathering and erosion 
 Sol.780.(c)  rock  cycle.  It  is  a  series  of 
 processes  that  create  and  transform  the 
 types  of  rocks  in  Earth's  crust. 
 Lithification:  This  is  specifically  the 
 process  by  which  sediments  are 
 compacted  and  cemented  to  form 
 sedimentary  rocks.  Metamorphism  is  the 
 process  by  which  existing  rocks 
 (Igneous,  Sedimentary,  or  even  other 
 Metamorphic  rocks)  are  transformed  into 
 metamorphic  rocks  under  high  pressure, 
 and  temperature.  Weathering  and 
 erosion:  These  are  processes  that  break 
 down  existing  rocks  into  smaller 
 particles. 
 Q.781.  The  layer  made  up  of 
 unconsolidated  rock,  dust,  soil,  etc.  that 
 is  deposited  on  a  bed  rock  surface  like  a 
 blanket is known as: 
 (a) Talus  (b) Regolith 
 (c) Scree  (d) Protolith 
 Sol.781.(b)  Regolith.  It  can  be  found 
 both  at  the  surface  and  at  varying 
 depths.  This  layer  of  fragmented  soil 
 material  forms  the  outermost  covering  of 
 the  land  and  is  present  almost 
 everywhere,  covering  the  underlying 
 bedrock.  Regolith  is  primarily  inorganic 
 and  acts  like  a  blanket  over  unbroken 
 rock. 
 Q.782.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Sedimentary  rocks  are  formed  by  the 
 deposition  and  compaction  of 
 sediments, usually in layers. 
 II. 
 Igneous  rocks  are  also  layered  due  to 
 volcanic eruptions. 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.782.(a)  Only  I.  Igneous  rocks 
 (primary  rocks)  form  out  of  magma  and 
 lava  from  the  interior  of  the  earth. 
 Examples  -  Granite,  gabbro,  pegmatite. 
 Sedimentary  rocks  are  formed  by  the 
 deposition  and  compaction  of  sediments 
 over  time,  often  in  layers.  These  layers, 
 also  called  strata.  Examples  -  Sandstone, 
 conglomerate,  limestone,  shale,  loess. 
 Metamorphic  Rocks  form  under  the 
 action  of  pressure,  volume  and 
 temperature  changes.  Examples  - 
 Gneissoid, granite, syenite, slate. 
 Q.783.  Exfoliation is a form of _________. 
 (a) physical weathering 
 (b) chemical weathering 
 (c) mass wasting 
 (d) biochemical weathering 
 Sol.783.(a)  Physical  weathering  is 
 caused  by  the  effects  of  changing 
 temperature  on  rocks,  causing  the  rock 
 to  break  apart.  The  process  is 
 sometimes  assisted  by  water.  Exfoliation 
 occurs  as  cracks  develop  parallel  to  the 
 land  surface,  a  consequence  of  the 
 reduction  in  pressure  during  uplift  and 
 erosion. 
 Q.784.  The  outermost  part  of  the  Earth  is 
 known as __________. 
 (a) volcano  (b) the crust 
 (c) the core  (d) the mantle 
 Sol.784.(b)  the  crust.  The  Earth's  crust  is 
 the  thinnest  of  all  its  layers,  about  35  km 
 thick  on  the  continental  masses  and  only 
 5  km  on  the  ocean  floors.  The  main 
 minerals  in  the  continental  crust  are 
 silica  and  alumina,  while  the  oceanic 
 crust  consists  primarily  of  silica  and 
 magnesium.  Beneath  the  crust  lies  the 
 mantle,  which  extends  to  a  depth  of  2900 
 km.  The  innermost  layer  is  the  core,  with 
 a  radius  of  about  3500  km,  made  mostly 
 of nickel and iron. 
 Q.785.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 correct  match  between  column-A  and 
 column-B? 
 Column-A 
 (Type of rock) 
 Column-B 
 (Method of formation) 
 i. Sedimentary 
 Rock 
 a. Transformed its 
 chemical qualities 
 from other type of 
 rocks 
 ii. Metamorphic 
 Rock 
 b. Derived from  magma 
 that was poured out 
 or ejected at the 
 Earth's Surface 
 iii. Igneous 
 Rock 
 c. Formed from 
 fragments of other 
 rocks or the remains 
 of plants or animals 
 (a) i-a, ii-b, iii-c  (b) i-b, ii-a, iii-c 
 (c) i-c, ii-a, iii-b  (d) i-a, ii-c, iii-b 
 Sol.785.(c)  i-c,  ii-a,  iii-b.  The  rock  cycle 
 is  a  web  of  processes  that  outlines  how 
 each  of  the  three  major  rock  types  - 
 igneous,  metamorphic,  and  sedimentary  - 
 form  and  break  down  based  on  the 
 different  applications  of  heat  and 
 pressure over time. 
 Q.786.  Structural geology deals with: 
 (a) the age of rocks 
 (b) the components and chemical nature 
 of soil 
 (c) the form, classification, mechanism, 
 and causes of rock structures’ 
 evolution 
 (d) the cause of volcano formation 
 Sol.786.(c)  Petrology  is  the  science  of 
 rocks.  Rock:  Any  natural  mass  of  mineral 
 matter that makes up the Earth's crust. 
 Q.787.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 yellowish  green  to  green  coloured 
 gemstone  often  found  in  mafic  and 
 ultramafic  igneous  rocks  such  as  basalt, 
 gabbro, dunite, diabase and peridotite? 
 (a) Bornite                     (b) Olivine 
 (c) Tremolite                 (d) Garnet 
 Sol.787.(b)  Olivine.  The  mineral  olivine  is 
 a  magnesium  iron  silicate  with  the 
 chemical  formula  (Mg,Fe) 
 2 
 SiO 
 4 
 .  It  is  a 
 type  of  nesosilicate  or  orthosilicate. 
 Bornite,  also  known  as  peacock  ore,  is  a 
 sulfide  mineral  with  a  chemical 
 composition of Cu 
 5 
 FeS 
 4 
 . 
 Q.788.  The  steep  rocky  coast  rising 
 almost  vertically  above  sea  water  is 
 called ______. 
 (a) sea caves  (b) sea cliff 
 (c) sea arches  (d) ox-bow lake 
 Sol.788.(b)  Sea  cliffs  are  steep  faces  of 
 rock  and  soil  that  are  formed  by 
 destructive  waves.  A  sea  cave  is  a  cave 
 formed  on  a  cliff  by  the  wave  action  of 
 an  ocean  or  lake.  A  sea  arch  is  a  natural 
 arch  or  bridge  made  of  stone  that  has 
 been  created  when  water  wears  away  the 
 underside  of  a  rock,  leaving  just  the  top 
 behind. 
 Q.789.  The core of the Great Himalayas 
 is composed of ______. 
 (a) Slate  (b) Granite 
 (c) Sandstone  (d) Limestone 
 Sol.789.(b) Granite rock  (Igneous rock)  . 
 Lesser  Himalaya  consists  of 
 metamorphosed  sedimentary  rocks 
 (quartzite,  marble,  slate,  phyllite,  schist 
 and gneiss). 
 Q.790.  Which  of  the  following 
 determines  the  colour,  texture,chemical 
 properties,  mineral,  content  and 
 permeability of the soil? 
 (a) Parent rock  (b) Time 
 (c) Relief  (d) Climate 
 Sol.790.(a)  Parent  rock  .  The  color  in  the 
 soil  is  mainly  due  to  two  factors  – 
 organic  content  and  the  chemical  nature 
 of iron compounds found in the soil. 
 Q.791.  How many types of igneous rocks 
 are there? 
 (a) Four    (b) Two    (c) Six    (d) Five 
 Sol.791.(b)  Two.  Based  on  the  texture  of 
 rocks,  igneous  rocks  are  classified  into 
 Intrusive  igneous  rocks  (Plutonic  rocks) 
 and  Extrusive  igneous  rocks  (Lava  or 
 Volcanic rocks). 
 Q.792.  Mushroom  rocks  are  formed  due 
 to _____. 
 (a) glacial deposition 
 (b) deposition of sea waves 
 (c) running water erosion 
 (d) wind erosion 
 Sol.792.(d)  Wind  erosion.  A  mushroom 
 rock  (perched  rock)  is  a  typical 
 mushroom-shaped  landform  that  is 
 formed  by  the  action  of  wind  erosion. 
 These  rocks  are  mostly  found  in  arid 
 regions.  Such  rocks  are  found  in  the  Thar 
 Desert of India. 
 Q.793.  What  type  of  rocks  were  once 
 igneous  or  sedimentary,  but  have 
 metamorphosed  as  a  result  of  intense 
 heat  and/or  pressure  within  the  Earth's 
 crust? 
 (a) Granite  (b) Quartzite 
 (c) Sandstone  (d) Basalt 
 Sol.793.(b)  Quartzite.  Granite  is  a 
 coarse-grained  (phaneritic)  intrusive 
 igneous  rock  composed  mostly  of 
 quartz,  alkali  feldspar,  and  plagioclase. 
 Sandstone  is  a  clastic  sedimentary  rock 
 composed  mainly  of  sand-sized  silicate 
 grains.  Basalt  is  a  mafic  extrusive  rock,  is 
 the  most  widespread  of  all  igneous 
 rocks,  and  comprises  more  than  90%  of 
 all volcanic rocks. 
 Q.794.  Which  of  the  following  rocks 
 floats in water? 
 (a) Granite  (b) Obsidian 
 (c) Pumice  (d) Slate 
 Sol.794.(c)  Pumice  is a light-colored, 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 extremely  porous  igneous  rock  that 
 forms  during  explosive  volcanic 
 eruptions.  Obsidian  has  been  used  for 
 tools  and  ornaments.  Granite  is  a  hard 
 igneous  rock  made  of  quartz,  feldspar, 
 and mica. Slate is a metamorphic rock. 
 Q.795.  ________  plains  are  formed  due  to 
 the  weathering  of  soluble  rocks  such  as 
 limestone and  dolomite. 
 (a) Glacial  (b) Karst 
 (c) Deposition  (d) Desert 
 Sol.795.(b)  Karst  regions  contain 
 aquifers  that  are  capable  of  providing 
 large  supplies  of  water.  Typical  karst 
 forms  include  sinkholes,  caves,  natural 
 bridges and sinking streams. 
 Mountain 
 Q.796.  Read the following statements: 
 Statement  1:  Diastrophism  includes  all 
 processes  that  move,  elevate,  or  build  up 
 portions of the Earth's crust. 
 Statement  2:  Exogenic  processes  derive 
 their  energy  from  the  atmosphere, 
 influenced  by  solar  energy  and  tectonic 
 gradients. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Only Statement 1 is correct 
 (b) Both statements 1 and 2 are correct 
 (c) Both statements 1 and 2 are incorrect 
 (d) Only Statement 2 is correct 
 Sol.796.(b)  Both  statements  1  and  2  are 
 correct.  Diastrophism  encompasses  all 
 slow,  large-scale  movements,  such  as 
 uplift  and  folding,  that  deform  and  build 
 up  portions  of  the  Earth's  crust,  leading 
 to  mountain  building  (orogeny)  and 
 continental  warping  (epeirogeny). 
 Exogenic  processes  derive  their  energy 
 from  both  the  atmosphere,  powered  by 
 solar  energy,  and  from  tectonic  factors 
 that  create  gradients.  The  Sun's  energy 
 influences  atmospheric  conditions  like 
 temperature  and  precipitation,  which  are 
 key components of weather and climate. 
 Q.797.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 K2  is  the  highest  peak  of  the 
 Karakoram Range. 
 II.  Nanda Devi lies in the Zanskar Range. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.797.(a)  Only  I.  K2,  also  known  as 
 Mount  Godwin  Austen  (8,611  meters),  is 
 the  world’s  second-highest  peak  in  the 
 Karakoram  range,  spanning  Pakistan, 
 China,  India,  and  extending  into 
 Afghanistan  and  Tajikistan.  Trishul  Peak 
 is  in  the  Himalayas,  while  Kamet  Peak 
 (7,756  meters)  is  the  highest  in  the 
 Zanskar  range.  Nanda  Devi  (7,817 
 meters)  is  the  third-highest  peak  in  the 
 Himalayas. 
 Q.798.  Which  of  the  following  mountains 
 of  India  is  famous  for  tea  and  coffee 
 production? 
 (a) Nilgiri  (b) Aravali 
 (c) Himalaya  (d) Vidhyanchal 
 Sol.798.(a)  Nilgiri  Hills  are  where  the 
 Western  Ghats  and  Eastern  Ghats  meet. 
 They  are  located  at  the  tri-junction  of 
 Karnataka,  Kerala,  and  Tamil  Nadu.  The 
 highest peak in this region is Doda Betta. 
 The  Aravalli  Range  is  known  for  its 
 mineral  resources,  such  as  copper  and 
 zinc.  The  Himalayas  are  famous  for 
 saffron cultivation in the Kashmir region. 
 Q.799.  What  is  the  name  of  the  highest 
 peak  of  Indian  Garhwal  Himalayas 
 located  in  Chamoli  district  of 
 Uttarakhand  state  with  a  height  of  7,816 
 metres ? 
 (a) Trisul  (b) Hardeol 
 (c) Mukut Parbat  (d) Nanda Devi 
 Sol.799.(d)  Nanda  Devi.  It  is  the 
 second-highest  mountain  in  India,  after 
 Kangchenjunga  (8598  m),  and  the 
 highest  entirely  within  the  country.  It 
 ranks  as  the  23rd  highest  peak  in  the 
 world.  The  Garhwal  Himalayas  lie 
 between  the  Sutlej  and  the  Kali  river  and 
 is  320  km  long.  It  is  a  part  of  the 
 Himalayan  Shivalik  Hills  (outermost  hills 
 of  the  Himalayas  found  in  Himachal 
 Pradesh and Uttarakhand). 
 Q.800.  The  Garo,  Khasi  and  Jaintia  hills 
 are a subdivision of: 
 (a) Assam valley    (b) Barak basin 
 (c) Surma basin     (d) Meghalaya plateau 
 Sol.800.(d)  Meghalaya  Plateau.  The 
 plateau  is  separated  from  the  main  block 
 of  the  peninsular  plateau  by  a  gap  called 
 the  Garo-RajMahal  gap.  It  is  located  on 
 the  northeastern  flank  of  Peninsular 
 India.  It  is  one  of  the  rainiest,  most 
 tectonically  active  areas  in  the  world. 
 Hills  in  North  East  India  -  Lushai  Hills  or 
 Mizo  Hills  (Mizoram  and  Tripura),  Chin 
 Hills  (Manipur  to  Chin  State  of  Myanmar), 
 Japfu  Mountain  Range  (Nagaland),  Barail 
 Range  (Assam),  Dafla  Hills  and  Abor  Hills 
 (Arunachal Pradesh). 
 Q.801.  The  holy  mountain  'Kailash'  is 
 located in which neighbouring country of 
 India ? 
 (a) Pakistan                (b) Afghanistan 
 (c) Bangladesh          (d) China 
 Sol.801.(d)  China.  Mount  Kailash  - 
 Located  near  Lake  Mansarovar  and  Lake 
 Rakshastal.  The  Indus  River  originates 
 from  Mansarovar  lake  (Tibet).  Some 
 highest  mountain  peaks  :  India  - 
 Kanchenjunga  (8598m),  Nanda  Devi 
 (7817m).  Nepal  -  Mount  Everest  (8848.86 
 m),  Dhaulagiri  (8172m).  Hindu  temple 
 outside  India  :  Nepal  -  Pashupatinath 
 Temple,  Indonesia  -  Prambanan  Temple, 
 Bangladesh  -  Dhakeshwari  temple, 
 Cambodia - Angkor Wat. 
 Q.802  .  Name  an  oval-shaped  range  of 
 hills sculpted by glacial flow. 
 (a) Sandur  (b) Drumlins 
 (c) Moraines  (d) Esker 
 Sol.802.(b)  Drumlins  -  It  is  formed  by  the 
 filling  of  heavy  rocky  debris  in  the  glacial 
 crevices  and  its  stay  under  the  ice.  One 
 end  of  the  drumlins  facing  the  glacier 
 called  the  stoss  end  and  the  other  end 
 called  tail.  Eskers:  When  glaciers  melt  in 
 summer,  the  water  flows  on  the  surface 
 of  the  ice.  These  waters  accumulate 
 beneath  the  glacier  and  flow  like  streams 
 in a channel beneath the ice.

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Q.805.  The  Himalayan  Mountain  range 
 was formed by the collision of ________. 
 (a) African Plate and Australian Plate 
 (b) Indian Plate and African Plate 
 (c) Eurasian Plate and African Plate 
 (d) Indian Plate and Eurasian Plate 
 Sol.805.(d)  Indian  Plate  and  Eurasian 
 Plate.  Plates  -  They  are  large,  rigid  pieces 
 of  the  Earth's  lithosphere  that  fit  together 
 like  a  jigsaw  puzzle,  and  their 
 movements  are  responsible  for  shaping 
 the  Earth's  surface  through  processes 
 such  as  earthquakes  and  the  formation 
 of  mountains.  Major  plates  (7)  -  African 
 Plate,  Antarctic  Plate,  Eurasian  Plate, 
 North  American  Plate,  South  American 
 Plate,  Pacific  Plate  and  Indo-Australian 
 Plate. 
 Q.806.  Dhinodhar  Hills  is  located  in

 (b) Maharashtra 
 (c) Gujarat 
 (d) West Bengal

 tourist  and  pilgrimage  spot.  Other  hills  in 
 Gujarat  -  Chotila,  Pavagadh,  Palitana, 
 Saputara,  Girnar,  Bardo,  Gabbar.  Hills  : 
 Maharashtra  -  Mahabaleshwar, 
 Panchgani,  Matheran,  Lonavala,  Amboli 
 etc.  West  Bengal  -  Gorgaburu  Hill, 
 Ajodhya  Hills,  Susunia  Hill  etc.  Andaman 
 and  Nicobar  Islands  -  Ross  Island,  Barren 
 Island,  Saddle  Peak,  Katchal,  Narcondam 
 Island etc.

 Q.808.  Which  of  the  following 
 statements is NOT correct? 
 (a) Mahendragiri is the highest peak in the 
 Eastern Ghats. 
 (b) The height of the Western Ghats 
 progressively decreases from north to 
 south. 
 (c) The Western Ghats cause orographic 
 rain. 
 (d) The Eastern Ghats stretch from the 
 Mahanadi Valley to the Nilgiris in the 
 south. 
 Sol.808.(b)  The  height  of  the  Western 
 Ghats  progressively  increases  from  north 
 to  south.  The  highest  peaks  of  Western 
 Ghats  include  the  Anai  Mudi  (2,695 
 metres)  and  the  Doda  Betta  (2,637 
 meters).The  Western  Ghats,  also  known 
 as  the  Sahyadri  Hills,  Mainly  traverse  the 
 States  of  Kerala,  Tamil  Nadu,  Karnataka, 
 Goa, Maharashtra and Gujarat. 
 Q.809.  Which  among  the  following  is  the

 (c) DehraDun  (d) Kotli Dun

 Width  -  22-25  km).  Dun-  Valleys  that  lie 
 between  the  Shivalik  and  Lesser 
 himalaya.  Other  duns  of  Uttarakhand  - 
 Kota  dun,  Patli  dun,  Har  ki  dun.  Kotli  dun  -

 Q.810.  Kalsubai  peak  is  located  in  which 
 of the following Indian states?

 (c) Andhra Pradesh   (d) Himachal Pradesh

 Q.811.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT  a 
 type of a fold mountain? 
 (a) Aravali range  (b) The Rhine Valley 
 (c) Appalachians  (d) Ural Mountains 
 Sol.811.(b)  Rhine  Valley  .  Fold  mountains 
 are  the  most  common  type  of  mountain 
 in  the  world.  The  rugged,  soaring  heights 
 of  the  Himalayas,  Andes,  Alps  and 
 Aravalli  Range  (oldest  range)  are  all 
 active  fold  mountains.  Rhine  Valley 
 (Germany)  is  the  valley  of  the  river  Rhine 
 in  Europe.  It  is  also  known  as  Alpine 
 Rhine.  The  Appalachian  Mountains  are 
 found in North America. 
 Q.812.  Match the Following 
 List I (Peak)        List II (Height in metres) 
 A. Mount Everest                (i) 8,598 m 
 B. Kanchenjunga                (ii) 8,172 m 
 C. Makalu                            (iii) 8,848 m 
 D. Dhaulagiri                       (iv) 8,481 m 
 (a) A–iii, B–i, C–iv, D–ii 
 (b) A–ii, B–iv, C–i, D–iii 
 (c) A–iv, B–iii, C–ii, D–i 
 (d) A–i, B–ii, C–iii, D–iv 
 Sol.812.(a)  A–iii,  B–i,  C–iv,  D–ii.  The 
 great  Himalayan  range  width  varies 
 between  160-400  km  from  north  to 
 south.  Famous  peaks  of  Himalayas: 
 Mount  Everest  (8848  m),  Mt. 
 Kanchenjunga  (8598  m),  Mt.  Makalu 
 (8481  m),  Mt.  Dhaulagiri  (8172  m).  The 
 Himalayas  consist  of  three  parallel 
 ranges  -  Himadri  (Great  Himalayas  or  the 
 Higher  Himalayas),  Himachal  (Lesser 
 Himalayas  or  the  Lower  Himalayas)  and 
 Shiwaliks (Outer Himalayas). 
 Q.813.  What  is  the  average  height  of 
 inner Himalayas? 
 (a) 6000 metres  (b) 4500 metres 
 (c) 5200 metres  (d) 7100 metres 
 Sol.813.(a)  6000  metres.  The  Himalayas 
 consist  of  three  parallel  ranges,  the 
 Greater  Himalayas  or  the  Inner 
 Himalayas  or  the  Himadri,  the  Lesser 
 Himalayas  called  the  Himachal  (with  an 
 average  height  of  3,700  to  4500  meters), 
 and  the  Shivalik  hills  (with  an  average 
 height  of  900  to  1100  meters),  which 
 comprise the foothills. 
 Q.814.  Asia  is  separated  from  Europe  by 
 ______ mountains. 
 (a) Himalayan     (b) Andes Mountains 
 (c) Alps                (d) Ural 
 Sol.814.(d)  Ural  .  It  is  formed  as  a  result 
 of  the  compression  of  tectonic  plates, 
 leading  to  the  formation  of  large  fold-like 
 structures  on  the  Earth's  crust.  Alps 
 mountain  (natural  boundary  between 
 France  and  Italy),  Andes  Mountains 
 range  (South  American  countries  of  Chile 
 and  Argentina),  Himalaya  (plains  of  the 
 Indian  subcontinent  from  the  Tibetan 
 Plateau).  Caucasus  mountains  (Asia  and 
 Europe). 
 Q.815.  Which  of  the  following  have 
 mountain range young fold mountains? 
 (a) Appalachians mountain range 
 (b) Alps mountain range 
 (c) Aravali mountain range 
 (d) Ural mountain range 
 Sol.815.(b)  Alps  mountain  range.  Fold 
 mountains  form  when  two  or  more  of  the 
 Earth's  tectonic  plates  collide.  Types  - 
 Anticline,  Syncline,  Monocline,  Isocline. 
 Features  -  Rugged  relief,  imposing  height 
 (lofty),  high  conical  peaks.  Examples  are 
 the  Rockies,  the  Andes,  the  Alps,  the 
 Himalayas,  etc.  Old  fold  Mountain  -  An 
 Appalachian  mountain  range,  Aravali 
 mountain  range,  and  Ural  mountain 
 range. 
 Q.816.  The  uplifted  blocks  of  block 
 mountains are called ______. 
 (a) horsts  (b) grabens 
 (c) slopes  (d) cones 
 Sol.816.(a)  Horsts.  Block  mountains 
 are  formed  due  to  the  upward  movement 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 of  the  middle  block  between  two  normal 
 faults.  The  up-thrown  block  is  also  called 
 a  horst.  Graben  is  bounded  by  normal 
 faults  dipping  toward  each  other.  These 
 mountains  include  the  Great  African  Rift 
 Valley  ,  the  Rhine  Valley  (graben)  in 
 Germany, etc. 
 Q.817.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  about  mountain  passes  in 
 India: 
 I.  Fotu La is the highest point in Ladakh. 
 II. 
 Namika  La  is  located  in  Himachal 
 Pradesh. 
 III. 
 Rohtang  Pass  is  in  Himachal 
 Pradesh. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 incorrect? 
 (a) Only II  (b) Only I 
 (c) Only III  (d) I and II 
 Sol.817.(a)  Only  II.  Namika  La  is  a  high 
 mountain  pass  in  the  Zaskar  Range  of 
 the  Himalayas  in  Ladakh.  Fatu  La  is  a 
 mountain  pass  on  the  Srinagar-Leh 
 highway  in  the  Zanskar  Range  of  the 
 Himalayas  in  Ladakh,  UT.  It  is  one  of  two 
 high mountain passes between Leh and 
 Kargil, the other being Namika La. 
 Q.818.  Which  of  the  following  is  also 
 known as the ‘White Mountain’? 
 (a) Cho Oyu  (b) Makalu 
 (c) Dhaulagiri  (d) Lhotse 
 Sol.818.(c)  Dhaulagiri  is  in  west-central 
 Nepal.  Cho  Oyu  lies  on  the  Nepal-Tibet 
 border.  Makalu  is  southeast  of  Everest, 
 on  the  Nepal-Tibet  border.  Lhotse  is  on 
 the  border  between  Tibet  and  Nepal's 
 Khumbu region. 
 Q.819.  Which  of  the  following 
 statement(s)  is/are  correct  regarding 
 mountains? 
 I. 
 Mountains  may  be  arranged  in  a  line 
 known as range. 
 II. 
 The  Aravali  range  in  India  is  one  of 
 the  oldest  fold  mountain  systems  in 
 the world. 
 III. 
 Mt.  Kilimanjaro  in  South  America  is 
 an example of Volcanic Mountain 
 (a) Only I and II  (b) Only II 
 (c) I, II and III  (d) Only I 
 Sol.819.(a)  Only  I  and  II.  Mount 
 Kilimanjaro  is  a  dormant  volcano  in 
 Tanzania.  It  is  the  highest  mountain  in 
 Africa  .  It  was  formed  when  molten  rock 
 from  the  depths  of  the  earth  rose  from 
 the  crust  and  piled  up  on  its  own.  Top 
 mountains  in  Africa:-  Mount  Kenya, 
 Mount  Stanley,  Mount  Speke,  Mount 
 Baker, Mount Emin.

 Q.823.  Mountaineers  refer  to  altitudes 
 above about _______ meters as the ‘death 
 zone’. 
 (a) 5,800  (b) 8,000  (c) 6,700  (d) 7,200 
 Sol.823.(b)  8,000.  The  death  zone 
 refers  to  altitudes  where  oxygen  pressure 
 is  too  low  to  sustain  human  life  for 
 extended periods. 
 Q.824.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 mountain  peak  that  rises  above  the 
 surrounding  peaks  at  the  mountainous 
 border  of  the  Indian  state  of  Nagaland 
 and the Sagaing region of Myanmar? 
 (a) Mount Kalsubai  (b) Mount Doddabetta 
 (c) Mount Saramati (d) Mount Dhupgarh 
 Sol.824.(c)  Mount  Saramati.  Doddabetta 
 is  the  highest  mountain  in  the  Nilgiri 
 Mountains  at  2,637  meters.  Kalsubai 
 (highest  point)  is  a  mountain  in  the 
 Western  Ghats,  located  in  the  Indian 
 state of Maharashtra. 
 Q.825.  Generally,  a  steep  hill  with  an 
 elevation  of  more  than  _______  meters  is 
 classified as a mountain. 
 (a) 600      (b) 500      (c) 1000      (d) 800 
 Sol.825.(a)  600.  Within  the  earth,  a 
 continuous  movement  is  taking  place. 
 The  internal  process  leads  to  the 
 upliftment  and  sinking  of  the  earth's 
 surface  at  several  places.  The  external 
 process  is  the  continuous  wearing  down 
 and  rebuilding  of  the  land  surface.  Eg: 
 Erosion and Deposition. 
 Q.826.  _______  is  created  by  the  collision 
 of convergent plate boundaries. 
 (a) Mid-ocean ridge (b) Land erosion 
 (c) Oceanic trench   (d) Mountain building 
 Sol.826.(d)  Mountain  building  .  A 
 convergent  boundary  is  an  area  on  earth 
 where  two  or  more  lithospheric  plates 
 converge  or  collide  and  one  plate 
 eventually slides beneath the other. 
 Q.827.  'Pidurutalagala'  is  the  highest 
 mountain peak in ______. 
 (a) Bhutan  (b) Sri Lanka 
 (c) Myanmar  (d) Nepal 
 Sol.827.(b)  Sri  Lanka  .  Pidurutalagala 
 (Mount  Pedro)  is  the  tallest  mountain  in 
 Sri  Lanka,  standing  at  2,524  m  in  Nuwara 
 Eliya.

 Volcano 
 Q.835.  Which  Indian  plateau  is  formed 
 primarily by volcanic activity ? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Malwa Plateau 
 (b) Shillong Plateau 
 (c) Chota Nagpur Plateau 
 (d) Deccan Plateau 
 Sol.835.(d)  Deccan  Plateau.  The  Deccan 
 Plateau  was  formed  due  to  volcanic 
 activity  around  65  million  years  ago.  The 
 eruption  created  the  Deccan  Traps,  one 
 of  the  largest  volcanic  features  on  Earth. 
 Malwa  Plateau  -  Formed  by  lava  flow 
 partly,  but  not  the  largest  volcanic 
 plateau.  Shillong  Plateau  -  Tectonic 
 origin.  Chota  Nagpur  Plateau  -  Rich  in 
 minerals,  formed  due  to  uplift  and 
 erosion. 
 Q.836.  Consider  the  following  statements 
 regarding volcanic eruptions: 
 I. 
 Caldera  eruptions  are  the  most 
 explosive  type  of  volcanic  activity  on 
 Earth. 
 II. 
 Flood  basalt  provinces,  like  the 
 Deccan  Traps,  are  examples  of  highly 
 explosive eruptions. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.836.(a)  Only  I.  A  caldera  eruption  is 
 the  most  explosive  type  of  volcanic 
 activity,  occurring  when  a  volcano's 
 magma  chamber  empties  and  the 
 ground  above  collapses,  forming  a  large, 
 circular  depression.  Example: 
 Yellowstone  Caldera.  Dykes:  It  is  a 
 vertical  or  steeply  inclined  geological 
 formation  that  occurs  when  magma  cuts 
 through  rock  layers  and  solidifies.  Flood 
 basalt  provinces:  Flood  basalt  refers  to 
 large,  extensive  lava  flows  that  spread 
 out  over  vast  areas.  Example:  Deccan 
 Traps in India. 
 Q.837.  What  is  the  name  of  the  process 
 of  gases  being  outpoured  from  the 
 interior of the solid earth ? 
 (a) Evaporation  (b) Transpiration 
 (c) Degassing  (d) Gas evolution 
 Sol.837.(c)  Degassing  is  the  process  by 
 which  gases  are  released  from  the 
 interior  of  the  solid  Earth,  typically 
 through  volcanic  activity  or  other 
 geological  processes.  This  includes 
 gases  such  as  water  vapor,  carbon 
 dioxide,  sulfur  dioxide,  and  others  that 
 are  expelled  from  magma  or  other 
 subsurface materials. 
 Q.838  .  Which  of  the  following 
 statements  is  best  related  to  the  'pacific 
 ring of fire'? 
 (a) Known for thermal power station 
 (b) Known for volcano and earthquakes 
 (c) Known for only glacial formations 
 (d) Known for tropical rainforests 
 Sol.838.(b)  The  'ring  of  fir e'  is  a  ring  of 
 volcanoes in the Pacific Ocean. 
 World Geography and Map 
 Q.839.  Which  is  the  largest  island  of 
 India in terms of area? 
 SSC Stenographer 6/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Rameswaram 
 (b) Great Nicobar Island 
 (c) Sriharikota 
 (d) Majuli 
 Sol.839.(b)  Great  Nicobar  Island.  Great 
 Nicobar  Island,  located  in  the 
 southernmost  part  of  the  Andaman  and 
 Nicobar  Islands,  is  the  largest  island  of 
 India  with  an  area  of  about  1045  sq  km. 
 It  is  strategically  important  as  it  lies 
 close  to  the  Malacca  Strait. 
 Rameswaram  is  a  small  island  in  Tamil 
 Nadu,  Sriharikota  is  a  barrier  island  in 
 Andhra  Pradesh  housing  ISRO’s  launch 
 centre,  and  Majuli  in  Assam  is  the  world’s 
 largest  river  island  but  not  the  largest 
 overall island of India. 
 Q.840.  Where  is  the  Gulf  of  Mannar 
 located? 
 (a) Between the south-eastern tip of India 
 and the eastern coast of Sri Lanka 
 (b) Between the south-eastern tip of 
 India and the western coast of Sri 
 Lanka 
 (c) Between the south-eastern tip of India 
 and the north-western coast of Sri 
 Lanka 
 (d) Between the south-eastern tip of 
 India and the southern coast of Sri 
 Lanka 
 Sol.840.(b)  Gulf  of  Mannar:  It  is  located 
 in  the  Coromandel  Coast  region.  The  Gulf 
 of  Mannar  is  separated  from  the  Palk 
 Bay  by  a  chain  of  islands  and  coral  reefs 
 known  as  Adam's  Bridge  or  Ram  Setu.  It 
 is  bound  to  the  northeast  by 
 Rameswaram  (island),  Adam’s  (Rama’s) 
 Bridge  (a  chain  of  shoals),  and  Mannar 
 Island.

 Sol.841.(a)  Only  I.  Bosporus  Strait  (the

 Q.842.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 largest  inhabited  riverine  island  in  the 
 world?

 (c) Bhavani  (d) Lakshadweep

 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 island  in  the  country  and  is  quite  close  to 
 Jorhat.  The  island  is  formed  by  the 
 Brahmaputra  River  in  the  south  and  the

 Q.843.  A  long,  winding  ridge  of  stratified 
 sand and gravel is known as: 
 (a) Moraine  (b) esker 
 (c) Aquifer  (d) Erratic 
 Sol.843.(b)  Esker.  An  esker  is  formed  by 
 meltwater  flowing  beneath  a  retreating 
 glacier  or  ice  sheet  during  the  last  Ice 
 Age.  This  debris  was  deposited  through 
 tunnels  and  channels  under  the  ice, 
 leaving  behind  a  hill  or  mound  once  the 
 ice  melted.  Eskers  are  commonly  found 
 in  regions  that  were  once  glaciated,  such 
 as  Canada,  Northern  Europe,  and  parts  of 
 the United States. 
 Q.844.  How  much  percentage  of  Earth's 
 water make up the oceans? 
 (a) 57%  (b) 7%  (c) 37%  (d) 97% 
 Sol.844.(d)  97%.  Water  covers  71 
 percent  of  Earth's  surface.  Distribution  of 
 Earth’s  water:  Oceans  (97.2%),  Glaciers 
 (2.1%),  Groundwater  (0.61  percent); 
 Freshwater  lakes  (0.009  percent),  Inland 
 seas  (0.008  percent),  Soil  Moisture 
 (0.005  percent),  Atmosphere  (0.001 
 percent), Rivers (0.0001 percent). 
 Q.845.  Which  of  the  following  are  some 
 regions  in  the  world,  known  for  their 
 thriving citrus fruit production? 
 (a) Africa           (b) Equatorial regions 
 (c) Caribbean   (d) Mediterranean regions 
 Sol.845.(d)  Mediterranean  regions  - 
 These  are  known  as  ‘Orchards  of  the 
 world’.  Plants  grow  in  Mediterranean 
 regions  -  Citrus  fruits  like  lemons,  orange 
 and  grapefruit,  Rosemary,  Olive. 
 Mediterranean  woodlands  are  found  in 
 the  Mediterranean  Sea  region,  Central 
 Chile,  the  Southwest  United  States, 
 Australia, and Africa.

 Q.847.  Mid-latitude  deserts  (BWK)  are 
 found  between  35°  and  _________N  and  S, 
 which  are  poleward  of  subtropical  high- 
 pressure areas. 
 (a) 70°   (b) 60°      (c) 79°     (d) 40° 
 Sol.847.(b)  60°.  Mid-latitude  deserts  - 
 An  excessively  dry  region  with  an  arid 
 climate.  Location  -  Found  in  the  interior 
 parts  of  the  continents.  Examples  - 
 Argentina's  Patagonian  Desert 
 (Argentina),  Gobi  Desert  (China  and 
 Mongolia),  Turkestan  (Kazakhstan), 
 Ladakh  (Jammu  and  Kashmir).  Crops  - 
 Cacti,  Prickly  Shrubs,  Long-rooted  wiry 
 grasses.

 Q.849.  Which  of  the  following

 (Hominidae).  These  are  large,

 rainforests  of  Borneo  and  Sumatra. 
 Kangaroos:  Scientific  name 
 (Macropodidae),  Phylum  (Chordata). 
 Ostriches:  Phylum  (Chordata),  found  in 
 savanna  and  desert  regions.  Lemurs:

 (Chordata), found in Madagascar. 
 Q.850.  As  of  2025,  which  country  has  the 
 largest  proven  coal  reserves  in  the

 America  (Largest  Coal  Reserves)  . 
 Largest  coal  producer  (1st  China,  2nd 
 India).  Jharia  Coal  Field  (Jharkhand) 
 produces  the  highest  coal  in  India. 
 Central  Institute  of  Mining  and  Fuel 
 Research  (2007)  -  Dhanbad.  Largest 
 Producer:  Bauxite  (Australia),  Gold 
 (China),  Diamond  (Russia),  Silver

 Copper  (Chile),  Uranium  (Kazakhstan),

 Q.851.  "Eskimos"  are  associated  with 
 which geographical region? 
 (a) Arid Region 
 (b) Polar Region 
 (c) Savanna Grassland 
 (d) Tropical Rainforest Region 
 Sol.851.(b)  Polar  Region  (the  area 
 around  the  Earth’s  North  Pole  or  the 
 South  Pole).  Igloo  (small  hut-like  house 
 made  of  snow  where  Eskimos  live).  Arid 
 regions  are  dry  and  have  little  rainfall. 
 Example  -  Central  and  western  Asia, 
 North  and  South  America,  central  and 
 northwest Australia.

 South and East Asia.

 Q.855.  Death  Valley  is  a  desert  valley  in

 (b) Atacama Desert of Chile 
 (c) Lut Desert of Iran 
 (d) Sahara Desert of Africa 
 Sol.855.(a)  Mojave  Desert  of  California.

 Q.856.  Which  country  has  the  most  time 
 zones in the world? 
 (a) France     (b) United States of America 
 (c) Russia     (d) United Kingdom 
 Sol.856.(a)  France  (12).  Russia  has  11 
 time  zones,  the  United  Kingdom  has  1 
 time  zone  and  India  has  only  one-time 
 zone. 
 Q.857.  A  major  portion  of  'Sufed  Koh' 
 mountain range lies in ______. 
 (a) Pakistan  (b) China 
 (c) India  (d) Myanmar 
 Sol.857.(a)  Pakistan.  Safed  Koh  is  a 
 mountain  range  on  the  Pakistan 
 -Afghanistan  border.  It  parallels  the  Kabul 
 River.  A  spur  of  the  range  is  crossed  by 
 the Khyber Pass. 
 Q.858.  A  mass  of  snow  and  ice  that 
 moves  slowly  under  the  influence  of 
 gravity  along  a  confined  course  away 
 from its place of accumulation is called a 
 ______. 
 (a) lagoon  (b) glacier 
 (c) block mountain    (d) plateau 
 Sol.858.(b)  Glacier  is  a  slowly  moving 
 mass  or  river  of  ice  formed  by  the 
 accumulation  and  compaction  of  snow 
 on  mountains  or  near  the  poles.  A 
 plateau  is  a  flat  area  of  land  that  is 
 elevated above sea level. 
 Miscellaneous 
 Q.859.  If  a  person  view  the  Earth  from 
 above  the  North  Pole,  he  will  observe 
 that the Earth rotates in: 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Anti-clockwise         (b) No rotation 
 (c) Random spin  (d) Clockwise 
 Sol.859.(a)  Anti-clockwise.  The  Earth 
 rotates  west  to  east.  If  viewed  from 
 above  the  North  Pole,  this  rotation 
 appears  anti-clockwise.  If  viewed  from 
 above  the  South  Pole,  it  appears 
 clockwise. 
 Q.860.  Consider  the  following 
 statements  about  Udayagiri  and 
 Khandagiri caves: 
 1. 
 These  caves  are  near  Bhubaneswar 
 and were meant for Jain monks. 
 2. 
 All  caves  are  shaped  like  flowers  and 
 are  carved  from  independent 
 boulders. 
 3. 
 Each  cave  has  multiple  shrines 
 dedicated  to  different  Buddhist 
 deities. 
 Which of the above is/are correct ? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Only 1 
 (b) All 1, 2, and 3 are correct 
 (c) 1 and 2 Only 
 (d) 2 and 3 Only 
 Sol.860.(a)  Only  1.  Statement  2  is 
 incorrect:The  caves  are  rock-cut  into  hill 
 faces,  not  from  independent  boulders. 
 They  are  not  shaped  like  flowers;  instead, 
 they  are  simple  cells  with  some  ornate 
 carvings  (e.g.,  Rani  Gumpha).  Statement 
 3  is  Incorrect.:  These  caves  are  Jain,  not 
 Buddhist.They  are  dedicated  to  Jain 
 monks  and  Tirthankaras,  not  Buddhist 
 deities.

 Q.862.  What  evidence  supports  the  Big 
 Bang theory of universe origin? 
 SSC Stenographer 7/08/2025 (Shift 3) 
 (a) Sunspot cycles 
 (b) Redshift of galaxies 
 (c) Solar wind 
 (d) Volcanic eruptions on Earth 
 Sol.862.(b)  The  redshift  of  galaxies  is  a 
 major  piece  of  observational  evidence 
 supporting  the  Big  Bang  Theory.  Redshift 
 refers  to  the  phenomenon  where  light 
 from  distant  galaxies  shifts  toward  the 
 red  end  of  the  spectrum,  indicating  that 
 they  are  moving  away  from  us.  This 
 supports  the  idea  that  the  universe  is 
 expanding,  and  if  it's  expanding  now,  it 
 must  have  originated  from  a  singular 
 point  —  as  proposed  in  the  Big  Bang 
 Theory. 
 Q.863.  What  is  the  main  objective  of 
 ISRO's SCATSAT-1 satellite? 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 1) 
 (a) Monitoring earthquake zones 
 (b) Measuring atmospheric ozone 
 depletion 
 (c) weather forecasting and cyclone 
 prediction 
 (d) Mapping forest cover across India 
 Sol.863.(c)  Weather  forecasting  and 
 cyclone  prediction.  SCATSAT-1 
 (Scatterometer  Satellite-1)  was  launched 
 by  ISRO  in  September  2016.  Its  main 
 purpose  is  to  provide  continuous  weather 
 forecasting,  cyclone  detection,  and 
 tracking  services.  The  satellite  carries  a 
 scatterometer  to  measure  ocean  surface 
 winds,  which  are  crucial  for  predicting 
 monsoons  and  cyclones.  It  was 
 developed  as  a  continuity  mission  for  the 
 Oceansat-2  scatterometer,  which  had 
 ceased to operate. 
 Q.864.  Match  the  port/corridor  with  its 
 strategic benefit to India 
 Column A 
 (Port/Corridor) 
 Column B (Strategic 
 Benefit to India) 
 A. Chabahar Port  1. Links India with 
 Europe via Middle 
 East 
 B. IMEC  2. New age of 
 transportation 
 between the two 
 countries India to 
 Southeast Asia via 
 Mizoram-Myanmar 
 link 
 C. Sittwe Port  3. Access to Central 
 Asia bypassing 
 Pakistan 
 SSC Stenographer 8/08/2025 (Shift 2) 
 (a) A → 3, B → 1, C → 2 
 (b) A → 2, B → 3, C → 1 
 (c) A → 3, B → 2, C→ 1 
 (d) A → 1, B → 2, C → 3 
 Sol.864.(a)  A  →  3,  B  →  1,  C  →  2. 
 Chabahar  Port  (Iran):  Located  on  the  Gulf 
 of  Oman,  it  is  strategically  important  for 
 India  as  it  provides  a  trade  route  to 
 Afghanistan  and  Central  Asia,  bypassing 
 Pakistan.  India-Middle  East-Europe 
 Economic  Corridor  (IMEC):  A  planned 
 corridor  to  enhance  connectivity  and 
 economic  integration  between  Asia,  the 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Persian  Gulf,  and  Europe,  boosting  trade 
 and  development.  Sittwe  Port 
 (Myanmar):  A  deep-water  port  in  Rakhine 
 State,  built  with  Indian  assistance  and 
 operationalized  by  India  Ports  Global 
 (IPGL).  It  is  India’s  second  overseas  port 
 after  Chabahar,  forming  a  key  part  of  the 
 Kaladan  Multi-Modal  Transit  Transport 
 Project  (KMMTTP),  which  connects 
 India’s  northeast  to  the  Bay  of  Bengal, 
 reducing  dependence  on  the  Siliguri

 Q.865.  A  section  of  an  active  fault  that 
 has  produced  earthquakes  in  the  past 
 but  is  now  quiet  and  is  believed  that  the 
 fault  segment  is  capable  of  producing 
 earthquake  on  some  other  basis  is 
 known as: 
 (a) Hypocenter  (b) Seismic Gap 
 (c) Epicenter  (d) Seismic Wave 
 Sol.865.(b)  Seismic  Gap  -  A  section  of  an 
 active  fault  which  has  the  potential  to 
 produce  significant  earthquakes. 
 Hypocenter:  The  point  of  origin  of  an 
 earthquake  beneath  the  Earth's  surface 
 where  the  fault  rupture  begins, 
 representing  the  actual  location  where 
 seismic  energy  is  first  released. 
 Epicenter:  The  point  on  the  Earth's 
 surface  directly  above  the  hypocenter, 
 marking  the  geographical  location  where 
 an  earthquake's  shaking  is  typically  most 
 intense  and  first  felt.  Seismic  Wave:  The 
 energy  waves  generated  and  propagated 
 through  the  Earth's  crust  during  an 
 earthquake,  which  can  be  classified  into 
 different  types  like  P-waves,  S-waves, 
 and  surface  waves  that  cause  ground 
 motion. 
 Q.866.  The  earthquakes  that  are 
 generated  due  to  the  explosion  of 
 chemical  or  nuclear  devices  are  known 
 as: 
 (a) Collapse earthquakes 
 (b) Explosion earthquakes 
 (c) Tectonic earthquakes 
 (d) Reservoir-induced earthquakes 
 Sol.866.(b) Explosion earthquakes. 
 Types  of  Earthquakes:  In  regions  with 
 heavy  mining  activity,  the  collapse  of 
 underground  mine  roofs  can  cause  minor 
 tremors,  known  as  collapse  earthquakes. 
 The  most  common  type  is  tectonic 
 earthquakes,  which  result  from  the 
 movement  of  rocks  along  a  fault  plane.  A 
 subcategory  of  tectonic  earthquakes, 
 known  as  volcanic  earthquakes,  occur  in 
 areas  with  active  volcanoes.  Additionally, 
 reservoir-induced  earthquakes  are 
 triggered  in  regions  where  large 
 reservoirs are present. 
 Q.867.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Project  Tiger  was  launched  in  1973  at 
 Corbett Tiger Reserve. 
 II. 
 The  Wildlife  Protection  Act  was 
 passed in 1975. 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.867.(a)  Only  I.  On  April  1,  1973, 
 Project  Tiger  was  officially  launched  at 
 the  Corbett  Tiger  Reserve,  initially 
 including  nine  tiger  reserves. 
 International  Tiger  Day  is  celebrated 
 annually  on  July  29.  The  Wildlife 
 Protection  Act  was  passed  in  1972. 
 Projects  in  India  related  to  animals 
 :Project  Hangul  (1970),  Project  Crocodile 
 (1975),  Project  Elephant  (1992),  Project 
 Rhino  (2005),  Project  Gangetic  Dolphin  - 
 (2009),  Project  Snow  Leopard  (2009), 
 Project Great Indian Bustard (2012). 
 Q.868.  Which  of  the  following  can  be 
 represented  as  a  functional  unit  of 
 nature? 
 (a) Vehicles  (b) Ecosystem 
 (c) Humans  (d) Plants 
 Sol.868.(b)  Ecosystem.  This  is  because 
 an  ecosystem  encompasses  all  living 
 organisms  (plants,  animals,  and 
 microorganisms)  interacting  with  each 
 other  and  with  the  non-living 
 components  (like  air,  water,  and  soil) 
 within  their  environment.  These 
 interactions  allow  for  energy  flow , 
 nutrient  cycling,  and  the  maintenance  of 
 life-supporting  processes,  which  are 
 critical functions of nature. 
 Q.869.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 man-made ecosystem? 
 (a) Aquarium  (b) Dessert 
 (c) Forest  (d) Grassland 
 Sol.869.(a)  Aquarium.  It  is  a  facility  or 
 park  where  a  collection  of  aquatic 
 animals  and  plants  is  maintained  for 
 study,  conservation,  and  public  display.  A 
 man-made  ecosystem,  also  known  as  an 
 artificial  or  anthropogenic  ecosystem, 
 refers  to  a  human-created  environment 
 where  biotic  and  abiotic  components 
 interact  to  sustain  life.  Examples  of 
 man-made  ecosystems  include  tree 
 plantations,  urban  landscapes,  crop 
 fields,  managed  ponds,  zoos,  and 
 gardens. 
 Q.870.  Which  of  the  following  are  the 
 two basic categories of an ecosystem? 
 (a) Forest and Aquatic 
 (b) Terrestrial and Aquatic 
 (c) Lakes and Ponds 
 (d) Forest and Rivers 
 Sol.870.(b)  Terrestrial  and  Aquatic.  An 
 ecosystem  is  a  chain  of  interactions 
 between  organisms  and  their 
 environment.  The  term  "ecosystem"  was 
 first  coined  by  English  botanist  A.G. 
 Tansley  in  1935.  A  terrestrial  ecosystem 
 refers  to  a  land-based  community  of 
 organisms  and  the  interactions  between 
 biotic  and  abiotic  components  in  a 
 specific  area.  An  aquatic  ecosystem 
 involves  interacting  organisms  that  rely 
 on  each  other  and  their  water 
 environment for nutrients and shelter. 
 Q.871.  Which  branch  of  ecology  deals 
 with  the  characteristics  of  the  ancient 
 environment  and  their  relationship  with 
 ancient plants and animals? 
 (a) Demecology        (b) Ichthyology 
 (c) Paleoecology  (d) Mycology 
 Sol.871.(c)  Paleoecology  studies  past 
 ecosystems  and  their  changes  over  time 
 using  fossils  and  geological  data.  Other 
 branches:  Demecology  (ecology  of 
 populations),  Ichthyology  (study  of 
 fishes), and Mycology (study of fungi). 
 Q.872.  Which  of  the  following  is  the  best 
 definition of ecological efficiency? 
 (a) The amount of energy utilised at 
 different trophic levels in a food chain 
 (b) The amount of energy stored at 
 different trophic levels in a food chain 
 (c) The ratio between the mass and the 
 energy flow at different trophic levels 
 in a food chain 
 (d) The ratio between energy flows at 
 different points in a food chain 
 Sol.872.(d)  Ecological  efficiency 
 describes  the  efficiency  with  which 
 energy  is  transferred  from  one  trophic 
 level  to  the  next.  The  number  of  trophic 
 levels  in  the  grazing  food  chain  is 
 restricted  as  the  transfer  of  energy 
 follows  10  percent  law  -  only  10  percent 
 of  the  energy  is  transferred  to  each 
 trophic level from the lower trophic level. 
 Q.873.  The  record  obtained  by  the 
 measurement of earthquake is called a: 
 (a) seismograph       (b) seismogram 
 (c) seismology  (d) seismic wave 
 Sol.873.(b)  seismogram.  Earthquakes 
 are  classified  into  three  zones  based  on 
 depth:  shallow  (0-70  km),  intermediate 
 (70-300  km),  and  deep  (300-700  km). 
 The  vibrations  produced  during  an 
 earthquake are known as seismic waves. 
 Q.874.  The  Environment  (Protection) 
 Act,  1986  empowers  the  _________  to 
 www.ssccglpinnacle.com                                                 Download Pinnacle Exam Preparation App 

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 establish  authorities  charged  with  the 
 mandate  of  preventing  environmental 
 pollution in all its forms. 
 (a) State Government 
 (b) Central Government 
 (c) Supreme Court 
 (d) local municipalities 
 Sol.874.(b)  Central  Government.  The 
 Environment  (Protection)  Act,  1986  was 
 enacted  to  safeguard  and  enhance  the 
 environment.  The  Central  Government 
 has  the  authority  to:  protect  and  improve 
 the  environment,  set  emission  and 
 discharge  standards,  regulate  industrial 
 locations,  manage  hazardous  wastes, 
 and  protect  public  health  and  welfare. 
 Some  key  environmental  acts  include  the 
 Biological  Diversity  Act  (2002),  Wild  Life 
 (Protection)  Act  (1972),  Forest 
 Conservation  Act  (1980),  Indian  Forest 
 Act  (1927),  and  Energy  Conservation  Act 
 (2001). 
 Q.875.  Who  introduced  the  concept  of 
 virtual water? 
 (a) MS Swaminathan 
 (b) Glubler Triplets 
 (c) John Anthony Allan 
 (d) Norman Borlaug 
 Sol.875.(c)  John  Anthony  Allan.  Virtual 
 water  is  the  freshwater  used  to  produce 
 goods  or  services,  which  is  then  traded 
 internationally as part of those products. 
 Q.876.  In  which  of  the  following  regions 
 of India is the Mundari language spoken? 
 (a) Chota-Nagpur plateau 
 (b) Baghelkhand 
 (c) Thar desert 
 (d) Malwa plateau 
 Sol.876.(a)  Chota-Nagpur  plateau.  This 
 plateau  covers  parts  of  Jharkhand, 
 Chhattisgarh,  Odisha,  and  West  Bengal. 
 Mundari  is  a  Munda  language,  belonging 
 to  the  Austroasiatic  language  family. 
 Chhota  Nagpur  plateau  is  called  the 
 “Heart  of  industrial  India”  because  it  is 
 rich  in  minerals  and  power  fuels.  The 
 highest  mountain  peak  of  Chota  Nagpur 
 Plateau is Parasnath. 
 Q.877.  Which  Greek  term  was  first  used 
 by  Ernst  Haeckel  in  1866  to  refer  to  ‘the 
 relation  of  animals  to  both  the  organic  as 
 well as the inorganic environment’? 
 (a) Ethnologie  (b) Ecophagie 
 (c) Oekologie  (d) Oologie 
 Sol.877.(c)  Oekologie.  Ernst  Haeckel,  a 
 German  biologist  and  philosopher,  made 
 significant  contributions  to  evolutionary 
 biology.  He  coined  terms  like  "ecology," 
 "phylogeny,"  and  "Protista"  and  is  known 
 for  his  recapitulation  theory,  stating  that 
 an  organism's  development  mirrors  its 
 species' evolutionary history. 
 Q.878.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 characteristics of epilimnion? 
 (a) Middle layer of a pond 
 (b) Bottom layer of a river 
 (c) Bottom layer of a lake 
 (d) Top-most layer in a thermally 
 stratified lake 
 Sol.878.(d)  Thermal  stratification  occurs 
 when  the  water  in  a  lake  forms  distinct 
 layers  through  heating  from  the  sun.  It 
 led  to  the  separation  of  the  lake  into 
 three  layers.  Epilimnion:  The  upper  layer 
 of  warmer  water.  Metalimnion:  The 
 middle  layer  with  a  zone  of  gradual 
 decrease  in  temperature.  Hypolimnion: 
 The bottom layer of colder water. 
 Q.879.  The  depositional  pattern  of 
 CaCO 
 3 
 and  other  materials  that  extend 
 upwards  from  the  floor  of  a  limestone 
 cavern is known as: 
 (a) Stalactite  (b) Dripstone 
 (c) Stalagmite  (d) Hatectite 
 Sol.879.(c)  Stalagmite.  It  rises  up  from 
 the  floor  of  the  caves.  Stalactite  -  It  is  an 
 icicle-shaped  formation  that  hangs  from 
 the  ceiling  of  a  cave  and  is  produced  by 
 the  precipitation  of  minerals  from  water 
 dripping  through  the  cave  ceiling.  The 
 stalagmites  and  stalactites  eventually 
 fuse  to  give  rise  to  columns  and  pillars  of 
 different diameters. 
 Q.880.  Epilimnetic,  metalimnetic,  and 
 hypolimnetic  water  strata  are  example 
 of: 
 (a) different layers of underground water 
 (b) typical thermal stratification of a lake 
 (c) typical layers of hard rock 
 (d) various ecological phases 
 Sol.880.(b)  typical  thermal  stratification 
 of  a  lake.  These  layers  are  referred  to  as 
 Epilimnion  (warm  surface  waters)  - 
 Top-most  layer  in  a  thermally  stratified 
 lake  and  Hypolimnion  (cold  bottom 
 waters)  which  are  separated  by  the 
 metalimnion,  or  thermocline  layer,  a 
 stratum of rapidly changing temperature. 
 Q.881.  In  which  of  the  following 
 schedules  of  the  Wildlife  (Protection) 
 Act,  1972  is  the  most  common  wild  cat 
 in India 'Felis chaus' listed? 
 (a) Schedule I  (b) Schedule III 
 (c) Schedule IV  (d) Schedule II 
 Sol.881.(d)  Schedule  II.  Under  this 
 schedule,  the  species  receives 
 protection,  and  poaching  or  capturing  it 
 without  proper  authorization  is 
 prohibited.  The  Wild  Life  (Protection)  Act, 
 1972  is  an  Indian  law  enacted  to  protect 
 plant and animal species. 
 Q.882.  What  is  a  population  (or 
 subspecies  or  race)  that  is  adapted  to 
 local environmental conditions called? 
 (a) Niche  (b) Ecotype 
 (c) Prototype  (d) Biome 
 Sol.882.(b)  Ecotype  is  a  genetically 
 distinct  group  within  a  species  adapted 
 to  specific  environmental  conditions.  An 
 ecological  niche  describes  how  a 
 species  interacts  within  an  ecosystem.  A 
 biome  refers  to  a  community  of  plants 
 and  animals  naturally  occurring  in  a 
 region with shared characteristics. 
 Q.883.  Identify  whether  the  given 
 statements  about  an  earthquake  are 
 correct or incorrect. 
 Statement  1  :  When  the  stress  on  the 
 edge  overcomes  the  friction,  there  is  an 
 earthquake  that  releases  energy  in  waves 
 that  travel  through  Earth's  crust  and 
 causes the shaking that we feel. 
 Statement  2  :  An  earthquake  cannot  be 
 identified  as  a  foreshock  until  after  a 
 larger  earthquake  in  the  same  area 
 occurs. 
 (a) Only Statement 1 is correct 
 (b) Both Statements 1 and 2 are correct 
 (c) Only Statement 2 is correct 
 (d) Both Statements 1 and 2 are incorrect 
 Sol.883.(b)  Both  Statements  1  and  2  are 
 correct.  When  lithospheric  plates  move, 
 the  Earth's  surface  vibrates,  causing 
 earthquakes.  The  point  inside  the  crust 
 where  movement  begins  is  called  the 
 focus,  while  the  point  directly  above  it  on 
 the  surface  is  the  epicenter.  Vibrations 
 spread  outward  from  the  epicenter,  with 
 the  strongest  impact  near  this  point, 
 decreasing as they move away. 
 Q.884.  Of  the  total  water  content 
 available  globally,  how  much  percentage 
 of  water  is  received  from  freshwater 
 resources? 
 (a) 0.5%   (b) 4.5%   (c) 3.5%   (d) 2.5% 
 Sol.884.(d)  2.5%.  Although  71%  of  the 
 Earth's  surface  is  covered  by  water,  only 
 2.5%  of  it  is  freshwater.  Out  of  total  water 
 resources  on  earth  Ocean  comprises 
 97.25%,  Ice  caps-  2.05%,  Groundwater- 
 0.68%,  Lakes-  0.1%,  Soil  moisture- 
 0.005%  ,  Atmosphere-  0.001%  ,  Streams 
 and  river-  0.0001%  ,  Biosphere-  0.00004% 
 .  India  accounts  for  about  2.45%  of  the 
 world's  surface  area,  4%  of  the  world's 
 water  resources,  and  approximately  16% 
 of the global population.
