 Pinnacle  Geography 
 Q.885.  The  highly  viscous, 
 mechanically-weak  and  ductile  region  of 
 Earth's upper mantle is called: 
 (a) lithosphere         (b) asthenosphere 
 (c) mesosphere       (d) exosphere 
 Sol.885.(b)  Asthenosphere.  Lithosphere 
 :  The  rigid  outer  layer  of  Earth,  consisting 
 of  the  crust  and  the  uppermost  part  of 
 the  mantle.  It  is  divided  into  tectonic 
 plates.  Mesosphere  :  The  mesosphere  is 
 the  third  highest  layer  of  atmosphere  and 
 occupies  the  region  above  the 
 stratosphere  and  below  the 
 thermosphere.  Exosphere  :  The 
 outermost  layer  of  Earth's  atmosphere, 
 where  air  is  extremely  thin,  and  particles 
 can escape into space. 
 Q.886.  Consider  the  following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Hydrology  is  the  study  of  distribution 
 and  movement  of  water  on  and  below 
 the Earth’s surface. 
 II. 
 Ecology  is  the  study  of 
 inter-relationship  between  living 
 organisms and their environment. 
 Which  of  the  above  statement(s)  is/are 
 correct? 
 (a) Only I  (b) Only II 
 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.886.(c)  Both  I  and  II.  Different 
 Branches  and  Their  Studies:  Bryology  - 
 The  Study  of  Bryophytes.  Biology  -  The 
 study  of  living  things  and  their  vital 
 processes.  Floriculture  -  The  Study  of 
 flowers and flowering plants. 
 Q.887.  Which  of  the  following  is  NOT 
 considered  a  method  of  conservation  of 
 natural resources? 
 (a) Extraction  (b) Afforestation 
 (c) Recycling           (d) Terrace farming 
 Sol.887.(a)  Extraction  refers  to  the 
 process  of  removing  natural  resources 
 from  the  Earth,  such  as  mining,  drilling 
 for  oil,  or  harvesting  timber.  Conservation 
 is  the  proper  management  of  a  natural 
 resource  to  prevent  its  exploitation, 
 destruction  or  degradation.  Method  of 
 conservation  of  Natural  Resources  : 
 Reforestation,  Rainwater  Harvesting, 
 Treatment  of  Industrial  Effluents,  In-Situ 
 conservation  of  Biodiversity,  Ex-Situ 
 conservation  of  Biodiversity,  Sustainable 
 development,  Crop  rotation,  Contour 
 ploughing, etc. 
 Q.888.  The  Vienna  Convention  is  related 
 to ____________. 
 (a) protection of wild life 
 (b) protection of ozone layer 
 (c) Disposing of harmful electronic waste 
 (d) sustainable agriculture 
 Sol.888.(b)  protection  of  ozone  layer. 
 The  Vienna  Convention  multilateral 
 environmental  agreement  was  signed  in 
 1985,  came  into  effect  in  1988,  and 
 achieved  universal  ratification  by  2009. 
 The  primary  goal  of  the  Vienna 
 Convention  is  to  safeguard  human  health 
 and  the  environment  from  the  harmful 
 effects caused by ozone layer depletion. 
 Q.889.  Select the correct statement. 
 (a) The pyramid of energy is always 
 upright. 
 (b) The pyramid of biomass is always 
 inverted. 
 (c) The pyramid of mass is always 
 upright. 
 (d) The pyramid of number is always 
 upright. 
 Sol.889.(a)  The  pyramid  of  energy  is 
 always  upright,  and  can  never  be 
 inverted.  According  to  the  10%  law 
 proposed  by  Raymond  Lindeman  of 
 energy  flow ,  only  10%  of  energy  is 
 transferred  from  one  trophic  level  to 
 another  and  90%  of  the  energy  remains 
 at  the  former  trophic  level  for  its  needs 
 like  respiration  and  digestion  processes. 
 The  direction  of  energy  flow  in  an 
 ecosystem is always unidirectional. 
 Q.890.  Select  the  correct  statement 
 about climax community. 
 (a) It is an ecological community in 
 which populations of plants or 
 animals, which are very unstable and 
 exist for very few time. 
 (b) It is a very first community of the 
 ecosystem. 
 (c) It consists of only plant population 
 that makes a new ecological system. 
 (d) It is an ecological community in which 
 populations of plants or animals remain 
 stable and exist in  balance with each 
 other and their environment. 
 Sol.890.(d)  A  climax  community  is  the 
 “endpoint”  of  succession  within  the 
 context  of  a  particular  climate  and 
 geography.  The  climax  community 
 remains  stable  as  long  as  the 
 environment remains unchanged. 
 Q.891.  Which  concept  was  proposed  by 
 Hutchinson  in  1957  to  express  the 
 relationship  of  an  individual  or  population 
 to all aspects of its environment? 
 (a) Ecological stratification 
 (b) Ecological pyramid 
 (c) Ecological succession 
 (d) Ecological niche 
 Sol.891.(d)  Ecological  niche  -  A  term  for 
 the  position  of  a  species  within  an 
 ecosystem.  It  refers  to  the 
 Interrelationship  of  a  species  with  all  the 
 biotic and abiotic factors affecting it. 
 Q.892.  Which  of  the  following  companies 
 had  constructed  Asia’s  first  cross  - 
 country  pipeline  as  a  mode  of  transport 
 for  liquids  and  gasses  over  long 
 distances ? 
 (a) Oil and Natural Gas Corporation 
 (b) Indian Oil Corporation 
 (c) Bharat Petroleum 
 (d) Oil India Limited 
 Sol.892.(d)  Oil  India  Limited.  Naharkatia 
 (Assam)-Barauni  (Bihar)  Pipeline  (First 
 cross-country  pipeline).  The  Pipeline 
 between  Naharkatia  and  Nunmati 
 became  operative  in  1962  and  that 
 between Nunmati and Barauni in 1964. 
 Q.893.  The  term  physical  geology  was 
 coined by __________. 
 (a) William Hopkins  (b) Georgius Agricola 
 (c) Louis Agassiz      (d) Aziz Ab'Saber 
 Sol.893.(a)  William  Hopkins  .  Fathers  of 
 Various  Fields  -  Economics  (Adam 
 Smith),  Telephone  (Alexander  Graham 
 Bell),  Modern  Chemistry  (Antoine 
 Lavoisier),  Classification/  Taxonomy 
 (Carl Linnaeus), Evolution (Charles 
 Darwin), Geography (Eratosthenes). 
 Q.894.  Who  was  the  first  ecologist  to 
 describe  the  specific  shape  of 
 eco-trophic  interactions,  which  he  called 
 the pyramid of numbers, in the 1920s ? 
 (a) Ernst Haeckel 
 (b) Howard Thomas Odum 
 (c) Edward O Wilson 
 (d) Charles Elton 
 Sol.894.(d)  Charles  Elton.  He  introduced 
 the  concept  of  food  web,  food  chain  and 
 ecological  niche  in  the  classic  textbook 
 “Animal  Ecology”  in  1927.  Ernest  Haeckel 
 -  He  coined  the  term  ‘Ecology’  in  1866. 
 Edward  O  Wilson  -  Proponent  of 
 Sociobiology. 
 Q.895.  The  Karewa  formations  are  found 
 in the __________ . 
 (a) North Easter Hills 
 (b) Kashmir Himalayas 
 (c) Uttaranchal Himalayas 
 (d) Terai Region 
 Sol.895.(b)  Kashmir  Himalayas.  The 
 Karewa  term  means  “elevated  table 
 land”.  Firstly,  this  term  was  used  by 
 Godwin  Austin  in  1859.  “Vudr”  is  the  local 
 name  for  it  in  Kashmiri  language.  Karewa 
 formations  are  useful  for  the  cultivation 
 of  Zafran  (Saffron),  almond,  walnut, 
 apple, and orchards  in Kashmir valley. 
 Q.896.  Siachen in Himalayas is:

 Pinnacle  Geography 
 (a) a glacier                       (b) a dam 
 (c) a national park            (d) a lake 
 Sol.896.(a) A glacier.  The Siachen 
 Glacier  is  located  in  the  Eastern 
 Karakoram  range  in  the  Himalayas.  It  is 
 the  world's  highest  battlefield.  Nubra 
 river  originates  from  Siachen  Glacier.  It  is 
 situated to the North of Nubra Valley. 
 Q.897.  ____________  is  characterized  by 
 abundant  dissolved  oxygen,  sunlight, 
 nutrients,  generally  high  wave  energies 
 and  water  motion,  and,  in  the  intertidal 
 subzone,  alternating  submergence  and 
 exposure. 
 (a) The Lentic Zone 
 (b) The Limnetic Zone 
 (c) The Littoral Zone 
 (d) The Benthic Zone 
 Sol.897.(c)  The  Littoral  Zone  -  It  is  an 
 area  close  to  the  shore  and  submerged 
 in  water  to  some  degree.  Lentic  Zone  - 
 Ecosystem  entails  a  body  of  standing 
 water,  ranging  from  ditches,  seeps, 
 ponds,  seasonal  pools,  basin  marshes 
 and  lakes.  Benthic  Zone  -  The  lowest 
 ecological  zone  in  a  water  body.  Limnetic 
 zone  -  The  top  layer  of  lake  water  distant 
 from  the  coast,  and  extends  only  as  deep 
 as sunlight can penetrate (200 m). 
 Q.898.  Which  ecologist  showed  in  a 
 long-term  experiment  on  outdoor  plots 
 that  biodiversity  is  of  central  importance 
 to  the  productivity,  stability,  resistance  to 
 the  invasion  of  ecosystems  and  fertility 
 of their soils? 
 (a) Karl Mobius 
 (b) David Tilman 
 (c) Herbert Spencer 
 (d) Edward Osborne Wilson 
 Sol.898.(b)  David  Tilman.  Edward  O. 
 Wilson  -  Father  of  biodiversity.  Karl 
 Möbius  -  First  to  develop  the  concept  of 
 ecological  community,  biocenosis,  or 
 living community. 
 Q.899.  Which  hypothesis  was  proposed 
 by  Paul  Ehrlich  to  suggest  the 
 importance  of  species  richness  in  the 
 maintenance of ecosystems ? 
 (a) Portfolio hypothesis 
 (b) Redundancy hypothesis 
 (c) Predation hypothesis 
 (d) Rivet popper hypothesis 
 Sol.899.(d)  Rivet  popper  hypothesis. 
 The  hypothesis  suggests  the  importance 
 of  species  richness  in  the  maintenance 
 of  the  ecosystem.  Redundancy 
 hypothesis  -  It  predicts  that  the  species 
 redundancy  in  a  plant  community 
 enhances  community  stability.  Predation 
 hypothesis  -  It  predicts  that  group-living 
 animals  are  adapted  to  make  alarm  calls 
 that distract or confuse predators. 
 Q.900.  Which  states  are  involved  in  the 
 joint venture of the Chambal Project? 
 (a) Rajasthan and Haryana 
 (b) Rajasthan and Uttar Pradesh 
 (c) Uttar Pradesh and Madhya Pradesh 
 (d) Rajasthan and Madhya Pradesh 
 Sol.900.(d)  Rajasthan  and  Madhya 
 Pradesh.  Chambal  Project  :  Initiated  in  - 
 1954.  Aim  -  Harnessing  the  Chambal 
 River  for  irrigation,  Power  generation  and 
 for  prevention  and  control  of  soil  erosion 
 in the valley. 
 Q.901.  In  the  context  of  ecology,  the 
 given  equation  shows  the  relationship 
 between  the  size  of  an  area  and  the 
 number  of  species  present  in  it. 
 Accordingly,  by  which  letter  is  the 
 regression coefficient denoted? 
 log S = log C + Z log A 
 (a) S  (b) A  (c) Z  (d) C 
 Sol.901.(c)  Z.  The  species-area 
 relationship  is  often  evaluated  in 
 conservation  science  in  order  to  predict 
 extinction  rates  in  the  case  of  habitat 
 loss  and  habitat  fragmentation.  The 
 equation:  log  S  =  log  C  +  Z  log  A,  where  S 
 =  species  richness,  A  =  area,  C  = 
 Y-intercept. 
 Q.902.  In  the  1930s,  which  of  the 
 following  theories  was  propounded  by 
 Arthur Holmes about the mantle ? 
 (a) Theory of Faunal Succession 
 (b) Slab Pull Theory 
 (c) Convectional Current Theory 
 (d) Theory of Faulting 
 Sol.902.(c)  Convectional  current  Theory. 
 The  Convectional  currents  are  generated 
 due  to  radioactive  elements  causing 
 thermal  differences  in  the  mantle 
 portion.  Arthur  Holmes  -  The  first  earth 
 scientist  to  grasp  the  mechanical  and 
 thermal  implications  of  mantle 
 convection,  which  led  eventually  to  the 
 acceptance of plate tectonics. 
 Q.903.  Name  a  unit  designed  for  wet  and 
 dry  bulb  thermometers  to  protect 
 temperature  sensors  from  being  affected 
 by direct or reflected sunlight. 
 (a) Stevenson screen (b) Skywarn screen 
 (c) Clarinet screen       (d) Cardinal screen 
 Sol.903.(a)  Stevenson  screen  -  It  is  also 
 known  as  a  cotton  region  shelter,  an 
 instrument  shelter,  a  thermometer 
 shelter,  a  thermoscreen,  or  a 
 thermometer  screen.  Skywarn  screen  -  It 
 is  a  national  network  of  volunteer  severe 
 weather  spotters.  It  was  established  to 
 obtain  critical  weather  information  by  the 
 National Weather Service. 
 Q.904.  Which  state  is  also  known  as  the 
 'Molasses  basin',  which  is  made  up  of 
 soft unconsolidated deposits? 
 (a) Mizoram  (b) Manipur 
 (c) Nagaland  (d) Meghalaya 
 Sol.904.(a)  Mizoram  (The  Land  of  rolling 
 mountains).  Manipur  is  known  as  ‘The 
 Jewel  of  land’  in  India.  Nagaland  -  The 
 falcon  capital  of  the  world.  Meghalaya  - 
 The Abode of Clouds. 
 Q.905.  In  which  year  was  an  approach 
 and  program  named  Joint  Forest 
 Management  (JFM)  launched  in  the 
 context of National Forest Policy? 
 (a) 1965   (b) 1988   (c) 1996   (d) 1947 
 Sol.905.(b)  1988.  Joint  Forest 
 Management  is  about  involving  local 
 communities  in  the  management  and 
 restoration of degraded forests. 
 Q.906.  Consider the following 
 statements: 
 I. 
 Arthur  G.  Tansley  is  regarded  as  the

 II.

 "Ecosystem" in 1935. 
 Which  of  the  above  statements  is/are 
 correct?

 (c) Both I and II  (d) Neither I nor II 
 Sol.906.(d)  Neither  I  nor  II.  Eugene 
 Odum  is  well  known  for  Contribution  in 
 ecology.  Eugene  Pleasants  Odum  (Father 
 of  modern  ecology).  Alexander  von 
 Humbolt  -  Father  of  ecology.  Arthur  G. 
 Tansley  -  First  to  coin  the  term

 Q.907.  Which  of  the  following  is  the 
 scientific  discipline  concerned  with  the 
 description  and  classification  of  the 
 Earth's topographic features? 
 (a) Geomorphology     (b) Acarology 
 (c) Ethology                  (d) Meteorology 
 Sol.907.(a)  Geomorphology  is  a  term 
 that  studies  change  in  the  earth’s  surface 
 due  to  these  exogenic  and  endogenic 
 forces,  where  exogenic  forces  are  the 
 external  forces  originating  within  the 
 earth’s  atmosphere  and  endogenic 
 forces  are  internal  forces  generated  from 
 within  the  earth.  Acarology  -  Study  of 
 mites  and  ticks.  It  also  involves  the  study 
 of  the  Acarina  order  -  it  means  study  of 
 arachnids like spiders, scorpions,etc.

 Q.908.  _______  species  are  those  species 
 of  plants  and  animals  which  are  found 
 exclusively in a particular area. 
 (a) Endemic      (b) Endangered 
 (c) Threatened (d) Critically Endangered 
 Sol.908.(a)  Endemic.  Example  of 
 Endemic  Species  -  Kangaroos  originally 
 endemic  to  Australia  and  found  nowhere 
 else  in  the  world.  Endemic  Species  of 
 India  -  Asiatic  Lion  (Gir  Forest),  Kashmir 
 Stag  (Kashmir  Valley),  Purple  Frog 
 (Western  Ghats),  Sangai  Deer  (Loktak 
 Lake), Nilgiri Tahr (Nilgiri Hills). 
 Q.909.  In  countries  like  Japan,  _________ 
 birth  rates  make  the  population  pyramid 
 _______ at the base. 
 (a) Low, Broad  (b) High, Broad 
 (c) Low, Narrow  (d) High, Narrow 
 Sol.909.(c)  Low,  Narrow.  A  population 
 pyramid  is  a  way  to  visualize  age  and  sex 
 of  a  specific  population.  Decreased 
 Death  rates  allow  numbers  of  people  to 
 reach  old  age.  A  country  in  which  birth 
 and  death  rates  are  high,  the  pyramid  is 
 broad  at  the  base  and  rapidly  narrows 
 towards the top (Kenya). 
 Q.910.  Which  is  the  second  largest 
 family in modern Indian languages? 
 (a) Sino-Tibetan (Kirata) 
 (b) Indo-European (Aryan) 
 (c) Austric (Nishada) 
 (d) Dravidian (Dravida) 
 Sol.910.(d)  Dravidian  (Dravida).  Major 
 Dravidian  Languages  -  Tamil,  Telugu, 
 Kannada  and  Malayalam.  They  are 
 mainly spoken in southern India, central 
 India, north-east Sri Lanka. 
 Q.911.  The  point  on  the  surface,  nearest 
 to  the  focus  of  the  earthquake,  is  called 
 _______. 
 (a) hypocentre  (b) epicenter 
 (c) centripiece  (d) semi centre 
 Sol.911.(b)  Epicenter.  Hypocenter  -  A 
 point  beneath  the  Earth's  surface  where 
 the  vibrations  of  an  earthquake  originate. 
 Richter  scale  is  the  most  common 
 standard  of  measurement  for 
 earthquakes.  The  Richter  Magnitude  -  It 
 is  a  measure  of  the  amount  of  energy 
 released  during  an  earthquake  and 
 expressed by the Richter scale. 
 Q.912.  Identify  the  southernmost  range 
 of the Himalaya from the following. 
 (a) Shiwaliks Range 
 (b) Trans Himalaya Range 
 (c) Lesser Himalaya Range 
 (d) Greater Himalaya Range 
 Sol.912.(a)  Siwaliks  Range.  Himalayan 
 Ranges:  Himadri  (Greater  Himalayas  or 
 Inner  Himalayas),  Himachal  (Lesser 
 Himalayas),  Shiwaliks  (Outer  Himalayas). 
 Divisions  (West  to  east)  :  Punjab 
 Himalayas  (between  rivers  Indus  and 
 Sutlej),  Kumaon  Himalayas  (Between 
 Sutlej  and  Kali  rivers),  Nepal  Himalayas 
 (between  rivers  Kali  and  Teesta),  Assam 
 Himalayas  (between  rivers  Tista  and 
 Dihang),  Purvanchal  (beyond  the  Dihang 
 river). 
 Q.913.  The  western  Ghats  and  the 
 Eastern  Ghats  meet  at  which  of  the 
 following places in southern India? 
 (a) Satpura Hills    (b) Nallamala Hills 
 (c) Nilgiri Hills        (d) Mahendragiri Hills 
 Sol.913.(c)  Nilgiri  Hills:  Highest  Peak  - 
 Doda  Betta.  Located  at  the  tri-junction  of 
 the  states  of  Karnataka,  Kerala  and  Tamil 
 Nadu.  Named  after  the  Neelakurinji 
 flowers  (purplish-blue  flowers)  found  in 
 Shola  forests.  Satpura  Hills  -  The  range 
 begins  in  eastern  Gujarat  and  runs  east 
 through  Maharashtra  and  Madhya 
 Pradesh  before  ending  in  Chhattisgarh. 
 Nallamala  Hills  -  Andhra  Pradesh, 
 eastern  ghats.  Mahendragiri  Hills  is 
 located  in  the  district  of  Gajapati,  Odisha 
 (eastern ghats). 
 Q.914.  Which of the following earthquake 
 waves are longitudinal waves? 
 (a) L waves  (b) P waves 
 (c) N waves  (d) S Waves 
 Sol.914.(b)  P  waves.  They  vibrate 
 parallel  to  the  direction  of  the  wave.  This 
 exerts  pressure  on  the  material  in  the 
 direction  of  the  propagation.  Earthquake 
 waves  (Seismic  waves)  are  vibrations  in 
 the earth that transmit energy. 
 Q.915  .  ___________species  are  the  species 
 which  are  not  found  after  searches  of 
 known  or  likely  areas  where  they  may 
 occur. 
 (a) Extinct  (b) Endangered 
 (c) Normal  (d) Vulnerable 
 Sol.915.(a)  Extinct.  An  endangered 
 species  is  a  type  of  organism  that  is 
 threatened  by  extinction.  Examples: 
 Black  Rhino,  Tiger,  Whale,  Red  Panda, 
 Chimpanzee, Asian Elephant etc. 
 Q.916.  Which  of  the  following  is  a 
 vulnerable species? 
 (a) Himalayan brown bear 
 (b) Horn bill 
 (c) Asiatic cheetah 
 (d) Asiatic elephant 
 Sol.916.(d)  Asiatic  elephant.  A 
 vulnerable  species  is  a  species  which 
 has  been  categorized  by  the  International 
 Union  for  Conservation  of  Nature  (IUCN) 
 as being threatened with extinction. 
 Q.917.  Which  of  the  following  pairs  of 
 "term - definition" is correct? 
 I. 
 Strait  -  a  narrow  strip  of  land  joining 
 two landmasses 
 II. 
 Isthmus  -  a  narrow  passage  of  water 
 connecting two large water bodies 
 (a) Only II  (b) Both I and II 
 (c) Neither I nor II  (d) Only I 
 Sol.917.(c)  Neither  I  nor  II.  Strait  - 
 Narrow  body  of  water  that  connects  two 
 larger  bodies  of  water,  Examples  -  Cook 
 strait  (New  Zealand),  Malacca  strait 
 (Indonesia-Malaysia),  Mesina  strait 
 (Italy-Sicily),  Bering  strait  (Alaska 
 -Russia),  Hormuz  strait  (Gulf  of 
 Persia-Gulf  of  Oman),  etc.  Isthmus  - 
 Narrow  strip  of  land  that  connects  two 
 larger  landmasses  and  separates  two 
 bodies  of  water,  Examples  -  Isthmus  of 
 Panama, Isthmus of Suez. 
 Q.918.  In  1962,  who  published  'The 
 History  of  Ocean  Basins',  which  outlined 
 the  theory  of  how  tectonic  plates  could 
 move, later called 'sea floor spreading'? 
 (a) George Edward Backus 
 (b) Alfred Wegener 
 (c) Louis Bauer 
 (d) Harry Hammond Hess 
 Sol.918.(d)  Harry  Hammond  Hess. 
 According  to  the  theory  of  plate 
 tectonics,  the  earth’s  lithosphere  is 
 broken  into  distinct  plates  which  are 
 floating  on  a  ductile  layer  called 
 asthenosphere. 
 Q.919.  The  national  census  does  NOT 
 recognise ________ groups within India. 
 (a) tribal  (b) ethnic 
 (c) religious  (d) scheduled caste 
 Sol.919.(b)  Ethnic.  Census  is  the  process 
 of  collecting,  analyzing,  and  sharing 
 demographic,  economic,  and  social  data 
 of  all  people  in  a  country  or  specific 
 region at a given time. 
 Q.920.  Which  of  the  following  mountains 
 is  formed  as  a  result  of  the  compression 
 of  tectonic  plates,  leading  to  the 
 formation  of  large  fold-like  structures  on 
 the Earth's crust? 
 (a) Ural mountain in Russia 
 (b) Vosges mountain in Europe 
 (c) Mt.Kilimanjaro in Africa 
 (d) Harz mountains in Germany 
 Sol.920.(a)  Ural  mountain  in  Russia. 
 Fold  Mountains  -  Himalayan  Mountains 
 in  Asia,  the  Alps  in  Europe,  the  Andes  in 
 South  America,  the  Rockies  in  North 
 America.

 (a) Mount Kilimanjaro in Africa

 Q.922.  'Anaconda'  ,  one  of  the  world's 
 largest snakes, is found in _______. 
 (a) Tropical evergreen forest 
 (b) Monsoon forest 
 (c) Temperate evergreen forest 
 (d) Temperate grasslands 
 Sol.922.(a)  Tropical  Evergreen  Forests 
 (Amazon  forest).  They  are  categorized 
 under  the  genus  Eunectes  (great 
 swimmers)  and  family  (Boidae).  They 
 thrive  very  well  in  hot,  humid  places  with 
 dense  foliage.  Tropical  rainforests  are 
 found  in  South  America,  Africa,  and 
 Southeast Asia around the equator. 
 Q.923.  Which  floating  fern  damages 
 aquatic  ecosystems  by  outgrowing  and 
 replacing  native  plants  that  provide  food 
 and  habitat  for  native  animals  and 
 waterflow? 
 (a) Salvinia minima  (b)  Osmunda regalis 
 (c) Giant salvinia      (d) Azolla pinnata 
 Sol.923.(c)  Giant  salvinia  (aquatic  fern, 
 native  to  south-eastern  Brazil,  Scientific 
 name  -  Salvinia  molesta).  Salvinia 
 minima  (common  salvinia  or  water 
 spangles),  Osmunda  regalis  (Royal  fern), 
 Azolla pinnata (mosquito fern, feathered 
 mosquito fern and water velvet). 
 Q.924.  ___________  refers  to  a  sanitation 
 system  in  which  toilets  collect  human 
 excreta  in  sealable,  removable 
 cartridges  that  are  transported  to 
 treatment facilities. 
 (a) Ecological sanitation 
 (b) Container-Based sanitation 
 (c) Community-led total sanitation 
 (d) Dry sanitation 
 Sol.924.(b)  Container  Based  Sanitation  . 
 Ecological  sanitation  is  that  protects 
 human  and  ecosystem  health  while 
 preventing  water  pollution,  conserving 
 energy, and capturing nutrients. 
 Q.925.  Which  of  the  following  mountain 
 ranges  is  home  to  the  second  highest 
 peak of the world, K2? 
 (a) Karakoram Range (b) Ladakh Range 
 (c) Pir Panjal Range    (d) Zanskar Range 
 Sol.925.(a)  Karakoram  Ranges  is  known 
 as  the  'backbone  of  high  Asia'.  It  is  also 
 known  as  Krishnagiri  which  is  situated  in 
 the  northernmost  range  of  the 
 Trans-Himalayan ranges.
